# SPEC FINAL — Kairos: a detector for process-level readiness and order violations in cFS

- Date: 2026-10-12. Role: lead architect of panel AR2. Status: **final architecture specification**. Nothing was built, installed, run against cFS, modified or committed for this document. Files written: this one, `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md` and `PI_DECISIONS.md` in the same directory.
- This document revises `SPEC_v1.md` after three adversarial reviews: [X1] formal soundness (`review_X1-soundness.md`), [X2] cFS fidelity (`review_X2-cfs-fidelity.md`), [X3] feasibility and evaluation (`review_X3-feasibility-eval.md`). Every critical and major issue is fixed in place; the minor ones are fixed as well. The disposition of each issue, and where its fix lives, is in `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md`. Decisions that only the PI can take are in `PI_DECISIONS.md`.
- Name: **Kairos** (tool prefix `kz`). The name is a label only; no name-collision search was made, and naming is a PI decision (`PI_DECISIONS.md`, P-NAME).
- Inputs and how they are cited:
  - AR1 reports [R01]–[R07] in `scratchpad/arch/`, cited with their own section and `path:line` citations.
  - Proposals [A] (`proposal_A-static-model.md`, CAIRN), [B] (`proposal_B-dynamic-predictive.md`, Sextant), [C] (`proposal_C-hybrid-layered.md`, Kairos). Judgments [J1] (formal), [J2] (cFS), [J3] (methods). Reviews [X1], [X2], [X3].
  - Research records: `CONDITIONS_POLICY.md` [POL], `cases/ledger.md` [LED], `results/R2_gdb_v701.md` [R2g], `results/R2_cord_v701.md` [R2c], `env/BASELINE_MEASURE.md` [BM]; the user's v0.1 design [v0.1].
  - The pinned tree `/home/user/work/procrace/cfs_ref/cFS` (cFS `088b2fa8`; cFE `c5fb2b4d`, OSAL `d2d877a6`, PSP `c4b3b0b6`). Code paths are relative to it. Lines re-read for this document are marked "(re-read)".
- Labels: **[F]** fact with citation (code, record or report). **[M]** measured, in [R07], a record, a review ("[X3, here]") or for this document ("measured here"). **[I]** inference or design reasoning; not executed. **[D]** design decision (an ADR in §11 records it). **[U]** unverified. Literature is cited only as [R04]/[R05]/[R06] verified it, with their status tags (V-doc, V-abs, V-me, P, UNVERIFIED). Where a concept is used without a verified citation, the text says "citation: open literature check".
- Policy [POL] (line numbers of `CONDITIONS_POLICY.md`): every bound, budget and parameter is one of
  - `from-reference` (POL L15): a code constant or pinned document, cited;
  - `deviation` (POL L16, L18–21): differs from or is absent from every reference, recorded with reason, effect and necessity. **Every value that a person chooses because resources are finite** (numbers of runs, work-unit caps, sample sizes) is a deviation, never `unspecified_by_reference`;
  - `unspecified_by_reference` (POL L17): no reference fixes it and **no value is chosen**; if the analysis needs a premise, it is recorded as an assumption.
  - Tool parameters follow POL L22: event-based control, or constants that a reference contains. Tool constants appear in code only as `KZ_REF(value, "path:line")` (§9.2). Values used in Kairos's own unit tests are not research conditions (POL L23).

---

## 0. Summary

**Revision 1.1 (2026-10-12):** answers the external review `history/review_EXT_20261012.md`; see `spec/REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md`. The amendment is summarized in §0.7 and recorded in ADR-039 to ADR-044 (§11).

### 0.1 What Kairos decides

For every **readiness-contract instance** in a cFS build (a use of a framework resource that requires that resource to be in some lifecycle state), Kairos produces a verdict record. Its axes are orthogonal (§1.10):

- **per semantics σ and OSAL model level M** (a cell for each (σ, M) that was analysed), and per bound b (b = ∞ for certificates, runs and transfers from them; each completed h for the explorer): the **safety record** (the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i`, with their routes and the decision route of `¬OK_i`, and the bad and good witnesses with their occurrence ordinals), the **class** derived from it, its refinements, the **reach** of the use in that σ (`proved(run | confirmed model path)`, `assumed`, `model(b)`, `unreached(b)`, `contested`), and the **progress result** (`stall-witness`, `no-stall-within(b)` or `unknown(reason)`, by default `unknown(not_checked)`) (§1.8, §1.10);
- `provider ∈ {present, none, env-absent, unknown}`;
- evidence on two axes: **realization** (`certificate`, `model(b)`, `predicted`, `predicted-modelled`, `predicted-extrapolated`, `realized`) and **naturalness** of realized runs (`natural`, `held-F0`, `gated-F1`, `timeout-exercised`; flag `derived`), with `n of N` and the pool key;
- shape; at the caller level the outcome label and harm; at the framework level `fw_doc` (per instance) and `fw_conf` (per cell); the observed consequence; chain links, scope (`in-scope` or `teardown`), B hash, Φ hash, tool hash (§1.6 "Three judgements", §1.10).

`class ∈ {inactive, protected (refinement: tolerated), fixed-order, no-final-status, schedule-dependent (refinement: timeout-dependent), ordinal-split, unknown(reason)}` is a **safety** classification derived from statements over finite executions: `¬Bad_i` (no in-scope occurrence of the use that is reached is VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED), `¬OK_i` (none is READY or TOLERATED) and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i` (none is decided not ready). An occurrence whose call returns before one of its conjunct LPs is `NOT_EVALUATED` and counted by none of them. `protected` establishes `¬Bad_i`; `fixed-order` establishes `¬OK_i` together with a decision route (a bad witness, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the occurrence's decision); `no-final-status` both. `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)`. Whether the use is reached at all is recorded on the reach axis. `schedule-dependent` needs a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal; witnesses at different ordinals only give `ordinal-split`. Permanent waiting, deadlock and other progress failures belong to no class: they are the per-cell **progress** result, which has the per-instance refinement `timeout-masked-liveness` and the root flag `tml-root`. A good-side flag `timeout-rescued` marks instances whose good occurrences all need a time-based transition. `σ ∈ {σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched], σ_E}`; `M ∈ {M_api, M_impl}`.

**No class means "safe", and no progress value means "live" or "deadlock-free".** The absence of an alarm is the absence of evidence and maps to `unknown(reason)`. No σ_E class is ever derived from the absence of bad runs. A universal class is never reported without its reach and progress values (§1.10).

### 0.2 The formal core on one page

1. **One typed event model (KEM) and one framework profile Φ_cFS.** Φ is written once in a small declarative DSL with a **typed core expression language over resource state** (§2.3). It declares resource kinds with lifecycle automata, operations with effects, a **linearization point (LP) per conjunct of every requirement** (with **capture points** for values that a caller loads earlier), HB rules with stratum and robustness tag, and contracts with a required state, a framework-predicate oracle, allowed not-ready outcomes with a reference horizon, the application or mission requirement `appreq`, harm, provenance (each record with its side: supplier, caller or rule) and an implicit **scope** ("before the shutdown request").
2. **Φ is interpreted three ways over the same event type**, through one evaluator in `libkz-core`:
   - **abstractly**, over configuration-closed per-actor skeletons in two views: a **certificate view** in which every unbounded loop is star-closed, and an **exploration view** cut at a horizon h. Census, MustBefore, PWB and certificates use the certificate view only;
   - **concretely**, over traces of the unmodified gcc binary: HB with vector clocks (handler-scoped program order on loopers), contract checking at LPs, lock-reversing prediction;
   - **operationally**, in a bounded per-candidate explorer (gated, late).
3. **Static ordering is a must-analysis.** MustBefore is a greatest fixpoint with meets over predecessors (T-PO) and over all spawn sites (T-SPAWN), with ∅ at every root. Observation edges (T-OBS) need a complete, transition-aware writer set, an **await predicate extracted from the code** and a falsifier. Writers that cannot lie between the falsifier and the observation are removed by checkable rules: own-actor no-path, PWB before the falsifier or after the observation (by the frontier or the once rule), and key separation. Branches that can only end in a terminal event are cut (T-TERM). Locks alone give no order.
   - Fixed-order and protected are certified over **precedes-whenever-both-occur** (PWB). PWB needs **once(τ)**: the actor runs at most once per execution, proved by an at-most-once analysis.
   - `kz proofcheck` re-validates the inference steps of every certificate. It is a separate module that shares no analysis code with the producer, except the shared `libkz-core`, and it works **relative to a stated trusted base**: the extracted facts, the skeleton, the closure lists, Φ and `libkz-core`. Steps that call `libkz-core`'s evaluator are marked "via T5". Every certificate and verdict carries a four-part **trust ledger**: re-validated steps, extracted facts, trusted entries and refutable items (§4.5.1). Certificate-mutation tests check the checker; **program mutants** test the analysis itself (§8.5).
4. **Writer sets** come from a field-based, memset- and out-pointer-aware writer extraction at the Clang AST level under an explicit typed-access assumption A-TYPE (stage S3a), with Φ-declared effects supplying keys and guards. Each writer entry carries a transition status and a **key status** (`MUST_KEY`, `MAY_KEY`, `NO_KEY`). Agreement between the two oracles is obligation O7. Andersen-style points-to over IR is a later option (S3b), adopted only if it removes measured blockers.
5. **Semantics.**
   - The four semantics: σ_U (untimed; retries exhaust only after their reference bound); σ_U∞ (σ_U without **time-based transitions**: no non-periodic timed wait times out; the last attempt of a delay-paced retry has no failing outcome, so it waits until it can succeed when it is one transition at level M, and otherwise leaves its actor stranded; the ES kill timer never kills an app that has not exited; periodic waits, timer ticks and the cleanup of exited apps stay; give-ups that Φ has not yet declared time-based, such as HS's application monitor and TIME's flywheel entry, also stay, and results that depend on them are labelled `tb-undeclared`); σ_R1[P_sched] (fixed priority on one CPU, parameterized by a cited scheduling profile; a separate transition system whose projection is contained in σ_U); σ_E (the real binary).
   - **Safety is defined on finite executions and progress on reachable states** of the product with an instance monitor (§1.8).
   - **Containment is prefix containment, stated per model level**:
     - the finite executions of σ_U∞ are exactly the finite executions of σ_U without time-based transitions;
     - after projection, the finite executions of σ_R1 are finite executions of σ_U at the same M;
     - every finite real run is a finite execution of (σ_U, M_impl).
   - **Maximal executions are not contained.** A timed wait whose predicate never holds ends a σ_U∞ execution that σ_U extends with its timeout.
   - M_api verdicts are claims relative to the OSAL API contract (portability claims). They transfer to σ_E only when the cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation, or when the M_impl verdict agrees.
   - **What transfers.** Each universal safety statement transfers down at the same M and b (a bounded statement to σ_R1 only from a model whose cone contains the σ_U cone), and to σ_E only with b = ∞. Good and bad witnesses transfer up. Reach, `tolerated`, `schedule-dependent`, `ordinal-split` and progress are computed per σ, except that deadlock cycles and leaked locks are stall states in every σ. Nothing becomes a σ_E frequency.
6. **Strata.** S (structural, must-order), O (observed synchronization, reorderable in prediction), C_σ (schedule constraints of σ_R1, never HB). Between-handler program order on a looper is **not** an HB edge; it survives only as a FIFO-derived constraint in prediction (CAFA, DCatch).
7. **Layers check each other.** Obligations O1–O10 plus the certificate checker. A contradiction suspends the rule or model involved, sets `unknown(model_fault)` with a model-fault record, ends the feedback loop for that Φ version, and is never resolved silently. A contradiction of an M_api-only certificate by the real binary is portability-hazard evidence, not a model fault.
8. **Evaluation measures misses.** A pre-registered recall protocol on history-derived program mutants and pre-fix/post-fix pairs, frozen before F2, reports detected / unknown / missed per layer, with class-correct reported apart where a class label exists, per mutant and per originating fix, with intervals whose unit and independence assumption are stated (§8.7); blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record (§7.5); a certificate contradicted by a mutant's documented behaviour is reported as a soundness failure.

### 0.6 What the reviews changed (summary; the full table is `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md`)

57 issues: X1 2 critical / 12 major / 9 minor; X2 1 / 9 / 4; X3 1 / 11 / 8. All are resolved in this document; none is rejected. The ten changes with the largest effect:

1. **Two skeleton views** (X1-C1). Universal verdicts and `inactive` come only from the certificate view, in which message loops are star-closed. Results from the h-cut view carry h in b. `kz proofcheck` rejects any certificate step in a truncated region.
2. **Containment per M** (X1-C2). M_api results are API-contract (portability) claims; σ_E transfer goes through M_impl. Since EXT-20261012 §4.2 the containment is stated over finite executions only (prefix containment); maximal containment is not claimed (§1.8).
3. **The R2 certificate is repaired and fully written out** (X2-01, X1-M2, X1-M3, X1-M8, X1-M1): own-actor no-path exclusion, once-PWB, MUST_KEY and allocation records, a code-extracted await predicate, a capture point for the stamped AppId, and every `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entry listed (App. A.1); the full four-part trust ledger follows EXT-20261012 §4.3 (§4.5.1, App. A.1 step 9a).
4. **σ_U∞ and the refinements are well defined** (X1-M5; revised by EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2). σ_U∞ removes time-based transitions. Stall is a progress result defined by dead actors, not a class outcome. timeout-dependent and timeout-rescued are derived from the per-σ safety results, and timeout-masked-liveness from the per-σ progress results.
5. **Shutdown is modelled as on pc-linux** (X1-M6, X2-04): the requester never returns and aborts after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; `OS_DeleteAllObjects` runs only on the PSP main thread after `CFE_ES_Main` returns and `ShutdownReq` is observed; contracts are scoped to "before the shutdown request".
6. **cFS mechanisms corrected** (X2-02 … X2-10): K-SELF.es at seven call sites; OSAL timed waits; SB-actor K-MODE; two-phase transmit and MsgLim; σ_R1 scheduling profiles; basis closure; header-carried MsgIds; loader scope.
7. **Evaluation measures what Kairos misses** (X3-C1): recall on program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, with soundness failures counted.
8. **Feasibility repairs** (X3-M1, X3-M4, X3-M5): attach cookies instead of IP keys; S3a AST-level writer extraction; a thin MVC with estimates and a cut list (its role as the first target is revised by EXT-20261012 §7: the validation ladder, ADR-044).
9. **POL compliance** (X3-M9, X3-M10): N and caps are deviations; budgets are deterministic work units; replay is byte-exact.
10. **Honest prior-work positioning and stronger baselines** (X1-M12, X3-M6): InitRacer, ExceptioNULL, RVPredict/GPredict credited; comparators given the same property information (a GPredict-style lock-reversing predictor with Req and the caller-level Allowed entries encoded as far as its formalism permits, and a plain state monitor), a DefUse-style learner, and helgrind/DRD as an external reference point; a stripped variant is never a baseline and, if kept, is a bundle ablation (P-ABL-ALLOWED) (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.1).

### 0.7 Amendment EXT-20261012 (external review of commit b4ac55f: items §4.1–§4.3, §5.1–§5.4, §6.4, §7 and §8)

1. **Safety and progress are separate** (§4.1). Classes are derived from safety statements over finite executions, `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i`, which certificates prove (L-PR, L-FO); both of the first two together are `no-final-status`, and `fixed-order` also needs a decision route. `schedule-dependent` needs a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal; otherwise `ordinal-split`. An occurrence begins at its call; a call that returns before one of its conjunct LPs gives `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts; `returned_and_tested` accepts only at a normal continuation; and no status or horizon verdict is decided after or by a process-terminal step. Permanent waits and deadlocks form a per-cell progress result, evaluated on the product with an instance monitor, with kinds (`deadlock-cycle`, `leaked-lock`), qualifiers (`idle`, `terminal-pending`, `choice-dependent`), wait-for roots and progress chains. Only `exh` decides it, within b; a realized hang with a wait-for fact can witness a stall; otherwise the result is `unknown(not_checked)`. (ADR-039)
2. **Finite executions, prefix containment and per-σ existence** (§4.2). Pref and Max are defined separately, and L-CONT is prefix containment. Maximal containment is not claimed. Reach, witnesses and progress are computed per σ and per bound. σ_U∞ removes every time-based transition (non-periodic timeouts, delay-paced last-attempt failures at their deciding transition at level M, and the give-ups that Φ declares, among them the forced kill of an app that has not exited), and stall is defined by deadness, with excluded states as sinks, instead of global quiescence. σ_R1 is a separate system with a projection. Without the explorer no refinement is established. A disagreement between an `exh` witness and a certificate goes to the new obligation O10. (ADR-040)
3. **The checker's trust scope is explicit** (§4.3). `kz proofcheck` re-validates inference steps relative to a named trusted base, marks the steps that call the shared `libkz-core`, and every certificate and verdict carries a four-part trust ledger. (ADR-041)
4. **Three judgements of a not-ready use** (§6.4). A not-ready use is judged at the framework level (what the supplier documents and does: `fw_doc` per instance, `fw_conf` per cell), at the caller level (what the using code must achieve: the new contract field `appreq`, and the Allowed entries that have a `caller` record or, under the default of PI item QT-4, only a `rule` record, whose acceptances are reported as rule-backed), and as the observed consequence, which is recorded for every not-ready occurrence. Every provenance record carries `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}`, and the kind `requirement` is added. A supplier's documented result never makes an occurrence TOLERATED, and a conflict between `appreq` and a `caller` entry leaves the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named until the PI sets a precedence. The SB no-subscriber loss is therefore reported as a framework-level `documented-not-error` result (cSB4305.5) beside a caller-level LOSS-UNDER-ORDER unless a consumer or mission reference decides (§1.11, §3.9, App. A.1 step 13a), not as a `documented_drop`. No status value, class, horizon kind or monitor kind is added. These edits follow the proposal of the review response; the PI items behind them (STATUS decision T2, re-asked; the adoption of this minimal design; QT-4) are open, and ADR-043 is revised if the PI decides otherwise. The word "contract" is kept while the renaming T1 is pending. (ADR-043)
5. **Scope of the checking beyond R2.** The definitions of items 1–4 were walked through C1, C2, C3, C5, R2 and the examples of App. A as expectations [I] for the review response; nothing was run for this amendment. Only R2 has path-execution evidence on v7.0.1 ([R2g]). C1's derived bases (D22), C2's role-derived pair (P-C2) and the 6.4.x bases of C3 and C5 (D13) are not decided, and the results that would distinguish the other cases (`timeout-dependent`, timeout-masked-liveness, stall witnesses, `tolerated`, `ordinal-split`) need the explorer (S7). The litmus programs LP-CASE-C1/C2/C3/C5 and the real code shapes of LP-ALLOW (App. A.8) reproduce each case's mechanism on the v7.0.1 OSAL; they are planned, not run. No validation on the other cases is claimed until they and the bases exist. Item 7 makes each case's path and control normative and requests the basis decisions now; without D13, D22 and P-C2, tool-level evidence on a case's own phenomenon exists for R2 only, and a result confined to R2 is reported as a single-case result (item 6).
6. **Evaluation: comparison kinds, three outcomes, an exposure record and units** (§5.1–§5.4). Original tools, re-implementations of prior techniques given the same property information or their own, and one-element ablations of Kairos are kept apart; neither an ablation result nor a re-implementation's silence is evidence about what a prior technique can express or what an original tool would report, and GPredict is described only as [R04] records it (P-PAPERS). Every tool is reported per labelled unit by detection, classification and evidence; detection counts an alarm and a `not-ready-unjudged` report alike for every tool, and only detection is compared by a paired test. Classification is compared with a pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1. The references call C1, C2, C3 and C5 races and make no class statement for R2; how "race" maps to the per-(σ, M) classes is PI decision P-CLASSGT, and until it is decided agreement or disagreement with those statements is reported as an outcome, never scored. A fix text is a ground-truth source, not a source of blindness: blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record. Every count names its unit (U1–U6), every test and interval states its unit and independence assumption (P-STATS), and the five design cases, several of which share a resource family, a fix release or a shape (§8.2), are not counted as independent samples. (ADR-042)
7. **First target and per-case paths** (§7, §8). The first target is rung V1 of a validation ladder: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates taken from the stage gates. The full thin-MVC gates stay, as rung V1b. R2's corrected-order control (V2, P-R2CTRL) and C1's rung (V3, D22) follow in parallel, and no V1 result is worded as a certificate before the lemma obligations of that wording are written (V0-P). Every design case has a normative path in §9.3: its cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned revisions and an expectation sheet frozen before any tool output); the first tool step that needs no new decision, which for C1, C2, C3 and C5 is a generic-layer result on a v7.0.1 counterpart and is never reported as case evidence; the first tool step on its own phenomenon, with the decision it needs (D22 for C1, P-C2 for C2, D13 with Q16 for C3 and C5); and its control: the pre/post-fix pairs of C1, C3 and C5, C2's 2 × 2, and, since R2 has no merged fix, R2's history and variant substitutes (§8.2). These decisions are requested now, and F1 waits until each is decided or declined with the consequence recorded (P-F1GATE). The review's sum of the S0–S3a estimates is correct arithmetic on [I] inputs and is not used as a plan value; no estimate exists for any rung. (ADR-044)

The disposition is recorded in `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md`, section EXT-20261012, and item by item in `REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md`.

---

## 1. Problem definition

### 1.1 Scope: "process-level"

Following v0.1 §2.1 and [R06 F1], *process-level* means ordering between the logical work of different cFS applications, service tasks and execution contexts, independent of OS process boundaries. In the native POSIX port they are all threads of one process ([F] [R05 §1 F1]; `osal/src/os/posix/src/os-impl-tasks.c:568`). The objects of interest are **framework resources**: named OSAL objects, ES app/task/lib records, service identities, SB routes and pipes, pipe capacity, TBL registry entries, phases and app states, CDS blocks, callbacks, loader symbols and files. Raw memory words are not objects of interest.

A memory-level data race found in passing (e.g. the unlocked EVS filter counters, [R02 §3.3]) goes into a separate class **M**. It is never counted as an order violation ([R02 §8.4 item 7]).

### 1.2 Actors, instances, once(τ) and the four identities

**Actors** `T = T_osal ∪ T_raw ∪ T_env` [F] ([R01 §2.6]; [R02 §1.1]):

- `T_osal`: tasks created by `OS_TaskCreate`: cFE core main tasks, app main tasks and child tasks (`ES_BG_TASK`, TIME's tone and local children, FM's child, …).
- `T_raw`: threads without an OSAL task record: the PSP main thread (the "ES startup thread" running `CFE_ES_Main`, later `OS_Application_Run`), the console writer, the OSAL timebase thread `cFS-Master`, signal-handler contexts, and the `linux_sysmon` worker (absent here, [R01 §2.6]).
- `T_env`: environment roots (ground commands, SBN peers, the stop command). They have no incoming HB edges ([R04 §4 row 17]).

A **static actor** τ is a closure entry: a spawn site plus an entry function (§3.1). An **actor instance** is `(τ, generation)`. Skeleton nodes belong to static actors.

**Actor-instance names across executions** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Occurrence ordinals compare occurrences of different executions (§1.6, §1.10), so actor instances need names that do not depend on the execution's identifiers. In the model an actor instance is named `(τ, generation, n)`, where n counts the executions of τ's spawn sites by its own canonically named spawner; n = 1 for once actors and raw threads. A callback is not an actor instance: its occurrences belong to the actor that runs it (the timebase thread for OSAL timer callbacks, TIME's task for synch callbacks, `ES_BG_TASK` for its jobs). In σ_E, the run-level identity `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)` below plays this role. It is mapped to `(τ, generation, n)` through the closure: the creator's canonical name and the call site of its creation event (`creator_event`), matched to τ's spawn sites (for a script app, its startup-script line, §1.7), give τ and its spawner; n is the creation ordinal among that spawner's executions of τ's spawn sites; the generation counts the app's restarts in the run. O5 checks the closure that the map uses. A pair of a run witness and a model witness at the same ordinal is formed only through this map; an identity it does not resolve forms no mixed pair.

**once(τ)** [D] (fixes [X1-M2]). τ is *once* iff in every execution at most one instance of τ exists. It is proved, never asserted:
- the PSP main thread is once by definition (it is the process entry, A3);
- an actor with spawn sites `s_1 … s_k` is once iff (i) every `s_i` lies in a once actor, (ii) no `s_i` lies in a star-closed loop region of the certificate view (§4.3), and (iii) the sites are pairwise exclusive: they lie in the same once actor and no skeleton path connects any two of them in either direction (a run of an actor is one path, so two exclusive sites cannot both execute);
- environment roots and asynchronous contexts (signal handlers, the exception path) are never once.

The facts used are recorded in every certificate that relies on them, and `kz proofcheck` re-validates them with its own once routine on the skeleton, which it trusts (§4.5, T2). For actors that are not once, frontier and once-PWB arguments are forbidden. In the restart family `B_restart` (one documented request, §3.8), the skeleton is duplicated per generation `g ∈ {0, 1}`, each copy with its own spawn site, so that each copy is once.

A node `n` is **once** iff its actor is once and `n` is not inside a star-closed loop region: `n` then occurs at most once per execution.

**Identity record** per instance: `⟨kind, osal_id, serial, tid, name, creator_event, generation⟩`. OSAL IDs are `(type << 16) | serial` and change across runs; cross-run correlation uses `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)` ([F] [R01 §1.2, §6.2 item 3]).

**Four identity fields per event**, never merged ([R06 F1]; v0.1 §2.1):

| Field | Definition | Why it is separate (cFS fact) |
|---|---|---|
| `exec(e)` | the actor instance that executes `e` | SB code runs in the caller's task ([R02 §2.4, §2.8]) |
| `owner(e)` | the ES app whose task set contains `exec(e)` at `e` (generation-aware); `⊥` for raw threads | an app has a main task and child tasks ([R02 §1.12]) |
| `svc(e)` | the stack of active frames: **API frames** (SB, EVS, TBL, …) and **on-behalf frames** `cb(registrant)` | TIME synch callbacks run in TIME's task for the registering app ([R02 §5.3]); FS callbacks run in `ES_BG_TASK` for SB/TBL ([R02 §6.1]); OSAL timer callbacks run in the timebase thread for the timer's creator ([R01 §3]) |
| `stamp(e)` | an identity value the code passes or stores explicitly (the `AppID` argument of `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID`; the pipe owner stored by `CFE_SB_CreatePipe`) | R2: the ES task executes SB code that stamps SB's unpublished AppId ([R2g §2]) |

### 1.3 Resources, kinds, lifecycle automata, generations and keys

A resource is `r = ⟨kind, scope, key, gen⟩`.
- `scope ∈ {process, persistent}`. Persistent resources survive the process: CDS, boot record, reset area, files on `/dev/shm/osal:RAM` (mounted as `/ram`), `EEPROM.DAT` ([R01 §5.3]).
- `key` is a name, numeric ID, `(MsgId, pipe)` pair, slot index, path, symbol with scope, or an **allocation record** `ρ(n)` (the record allocated by reservation node `n`, §4.2). In static mode a key may be **symbolic** (e.g. "the string in CF table field `chan[i].sem_name`") or `⊤`.
- `gen` is the OSAL serial, the cFE resource-ID serial ([R02 §6.2]) or a restart generation.

Each kind `k` has a lifecycle automaton `A_k = (Q_k, q0_k, Σ_k, δ_k)` (typestate; Strom & Yemini, IEEE TSE 1986 [V-me, R06 §9]). Anchors are in [R01 §1.5–1.7], [R02 §1–7] and §3. Examples:

| Kind | States | Publication anchor (LP) |
|---|---|---|
| `osal.obj<T>` | FREE → ALLOC → RES_C (name invisible) → RES_CN (name visible, RESERVED, type lock **not** held) → LIVE → RES_D → FREE | final ID write under the type lock, `osapi-idmap.c:1092`, unlock `:1105` ([R01 §1.5]) |
| `es.app` / `es.task` / `es.lib` | FREE → RESERVED → LIVE → RESERVED (cleanup) → FREE | script apps: reserve `cfe_es_apps.c:821`, publish `:869`, failure free `:893`; core apps: reserve `cfe_es_start.c:803`, publish `:818`, failure clear `:823` (re-read); task record `cfe_es_apps.c:694–703` |
| `es.self(exec)` | ABSENT → LIVE (both the executing task's task record and its owner's App record resolvable) | the publications above; read by `CFE_ES_GetAppRecordByContext` / `GetTaskRecordByContext` (§3.3) |
| `cell(loc)` | INVALID → VALID (generic: a shared location whose value is a resource identity) | the stores to `loc` that make its value valid; e.g. `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` written through `CFE_ES_GetAppID(&CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`). Display names such as `svc.identity(SB)` are labels of a cell, not Φ entries |
| `evs.reg(slot)` | UNREG → REG; `CFE_EVS_Register` first memsets the slot (`cfe_evs.c:56`, re-read): a disabler on re-registration | store `cfe_evs.c:106`, no lock ([R02 §3.1]) |
| `sb.pipe` | FREE → RES (OSAL queue name visible) → LIVE → RES_D → FREE | `cfe_sb_api.c:153,163,190–194` |
| `sb.route(m,p)` | NONE ↔ ROUTED | `CFE_SB_SubscribeFull` under the SB lock ([R02 §2.3]) |
| `sb.queue(p)` | depth `0..cap_B(p)` | `cap_B(p) = min(requested, msg_max)` **only when euid ≠ 0** (`osal/src/bsp/generic-linux/src/bsp_start.c:56–74`; truncation `os-impl-queues.c:98–105`, re-read); requested depth when euid = 0 [I: up to the kernel's hard limit]. [M] `msg_max` 10, 22 of 26 pipes truncated ([BM §3.1]) |
| `sb.msglim(m,p)` | `BuffCount 0..MsgId2PipeLim(m,p)` | limit per subscription; default `CFE_PLATFORM_SB_DEFAULT_MSG_LIMIT` = 4 (`cfe/cmake/sample_defs/example_platform_cfg.h:1098`); checked at the route snapshot (`cfe_sb_priv.c:1058–1064`, re-read) |
| `sb.mode.subreport` | OFF ↔ ON | store in `CFE_SB_SetSubscriptionReporting` (`cfe_sb_task.c:1226`), reached from SB's command handler `cfe_sb_task.c:296` (re-read) |
| `es.phase` | EARLY_INIT < CORE_STARTUP < CORE_READY < APPS_INIT < OPERATIONAL (monotone) | `cfe_es_start.c:87,183,194,216,234` |
| `es.appstate(rec)` | UNDEFINED < EARLY_INIT < LATE_INIT < RUNNING < WAITING < STOPPED | raise `cfe_es_api.c:573` (level by caller type and requested state, `:536–563`), `:471`, `:399` (external apps only); scan `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` (external apps, from RUNNING) (re-read) |
| `es.shutdown` | RUN → SHUTDOWN_REQ → TEARDOWN (process scope; §3.5) | `ShutdownReq = true` (`psp/fsw/pc-linux/src/cfe_psp_support.c:87`); `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (`cfe_psp_start.c:507`) (re-read) |
| `tbl.table(name)` | FREE → REGISTERED → LOADED → ACTIVE, with pending substates and an access lock flag | [R02 §4.2–4.5] |
| `es.cds(name)` (persistent) | ABSENT → REGISTERED(new \| restored) | `es/cfe_es_cds.c:311–477`; restore also within one run after a restart or reload (`:342–348, :461–472`) |
| `time.synchcb(slot)` | EMPTY → SET → EMPTY; keyed by **app index, without generation**; unlocked check-then-set | `cfe_time_api.c:628–640` (re-read); cleared by index `cfe_time_utils.c:1026–1034` |
| `loader.sym(name, scope)` | ABSENT → DEFINED; `scope ∈ {GLOBAL, MODULE(m)}` | provider `OS_ModuleLoad` with constant-evaluated flags: apps LOCAL, libraries GLOBAL (`cfe_es_apps.c:470–491`) |

### 1.4 Operations, effects, linearization points, capture points and model levels

An **operation** `op ∈ Φ.ops` has a binding (symbol or AST pattern, §3.17), parameters with roles (name, id, out-id, AppId, stamp, buffer, msg), an outcome map from result codes to `{ready, not_ready(q), error}` (each not-ready code with its **documented result**: the side effects that a supplier-side reference documents for it, such as a counter, an event or a drop, and the `prov` of that documentation, `side = supplier`, §1.6; e.g. `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg` without a route: `NoSubscribersCounter++`, a `SEND_NO_SUBS` event and `CFE_SUCCESS`, `cfe_sb_priv.c:1082–1087`, cSB4305.5), and a list of **effects**:

- `lifecycle(r, a ∈ Σ_k) when guard`;
- `use(r)`;
- `sync(obj, role)`, `role ∈ {acq, rel, give, take, flush, put, get, create, start, exit, join, observe, timeout}`;
- `env(input)`;
- `mark(handler_begin | handler_end)`.

**Every conjunct of a requirement has its own LP** [D] (fixes [X1-M1]). For a lifecycle effect, the LP is the instruction-level event inside the API at which the effect takes place. For a use, each conjunct `P_j` of `Req` declares the point at which the program reads the state that `P_j` is about:

- **registry conjuncts** (the state lives in a framework table): the LP is the read inside the API, e.g. EVS's slot check;
- **value conjuncts** (the requirement is about a value the caller passes, role `stamp`, `key` or `id`): the LP is the **capture point**, the load from which the argument's value derives. `kz-static extract` (AST def-use, S3a) emits `derives(arg, loc, site)` when the argument is a copy chain (loads, stores, parameter passing, casts without arithmetic) from a single shared location `loc` read at `site`. The conjunct is then evaluated at `site` over the support location `loc`, i.e. over `cell(loc)`. This is the **translation rule T-DERIVE**: it is generic and carries no knowledge of SB. If no unique `derives` fact exists, the capture point is the call itself, and every certificate that would need it is withheld (`unknown(writer_incomplete)`).

Examples [F]:

| API | Effect / conjunct | LP |
|---|---|---|
| `OS_*Create` | `lifecycle(osal.obj, name_visible)` | `OS_OBJECT_INIT` writes `name_entry` (`os-shared-idmap.h:106–111`), type lock released |
| | `lifecycle(osal.obj, publish)` | final ID write under the type lock, `osapi-idmap.c:1092` |
| `OS_*GetIdByName` | `use(osal.obj(name))` | the name search under the type lock |
| `CFE_ES_GetAppID` | `use(es.self(exec))`; out-pointer store | `CFE_ES_GetAppRecordByContext()` under the ES lock (`cfe_es_api.c:748–763`, re-read) |
| `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg` | `use(sb.route(hdr.MsgId, ·))` | **route_lookup** under the SB lock (`cfe_sb_priv.c:1007–1104`); then one **put** LP per destination outside the lock (`:1177–1216`) (§3.9) |
| `CFE_EVS_Register` | `lifecycle(evs.reg(slot(owner)), clear)` then `REG` | memset `cfe_evs.c:56`; store `:106` |
| `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` | `valid(arg.AppID)` (value conjunct) | capture point from `derives` (e.g. the load of `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` at argument evaluation in SB code, `cfe_sb_api.c:226–228`) |
| | `evs.reg(arg.AppID) ∈ {REG}` (registry conjunct) | the slot check, `cfe_evs.c:185–194` |
| `CFE_ES_WaitForSystemState` | raise (`lifecycle(es.appstate(owner))`) then `observe(SystemState)` | the store under the ES lock at `cfe_es_api.c:573`, only if `GetAppRecordByContext()` ≠ NULL; then each unlocked read of `SystemState` (`:585`) |

**Readiness is evaluated per conjunct at its LP.** The program's own outcome code at the use is a consistency check on the computed state (O4, §2.5). Value conjuncts map to their own outcome codes (for K-ID: `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` ⟺ the value conjunct failed; `NOT_REGISTERED` ⟺ the registry conjunct failed, `cfe_evs.c:185–194`).

**Model levels** [D] (A's M_api/M_impl, [A §1.5]; containment per level fixes [X1-C2]):

- **M_api**: each OSAL API is one atomic transition at its main LP; create is atomic w.r.t. lookup; lookups return found or not found. `Pref(B,σ,M_api)` is the set of finite executions of a **hypothetical OSAL backend that conforms atomically to the OSAL API contract**. M_api verdicts are therefore **claims relative to the API contract** (portability claims), not claims about the POSIX binary.
- **M_impl**: for the OSAL idmap, task, binsem and queue paths, the skeleton builder projects the OSAL shared and POSIX source onto a lower vocabulary Φ_L-1 (pthread mutex and condvar incl. `pthread_cond_timedwait`, `sem_*`, `mq_*`, `clock_nanosleep`, `pthread_create/cancel/join`). The API is split at its LPs, the RESERVED windows appear, the bounded RESERVED wait is modelled with **both** wake causes (§3.2), and the lock-leak path exists ([R01 §1.5–1.8, §2.2]).
- OSAL-internal locks constrain interleavings only under M_impl. They never create shape-HB edges at either level ([R01 §3, §6.2 item 2]).
- **M-relevance.** An operation is *M_impl-relevant* if M_impl models it differently from M_api: idmap create, lookup, delete and token conversion; task create, prepare and delete; binsem; queue create, put and get. A cone (§4.10) or certificate is M_impl-relevant if it contains such an operation.
- **When the levels disagree**, the report says so:
  - protected under M_impl, violable under M_api → **portability hazard**;
  - violable only under M_impl → **implementation-defect candidate** of OSAL POSIX at the pin;
  - an M_api-only certificate contradicted by an observation of the real binary (O4(b)) → **portability-hazard evidence** (the binary is outside the API-contract model there), never a model fault.

### 1.5 Events, templates, traces and concretization

An event is `e = ⟨id, actor, seq, op, phase, args, outcome, keys, site, ι(e), lp_intervals, t⟩`:
- `seq` is the per-actor index (the program-order token);
- `phase ∈ {call, return, point}`;
- `outcome` is the result the program itself received ([R06 F11]);
- `ι(e)` is the identity quadruple;
- `lp_intervals` brackets the LP of each conjunct between two observed points (§4.8);
- `t` is a timestamp kept **as evidence only**: physical time gives no HB (Lamport, CACM 1978 [V-abs]).

A **trace** π is a sequence of events in which each actor's events appear in program order.

A **static template** has the same type with symbolic fields and a path condition. Templates are **context-qualified**: `(static actor, inlining call string, site)` [D]. The SB event site inside `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` reached from `CFE_ES_TaskInit` is therefore a different template from the same site reached from ES's command loop. Recursion makes the affected summary `unknown`. Inlining full call strings is worst-case exponential in call depth; the fallback is stated in §4.3 (fixes [X1-m7]).

A template `t` *concretizes* to an event `e` (`t ⊒ e`) iff the actor and call string of `e` match `t`, `site(e) = site(t)`, and the fields of `e` satisfy `t`'s constraints. This single type lets the static, trace and model layers share one checker.

### 1.6 Readiness contracts

A contract is `κ = ⟨id, U, sel, Req, Oracle, Allowed, appreq, harm, prov, authored, guard, scope⟩`:

- `U`: a use pattern (operation plus site predicate, in the pattern language of §2.3).
- `sel`: a resource selector from event fields to a key (e.g. `osal.countsem(arg.name)`, `evs.reg(arg.AppID)`).
- `Req`: a conjunction `P_1 ∧ … ∧ P_n` of required lifecycle or value predicates, each written in the core expression language (§2.3) and each with its own LP (§1.4).
- `Oracle`: the **framework validity predicate** for each conjunct. Examples: `CFE_ES_AppRecordIsMatch(CFE_ES_LocateAppRecordByID(a), a)`; `EVS_GetAppDataByID(a) != NULL && EVS_AppDataIsMatch(...)` ([A §7.2]). It is never executed by Kairos. Statically its read set defines the **support locations** of the conjunct (§4.2). Dynamically the program evaluates it, and the outcome code is the observed image of the conjunct (O4) ([J1 X9]).
- `Allowed`: the not-ready outcomes that the contract accepts, from a **finite vocabulary**, each with a **continuation horizon taken from a reference** [D] ([J1 X2]); which entries decide statuses (the caller-level Allowed) is stated under "Caller-level Allowed" below (EXT-20261012 §6.4):

| Allowed entry | Meaning | Horizon (reference) | Static check | Trace check | Explorer check |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `none` | no not-ready outcome is acceptable | the use itself | — | — | — |
| `documented_drop(src)` | the using code drops its own request by design, documented by a **caller-side** text in the module that owns the use, or, where no comment exists, read by the `rule` of an undocumented drop in the using code (side `rule`, §1.6): EVS's own-ID guard, whose comment belongs to EVS, the using code (`cfe_evs_utils.c:613–623`; `caller`); **SB's per-task event recursion guard** (`cfe_sb_priv.c:305–328, 773–790`; "This prevents recursive events from occurring", `cfe_sb_priv.h:550–558`; `caller`) and its missing-task-record drop (`cfe_sb_priv.c:315–318`, no comment; `rule`), whose user is SB's event code. A supplier's documented drop of a caller's request (SB's no-subscriber drop, cSB4305.5) is framework-level information in the op's outcome map (§1.4), not a `documented_drop` entry of the caller's contract | the use itself | outcome class | outcome code | outcome |
| `returned_and_tested(codes)` | the use returns a documented code; the caller branches on it and reaches its **normal continuation**: the node named in the entry with its citation, by default the nearest post-dominator of the test node, in the containing function's graph made acyclic per loop iteration, that is not the function's return or exit and at which neither the returned status nor any variable written on the not-ready branch is live (none: the occurrence can only be REJECTed). An **error exit** (`CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE`, `CFE_PSP_Restart`, a terminal op) or a return of a non-success status before it is not one, and a REJECT at the normal continuation wins, unless a reference documents that the API propagates the status to its own caller (e.g. `cfe_evs.h:98`); then test nodes inside the API are ignored and the caller's handling is judged by the same rule (§1.6 horizon monitors) | until the normal continuation (ACCEPT), or until a REJECT event: an error exit, an error return without documented propagation, the next operation of the same actor on the same resource, or actor exit | path check within the horizon: every `Sk^cert` path from the not-ready branch reaches the normal continuation without a REJECT event (ACCEPT), or none does (REJECT) | continuation events up to the normal continuation or a REJECT event; trace ends early → `unknown(horizon)` | suffix |
| `retry_until_ready(codes, bound)` | the caller retries on the not-ready codes | the loop's bound, constant-evaluated in the build (e.g. CF's effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`) | loop unrolled exactly (§4.3) | later iteration observed ready within the bound | suffix within the bound |
| `fallback(op′)` | the caller uses a documented alternative | the next operation of the actor | path check | next event | suffix |
| `defer_by_design(src)` | the reference documents the deferral (TO_LAB subscribes after `CFE_ES_WaitForStartupSync`, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`) | the documented deferral point, executed by the deferring actor, which may not exist yet when the use is decided | path check from the deferring actor's control node, or, if it does not exist yet, from its nearest existing spawn-chain ancestor through the spawn edges; no REJECT while a path to a process-terminal step exists, and none by or after such a step | event | suffix |
| `exit(code, src)` | a documented exit | actor exit | path check | event | suffix |
| `unspecified_by_reference` | no reference says whether the not-ready outcome is acceptable | — | — | — | — |

  `unspecified_by_reference` sets harm `unspecified_by_reference`. For route and capacity contracts with no must-deliver `appreq` it also sets the outcome label **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER**. Neither erases the observed effect, which the verdict records as its `consequence` (§1.10).

  **Horizon monitors** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2). Each Allowed entry other than `none` and `unspecified_by_reference` is read as a monitor μ over a finite execution ρ. The entry names its **monitored actor**, its ACCEPT event and its REJECT event:
  - `documented_drop(src)`: the using actor; ACCEPT at decision if the outcome is the documented drop; REJECT at decision otherwise.
  - `returned_and_tested(codes)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when, after a not-ready outcome in `codes`, it passes a **test node** (a branch on the returned status) and then reaches the entry's **normal continuation**; REJECT if the outcome is not in `codes`, or if, before ACCEPT, the actor reaches an **error exit**, returns a non-success status from the function that contains the test, performs its next operation on the same resource, or exits; a REJECT event at the normal continuation wins. The normal continuation is named in the entry with its citation where the reference documents one. Otherwise the **default normal continuation** of a test node n in function f is the nearest post-dominator c of n in f's certificate-view control-flow graph made acyclic per loop iteration (loops unrolled exactly are taken unrolled; in every other loop of f that contains n, the back edge is redirected to the loop's exit) such that c is neither f's return nor f's exit, and neither the returned status nor any variable written on the not-ready branch between n and c is live at c (f's return value counts as a use); if there is no such c, there is no default normal continuation and the occurrence can only be REJECTed. An error exit is a call of `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_PSP_Restart`, or a terminal op (§4.3). Where a reference (`prov` of kind `api_doc` or `code_admission`) documents that an API returns the not-ready status to its own caller, the entry may say so (**documented propagation**; e.g. `cfe_evs.h:98` for `CFE_EVS_Register`); test nodes and joins inside the API are then ignored, its return of the status is not a REJECT, and the obligation starts at the API's return in its caller, whose handling of the returned status is judged by the same rules. A propagation read only from code gives no such entry.
  - `retry_until_ready(codes, bound)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when a later attempt of the same loop instance is READY (a `NOT_EVALUATED` attempt is not); REJECT when the loop takes its exhaustion path, or an attempt's outcome is outside `codes`.
  - `fallback(op′)`: the using actor; ACCEPT if its next operation is `op′`; REJECT if it is another one, or the actor exits first other than by a process-terminal step.
  - `defer_by_design(src)`: the actor that executes the documented deferral point (for TO_LAB, its main task, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`), which may not exist yet when the occurrence is decided; ACCEPT when it executes the deferral point; REJECT if it terminates first, other than by a process-terminal step.
  - `exit(code, src)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when it reaches the documented exit with `code`; REJECT if it exits otherwise, other than by a process-terminal step.

  μ also gives REJECT as soon as no `Sk^cert` path from the monitored actor's control node reaches the ACCEPT event and none reaches a process-terminal step (the path check); for an actor that does not exist yet, the path starts at its nearest existing spawn-chain ancestor and runs through the spawn edges. μ makes no ACCEPT and no REJECT on or after a process-terminal step (§4.3), so an occurrence that is PENDING when the process ends stays PENDING; real runs have no events after such a step. ACCEPT and REJECT are final in every extension of ρ; otherwise μ is OPEN. The static, trace and explorer checks of the table compute μ on `Sk^cert` paths, on traces and on explorer prefixes respectively. **OPEN never counts as ACCEPT.** A horizon that never ends is a progress matter (§1.8). A trace that ends while μ is OPEN gives `unknown(horizon)`.

  **Three judgements** [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4). A not-ready use is judged three times, and the record keeps the three apart:
  - the **framework-level judgement** (API behaviour): what the supplier returns and does when the resource is not ready. It is stated by the op's outcome map with its documented results (§1.4), checked by O4(a), `kz conform` (§4.11), the §3.9 counter oracles and the trace's event attempts, and recorded as `fw_doc` and `fw_conf` (§1.10). It never makes an occurrence TOLERATED;
  - the **caller-level judgement** (application or mission requirement): what the using code must achieve and which not-ready outcomes it accepts. It is stated by `appreq` and the Allowed entries read below, and it alone decides the statuses below, the classes of §1.10, the outcome label and `harm`;
  - the **observed consequence**: what happened (failure, loss, exit), recorded as `consequence` with its observed counts whatever the other two judgements say (§1.10).

  **Side of a provenance record.** Each `prov` record carries `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}`, judged by the author for the pair (record, κ) and recorded. `supplier`: the record says what the supplier returns or does when the resource is not ready, or whether the framework counts that result as an error (`\retval`/`@retval` lines; cEVS3103.1 and cSB4305.3–.5 for uses of applications that call EVS or SB; a documented propagation such as `cfe_evs.h:98`; a barrier's documented timeout; a `code_pattern` record over the supplier's own code, such as K-SELF.es's source in §2.3). `caller`: a text that says what the using code — the caller, or for route and capacity contracts the consumer, or the application or mission on whose behalf it runs — must achieve, or how it handles the not-ready outcome and that this handling is acceptable (`cfe_evs.h:169–172`; CF's admission `cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`; TO_LAB's deferral `to_lab_app.c:64–68`; a guard comment in the module that owns the use; an application's or a mission's requirement). `rule`: no text; the record names a fixed rule of Kairos and the code it was applied to, and states no acceptance (the default normal continuation of `returned_and_tested`; the loop-derived contracts of §7.1 source 4; a drop in the using code that no comment documents). The side belongs to the pair (record, κ): cSB4305.5 is `supplier` for K-ROUTE, whose user is the transmitting application, and `caller` for the K-ID instance at SB's `SEND_NO_SUBS` event site, whose user is SB's own event code.

  **Caller-level Allowed.** An Allowed entry is read as a horizon monitor if it has at least one `caller` record, or if it has a `rule` record and no `caller` record (a `rule`-only entry; default, PI item QT-4). An entry whose records are all `supplier` decides no status and is kept as framework-level information. **Under this default a rule, not a caller reference, decides the statuses of `rule`-only entries**: their ACCEPTs are reported as rule-backed TOLERATED, counted apart from caller-backed ones, tier `code-derived`, and a documented return code never discharges the caller's obligation by itself. Under the strict alternative, `rule`-only entries are `unspecified_by_reference`. `appreq` and the precedence between it and the entries are given in the `appreq` bullet that follows. **In the statuses below, in §1.10 and in the outcome label, "Allowed" means the caller-level Allowed.**

- `appreq` [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4): the reference that states what this use must achieve for the calling application or the mission — an obligation addressed to the caller (K-ID: `cfe_evs.h:169–172`), an application's own requirement, a consumer's must-deliver statement, or a mission requirement — or `unspecified_by_reference` (the default). It is never taken from a supplier-side text or from a rule (§1.6 "Three judgements"). The **caller-level Allowed** is derived in this order: (1) with no `caller` or `rule` entry, it is `none` when `appreq` is specified and `unspecified_by_reference` otherwise, and `Allowed = none` requires a specified `appreq`; (2) a specified `appreq` that requires the effect that `rule`-only entries give up prevails over them, and they are dropped from the caller level; (3) a `caller` entry that accepts an outcome a specified `appreq` excludes, or two `caller` entries that accept incompatible continuations, are recorded as a **conflict** with both references; until the PI sets a precedence, the conflicting entries give the caller-level Allowed `unspecified_by_reference`, so their occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named in the verdict, never TOLERATED, with the outcome label `not-ready-unjudged` (not LOSS-UNDER-ORDER where the conflict involves a must-deliver `appreq`); (4) otherwise the entries decide. Whether an entry and `appreq` conflict, or whether `appreq` requires what a rule gives up, is an authoring judgement recorded with both references. A change of `appreq` or of a recorded conflict after F1 is an `oracle refinement` (§7.2).
- `harm`: the effect class when the outcome is not allowed. Vocabulary: `event_lost_silently`, `status_ignored`, `wrong_identity_stamped`, `app_error_exit`, `app_zombie` (record used forever, never cleaned), `service_task_absent` (a core task exits while the system continues), `processor_reset_requested`, `process_abort` (on pc-linux a reset request aborts after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`, §3.5), `panic` (core-sync failure), `task_never_runs`, `hang_or_deadlock`, `terminal_pending(panic | abort)`, `message_lost`, `unspecified_by_reference`. Two values have two uses that the record keeps apart. `task_never_runs` and `hang_or_deadlock` can be the harm of a VIOLATING occurrence: an Allowed analysis assigns them, for example K-SPAWN.osal (§3.3), where a self-lookup fails and the entry then never runs. That is a safety result of the instance that violated. Under the caller-level rule of §1.6 (`Allowed = none` requires a specified `appreq`), K-SPAWN.osal and K-LOCKBAL, whose `none` rests on supplier-side texts only, are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with harm `unspecified_by_reference` and the effect recorded as their consequence, until an `appreq` is recorded or the PI moves them to an implementation-defect family (PI item QT-9). They can also be the harm of a **progress result** (§1.8): a dead actor on the way to the use, inside the use, or holding the use's horizon open. That is a progress result of the instance that waits. `terminal_pending(panic | abort)` is a progress harm only: the waiting actor is dead, but a process end that the stop input does not cause stays reachable from every state, so the process ends by a panic (`panic`) or by another abort, a restart request or `main`'s return (`abort`) rather than hanging; the two values keep §3.3's distinction between `panic` and `process_abort`.
- `prov`: provenance records `(kind ∈ {api_doc, requirement, code_admission, code_pattern, issue, researcher}, side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}, path:line or requirement ID, sha256 of the text)`. `requirement` is a cFS functional requirement (`cfe/docs/cFE_FunctionalRequirements.csv`, an application's `*_FunctionalRequirements.csv`) [D, PI decision T2]. `side` is defined under "Three judgements" above; it is recorded for the pair (record, κ).
- `authored`: authoring time, author, and which design cases (C1, C2, C3, C5, R2) the author had inspected for that API ([R06 §5.3 item 4]).
- `guard`: version guard (e.g. `cfe ≥ 6.6.0`) for profile reuse across revisions (§7.6).
- `scope` [D] (fixes [X1-M6], [X2-04]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.1): every contract carries the implicit scope conjunct **"no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP of `es.shutdown` precedes the occurrence's decision event"**, decided when the occurrence's readiness is decided (§1.6 Instances); conjunct LPs after the decision do not matter. For a single-conjunct contract this is "precedes the use's LP". Scope is part of the instance, `i = (κ, t, key, scope)`: occurrences outside the default scope belong to instances of the separate family **K-TEARDOWN** (§3.5), the complement of the default scope, classified by the same statuses and reported apart; their progress is `unknown(teardown_scope)` (§1.8). The exclusion of states after a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP applies to default-scope instances only.

**Instances.** An instance is `i = (κ, t, key, scope)` for a context-qualified template `t` matching `U` and `scope ∈ {default, teardown}`. Statuses are defined on **finite executions** ρ (§1.8). Take an occurrence `u` of `t` in ρ, executed by actor instance `a`:
- `u` **begins at its call**, the execution by `a` of t's call node. A registry conjunct's LP is the read inside the call's extent. A value conjunct's LP is its capture point: the last execution by `a`, before the call, of a capture site that the `derives` fact links to the call's argument (or the call itself, §1.4); the capture is evaluated when it executes and is attached to the call it feeds. A capture that no call follows begins nothing.
- `u` is **decided** at the earliest of: its call, if an attached capture is false there; the first later conjunct LP at which the conjunct is false; the last conjunct LP, when every conjunct holds; the return of the call. If the call returns without passing every conjunct LP (e.g. on an argument check before a name search), `u`'s readiness is not evaluated: its status is `NOT_EVALUATED`. Its **decision event** is the call, the deciding conjunct LP, or, when the call returns first, the last conjunct LP it passed (or the call). Between its call and its decision, `u` is **open**. Every occurrence is decided by the return of its call, so an actor has at most one open occurrence of `t`.
- Its **ordinal** is `(a, k)`: it is the k-th occurrence of `t` with key `key` begun by `a` in ρ, where `a` is named across executions by `(τ, generation, n)`, n counting the executions of τ's spawn sites by its spawner (§1.2).
- It **belongs to** the instance `(κ, t, key, default)` iff no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes its decision event in ρ, and otherwise to the K-TEARDOWN instance `(κ, t, key, teardown)`; "in scope for i" means "belongs to i". Conjunct LPs after the decision do not affect scope. Both instances have the statuses below; they are classified and reported apart.
- An occurrence whose decision event follows a process-terminal step (§4.3) has no status and belongs to no instance; real runs have no events after such a step.
- For a conjunct `P_j`, `state_ρ(r, P_j, u)` is the state of `A_k` after the lifecycle effects on `r` (same generation) whose LPs precede the LP of `P_j` at `u` in ρ.

The **status** of an occurrence that belongs to i and is decided in ρ is:
- `NOT_EVALUATED` iff its call returned without passing every conjunct LP (above): it is neither ready nor not ready, is no witness, is never an ACCEPT of `retry_until_ready`, and is counted by no statement of §1.10;
- otherwise `READY` iff every conjunct holds at its LP;
- otherwise one of the following, by the horizon monitor μ of `Allowed` (above):
  - `TOLERATED` iff μ = ACCEPT in ρ;
  - `VIOLATING` iff μ = REJECT in ρ, or `Allowed = none`;
  - `PENDING` iff μ = OPEN in ρ;
  - `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` iff `Allowed = unspecified_by_reference`.

`READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` and `NOT_EVALUATED` are final: they hold in every extension of ρ (L-STATUS, §6.2). `PENDING` may become `TOLERATED` or `VIOLATING` in an extension, or stay `PENDING` in every extension. **A `PENDING` occurrence is never counted as `TOLERATED`.**

A finite execution is **bad** for `i` iff it contains a `VIOLATING` in-scope occurrence, or, where Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference`, a `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` one. Every extension of a bad finite execution is bad. A finite execution is a **good witness** for `i` iff it contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence. Witnesses record the ordinal of that occurrence. The outcome label says which meaning was used. A permanent wait, before the use, inside it or inside a horizon, is never a violation: it is a **progress** result (§1.8).

**Violation chains** ([v0.1] §8; [R06 §3]): `i₁ ⇝ i₂` when an occurrence of `u₂` exists only because of `u₁`'s not-ready outcome (control or data dependence; for traces, frame containment plus Φ `emits` annotations). A chain records **every** branch of the consequence, not one (fixes [X2-02]). Example: 17 of R2's 24 identity uses are `SEND_NO_SUBS` events in ES's context ([F], [R2g §2]); they come from ES's EVS packets, MsgId 0x808, transmitted before any route ([I], [R2g §2]).

**Progress chains** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Violation chains link occurrences, and a stall state has no occurrence of the waiting use. A second kind of chain therefore links progress results: `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` iff some stall state of `i₂` (§1.8) has a wait-for root that (P1) waits inside `u₁`'s open occurrence or holds open the horizon of a PENDING occurrence of `u₁`; or (P2) can be satisfied only by events that the certificate view dominates by the READY outcome of `u₁`'s call, while the finite execution contains an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready on that path (e.g. a K-SELF.es raise skipped inside the core App-record window); or (P3) waits on a lock leaked by, or an actor lost on, the not-ready branch of an occurrence of `u₁` (K-LOCKBAL, K-SPAWN). The conditions are decidable on the stall state, its finite execution and the certificate view. The root instance is reported and the dependent progress results are attached to it, as for violation chains (App. A.2 item 4). A stall whose wait-for root satisfies none of (P1)–(P3) (a cycle of predicate waits, a provider that simply never gives) has no chain root; it is grouped by its wait-for root set only (§1.8), and an unchained instance with timeout-masked-liveness carries `tml-root` itself. Progress chains are stored per cell (`kz_verdict_cell.progress_chain`), apart from violation chains.

### 1.7 The analysis basis B

`B = ⟨Build, Config, Init, Env, Input⟩`. Every item is recorded with its value, its source and its policy status (fixes [X2-08], [X3-M3]):

- **Build**: commits; gcc 13.3.0 and the recorded flags ([R06 K1]); `link.txt` archive semantics ([R07 §3.4]); generated files by hash, including `cfe_static_module_list.c` (core order, [R02 §1.3]) and the generated module version table, whose absence changes R2's count from 24 to 11 ([R2c §4.2, §7]).
- **Config**:
  - the **startup-script selection rule**, evaluated by `kz-static basis`: on a PROCESSOR reset ES first tries the volatile `/ram/cfe_es_startup.scr` (`CFE_PLATFORM_ES_VOLATILE_STARTUP_FILE`) and falls back to the non-volatile script (`cfe_es_apps.c:130–168`, re-read); on POWERON it uses the generated script (2 libraries + 15 apps, [R03 §6.1]). The path actually opened is recorded by the closure boot (O5);
  - built tables (`*.tbl`) cross-checked byte for byte against their source initializers through the gcc DWARF layout ([A §4.1 step 4]); platform constants constant-evaluated in their TU; `osconfig.h` limits; the PSP module list.
- **Init** (the persistent state before the run): boot record, CDS and reset-area SysV segments, user-reserved memory, `/dev/shm/osal:RAM`, `EEPROM.DAT` ([R01 §5.2–5.3]); **decoded CDS contents and critical-table images**, since critical tables are restored from CDS on PROCESSOR resets (`cfe_tbl_api.c:117–131`, re-read). A snapshot S0 is taken per run. The **Init hash is content-only** (no mtimes; `EEPROM.DAT`'s mtime changes every boot, [BM]).
- **Env**: kernel (`6.18.44-fc-v114`, drifted from the recorded `-fc-v80`, [R07 §1]); 4 vCPU; scheduling policy as realized (`SCHED_OTHER` for all 27 threads, [M] [BM §0]); affinity (the PSP pins `CFE_*` tasks to CPU 0, `cfe_psp_start.c:184–189`, not yet measured, [R06 K5]); **euid** (decides queue truncation, §1.3); **CWD** (fixes the `/cf` mapping `cfe_psp_start.c:378`, the SysV key files and `EEPROM.DAT`); effective queue depth (`msg_max` 10); platform capabilities relevant to providers (`/proc/schedstat` absent, [R01 §2.6]); **stratum** cold (first run after a VM reboot) or warm ([BM]: 2570 ms vs 1613–1633 ms to OPERATIONAL; 849 ms vs 2.2–3.3 ms load span).
- **Input**: external commands with **event-based** triggers; the **reset-type input** (`-R` on argv, else the boot record's `NextResetType`, `cfe_psp_start.c:411–434`, re-read); the stop method (PI decision D17).

**INIT** [D] (fixes [X1-m1], [X2-12]). INIT is the **program-entry state**: the static-initializer image of the closed program for process-scope locations, and the decoded S0 for persistent-scope locations. EarlyInit stores (e.g. `memset(&CFE_ES_Global, 0, …)` at `cfe_es_start.c:82`; `memset(&CFE_SB_Global, 0, …)` at `cfe_sb_init.c:50`, re-read) are ordinary W/D writer entries, ordered against falsifiers and uses by MB, once-PWB and the frontier rule like any other writer.

**Run sequences and generations.** For cross-run contracts (K-PERSIST), the end state of run *n* becomes `B_{n+1}.Init`; the reset type of run *n+1* depends on how run *n* ended ([M] PO → PR → PO after signal stops, [R01 §5.2]). Within one run, restarts and reloads create generations (`B_restart`), and K-PERSIST is also checked across generations (CDS `ALREADY_EXISTS` within one run, `cfe_es_cds.c:342–348`).

**Normalization** (§4.13): a run that did not end through `CFE_PSP_Restart(POWERON)` leaves the SysV segments and a boot record with `NextResetType = PROCESSOR` ([X3-M3]). Before any further pooled run, one unobserved normalization boot is made; Init is then checked against the canonical S0.

Every verdict carries the hash of B. **A configuration variant is a new basis B′, never a "new bug"** ([R06 §5.4]).

### 1.8 Scheduling and timing semantics σ

**Transition systems and executions** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2; fixes [X1-C1]). For σ ∈ {σ_U, σ_U∞} and level M, `TS(B,σ,M) = (Q, q₀, T, en_σ, δ)`. Its states and transitions are those of §4.10 item 2 (effects at LP granularity and explicit local choices), built over the **certificate view** with no horizon. `en_σ(q)` is the set of transitions σ enables in state q; σ_U and σ_U∞ share Q, q₀, T and δ. A Φ delay op is a transition of its actor that changes only its control state (σ_U's yield). The ES kill timer is abstract: at each scan of a used external app with `AppState > RUNNING` (`cfe_es_apps.c:1114`), `ES_BG_TASK` either finds the timer not yet expired (`not-yet-expired`) or expires it (`:1126–1136`), and the expiry is one of two outcome-labelled transitions, `expire-stopped` when the `AppState` read in the same scan is STOPPED (stored by the app's `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `cfe_es_api.c:399`) and `expire-waiting` when it is WAITING (a commanded app that has not exited, `:1139–1146`); a timer of 0, which an app has from creation (`:819`) unless a command set it (`:1139–1146`), always expires at the scan. σ_R1[P_sched] is a **separate system** `TS(B,σ_R1,M)` whose states add a discrete clock and a ready/blocked status per actor: a delay op's transition is the actor's block step, which moves its control as σ_U's delay transition does; clock-advance and wake steps change only the added components; the clock advances to the earliest pending wake only when no actor is ready; timeouts are enabled as in σ_U. The projection π erases clock-advance and wake steps and forgets the added components (L-CONT). Analyses evaluate statuses and stall predicates on the product of `TS` with a deterministic, non-blocking **instance monitor** that records, per actor, the open occurrence, the last captured value per capture site, the PENDING occurrences with their horizon-monitor states and the occurrence ordinal per key, and whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP has occurred (L-MON, §6.2). The explorer's bounded models use the exploration view, and their results carry h in b.
- **Finite executions** `Pref(B,σ,M)`: the sequences `t₁…t_n` with `t_k ∈ en_σ(q_{k−1})` and `q_k = δ(q_{k−1}, t_k)`. The set is prefix-closed. `Pref_b` is the same set for a bounded model. **Reachable states** `Reach_σ(q)` are the states that finite executions reach from q.
- **Maximal executions** `Max(B,σ,M)`: finite executions that end in a state q with `en_σ(q) = ∅`, and infinite executions that satisfy σ's fairness condition. σ_U and σ_R1 have no fairness condition; σ_U∞ is strongly fair. In this document `Exec(B,σ,M)` denotes `Max(B,σ,M)`; for σ_U, statements over every execution in which a node occurs (MustBefore) read the same over Pref and over Max.
- **Real runs** (σ_E): finite LP-granular event sequences of the gcc binary under B. A run ends by the stop method (D17), by `abort()` (`cfe_psp_support.c:107, :120`), by the return of `main` after the teardown, by a harness kill or by a watchdog. A run is a finite execution; it need not be maximal, and no claim depends on whether it is.
- **Time-based transitions** `TB ⊆ T`:
  - the timeout transition of every timed wait that is not a **periodic wait**, i.e. every await with a timeout: soft barriers, polls including L1 summaries, and the OSAL RESERVED wait's timed wake. A periodic wait is a timed wait inside a star-closed loop whose timeout and success outcomes both continue the loop (e.g. `ES_BG_TASK`'s wait, `cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:185`); its timeout, like an OSAL timer expiry, paces periodic work and is not in TB;
  - the failing outcome of the **last attempt** of every delay-paced retry, taken at the attempt's deciding transition at level M: the failing outcome of the attempt itself when it is one transition at M (an L1 summary; a Φ op that M models as one transition, such as CF's lookup at M_api); otherwise the outcome-labelled branch that takes the attempt's stale failing result into the exhaustion path (for CF at M_impl, `cf_cfdp.c:1285` taken with `NAME_NOT_FOUND` in the last iteration). The attempt's own transitions are not in TB, so removing TB never leaves an actor holding a lock;
  - every transition that Φ declares time-based. Φ must declare every countdown or clock comparison whose expiry abandons a wait or acts on another actor, with its paired "not yet expired" outcome. Declared here: the **forced kill**, i.e. the outcome `expire-waiting` of the ES kill timer (`AppTimerMsec`, §3.8, §3.14), its expiry for an app whose main task has not reached its STOPPED store (`cfe_es_api.c:399`). The outcome `expire-stopped`, including the expiry after a voluntary `CFE_ES_ExitApp` (timer 0), only paces the app's cleanup and is not in TB, and `not-yet-expired` is never in TB. Not yet declared: HS's application monitor, whose countdown, decremented once per HS wakeup cycle, calls `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_ES_RestartApp` on a monitored app at zero (`hs_monitors.c:107–160`; inactive with the default `NOACT` tables, §4.3), and TIME's flywheel entry after `CFE_PLATFORM_TIME_CFG_START_FLY` seconds without a tone (`cfe_time_tone.c:1172–1186`). Until Φ declares them, σ_U∞ keeps them, and a σ_U∞ result of an instance in a basis with an active HS application-monitor entry, or of an instance whose contract reads TIME's clock state, carries the sub-label `tb-undeclared` (§1.10).

  A TB transition of an actor is **paired** if the actor has, at the same control node, a non-TB transition whose enabling depends on shared state (the success outcome of a timed wait or of a one-transition last attempt); otherwise it is **unpaired**.
- **What each notion uses.**
  - Safety (statuses, bad finite executions, the safety statements and classes of §1.10, witnesses) is defined on finite executions.
  - Progress (dead actors and stall states, below) is defined on reachable product states.
  - No verdict depends on Max or on a fairness assumption. Max is used only to state what is not claimed (L-CONT).

| σ | Definition | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **σ_U** (untimed) | Any interleaving of enabled transitions. `OS_TaskDelay` is a yield. Every **timed wait** (an await with a timeout: soft barriers, polls, the OSAL RESERVED wait's timed wake) may take its timeout transition at any point after it starts. A **count-bounded retry** takes its exhaustion branch **only after the reference number of attempts** (exact unrolling, §4.3). Polls read the current value. Priorities and affinity impose nothing. `CFE_PSP_Panic` (→ `abort()`, `cfe_psp_support.c:116–121`) is terminal. `CFE_PSP_Restart` is terminal **for its caller** (it never returns: `OS_TaskDelay(CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY)` then `abort()`, `cfe_psp_support.c:60–107`, re-read); the other actors continue (§3.5) | Reference semantics for certificates and model classification |
| **σ_U∞** (TB-free) | σ_U without time-based transitions: `en_σU∞(q) = en_σU(q) \ TB`. No non-periodic timed wait times out, and the ES kill timer never kills an app that has not exited (`expire-waiting` is removed); an app that has exited is still cleaned up (`expire-stopped` stays), so restarts, reloads, deletes and the K-LIFE window still occur. Give-ups that Φ has not declared time-based stay (sub-label `tb-undeclared`). Periodic waits and timer ticks stay. The last attempt of a delay-paced retry (a count-bounded loop whose iterations are separated by a Φ delay op, recognized by pattern, §4.3) has no failing outcome: when that attempt is one transition at level M it is enabled exactly when it would succeed; when it is several transitions at M (its test reads several values outside one critical section and outside one atomic read, so that L1 does not summarize it, §4.3; or it calls a Φ op that M splits at its LPs, such as an OSAL lookup at M_impl), its actor runs the attempt and is then **stranded** on the stale failing result, and deadness tests on it and on the actors it could enable are undecided (`unknown(unmodelled)`). `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M)` is therefore exactly the set of finite executions of σ_U that contain no time-based transition (L-CONT). An actor whose awaited predicate can never become true is **dead** (below); that is a progress result, not a class outcome. A poll that L1 summarizes (e.g. `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, whose test reads lie inside one ES-lock critical section) is one transition at M, so its actor is dead rather than stranded; with L1 switched off it is stranded. For Max only, scheduling is strongly fair (Musuvathi & Qadeer PLDI 2008 [V-abs, R05 §2.1 L111]); no verdict depends on it [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2; replaces the quiescence rule of [X1-M5]) | Separates "violable only through a time-based transition" from "violable in the ordering logic", and exposes permanent waits that time-based transitions mask |
| **σ_R1[P_sched]** (RT, one core) | Fixed-priority preemptive scheduling on one CPU, **parameterized by a cited target scheduling profile** `P_sched` (fixes [X2-07]): policy and timeslice; the priority-mapping function; the startup/root-thread priority; priority inheritance on mutexes; per OSAL backend. Delays **block** with a discrete-time wake order computed from constant-evaluated delay arguments (fixes [X1-m5]); computation between blocking points takes zero time (assumption A-ZT, recorded on every σ_R1 verdict that depends on a delay). Timeouts as in σ_U. Model-only by default | Platform classes where priorities order (C3 at 6.4.1, [LED §1a]) |
| **σ_E** (ENV) | The executions of the real binary under the recorded Env. Not modelled; sampled by observation and confirmation runs | Empirical witnesses and frequencies |

**The POSIX scheduling profile** `P_sched^posix` [F] (for any RT realization or σ_R1 claim about OSAL POSIX): when RT scheduling is permitted, OSAL selects `SCHED_RR` unless FIFO's priority spread exceeds `OS_MAX_TASK_PRIORITY` (255), which never holds on Linux (1..99) (`os-impl-tasks.c:348–365`, re-read); the root thread, which runs `CFE_ES_Main`, gets the highest priority (`:391–409`); OSAL priorities are compressed many-to-one by `OS_PriorityRemap` (`:78–106`); mutexes use `PTHREAD_PRIO_INHERIT` (`os-impl-mutex.c:82`). Profiles for RTEMS and VxWorks (6.4.x, H-V) are `unspecified_by_reference` until their backend sources are part of a basis (D13); no σ_R1 verdict is issued for them before that. [I] Under `P_sched^posix` the core App-record window cannot open on one CPU while the startup thread is runnable, because the startup thread outranks every task.

**Dead actors and stall states** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1; replaces the quiescence-based `stall` of [X1-M5]).
- **Terminated and dead.** In `TS(B,σ,M)`, an actor instance `a` is **terminated** in q if it is at its exit or after a terminal operation (T-TERM, §4.3). Deadness is evaluated per instance i on the reachable product graph `G_i` in which every state excluded for i (below) is a **sink**: paths stop at the first excluded state, and transitions out of excluded states do not count. `a` is **dead for i** in a non-excluded state q iff it is not terminated and no non-excluded state of `Reach_{G_i}(q)` enables a transition of `a`.
  - Deadness is a property of q, σ and i, not of a schedule. It needs no fairness, and an actor that is enabled but starved is never dead. An actor that only the teardown after a shutdown request, or only the model's continuation after a terminal step, could revive is dead.
  - Under σ_U a timed wait is never dead, because its timeout stays enabled. Under σ_U∞ a non-periodic timed wait is dead once its predicate can no longer become true.
  - Under σ_R1, deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation: `a` is dead iff no reachable σ_R1 state has a transition of `a` that σ_U enables in the projected state. Priority starvation never makes the starved actor itself dead; an actor waiting on a starved giver can be dead under unbounded σ_R1 (a higher-priority zero-time loop under A-ZT starves the giver forever).
