  - Under σ_U∞, an actor every one of whose σ_U-enabled transitions is an unpaired time-based transition is **stranded** (the failing-result branch of a last attempt that is several transitions at M). A deadness test on a stranded actor, or on an actor that a stranded actor's effects could enable (as for censored actors, §4.10 item 8), is undecided (`unknown(unmodelled)`).
  - Deadness of an actor is the same in a TS state and in every product state over it (L-MON).
- **Ahead, per occurrence.** `u` is **ahead** of `a` in a product state if a path of `Sk^cert`, using intra-actor edges and spawn edges, leads from `a`'s control node to the call node of `u`'s template (if `a` has no open occurrence) or to a remaining conjunct LP of its open occurrence. Earlier occurrences are irrelevant. The local choices on that path are its **ahead support**.
- **Process-terminal steps and excluded states.** Φ marks a terminal declaration `process_terminal` iff its terminal step ends the process. On pc-linux these are: `abort`; `CFE_PSP_Panic` (`abort()` at `cfe_psp_support.c:120`); the restart requester's step after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` (`abort()` at `:107`); and the PSP main thread's return from `main` after the teardown (`bsp_start.c:247`). A reachable product state is **excluded** for i if its finite execution contains a process-terminal step, or, for an instance i whose scope is `default`, a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP (scope, §1.6). The model lets the other actors continue after a terminal step (T-TERM cuts only the caller); those continuations are sinks for deadness and the qualifiers, and no status or horizon verdict is decided in them (§1.6). Instances of K-TEARDOWN have progress `unknown(teardown_scope)`.
- **Stall states.** A non-excluded reachable product state is a **stall state for i** iff one of the following holds:
  - **pre-use stall**: some actor instance dead in it has no open occurrence of `u` and has `u` ahead;
  - **in-use stall**: some actor instance dead in it has an open in-scope occurrence of `u` (it is inside the call, before the decision);
  - **horizon stall**: it holds a `PENDING` in-scope occurrence of `u` whose monitored actor (§1.6) is dead, or does not exist yet while every existing actor that can still spawn it is dead.

  The dead actors named by the clauses are the stall's **witnessing actors**; the record keeps the set of clauses that hold.
- **Kinds and qualifiers**, recorded on every stall witness:
  - kind `deadlock-cycle`: the dead actors contain a cycle of untimed acquisitions of holder-released objects (mutexes, OSAL type locks), each held by the next actor of the cycle; kind `leaked-lock`: a witnessing actor waits, untimed, to acquire a holder-released, non-recursive object (OSAL type locks are `PTHREAD_MUTEX_NORMAL`, `os-impl-idmap.c:222`) whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and either is terminated or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release of the object that does not first acquire it again (K-LOCKBAL); otherwise kind `other`;
  - `terminal-pending`: on `G_i` without the transitions that inject B.Input's stop event (D17), a **process end**, i.e. a process-terminal step or a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP (which on pc-linux is always followed by the requester's `abort()` or `main`'s return, §3.5), stays reachable from every state reachable from the stall state; the process ends by a panic or an abort that the stop did not cause. The qualifier is computed on the closed cone, so it is relative to the cone (G11);
  - `idle`: every witnessing actor is blocked at an **idle wait**, the first blocking wait of an iteration of a star-closed message or periodic loop (e.g. `ReceiveBuffer`, `cfe_time_tone.c:1266`, `sch_lab_app.c:98`);
  - `choice-dependent`: the **choice support**, i.e. the over-approximating local choices (a star-closure iterate/exit, a havoc value, or a data branch the certificate view leaves unresolved) made by the actors of the stall's wait-for closure (the witnessing actors, the actors they wait on transitively and the holders of the objects they wait on) on the finite execution, together with the ahead support, is non-empty;
  - the **wait-for graph** has an edge from each dead actor to every actor that could satisfy its wait (the holder of an object it acquires; every actor whose `Sk^cert` region contains a give, put or send to an object it takes or receives on; every actor whose region writes the support of a predicate it awaits; the actor it joins; its spawner if it is not yet spawned), restricted to the part reachable from the witnessing actors; a **wait-for root** is a terminal strongly connected component of that graph (a lock cycle for `deadlock-cycle`, or a cycle of predicate and object waits), or a node without outgoing edges together with the wait it leaves unsatisfied; for `leaked-lock`, the leaked object with its holder. The record keeps the stall's **root set**;
  - a stall witness is **confirmable** iff its kind is `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` and it is neither idle nor terminal-pending; a **hang state** is a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending.
- **Harm of a stall**, in this order of precedence:
  - `terminal_pending(panic)` or `terminal_pending(abort)` if the stall is terminal-pending, after the process end that stays reachable (`CFE_PSP_Panic`, or another abort, a restart request or `main`'s return);
  - otherwise `task_never_runs` if every witnessing actor lies on the spawn chain to `u`'s actor (or, for a horizon stall, to the monitored actor) and that actor instance does not yet exist;
  - otherwise `hang_or_deadlock`.

  The record lists every dead actor and the object or predicate it waits on.
- **Progress chains.** `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` iff some stall state of `i₂` has a wait-for root r such that (P1) an actor of r is inside an open occurrence of `u₁` or is the monitored actor of a PENDING occurrence of `u₁`; or (P2) every event that could still satisfy r's wait (a write to its predicate's support, a release, give, put or send on its object, its actor's spawn) is dominated in the certificate view by the READY outcome of `u₁`'s call, and the finite execution contains an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready on that path; or (P3) r's object is a lock acquired in an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready (K-LOCKBAL), or r waits on an actor that terminated on the not-ready branch of an occurrence of `u₁` (K-SPAWN). Each condition is read from the product state, its finite execution and the certificate view, so chains are decidable. The root instance is reported; dependents are attached (as in App. A.2 item 4). A stall whose wait-for root involves no not-ready outcome (a cycle of predicate waits; a provider that simply never gives) has no chain root and is grouped by its wait-for root only. Progress chains are stored per cell, apart from violation chains (§1.6).

**Progress result** per cell (σ, M) and bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). It takes one of three values:
- `stall-witness(S)`, S the non-empty set of {pre-use, in-use, horizon} clauses that hold, when a stall state for i is reachable. The witness is the finite execution to that state, with its kind, qualifiers, wait-for root, harm and realization: `model(b)` from the explorer, or `realized` from a run with a wait-for fact (§4.13).
- `no-stall-within(b)`, when `exh` shows that no stall state for i is reachable within b and no deadness test was undecided (§4.10).
- otherwise `unknown(reason)`, with reason `not_checked` when no route ran; `bound` (censored), `budget`, `unmodelled` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure), `teardown_scope`, `model_gap`, `model_fault`.

Certificates, prediction, observation without a wait-for fact and transfer between σ never set it, with one exception: a stall state of kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` is a stall state in every σ in which its finite execution is admissible (L-DEAD (c), (d)). No value means "live", "deadlock-free" or "safe". The progress result does not cover executions in which `u`'s actor instance, or an actor on its spawn chain, terminates without an occurrence (a task lost to F7, `osapi-task.c:128–137`; the timebase thread's silent return), or that end in a panic; their only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence that led to them, with the harm of its consequence chain (`task_never_runs` for a lost task, `panic` for a core-sync failure, `process_abort` for a restart request), and a σ_U panic reached through a spontaneous timeout has no such root and leaves no record (§6.4 item 34). Stall witnesses from a model are relative to that model, for two reasons:
- `Sk^cert` over-approximates paths (A-SKEL) and adds local choices, such as a star-closed loop's exit (hence the `choice-dependent` qualifier);
- deadness is preserved neither when behaviour is added nor when it is removed (L-DEAD).

A model stall witness is therefore a candidate until it is confirmed, only confirmable kinds can be confirmed, and `no-stall-within(b)` is a statement about the model only (G11).

**Containment, per model level** (L-CONT, §6.2; fixes [X1-C2]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). Under A7-impl (Φ_L-1 semantics over-approximate kernel, libc and OSAL POSIX on the M_impl paths), A7-api (the remaining OSAL primitives are linearizable at their LP on the real backend, A10), A3, A4, A9 and A-SKEL:
- `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M) = {ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M) : ρ contains no time-based transition}`, for each M;
- `π(Pref(B,σ_R1,M)) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, for each M. Here π erases σ_R1's clock-advance and wake steps, keeps every other step (a delay's block step is σ_U's delay transition), and projects states onto σ_U's components;
- the projection of every finite real run of B onto LP-granular events is in `Pref(B,σ_U,M_impl)`.

**Maximal containment is not claimed.**
- `Max(B,σ_U∞,M) ⊄ Max(B,σ_U,M)` in general. In litmus LP-TW (App. A.8), the σ_U∞ execution that ends with the waiter dead is maximal in σ_U∞, while σ_U still enables its timeout.
- Real runs are finite executions, and their end states may still enable σ_U transitions.
- For σ_R1, maximal containment modulo π is an unproved conjecture [I] and is not used.

The prefix version of `Pref(B,σ_E) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M_api)` is **not** claimed either: M_api excludes real POSIX behaviours (`INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` in RESERVED windows, the type-lock leak, silent task loss). For a cone that contains no M_impl-relevant operation, the two levels have the same projected finite executions (Lemma L-MLEVEL, §6.2) [I].

**Transfer rules** [D] (revised by EXT-20261012 §4.2):
1. Rule 1 has two parts.
   - (a) **Safety down, statement by statement.** A statement `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` or `¬NR_i` established under σ_U at level M and bound b holds, as the same statement, under σ_U∞ at the same M and b, and under σ_R1[P_sched] at the same M either with b = ∞, or with a bounded b when the σ_R1 model's cone contains the σ_U model's cone at the same h (L-CONE-R1). The receiving statement carries the flag `SAFETY_TRANSFERRED`; whether a transferred `¬OK_i` is deciding is recomputed in the receiving σ (§1.10); the receiving cell's class follows from its record, so transferred and locally established statements combine without order dependence.
   - (b) **Existence per σ.** Reach, witnesses (hence `tolerated`, `schedule-dependent` and `ordinal-split`) and the progress result are never transferred down, **except** stall states by L-DEAD (c), (d). Each is computed in its own σ; otherwise the cell reads `reach = assumed` and `progress = unknown(not_checked)`. If `exh` shows that no finite execution of σ′ reaches the use within b, the cell's reach at b is `unreached(b)`, and a transferred universal class reads "… if reached; not reached within b". The v1 label `inactive_σ′` is withdrawn; `inactive` is a static class only.
2. **Transfer to σ_E** is allowed only for safety statements with **b = ∞**, and only from M_impl, or from M_api when (i) the instance's cone and certificate contain no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL over prefixes, sub-label `mlevel-lemma`), or (ii) the M_impl result exists and agrees. The σ_E cell then states that no in-scope occurrence in any real run of B is `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` (`¬Bad_i`), `READY` or `TOLERATED` (`¬OK_i`), or other than `READY` (`¬NR_i`). Otherwise the σ_E cell stays `unknown(model_level)`, and the M_api result is reported as a portability claim.
3. **Witnesses up.** The following are good or bad witnesses under σ_U at the same M, with their occurrence ordinal, when they contain an in-scope occurrence with a final status (`READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`):
   - a finite execution of σ′ (σ_U∞, or σ_R1 after π);
   - a real run at M_impl, or at M_api under L-MLEVEL or with an M_api-conformance check of the prefix of every actor of the instance's closed cone (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2; §4.7 item 6).

   `PENDING` and `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences are not witnesses. A run witness is paired with a model witness at the same ordinal only through the map from run identities to canonical names (§1.2). **Stall witnesses do not transfer** (LP-TW: a σ_U∞ stall exists while σ_U has none), except by L-DEAD (c), (d).
4. Nothing transfers *into* σ_E as a frequency, an existential class, a reach value or a progress result. A σ_E `schedule-dependent` class needs two distinct natural runs with a bad and a good occurrence at the same ordinal (§1.10).
5. A blanket "no transfer across σ" ([A §6.3 item 3]) is dropped as too weak; B's transfer of a trace-local certificate ([B §4.3]) is dropped as too strong.

**Refinements, derived from the per-σ results and stored with their own bound** [D] (fixes [X1-M5], [X1-m3]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). `Bad_i(σ)` and `OK_i(σ)` denote the existence of a bad finite execution and of a good witness (§1.10), at the cell's M and b.
- **timeout-dependent** (refines schedule-dependent under σ_U): `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent`, `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` established, and the use reached under σ_U∞ (reach `model(b)` or `proved`). By L-CONT the first two conditions are equivalent to: every bad finite execution of σ_U contains a time-based transition. Without σ_U∞ reach the flag is not set; `timeout-rescued` and the σ_U∞ reach then show that the use is reached only through a time-based transition.
- If `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds, the violation lives in the ordering logic, and no refinement applies.
- **timeout-rescued** (good-side flag): `OK_i(σ_U)` and `¬OK_i(σ_U∞)` established. Equivalently, every good witness of σ_U contains a time-based transition: the instance is good only because some wait gave up.
- **timeout-masked-liveness** (progress refinement of the σ_U cell, **per instance**): some product state reachable under σ_U∞ is a **hang state** for i under σ_U∞ but not under σ_U. Every σ_U escape from it passes through a time-based transition, which turns a permanent wait into a violation, a panic or another continuation.
- **tml-root**: `tml-root(i₁)` iff no progress chain leads into `i₁` and some `i₂` with `i₁ ⇝_p* i₂` (`i₂ = i₁` included) has timeout-masked-liveness. It marks a progress-chain root with TML members, and an unchained instance with TML itself. The report shows each `tml-root` instance and the number of its chained members; each member's own cell carries timeout-masked-liveness and the flag `CHAINED`, and members are not reported one by one. The root's own σ_U∞ progress can be `no-stall-within(b)`. Example: a raise skipped inside the core App-record window makes `MainTaskSyncDelay` unsatisfiable (§3.3); under σ_U∞, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`, whose test reads lie inside one ES-lock critical section, §4.3), the startup thread is dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835`, and the core tasks already created are dead in their `CORE_READY` polls; under σ_U the startup thread panics. The instances behind it carry timeout-masked-liveness and are chained to that window's K-SELF.es instance, which carries `tml-root`.
- **Without the explorer, no refinement is established.** `timeout-dependent` and `timeout-rescued` need universal σ_U∞ statements, which neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide; prediction can only show `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` or `OK_i(σ_U∞)` and so rule a refinement out.
- **Correspondence with v1** [I]. v1's `timeout-dependent` required that the σ_U∞ continuations from each pre-timeout state reach only good outcomes; the new refinement keeps the "not bad" part, and the "no stall" part is the progress refinement. Every v1 `timeout-masked-liveness` instance whose v1 stall state is a hang state under σ_U∞ has the new refinement; v1 also counted states in which the use's actor waits at an idle wait, which the new refinement excludes. The converse does not hold, because the new one also covers hang states that no bad σ_U execution of the instance passes through, which progress chains attach to their root.

Confirmation runs in which a **time-based transition** (§1.8: the timeout of a non-periodic timed wait, the failing last attempt of a delay-paced retry, the forced kill) occurs during a hold, or after it and before the witness's deciding events, on an actor of the instance's closed cone, are labelled `timeout-exercised` (naturalness axis), never natural σ_E. It is decided from the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 (c), not from return statuses alone: a retry that exhausts because the hold keeps its provider back (C1, App. A.4 step 5) sees `NAME_NOT_FOUND`, not a timeout status, and an OSAL-internal timed wake is never returned. Timeouts of periodic waits do not count.

### 1.9 Ordering relations

Φ's HB rules are inference rules `e₁ → e₂` with a matching condition, a stratum, a robustness tag and a code citation.

**Strata** ([C §0]) [D]:

| Stratum | Meaning | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| **S** (structural) | Holds in every execution that contains both endpoints on the given path: program order within an actor (inside one handler for loopers), task create → child start, join, script sequencing, and **guarded observation edges** whose writer set is complete, whose predicate is extracted from the code, and whose only alternative continuation is terminal | MustBefore and certificates |
| **O** (observed synchronization) | Holds in the observed execution only: outermost mutex release → next acquire, semaphore give → take, delivered queue/SB message, poll exit by condition with a soft alternative, timer add/set → callback, TBL request and flag protocols, FS request → completion, outcome-implied reads-from | HB of a trace; reorderable in prediction, subject to the primitive's semantics |
| **C_σ** (schedule constraint) | Fixed-priority preemption on one CPU, affinity, under P_sched | Feasibility under σ_R1 only; never an HB edge ([R04 §4 row 20]; [R05 §2.7]) |

**Looper rule** [D] (fixes [X1-M7](b)). On an actor that loops over `ReceiveBuffer` and dispatches (a *looper*), program order holds **inside one handler** and from the loop's init prefix to every handler. **Between two handlers on the same looper there is no HB edge**, neither in VC nor in VC_R; shapes and the UNORDERED route are computed without it (CAFA PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; DCatch Rule-Pnreg ASPLOS 2017 [P]; [R04 §2.7, §4 row 8]). The sequential execution of handlers survives only as a **prediction constraint**: handlers of one looper do not overlap, and handlers of messages from the same pipe run in dequeue order, which is the FIFO order of their enqueues.

**Trace-side image: robustness tags** ([B §4.2]):
- `unconditional` (S-rules that need no observation);
- `obs-fatal` (S, guarded observation with terminal escape);
- `obs-soft` (O, log-and-continue escape);
- `schedule-chosen` (O: locks, semaphores, messages);
- `assumed` (holds only under assumption A4: volatile flags, seqlock, TBL result flags, the SB subscription-reporting flag).

**Relations computed:**

1. **MustBefore** (static; §4.4). `a ∈ MB(n)` iff in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` in which `n` occurs (for σ_U, the same relation as over `Exec(B,σ_U,M)`, since every finite execution extends to a maximal one and MB is prefix-closed), every occurrence of `n` is preceded by an occurrence of `a`. It is a greatest fixpoint over the certificate view, using S-rules only, computed per M.
2. **Precedes-whenever-both-occur** `PWB(a, b)`: in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` containing both, every occurrence of `a` precedes every occurrence of `b` (for σ_U, the same relation as over `Exec`). Certified by the **frontier rule** (`once(actor(a))`, `∃ m` in `a`'s actor with no skeleton path `m →* a` and `m ∈ MB(b)`) or by the **once rule** (`once(a) ∧ a ∈ MB(b)`), §4.5.
3. **HB_π** on a trace π: the transitive closure of S- and O-edges (looper rule applied), computed with vector clocks (Fidge 1988; Mattern 1989 [V-abs]). Two clocks per event ([B §4.2]):
   - `VC` over all definite edges;
   - `VC_R` over the edges tagged `unconditional` or `obs-fatal`.

   `a ≺_R b` is a cheap trace-side prefilter for a static certificate, never an Exec-level claim ([J1 X5]).
4. **C_σ** constraints only restrict σ_R1 feasibility, and only in the explorer (never in trace prediction, §4.9).

Rules not in Φ are listed as **unmodelled**. A loop that matches the poll pattern (§4.3) but whose predicate cannot be extracted, or a protocol over app-private memory with no Φ rule (e.g. FM's parent–child queue, `fm_child.c:65–100, 162`), becomes an `unmodelled-sync` mark, and every instance whose decision depends on it is `unknown(unmodelled)`. This is the KCSAN lesson that an HB model missing one ordering mechanism floods false positives (`kcsan.rst` L366–373 [V-doc, R04 §2.4]).

### 1.10 Classes, refinements, axes, shapes, evidence and the verdict record

Fix instance `i`, basis B, semantics σ, level M and bound b. Statuses are those of §1.6, evaluated on `Pref_b(B,σ,M)`; "in-scope" means belonging to `i`, and `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences are counted below by nothing. For an occurrence ordinal o, write:
- `Bad_i` (`Bad_i(o)`): some finite execution contains a `VIOLATING` (or, with Allowed unspecified, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`) in-scope occurrence of `u` (at ordinal o);
- `OK_i` (`OK_i(o)`): some finite execution contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence (at ordinal o);
- `NR_i`: some finite execution contains an in-scope occurrence decided not ready (`TOLERATED`, `PENDING`, `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`);
- `Reach_i`: some finite execution contains an in-scope occurrence whose readiness is decided and evaluated (not `NOT_EVALUATED`).

**Safety record** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Per cell and per bound, the record holds the statement `¬Bad_i` if established, the statement `¬OK_i` if established, and the statement `¬NR_i` ("no reached in-scope occurrence is decided not ready") if established, each with its route (certificate, `exh`, transfer); `¬NR_i` comes only from a PROTECTED certificate or a transfer of one, and implies `¬Bad_i`. With `¬OK_i` the record keeps its **decision route**: `¬OK_i` is **deciding** iff the record holds a bad witness in that cell at that bound, or the statement comes from a FIXED_ORDER certificate (or a transfer of one) whose badness item decides every not-ready occurrence at its decision event (`Allowed = none`; `unspecified_by_reference`; an outcome outside the entry's codes). Such a certificate decides in every σ. A certificate whose REJECT events lie after the decision (a retry's exhaustion, an error exit or return, a next operation, a termination) decides in no σ by itself: the path to the REJECT can be blocked by an untimed wait, by a transition that σ_U∞ removes, or by starvation under σ_R1. The record also holds the bad and good witnesses with their ordinals. The **class is a function of the record**:

| Class (safety axis) | Record | Proof obligation |
|---|---|---|
| `inactive(B)` | no execution reaches the use | static: the template is unreachable in the **certificate view** under B constants and message provenance (path condition as proof). An unobserved use is **not** inactive; it is `unknown(not_reached)` |
| `protected(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬Bad_i` established, `¬OK_i` not: every in-scope occurrence that is reached is `READY`, `TOLERATED` or `PENDING`, never `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`; with `¬NR_i`, every one is `READY` | certificate (§4.5, b = ∞; L-PR proves `¬NR_i`), `exh` with `Q_bad = no` within b, or transfer (§1.8 rule 1(a)) |
| ↳ refinement `tolerated` | protected, and some finite execution contains a `TOLERATED` occurrence | `exh` within b only. A PROTECTED certificate proves `¬NR_i`, so it never supports `tolerated`; a decided not-ready occurrence (`TOLERATED` or `PENDING` included) against it is a contradiction or an O10 disagreement (below) |
| `fixed-order(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬OK_i` established **and deciding**, `¬Bad_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is `READY` or `TOLERATED`, and a bad witness exists or not-ready occurrences are decided bad at their decision | certificate (L-FO with the badness item), `exh` with `Q_ok = no` and a bad witness within b, or transfer with a decision route in the receiving σ. **Never** from k-of-k failing runs ([R06 S9]). That every execution reaches a decision is not claimed |
| `no-final-status(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` both established: every reached in-scope occurrence is `PENDING` (or `NOT_EVALUATED`), or none is reached | the two statements by any of their routes (e.g. a transferred `¬OK_i` and an `exh` `¬Bad_i`) |
| `schedule-dependent(B,σ,M,b)` | a bad witness and a good witness **at the same ordinal**: two distinct finite executions | the two witnesses in the same cell and bound. The record keeps the weaker evidence per axis. A stall witness is never one of the two |
| ↳ refinement | `timeout-dependent` (§1.8) | derived from the σ_U and σ_U∞ cells, with its own bound; needs `exh` |
| `ordinal-split(B,σ,M,b)` | bad and good witnesses, but no established pair at a common ordinal | the witnesses with their ordinals. It does **not** state that any single occurrence depends on the schedule |
| `unknown(reason)` | nothing established, or `¬OK_i` alone without a decision route (reason `no_decision_route`; the statement is still shown) | reason ∈ {`not_reached`, `model_gap`, `bound`, `budget`, `key_unresolved`, `loss`, `unmodelled`, `writer_incomplete`, `horizon` (a trace ended while a horizon monitor was OPEN), `no_decision_route`, `conformance`, `model_fault` (incl. a `contested` bound), `model_level`} |

**Universal statements, existence and bounds** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2).
- **Scope of the universal statements.** `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` quantify over the reached occurrences, so they hold vacuously when no occurrence is reached, and both hold when every reached occurrence stays `PENDING`. They are **not exclusive**; `no-final-status` names the case where both are established. Neither says anything about executions that do not reach the use. A permanent wait before the use is covered by the progress result; an execution in which the use's actor instance terminates without an occurrence, or the process panics, is covered by neither axis: its only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence, if one led to it, with the harm of its consequence chain (`task_never_runs`, `panic`, `process_abort`), and a σ_U panic after a spontaneous timeout has none (§6.4 item 34).
- **Existence** is recorded on the cell's **reach** axis and is never part of a class. With `reach = assumed`, a universal class reads "… if reached". With `reach = unreached(b)`, it reads "… if reached; not reached within b".
- **Per bound.** The record, the class, reach and progress are kept per bound: b = ∞ for certificates, transfers from them, runs and confirmed model paths; each completed h for `exh`. A statement at b = ∞ holds at every b; a witness within b holds at every larger bound. One class is computed per (cell, bound), and the report renders the class at b = ∞ with every bounded class that adds information.
- **Contradictions and disagreements** (§4.12 item 5). At the same bound: (1) a realized bad witness against `¬Bad_i`, a realized good witness against `¬OK_i`, or an observed decided not-ready occurrence against `¬NR_i`, where the statement comes from a certificate or a transfer of one, is a contradiction (§4.14, suspension); (2) an `exh` witness against such a statement, or an `exh` result in one σ against a transferred `exh` statement from another, goes to obligation O10 (directed check; the explored bound becomes `contested`, the certificate's own record at b = ∞ is unchanged and carries a persistent contested mark for the Φ version); (3) a realized witness against an `exh`-only statement is a model-bound conflict (O6, the bound becomes `contested`). None is a lattice step. Evidence outside a bound never contradicts a statement within it.
- **Templates with several occurrences per execution** (§1.2). Classes quantify over occurrences, and witnesses record their ordinal (per key, over canonical actor-instance names). Where a template has at most one in-scope occurrence per execution, `¬Bad_i` coincides with v1's "no execution in `E_i` is bad" (without v1's `E_i ≠ ∅` and `S_i = ∅`), and every pair of witnesses is at the same ordinal.

The good-side flag `timeout-rescued` (§1.8) may accompany protected, schedule-dependent or ordinal-split (every class with a good witness under σ_U).

**Orthogonal axes** [D] (fixes [X1-m3]):
- **provider** ∈ {`present`, `none` (a conjunct is INIT_FALSE and its complete writer set is empty), `env-absent` (the only writers are Φ effects guarded by an `env` predicate that B.Env records false, with outcome agreement O4; e.g. HS `SET_RUNNING`, App. A.6), `unknown`}. A use with provider `none` is classified by its outcomes like any other. It is fixed-order if no reached occurrence can be `READY` or `TOLERATED` and a decision route exists (§1.10). A retry whose only REJECT is its exhaustion, which lies after the decision, therefore has `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown at b = ∞ in every σ from the certificate alone; with `exh` it is fixed-order within b under σ_U (the exhausting execution is a bad witness) and `no-final-status` within b under σ_U∞ when the exhaustion is time-based. It can be protected with `tolerated` only through `exh`: no certificate route exists for an allowed not-ready outcome, and FIXED_ORDER item 5 leaves such an instance open (§4.5);
- **reach**, per cell (σ, M) and per bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2): at b = ∞, `proved(run id | confirmed model path)` or `assumed`; at a bounded b, `model(b)` (`exh` found a decided in-scope occurrence within b; not confirmed) or `unreached(b)` (`exh` found none).
  - `proved` needs a finite execution **of that σ at that M**, realized on B, that contains a decided in-scope occurrence:
    - for σ_U at M_impl, any run (L-CONT) or a model path confirmed by a run;
    - for σ_U at M_api, a run only if the instance's cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL, sub-label `mlevel-lemma`), or if the run's prefix up to the occurrence passes an **M_api-conformance check on every actor of the instance's closed cone**: every OSAL call those actors made returned an outcome that M_api allows, and none of the M_impl-only behaviours (`OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`, a lost task, a leaked type lock) occurred on them; OSAL calls of actors outside the cone do not matter (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2). The check needs the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6;
    - for σ_U∞, the σ_U condition at that M, and a prefix whose transitions **by the actors of the instance's closed cone** contain no time-based transition: no timeout outcome of a non-periodic timed wait, no exhausted delay-paced retry, no forced kill, and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake (`OS_WaitForStateChange` returning by timeout); TB transitions of actors outside the cone do not matter (L-REACH-CONE, §6.2). The check needs the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6, and until they exist run-based σ_U∞ reach at M_impl is `assumed`;
    - for σ_R1, a confirmed model path only on a native σ_R1 arm (S10); otherwise `assumed` or `model(b)`;
    - for σ_E, an observed occurrence.
  - `unreached(b)` and `model(b)` come only from `exh` within b. A proof whose witness lies within b (a run whose conforming model prefix lies within b) meeting `unreached(b)` contests that bound (`contested`, §4.14).
  - Otherwise reach is `assumed`. A class with `reach = assumed` reads "… if reached";
- **scope** ∈ {`in-scope`, `teardown`} (§1.6).
- **progress**, per cell (σ, M) and per bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1), ∈ {`stall-witness(S)` with S a non-empty set of {pre-use, in-use, horizon}, `no-stall-within(b)`, `unknown(reason)`}. A stall witness carries its kind (`deadlock-cycle`, `leaked-lock`, `other`), its qualifiers (`idle`, `terminal-pending`, `choice-dependent` with the choice support), its wait-for root set, the `confirmable` flag, its realization (`model(b)` | `realized`), harm (`hang_or_deadlock` | `task_never_runs` | `terminal_pending(panic | abort)`), its progress chain, and the outcome label `liveness` (§1.8). The default is `unknown(not_checked)`; K-TEARDOWN instances have `unknown(teardown_scope)`; a bound refuted by evidence becomes `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`, §4.14).
  - The progress result never changes the class, and the class never implies a progress value. `protected` together with a stall witness is a consistent record (litmus LP-DL), not a contradiction.
  - **Rendering rule.** A universal class is always rendered together with the cell's reach and progress values and their bounds, and a stall witness with its kind, its qualifiers and its wait-for root. Example: "protected — every reached occurrence READY (certificate, b = ∞); reach proved; progress not checked". `terminal-pending` is rendered as relative to the closed cone.
  - **Counting rule.** A stall witness counts as a detection or as Reported (§8.5) only if it is `realized`, or a model witness that is confirmable and not choice-dependent. Counted witnesses are grouped by wait-for root set, and each group counts once per cell, with the instances it blocks attached. Other model stall witnesses are listed as progress candidates.

**Shape** ([A §1.7]) of a bad finite witness ρ with occurrence `u`, where `p*` is the first transition that would make the failing conjunct true; computed with the looper rule (no between-handler edges) and API-level edges only. Witnesses are finite executions (§1.8), so the shape is defined on ρ and on a **recorded extension** of it (the rest of the run that contains ρ, or the explorer path ρ belongs to) [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2):
- **WRONG-WAY**: `p*` occurs after `u` in ρ or in its recorded extension, and `u →hb p*`;
- **UNORDERED**: `u ∥hb p*`;
- **ABSENT**: no extension of ρ can contain `p*`. This is shown either statically (provider `none`; or `p*`'s node is once and has already been passed on ρ without producing `p*`, e.g. SB's `GetAppID` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` after its failure branch), or by `exh` from ρ's end state within b with no censored or stranded actor that could produce `p*` (the statement is then bounded by b). It is never inferred from a run that merely ends;
- **NOT-OBSERVED**: `p*` occurs neither in ρ nor in its recorded extension, and ABSENT is not shown;
- **STALE**: the conjunct held earlier, then a disabling transition occurred before `u` (generation mismatch).

**Evidence**, two axes ([R06 S1]; fixes [X1-M11], [X1-M4]):

| Axis | Values |
|---|---|
| **realization** | `certificate(id)`; `model(b)`; `predicted` (trace-sound under A-COMM, §4.9); `predicted-modelled` (A-COMM failed: model-based); `predicted-extrapolated` (summary-guided extrapolation: model-based); `realized` (the order and outcome occurred on the gcc binary) |
| **naturalness** (realized only) | `natural` (no hold, no gate); `held-F0`; `gated-F1`; `timeout-exercised` (a time-based transition of a cone actor occurred during or because of a hold, §1.8; periodic waits excluded); flag `derived` (the schedule was constructed) |

"Weaker" is decided per axis: realization `model(b)` ≈ `predicted-modelled` ≈ `predicted-extrapolated` < `predicted` < `realized`; naturalness `timeout-exercised` < `gated-F1` ≈ `held-F0` < `natural`.

**σ_E cells.**
- **schedule-dependent.** `schedule-dependent(σ_E)` needs a natural bad witness and a natural good witness **at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct realized runs whose occurrences at that ordinal have final statuses, each with its pool key. Bad and good occurrences at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)`, also from a single run. Realized but held or gated witnesses give the label `σ_E(held)`.
- **Universal classes.** No σ_E protected, fixed-order or no-final-status class is ever derived from runs. Such σ_E cells come only from transfer rule 2 of §1.8 (b = ∞), and they state the safety statement for every real run.
- **Counts.** Observation counts are written `no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown`, never "timing-protected".
- **Progress.** The σ_E progress result is `stall-witness(realized)` only with a wait-for fact (§4.13) that itself shows permanence, i.e. a `deadlock-cycle`, or a `leaked-lock` (K-LOCKBAL) whose holder has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock. Otherwise the result is `unknown(not_checked)`. It is never `no-stall-within(b)`.

**Outcome label** of the safety result: `violation` (Allowed specified) / `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` (route or capacity, Allowed unspecified) / `not-ready-unjudged` (other contracts, Allowed unspecified); `NONE` while no cell has a class that shows a not-ready outcome. The label names the meaning by Allowed, so it is one field per verdict; a cell renders it only when its class is fixed-order, schedule-dependent or ordinal-split. A `fixed-order` class carries its label (`violation`, `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` or `not-ready-unjudged`) only with a decision route (§1.10): App. A.6 is fixed-order with `not-ready-unjudged`. `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)` and renders no label. The label `liveness` belongs to the progress result (§1.8).

**Framework-level judgement** [D] (§1.6 "Three judgements"). Two verdict fields, never combined with the outcome label; their encoding is fixed at S0:
- `fw_doc`, **per instance** (a property of the op's outcome map): the set of documentation statuses of the not-ready result codes that the instance's outcome map lists at either model level, each `documented-not-error` (a supplier-side reference documents the result and says that it is not an error: cSB4305.5's rationale, `cFE_FunctionalRequirements.csv:352`), `documented-result` (documented without such a statement: an `@retval`; `\retval CFE_EVS_APP_ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, `cfe_evs.h:199`, whose code has error severity, `cfe_error.h:115–119, 267`) or `undocumented` (`OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` from `OS_CountSemGetIdByName` at M_impl, outside `osapi-countsem.h:157–160`); empty if the map has no not-ready code. Which code occurred where is in the witnesses and run records;
- `fw_conf`, **per (σ, M) cell**, ∈ {`as-documented`, `deviates`, `unknown`}: whether, in the cell's evidence, the result code (O4(a)) and the documented side effects (§3.9 counters; the event attempts and their outcomes in the trace) occurred as documented. M_api assumes conformance to the API contract (§1.4), so an M_api cell has `unknown`; `as-documented` and `deviates` come from M_impl cells and runs. Each run's conformance is in the conformance record (§4.11), reached through the cell's `runs` reference; a deviation is named there and suspends nothing.

A `documented-not-error` result can carry `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` or `violation` at the caller level, and a `violation` can rest on a `documented-result` that occurred `as-documented` (R2).

**Consequence** (the observed consequence of §1.6; [A §1.3], effect-oriented as in ConMem and Portend [V-abs, R04 §2.6, §2.10]), recorded for every not-ready occurrence whatever its Allowed, from the trace or the model continuation, with the observed counts that the run oracles give (counter deltas, event attempts and their outcomes, §3.9), read through the cell's `runs` reference: crash or undefined behaviour; deadlock (as the consequence of a VIOLATING occurrence; a permanent wait of the instance itself is its progress result, §1.8); process abort; panic; app error exit; app zombie; core service task absent; status ignored and effect lost silently; event then continue; delayed but recovered; message lost (no route, queue full or MsgLim reached); event rejected (an event attempt that EVS refuses, e.g. with `ILLEGAL_APP_ID`).

**Sub-labels**: `portability-hazard`, `implementation-defect-candidate`, `portability-hazard-evidence` (§1.4); `assumed-visibility` (§3.14); `A-ZT` (σ_R1 delay semantics); `tb-undeclared` (a σ_U∞ result that depends on a give-up that Φ has not declared time-based, §1.8). The sub-labels are a bitset (`sublabels`, §2.3). The count `trusted:<n>` of `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries, kept for continuity, and the sizes of the four parts of the trust ledger (§4.5.1) are rendered from the certificate's `TRUST_LEDGERS` record (`n_trusted` among the entries of part (iii); `n_rechecked`, `n_extracted`, `n_trusted`, `n_refutable`), not stored in the bitset.

**The verdict record** (`kz_verdict` plus per-(σ, M) `kz_verdict_cell` records, §2.3) holds the following:
- instance id; provider and scope;
- per cell:
  - class and refinements, and the safety record they derive from: the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` (with its decision route) and `¬NR_i` with their routes, and the witnesses with their ordinals;
  - bound reference: entries of the section `BOUNDS` (§2.3, `KZ_BOUND_FIELDS`), one per bound, each with the bound and its policy status (including h where the exploration view was used), the per-bound class (including `contested`), reach and progress values (including `contested`), the safety routes and the decision route, the refinements, the progress flags, reason and harm, and the bad, good and stall witnesses as ranges of witness records that carry their ordinals, and the progress chain at that bound, so that the fixed-point test can compare per-bound statements, routes, refinements, progress and witness ordinals (§4.14);
  - reach;
  - the framework-level conformance `fw_conf` of this cell (§1.10);
  - realization and naturalness sets; `n of N` with pool key;
  - the progress result: value, set of stall clauses, kind, qualifiers, wait-for root set, reason, harm, realization, bound, stall witness, progress chain and the flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`;
  - a reference to the trust ledger;
- shape; caller level: outcome label and harm; framework level: `fw_doc`; observed: consequence with references to its observed counts; violation chain links; unknown reason;
- B, Φ and tool hashes;
- typed references to the certificate (with its contested mark, if O10 set one for this Φ version), witnesses and runs.

**There is never a "safe" field, and no field means "live" or "deadlock-free".**

### 1.11 Why plain data-race HB detection is insufficient, and what is borrowed

| Phenomenon in cFS | What prior work does | What Kairos adds (no new mechanism claimed) |
|---|---|---|
| **R2**: all 24 violating occurrences per boot (one cause at 3 static sites, [R2g §6]) are HB-ordered **before** the publication by core serialization ([R2g §2]; [R2c §7]) | FastTrack (PLDI 2009 [V-abs]), TSan and Djit+ report unordered conflicting accesses; CP, WCP, SHB, M2 and SyncP (all [V-abs]) report reorderable pairs only ([R04 §3 L4]). DefUse (OOPSLA 2010 [V-abs]) learns def-use invariants from runs; [I] it would learn R2's wrong definition (the EarlyInit value) as normal, because every run reads it. GPredict (ICSE 2015 [V-abs]) predicts violations of user-written generic properties under a causal model ([R04] L249–251). [R04 L553]'s "no notion of a legal error outcome" is an inference of [R04 §5] at abstract level: [R04] records no built-in vocabulary of documented not-ready outcomes for GPredict (abstract-level, [I]); the tool and its property formalism (adapted from MOP, `background/terminology/T4_term_census.md` #52, P) are unverified (P-PAPERS) [U]; we infer that the finite-horizon Allowed entries can be encoded in a GPredict-style property (§8.3) [I] | A reference-sourced contract whose caller-level Allowed outcomes (§1.6 "Three judgements") carry provenance and reference horizons, checked on **ordered** pairs, and a σ- and M-qualified PWB certificate that lifts "ordered the wrong way" to `fixed-order` ([R04 §5 G2]) |
| **C1 / C5** and both App-record windows: create/lookup or publish/`GetAppID` under the same lock | HB orders them through the lock; lockset sees consistent protection ([R04 §2.3, §3 L5]). **RVPredict (PLDI 2014) and GPredict-style constraint encodings already treat the key as the conflict object and the lock as atomicity** ([R04 L259, L492]); ExceptioNULL (FSE 2012 [V-abs]) predicts "use before initialized" from one execution with static lockset pruning and SMT ([R04 L253–255]) — the closest predictive prior work for readiness | Explicit-state search instead of a solver (an engineering choice, not novelty); framework typestate, which has prior art (predictive typestate checking, ASE 2008; framework-lifecycle typestate checked over traces, Lifestate, ECOOP 2019; see Defensible contribution), to which Kairos adds only the not-ready outcomes that the caller's, consumer's or mission's references accept (the caller-level Allowed of §1.6; the framework's documented results are reported apart), each with a reference horizon and provenance, pending a check of Joshi & Sen and Lifestate (P-PAPERS), and expressible in a GPredict-style property as far as §8.3 infers [I], so not claimed as an expressiveness gap; per-σ classes with same-ordinal pairing; certificates only where an S-edge orders the pair, which is expected neither for an app provider in C1's B′ nor for C5's lock-scope fix (MustBefore has no lock rule, §4.4) [I]; held confirmation. Kairos's predictive soundness is **weaker** than RVPredict's on its sparse API-level traces (A-COMM, §4.9) |
| **No-subscriber loss**: a late subscribe makes a transmit a loss that the sender cannot see (a success return). SB counts it (`NoSubscribersCounter`) and attempts a `SEND_NO_SUBS` event, which SB's default filter passes four times and then stops (`CFE_EVS_FIRST_4_STOP`, `cfe_sb_internal_cfg.h:225`); in R2's window the event is rejected by EVS [M] and the window's counter increments are cleared later by SB's own initialization [I] (App. A.1 step 13a), so there the loss leaves no trace that survives the window [I] | **InitRacer's "late event handler registration"** (OOPSLA 2017 [V-abs]; [R04 L69, L367, L529]) | The cFS instance of that taxonomy, with registries as keys and LOSS-UNDER-ORDER when no `appreq` requires delivery; the framework-level judgement (cSB4305.5: not an error) is reported beside it, never merged (§1.10) |
| **Capacity loss**: queue full drops silently ([M] 4–49 drops per boot, [BM §0]) | Event-driven and distributed HB models assume delivery ([R04 §5 G5]) | Capacity as typestate (queue depth and MsgLim); prediction and exploration ask whether a feasible order changes the outcome. G5 is genuinely open only for capacity |
| Soft, opt-in, timed barriers (`WaitForStartupSync` returns void, `cfe_es_api.c:616–619`) | EventRacer and Tian et al. recognize ad hoc synchronization but not timeout-conditional edges ([R04 §5 G3]) | Conditional O-edges, guarded S-edges, σ_U/σ_U∞ split, timeout-dependent refinements. **The novelty of the G3 formalization is an open literature check**: R04 records that a targeted search was not done ([R04 §8 item 5]) |
| Executing task ≠ owner ≠ service ≠ stamped AppId (G6) | thread identity; CAFA/DroidRacer separate thread from event; DCatch abstracts RPC pools | four identity fields, on-behalf frames, capture points |
| Configuration decides reachability and capacity (G7) | configuration taken as given | B with configuration closure; `inactive`; provider axis |
| Priority order only on RT targets (G8) | no edge, but no verdict either | σ_R1[P_sched] as a schedule constraint, verdict per σ |

**Established theory used.** No mechanism is claimed as new ([R04 §5], last paragraph):

| Element | Source (status) |
|---|---|
| HB as a partial order; vector clocks | Lamport 1978; Fidge 1988; Mattern 1989 [V-abs] |
| Feasible vs apparent races; order violations as a major class | Netzer & Miller LOPLAS 1992 [V-abs]; Lu et al. ASPLOS 2008 [V-abs] |
| Framework/event HB rules (looper, post/begin, RPC/event) | DroidRacer PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; CAFA PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; DCatch ASPLOS 2017 [P] |
| Initialization races (late registration, access before definition) | InitRacer OOPSLA 2017 [V-abs] |
| Typestate contracts | Strom & Yemini 1986 [V-me] |
| Def-use invariants; generic predictive properties; predicted use-before-init | DefUse OOPSLA 2010 [V-abs]; GPredict ICSE 2015 [V-abs]; ExceptioNULL FSE 2012 [V-abs] |
| Sound prediction with control-flow abstraction; candidate + vindication | Maximal causal models RV 2012 [V-abs]; RVPredict PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; Vindicator PLDI 2018 [V-abs] |
| Ad hoc synchronization; race coverage | Xiong et al. OSDI 2010 [V-abs]; Tian et al. ISSTA 2008 [V-abs]; EventRacer OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs] |
| Atomicity / serializability | Velodrome PLDI 2008 [V-abs]; AVIO ASPLOS 2006 [V-abs]. Left/right-mover reduction (L3): citation open literature check |
| Systematic exploration | DPOR POPL 2005 [V-abs; guarantee wording UNVERIFIED]; source/optimal DPOR POPL 2014 [V-abs; JACM extension to disabling P]; BPOR OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs]; fair SMC PLDI 2008 [V-abs; strongly fair scheduler]; PCT ASPLOS 2010 [V-abs; mechanism P]; SCTBench PPoPP 2014 [V-abs] |
| Static action HB; environment models; harnesses | SIERRA ASPLOS 2018 [V-abs]; SDV EuroSys 2006 [V-abs]; Holzmann & Joshi SPIN 2004 [V-abs]; FlowDroid PLDI 2014 [V-me] |
| Points-to and indirect calls | Andersen 1994 [V-abs]; SVF CC 2016 [V-abs]; MLTA CCS 2019 [V-abs] |
| Triggering and confirmation; outcome triage | CTrigger ASPLOS 2009, RaceFuzzer PLDI 2008, RacePro SOSP 2011, Portend ASPLOS 2012, DataCollider OSDI 2010 [all V-abs] |
| Log/order mining and potential communications | CloudRaid ESEC/FSE 2018 [V-abs]; Snowboard SOSP 2021 [V-abs] |
| Belief deviance | Engler et al. SOSP 2001 [V-me] |
| Soundiness | Livshits et al. CACM 2015 [V-abs] |

**Defensible contribution** [I] (after [X1 §4]; revised by EXT-20261012 §3 and §5.1–§5.2): a σ- and M-qualified must-order certificate that lifts an observed HB-ordered contract violation to a universal fixed-order class, as a safety statement re-validated relative to a stated trusted base (trust ledger); caller-level Allowed outcomes with provenance and reference horizons (§1.6 "Three judgements"), and per-conjunct LPs; combined with lock-reversing prediction, held confirmation and an evaluation that reports detection, classification and evidence separately and measures misses on history-derived mutants and pairs; organized around the cFS semantics G1–G8. Every individual mechanism has prior art, and so do three closer lines of work: checking user-written properties on predicted traces (GPredict, ICSE 2015 [V-abs]); predictive typestate checking of multithreaded programs (Joshi & Sen, ASE 2008; bibliographic record V per `background/08_terminology.md` L754, content known from the abstract and the external review); and framework-lifecycle typestate and protocol specifications checked over traces (Lifestate, Meier, Mover and Chang, ECOOP 2019; V per `background/08_terminology.md` L758, content known from its abstract). None of the three papers has been read in full here (P-PAPERS). The candidate contribution is therefore the cFS-specific extraction, the classification and the evidence, not the use of a specification or of framework typestate. The only difference claimed from Joshi & Sen and Lifestate is that each not-ready outcome that the caller's, consumer's or mission's references accept carries a reference horizon and provenance, while the framework's documented results are reported apart (§1.6); this is pending a check of both papers (`background/08_terminology.md` L559–561; P-PAPERS). It is not a claim that other specification-based techniques cannot express Allowed outcomes (§6.4 item 37). It must be shown beyond R2: on C1, C2, C3 and C5 the classification differentiators need the explorer (S7) and the case bases (D22 for C1, P-C2 for C2, D13 for C3 and C5; §9.3, per-case paths and controls), so a result on R2 alone is a single-case result (§8.8).

---

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Layers and principles

```
L5  Orchestration & reporting      kz loop (feedback driver), kz report, kz dump, kz proofcheck (independent inference relative to its trusted base)
L4  Real-binary observation/control kz observe (F0: perf-uprobe + eBPF raw loader, attach cookies |
                                   perf-sample bring-up backend), kz hold (F0: ptrace + debug-register gates),
                                   kz gate (F1, PI-gated)
L3  Analyses (three interpreters)   abstract: kz-static static (census, MustBefore, PWB/once, certificates)
                                   concrete: kz trace (identity, HB, per-conjunct LPs, routing), kz predict, kz conform
                                   operational: kz explore (gated, late; exh and dpor engines)
L2  Formal core                     libkz-core: KEM types, Φ parser, typed core expression evaluator (one evaluator,
                                   three abstract domains), rule engine, typestate, vector clocks, verdict lattice,
                                   provenance, KZ container I/O  (no LLVM dependency)
L1  Static closure & skeletons      kz-static basis, extract (Clang AST facts, derives, writer extraction S3a),
                                   link (IR call graph, DWARF/MC binder, anchor audit), modref (S3b, conditional),
                                   skel (certificate view + exploration view)   (libkz-llvm)
L0  Inputs                          basis B (build tree, configuration, Env, Init, Input), profile Φ_cFS (text)
```

Principles [D]:

1. **One formal core.** Every component reads and writes KEM types through `libkz-core`. No component has a private notion of event, resource, edge, predicate or verdict. Φ predicates have one evaluator. The one deliberate exception: `kz proofcheck` re-derives extracted await predicates and decides `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own symbolic procedure (§4.5), so that this step does not reuse the producer's evaluator; only `C`'s syntax tree comes from the shared Φ parser. Φ conjuncts and guards are otherwise evaluated by the `libkz-core` evaluator everywhere, the checker included: the checker's INIT step and the falsifier condition of T-OBS call it. That evaluator is therefore part of the checker's trusted base, and those steps are marked "re-validated via T5" in the trust ledger (§4.5, §4.5.1).
2. **The profile is data, not code.** Framework knowledge lives in versioned Φ files with provenance. Engines are framework-neutral; a lint forbids case identifiers in engine code (§9.2). App-private protocols, if ever modelled, go into a separate, provenance-carrying **app-profile layer** whose authoring cost is measured (§7.2).
3. **Proof-carrying verdicts.**
   - **Certificates.** Every certificate is a list of rule instances, writer entries (with transition and key statuses), once facts, derives facts and extracted predicates, each with code sites.
   - **Checker.** `kz proofcheck` shares no analysis code with `kz-static static`; both link `libkz-core`. It re-validates each inference step relative to its trusted base: extracted facts, skeleton, closure lists, Φ, `libkz-core`, and run and prefix-probe artifacts for reach. Its inference is independent of the producer's except in the steps that call `libkz-core`'s evaluator or rule engine, which the ledger marks.
   - **Trust ledger.** Every certificate and verdict carries a trust ledger that separates re-validated steps, extracted facts, trusted entries and observation-refutable items (§4.5.1).
   - **Tests.** Certificate-mutation tests check the checker; program mutants (§8.5) check the analysis.
4. **Observation first, control second.** F0 observation of the unmodified gcc binary is the default ([R07 §7]). Control is used only for confirmation; every hold is recorded.
5. **Layers check each other.** Obligations O1–O10 (§2.5).
6. **Honest unknowns.** Every gap produces `unknown(reason)`.
7. **Zero-install core.** Every core capability works without new packages: perf-PMU uprobes, raw `bpf()` with hand-written helper prototypes and a glibc `<elf.h>` ELF reader, explicit search instead of SMT, plain C++ instead of MLIR. Every install is a PI decision (`PI_DECISIONS.md`).
8. **Deterministic analyses.** Every analysis budget is a deterministic **work-unit** count (states expanded, transitions executed, search nodes, fixpoint updates), with a deterministic search order. Wall time and peak memory are recorded as costs only (fixes [X3-M10]).

### 2.2 Components

| Component | Binary | Language, libraries | Inputs → outputs | Why this technology (measured or argued) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `libkz-core` | both | C++17, standard library only | — | shared formal core; LLVM-free |
| `basis` | `kz-static` | C++17 | build tree, `link.txt`, generated files, startup scripts, `.tbl`, Env/Init snapshots, argv → `basis.kz` | evaluates the script-selection rule and reset type; content-only Init hash |
| `extract` | `kz-static` | C++17, Clang LibTooling + ASTMatchers (LLVM 23 `1053047a`) | 448 FSW compile commands → `facts.kz` | [M] 448/448 TUs parse in 10 s ([R07 §3.3]); macro provenance (467 of 3,762 API call sites in macro expansions, [R07 §4.6]); constant evaluation; Doxygen extraction; **S3a writer extraction and `derives` facts at the AST level**, where field identity is explicit (fixes [X3-M4]); serialized loop-exit expressions for predicate extraction |
| `link` | `kz-static` | C++17, LLVM IR linker semantics, DebugInfo/DWARF, Object, MC | per-unit bitcode; the **gcc** `core-cpu1` and app `.so` → call graph, `anchors.kz`, anchor audit | [M] bitcode 4.4 s, closed `core-cpu1.bc` 0.35 s ([R07 §3.4]). IR is used **for the call graph only**; if IR is ever used for writes, the pass pipeline is pinned without InstCombine and SROA. The app-unit link probe of §4.1 step 6 is an early S3a item (fixes [X3-m5]) |
| `modref` (S3b) | `kz-static` | C++17 over LLVM IR | closed IR + Φ → points-to, writer sets | **conditional**: built only if the S3a O7 report shows `DISABLED` conjuncts blocking certificates; its gain is measured by ablation 17. Andersen-style ([V-abs, R05 §2.5]); SVF ([V-abs]) is a reference, not a dependency (SVF lists LLVM 21–22) |
| `skel` | `kz-static` | C++17 | IR call graph + AST facts + closure + Φ → `skel.kz` (certificate view, exploration view, once facts, message provenance) | §4.3 |
| `static` | `kz-static` | C++17 on `libkz-core` | skeletons + writer sets + Φ → census, MustBefore, certificates, verdicts, open candidates | §4.4–4.6 |
| `plan` | `kz-static` | C++17, LLVM MC | open candidates + sync skeleton + anchors + calibration → `plan.kz` | site validation by disassembly; joint debug-register allocation |
| `proofcheck` | `kz` | C++17, separate module; no analysis code shared with `static`; links `libkz-core` | certificates + `skel.kz` + `facts.kz` + `anchors.kz` + Φ + closure spawn lists + the run, `hb-*.kz` and prefix-probe artifacts that reach names → accept/reject per step, and the certificate's trust ledger | re-validates the inference steps (view, dominance, spawn lists, once facts, await predicates and their implication, `W_o` recomputation and exclusions, derives chains, frontier no-paths, scope, INIT evaluation, badness path check, status consistency, reach with its per-cell condition) **relative to** what it trusts: the extracted facts; the skeleton graph, which it does not rebuild (it links no LLVM); Φ; the closure lists; and `libkz-core`, whose evaluator the INIT and falsifier steps call (marked "via T5") (§4.5) |
| `observe` | `kz` | C (one generic BPF program per phase, system clang-18 `-target bpf`); C++17 loader on raw `bpf()`/`perf_event_open`; perf-sample backend | plan + binaries → `run-*.kzt` | [M] uprobe 0.57 µs at entry+4 ([R07 §4.7]); perf-PMU uprobes work without tracefs and count hits registered before the file is mapped ([R07] L340); attach-cookie kernel symbols present ([X3, here]) |
| `trace` | `kz` | C++17 | trace + Φ + anchors → events, identities, `HB_π`, per-conjunct LP states, observed violations, routed candidates, chains | §4.8 |
| `conform` | `kz` | C++17 | trace + skeletons → O1/O2; O8 (from S5, on S5's engine) | §4.11 |
| `predict` | `kz` | C++17; optional SMT-LIB2 emitter | trace cone + Φ + A-COMM facts → witnesses or `unknown(budget)` | explicit search; no solver (Z3 not installed, [R07 §2.4]) |
| `explore` | `kz` | C++17 | closed candidate model + σ + M + b → safety records and classes within b, witnesses with occurrence ordinals, reach, progress results with kinds, qualifiers and progress chains | `exh` and `dpor` engines (§4.10); no external model checker fits this LLVM without an install (GenMC needs LLVM 19–22; Nidhugg's README range is LLVM 8–19 and LLVM 18 is installed **as runtime only**: an `llvm-18-dev` install would be needed, [R05 F11]; [R07 §2]) |
| `hold` | `kz` | C++17, ptrace, x86 debug registers | hold plan → controlled run + trace | [M] ptrace from root works on uid 1000; #DB trap ≈34 µs; gdb ≈203 µs per hit ([R07 §4.7]) |
| `gate` | `kz` (PI-gated, D3) | C99 `__wrap_*` objects under cFS flags; C++17 controller | variant link → gated runs | [M] `--wrap` misses 88 of 3,762 intra-TU sites ([R07 §4.2]); a link-option deviation |
| `loop` | `kz` | C++17 | all artifacts → iteration log, fixed point with its run set | §4.14 |
| `report`, `dump` | `kz` | C++17 | any artifact → Markdown/CSV views; canonical one-record-per-line text | the store stays binary and typed |

**Two binaries** [D] (fixes [X3-m7]): `kz-static` links `libkz-llvm` (one static LibTooling link, [R07 §2.2]: 56.7 MB) and holds the L1 subcommands, `static` and `plan`; `kz` links only `libkz-core` and holds everything else. `kz proofcheck` therefore links no Clang and shares no analysis code with the producer. Both binaries link `libkz-core` (Φ parser, evaluator, rule engine, container I/O), which is a trusted base shared by producer and checker (§4.5, A-CORE).

### 2.3 Typed formats

**One container format, KZ** [D]. Every artifact is a KZ container:
- a header carrying magic, ABI version, B hash, Φ hash, tool hash and the hash of every **parent artifact** (Merkle-style, [A §2.3]);
- a section table; **each section type carries its own schema version** (fixes [X3-m6]); each section is an array of fixed-layout little-endian records with a CRC;
- a string/blob pool.

**Schema discipline** (fixes [X3-m6]). Record layouts are declared once, in `include/kz/schema/kz_schema.h`, as **X-macro field lists**. Each list expands into (i) the C struct, (ii) the canonical dump printer, (iii) `_Static_assert`s on every offset, and (iv) an assert that `sizeof` equals the sum of the field sizes, so that no record has implicit padding. Explicit `rsv` fields fill alignment gaps and must be zero; writers zero every record before filling it. No file is generated and no external schema compiler is used. The header is included by the BPF program, the gcc-built gate objects (it compiles under `gcc -std=c99 -pedantic -Wall -Werror`, [X3, here]) and the C++ tools. Cross-artifact references are typed: `kz_ref = (artifact sha256, section type, index)`.

```c
/* kz_schema.h (excerpt; layouts are reviewed at S0 and versioned per section afterwards) */
#define KZ_REF_FIELDS(X) \
  X(uint8_t,  artifact_sha256, [32]) X(uint32_t, section, ) X(uint32_t, index, )
#define KZ_RAW_EV_FIELDS(X)                                                                  \
  X(uint64_t, t_ns,   )   /* CLOCK_MONOTONIC: evidence and search heuristic only, never HB */ \
  X(uint32_t, tid,    )   /* kernel thread id */                                              \
  X(uint32_t, tseq,   )   /* per-thread sequence assigned in kernel: program order, loss */   \
  X(uint32_t, site,   )   /* site id = the uprobe's attach cookie (never the runtime IP) */   \
  X(uint16_t, phase,  )   /* ENTRY | POINT | RET | WATCH | GATE | MARK */                     \
  X(uint16_t, flags,  )   /* STR_FOLLOWS, STACK_FOLLOWS, TRUNC, OBJSEQ_VALID, ... */          \
  X(uint64_t, objseq, )   /* per-object token taken inside the critical section; 0 = none */  \
  X(uint64_t, a, [4])     /* raw values per site decoder: registers, stack slots, derefs */
#define KZ_CERT_STEP_FIELDS(X)                                                               \
  X(uint32_t, rule, )       /* T_PO|T_SPAWN|T_OBS|T_TERM|FRONTIER|ONCE|ONCE_PWB|OWN_NOPATH|   \
                               KEY_SEP|SCOPE|WRITER|INIT_FALSE|INIT_TRUE|DERIVES|PRED_EXTRACT|BADNESS|STATUS_CONSIST|REACH|... */    \
  X(uint32_t, from_node, ) X(uint32_t, to_node, )  /* certificate-view nodes */              \
  X(uint32_t, site_from, ) X(uint32_t, site_to, )  /* -> ANCHORS */                          \
  X(uint32_t, phi_entry, )                         /* Φ rule / effect / binding id */        \
  X(uint16_t, tstatus,  )   /* COMPUTED|DECLARED_VERIFIED|DECLARED_TRUSTED|ASSUMED_VIS */     \
  X(uint16_t, kstatus,  )   /* MUST_KEY|MAY_KEY|NO_KEY|NA */                                  \
  X(uint32_t, view,     )   /* VIEW_CERT; any other value is rejected by kz proofcheck */
#define KZ_VERDICT_FIELDS(X)                                                                 \
  X(uint64_t, instance, )   /* (contract, context-qualified template, key) */                \
  X(uint8_t,  provider, )   /* PRESENT|NONE|ENV_ABSENT|UNKNOWN */                             \
  X(uint8_t,  rsv1,     )   /* was reach; reach is per cell (EXT-20261012 §4.2); must be 0 */ \
  X(uint8_t,  scope,    )   /* IN_SCOPE|TEARDOWN */                                           \
  X(uint8_t,  outcome_label, ) /* NONE|VIOLATION|LOSS_UNDER_ORDER|NOT_READY_UNJUDGED (safety) */\
  X(uint8_t,  shape, )      X(uint8_t, consequence, )                                         \
  X(uint8_t,  harm, )       X(uint8_t, fw_doc, ) /* set: DOC_NOT_ERROR|DOC_RESULT|UNDOCUMENTED */ \
  X(uint32_t, sublabels, )  X(uint32_t, ncells, )   /* followed by ncells kz_verdict_cell */   \
  X(kz_ref,   chain, )      X(kz_ref, provenance, ) /* chain: violation chains only; 104 bytes */
#define KZ_VERDICT_CELL_FIELDS(X)                                                            \
  X(uint8_t,  sigma, )      /* SIGMA_U|SIGMA_U_INF|SIGMA_R1|SIGMA_E */                         \
  X(uint8_t,  mlevel, )     /* M_API|M_IMPL */                                                \
  X(uint8_t,  cls, )        /* INACTIVE|PROTECTED|FIXED|NO_FINAL|SCHED_DEP|ORDINAL_SPLIT|      \
                               CONTESTED|UNKNOWN, from the safety record at the primary bound */\
  X(uint8_t,  unknown_reason, ) /* incl. NO_DECISION_ROUTE (¬OK without a decision route) and \
                                   HORIZON (a trace ended while a monitor was OPEN) */         \
  X(uint16_t, refinements, )/* TOLERATED|TIMEOUT_DEP|TIMEOUT_RESCUED */                      \
  X(uint16_t, realization, )/* CERT|MODEL|PREDICTED|PRED_MODELLED|PRED_EXTRAP|REALIZED */      \
  X(uint8_t,  naturalness, )/* NATURAL|HELD_F0|GATED_F1|TIMEOUT_EXERCISED */                  \
  X(uint8_t,  flags, )      /* DERIVED|SIGMA_E_HELD|SAFETY_TRANSFERRED|DECIDES */             \
  X(uint8_t,  reach, )      /* PROVED|ASSUMED|MODEL_B|UNREACHED_B|CONTESTED (primary bound) */\
  X(uint8_t,  progress, )   /* P_UNKNOWN|P_STALL_WITNESS|P_NO_STALL_B|P_CONTESTED */          \
  X(uint8_t,  progress_reason, ) /* NOT_CHECKED|BOUND|BUDGET|UNMODELLED|TEARDOWN_SCOPE|        \
                                    MODEL_GAP|MODEL_FAULT */                                  \
  X(uint8_t,  progress_harm, )   /* NONE|HANG_OR_DEADLOCK|TASK_NEVER_RUNS|                     \
                                    TERMINAL_PENDING_PANIC|TERMINAL_PENDING_ABORT */          \
  X(uint8_t,  progress_realization, ) /* NONE|MODEL|REALIZED */                                \
  X(uint8_t,  safety_routes, )   /* two bits each: bits 0-1 ¬Bad, 2-3 ¬OK, 4-5 ¬NR;            \
                                    NONE|CERT|MODEL|TRANSFERRED; bits 6-7 zero */             \
  X(uint16_t, progress_flags, )  /* DEADLOCK_CYCLE|LEAKED_LOCK|IDLE|TERMINAL_PENDING|          \
                                    CHOICE_DEPENDENT|CONFIRMABLE|CHAINED|                     \
                                    TIMEOUT_MASKED_LIVENESS|TML_ROOT */                       \
  X(uint8_t,  progress_kind, )   /* set: PRE_USE|IN_USE|HORIZON */                             \
  X(uint8_t,  fw_conf, )    /* AS_DOCUMENTED|DEVIATES|UNKNOWN (framework level, §1.10) */     \
  X(uint32_t, observed_n, ) X(uint32_t, observed_N, )  /* pooled over identical pool keys */   \
  X(kz_ref,   bounds, )     X(kz_ref, refinement_bounds, ) X(kz_ref, progress_bounds, )       \
  X(kz_ref,   pool_key, )   X(kz_ref, certificate, )   X(kz_ref, trust, )                     \
  X(kz_ref,   witness_bad, ) X(kz_ref, witness_good, ) X(kz_ref, witness_stall, )             \
  X(kz_ref,   progress_chain, ) X(kz_ref, runs, )
  /* 20 bytes of small fields, 8 of counts, 11 kz_ref of 40 bytes: 468 bytes, no implicit padding.
     `bounds` points to the cell's entries in section BOUNDS (KZ_BOUND_FIELDS); the witness
     references are the first entries of the primary bound's ranges, whose counts are there. */
#define KZ_BOUND_FIELDS(X)                     /* one entry per (cell, bound), section BOUNDS */ \
  X(uint32_t, h, )          /* the bound; 0xFFFFFFFF = b = ∞ */                               \
  X(uint8_t,  policy, )     /* policy status of the bound (deviation, D15) */                  \
  X(uint8_t,  cls, )        /* as the cell's cls, incl. CONTESTED */                           \
  X(uint8_t,  reach, )      X(uint8_t, progress, )   /* progress incl. P_CONTESTED */          \
  X(uint8_t,  progress_kind, ) X(uint8_t, safety_routes, ) X(uint8_t, flags, )                \
  X(uint8_t,  unknown_reason, ) X(uint8_t, progress_reason, ) X(uint8_t, progress_harm, )     \
  X(uint16_t, refinements, ) X(uint16_t, progress_flags, ) X(uint16_t, rsv0, )               \
  X(uint32_t, n_bad, )      X(uint32_t, n_good, )    X(uint32_t, n_stall, )  /* range sizes */ \
  X(kz_ref,   witness_bad, ) X(kz_ref, witness_good, ) X(kz_ref, witness_stall, ) /* firsts */\
  X(kz_ref,   progress_chain, )                                                               \
  /* 32 bytes of fields, 4 kz_ref of 40 bytes: 192 bytes, no implicit padding. The witnesses of
     one (cell, bound) are written contiguously in WITNESSES; each witness record carries its
     occurrence ordinal (canonical actor-instance name, k) and status (S0 layout). */
#define KZ_TRUST_FIELDS(X)                     /* one trust-ledger entry (§4.5.1) */          \
  X(uint8_t,  part, )       /* RECHECKED|EXTRACTED|TRUSTED|REFUTABLE = parts (i)-(iv) */      \
  X(uint8_t,  kind, )       /* (i) CERT_STEP; (ii) FACT|SKEL|CLOSURE|ANCHOR|RUN|PROBE;        \
                               (iii) PHI_ENTRY|DECLARED_TRUSTED|ASSUMPTION|LEMMA|TOOL_BASE;    \
                               (iv) OBLIGATION */                                             \
  X(uint8_t,  status, )     /* (i) ACCEPTED|ACCEPTED_VIA_T5; (iv) NOT_RUN|                    \
                               NOT_REFUTED_ON_EXERCISED|REFUTED; otherwise NA */              \
  X(uint8_t,  rsv0, )                                                                         \
  X(uint32_t, ident, )      /* step index | Φ entry id | assumption, lemma or obligation id */ \
  X(uint32_t, site, )       /* -> ANCHORS, or 0 */                                            \
  X(uint32_t, refutes, )    /* (iv): index of the (ii)/(iii) entry it can refute; else 0 */   \
  X(kz_ref,   source, )     /* the record itself; its artifact sha256 binds the extraction */ \
  /* 56 bytes */
#define KZ_TRUST_LEDGER_FIELDS(X)              /* one per certificate and per verdict cell */ \
  X(uint32_t, n_rechecked, ) X(uint32_t, n_extracted, )                                       \
  X(uint32_t, n_trusted, )   X(uint32_t, n_refutable, )                                       \
  X(uint32_t, first, )       X(uint32_t, count, )     /* entry range in section TRUST */      \
  X(kz_ref,   facts, )  X(kz_ref, skel, )  X(kz_ref, anchors, )  X(kz_ref, profile, )          \
  X(kz_ref,   checker, )    /* tool-hash record of the kz proofcheck build that accepted */   \
  /* 224 bytes */
/* each list expands into: struct, dump printer, offset asserts, and
   _Static_assert(sizeof(T) == KZ_SUM_SIZES(T##_FIELDS)) */
```

Section types: `STRINGS, BASIS, FACTS, DERIVES, SITES, ANCHORS, TEMPLATES, SKEL_CERT, SKEL_EXPL, ONCE, PROVENANCE_MSG, WRITERS, ALLOCS, ACTORS, RESOURCES, RAW_EVENTS, EVENTS, STACKS, EDGES, INSTANCES, CERTIFICATES, TRUST_LEDGERS, TRUST, WITNESSES, HOLDPLANS, VERDICTS, VERDICT_CELLS, BOUNDS, LOSS, RUNMETA, OBLIGATIONS, PERTURBATION`.

| Artifact | Producer → consumer | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `basis.kz` | basis → all | B items with value, source, policy status; file hashes; selected script path and reset type; euid, CWD; canonical S0 and its content hash; stratum |
| `profile.kzp` (text) → `profile.kz` | human → libkz-core | Φ: kinds, ops, effects with per-conjunct LPs, bindings, rules, contracts, constants, version guards; provenance and authoring record per entry |
| `facts.kz`, `anchors.kz` | extract, link | call sites, arguments (constant, symbolic, ⊤), guards, table values, doc annotations, `derives` facts, writer sites per abstract location, escape and cast listings (A-TYPE), serialized loop-exit expressions; site → gcc address and cost class; audit |
| `skel.kz` | skel | certificate view and exploration view per actor; once facts; message-provenance sets; loop summaries with bounds and policy status; summary fallbacks |
| `census.kz` | static, proofcheck | instances, MustBefore (bitsets), certificates with their trust ledgers (written when `kz proofcheck` accepts), verdicts, open candidates, progress candidates |
| `plan.kz` | plan | probe sites (file inode, offset, instruction class, decoder, cookie), watchpoints, gates, debug-register allocation per thread, expected cost |
| `run-<id>.kzt` | observe / hold / gate | raw events, strings, stacks, loss table, run metadata (B hash, plan hash, S0 hash, stratum, holds, observer CPU time and placement, harness version, kernel, backend) |
| `hb-<id>.kz` | trace | events, identities, edges with tags, per-conjunct LP states, routed candidates, chains |
| `witness-<cand>.kz`, `holdplan-<cand>.kz` | predict / explore | schedules with realization labels; hold points, release events, watchdog with reference |
| `verdicts.kz` | analyses → report | append-only verdict store, versioned by B, Φ and tool hashes |
| `loop-<iter>.kz` | loop | run IDs, B/plan/Φ/tool hashes, deltas, obligation results, work units spent |

**The profile DSL Φ** [D] (fixes [X3-M11], [X1-M8]). It is parsed by a hand-written recursive-descent parser in `libkz-core` and **interpreted, never compiled**. It has three sublanguages, all fixed at S0:

1. **Typed core expression language** (for Req conjuncts, guards, falsifier conditions and HB-rule conditions). Sorts: `Bool`, `Int`, `Key<K>`, `Id<K>`, `State<K>` (an enum with the order declared in the kind), `Gen`. Terms: literals; `state(r)`, `key(r)`, `gen(r)`; bound parameters (`arg.name`, `hdr.MsgId`, `exec`, `owner(exec)`); comparisons (`=`, `≠`, and `<`, `≤` on ordered sorts); `in {…}`; `∧ ∨ ¬ ⇒`; bounded quantifiers `forall r: K where φ` and `exists r: K where φ` over **declared resource kinds only** (finite in every model state). There are no C arrays, C function calls or pointer expressions.
2. **Bindings.** C memory enters only through `bind` declarations on effects: an S3a abstract location `(struct type, field)` or a global field is bound to a component of a resource (`bind field CFE_ES_AppRecord_t.AppState as state(es.appstate(record))`). O7 checks every binding: each store to a bound location must lie at an effect LP. Oracles are **sets of C functions** whose read sets define support locations; they are never evaluated.
3. **AST-pattern language** with a match semantics:
   ```
   pattern := 'fn' '(' NAME ')' '.' op [ '#' ORDINAL ] [ 'macro' ('yes'|'no'|'any') ]
   op      := 'call' '(' NAME { ',' argpred } ')' | 'store' '(' TYPE '.' FIELD ')' | 'memset' '(' TYPE ')'
            | 'load' '(' TYPE '.' FIELD ')' | 'loop' '(' 'poll' ')' | 'return'
   argpred := 'arg' '[' INT ']' ( '==' CONST | '!=' CONST | 'is' 'addr' '(' TYPE '.' FIELD ')' | 'any' )
   ```
   The match set of a pattern is the set of AST nodes in the bodies of all definitions of function `NAME` in B's link set (same-named local copies distinguished by DWARF compile unit, §3.17) whose kind, callee or field match, whose constant-evaluated arguments satisfy the predicates, and whose macro-expansion provenance matches the flag; the ordinal selects the k-th match in source order. A pattern with exactly one match is bound; zero or several matches go to the anchor audit.

**One evaluator, three domains** (one exception, §2.1 principle 1: `kz proofcheck` decides `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own symbolic procedure; its other steps that evaluate Φ expressions call this evaluator and are marked "via T5" in the trust ledger, §4.5.1). `libkz-core` implements one evaluator of the core expression language behind an abstract-domain interface with three instances: concrete (trace states), abstract (the step-6 domain of §4.2: constants, intervals, defined/undefined/reserved IDs, record-type tags; results in {true, false, maybe}) and operational (explorer states). A property test on small random models checks that the concrete and operational instances agree exactly and that the abstract result always contains the concrete one.

Excerpt (citations from §3):

```
profile cfs "cFS 088b2fa8 / cFE c5fb2b4d / OSAL d2d877a6 / PSP c4b3b0b6";

const CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC = ref eval "CFE_PLATFORM_CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC"
                                  in "cfe/modules/es/fsw/src/cfe_es_start.c";   # constant-evaluated in its TU
const PSP_RESTART_DELAY     = ref eval "CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY"
                                  in "psp/fsw/pc-linux/src/cfe_psp_support.c";

kind evs.reg(slot: Int)        { states UNREG < REG; initial UNREG; }
kind es.shutdown()             { states RUN < SHUTDOWN_REQ < TEARDOWN; initial RUN; scope process; }

op CFE_ES_GetAppID(out AppIdPtr) {
  bind symbol "CFE_ES_GetAppID";
  effect use es.self(exec) lp at fn("CFE_ES_GetAppID").call("CFE_ES_GetAppRecordByContext");  # cfe_es_api.c:750
  effect store *AppIdPtr := id(owner(exec))        when result == CFE_SUCCESS;
  effect store *AppIdPtr := CFE_ES_APPID_UNDEFINED when result != CFE_SUCCESS;  # neutral for validity
  outcome CFE_ES_ERR_RESOURCEID_NOT_VALID : not_ready(es.self);
}

op CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID(EventID, EventType, stamp AppID, Spec) {
  bind symbol "CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID";
  conjunct valid(arg.AppID)              lp capture;                             # T-DERIVE (§1.4)
  conjunct state(evs.reg(arg.AppID)) = REG lp at fn("CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID").call("EVS_GetAppDataByID");
  outcome CFE_EVS_APP_ILLEGAL_APP_ID : not_ready(valid);                         # cfe_evs.c:185-189
  outcome CFE_EVS_APP_NOT_REGISTERED : not_ready(evs.reg);                       # cfe_evs.c:190-194
}
```
