On pc-linux the list is, everywhere in this document: `abort` itself; `CFE_PSP_Panic` (its `abort()` at `cfe_psp_support.c:120`); the requester's step after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` (its `abort()` at `:107`; the requester has already stored `ShutdownReq` at `:87`); and the PSP main thread's return from `main` after the teardown (`osal/src/bsp/generic-linux/src/bsp_start.c:247`). `exit` at `cfe_psp_start.c:554` runs before `CFE_ES_Main`; `OS_ApplicationExit` has no caller on a pc-linux cFE path. The model still lets the other actors continue, which over-approximates safety. States after a process-terminal step are excluded from stall evaluation and are sinks for deadness. A stall from whose every successor state a process end (a process-terminal step or a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP) stays reachable without B.Input's stop event is `terminal-pending` (§1.8). The attribute's surface syntax in the Φ grammar is fixed at S0.

**Message provenance** [D] (fixes [X1-M7](a)). For each pipe `p` (created at a site with a resolved key):
- `Routes_B(p)` = MsgIds subscribed to `p` at subscribe sites reachable in `Sk^cert`;
- `Tx_B` = keys of transmit sites reachable in `Sk^cert` (header-carried keys, §3.9; a `⊤` key contributes every MsgId routed anywhere);
- `Input_B(p)` = MsgIds of B.Input routed to `p`;
- `Prov(p) = (Routes_B(p) ∩ Tx_B) ∪ Input_B(p)`, with function codes carried the same way (`⊤` if not constant).

Reachability and `Prov` depend on each other (a transmit inside a handler counts only if its handler is reachable), so they are computed as the **least fixpoint** of: start from the code reachable without dispatch; add the handlers whose `(MsgId, code)` is in the current `Prov`; recompute `Tx_B` and `Prov`; repeat. Handlers outside the final `Prov` are folded away in both views. This is sound under A9 (B.Input is the complete set of external events) and the transmit-key analysis: every message that reaches a pipe was transmitted by a reachable site or injected by B.Input. Expected effects [I]: `K-ATOM.tbl-load` and HS AppMon (default tables `NOACT`) become `inactive` in the default B; ES's reset commands are unreachable without an input.

**Loops** (no arbitrary unrolling):
- **Poll loops, recognized by pattern** [D] (fixes [X1-M8]): a loop whose body calls a Φ delay op and whose exit edges test shared loads, results of Φ operations, or a count/time budget. The loop becomes `await(Pred_code) {success} {timeout}` where **`Pred_code` is extracted** from the serialized exit-by-condition branch by symbolic evaluation over the step-6 domain. Recognized forms: (a) a direct test of a shared load or a Φ-op result (`SystemState < MinSystemState`); (b) a counter-zero test after a constant-trip loop, `cnt := 0; for i in [0,N): if φ(i) then cnt++; exit iff cnt = 0`, which yields `∀ i < N: ¬φ(i)`; (c) Boolean combinations of these. Anything else → `unmodelled-sync`. The summary is valid (Lemma L1, §6.2) only if (i) the body's mod set contains no relevant location other than the counter and budget, and (ii) all reads of the test happen inside one critical section of one lock, or the test reads one location atomically (`MainTaskSyncDelay`: inside the ES lock; `WaitForSystemState`: one `volatile sig_atomic_t` read). If (i) or (ii) fails, the loop is not summarized: it is unrolled to its reference bound if it has one, else star-closed, and T-OBS does not apply to it. Under M_impl, loops containing OSAL lookups are not summarized, because lookups can leak the lock and their unlock broadcasts consume waiters' attempts ([A §4.2]). An OSAL call in `OS_LOCK_MODE_NONE` is not a lookup in this sense: it takes no type lock and fails at once on a RESERVED record instead of waiting (`osapi-idmap.c:329, 460`), so neither reason applies [D] (EXT-20261012). `MainTaskSyncDelay`'s loop (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`), whose `CFE_ES_LockSharedData` calls `OS_MutSemTake` with such a token conversion (`osapi-mutex.c:190`), is therefore summarized at both M. Under σ_U∞ its summarized poller is dead when the predicate can never hold, whereas the unsummarized loop would strand it; deadness preservation is part of L1's obligation (§1.8, §6.2).
  - Under σ_U∞, two time-based transitions are removed (§1.8): the timeout transition of the summary, and the budget exit of the same loop when it is not summarized. Switching L1 on or off therefore does not change σ_U∞'s finite executions on the loop's relevant events, up to the loop's failing test reads, which change no relevant state (stuttering; L1, §6.2) [I]. These are statements about finite executions. For progress the two forms differ: under σ_U∞ the summarized poller is dead when its predicate can never become true, the unsummarized one is stranded on its stale failing test (§1.8); deadness preservation is therefore part of L1's obligation (§6.2), and with L1 switched off (ablation, the directed check of O10) the results that rest on a summary are `unknown(unmodelled)`. A delay-paced loop whose last attempt is several transitions at level M (its test reads several values **outside one critical section and outside one atomic read**, i.e. L1's precondition (ii) fails; or it calls a Φ op that M splits at its LPs, such as an OSAL lookup at M_impl) is not summarized; under σ_U∞ only the branch that takes the stale failing result into the exhaustion path is removed, so its actor completes the attempt (releasing any lock it took) and can then be stranded (§1.8); deadness tests on it, and on the actors it could enable, are undecided.
- **Count-bounded retry loops** → unrolled exactly to the bound **constant-evaluated in the build** (CF: effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`; OSAL: 5 attempts, `osapi-idmap.c:475–480`). The exhaustion branch is reachable only after the last attempt, in every σ (fixes [X1-M5](c)). If a Φ delay op paces the iterations, the loop is tagged **delay-paced**: the failing outcome of its last attempt is then a time-based transition, which σ_U∞ removes (§1.8). If that attempt is one transition at level M (an L1 summary, or one Φ-op call that M models as one transition and whose outcome is the test, as CF's lookup at M_api), under σ_U∞ it is enabled exactly when it would succeed. If it is several transitions at M (CF's lookup at M_impl, which M_impl splits at its LPs), the time-based transition is the branch that takes the failing result into the exhaustion path (`cf_cfdp.c:1285` in the last iteration); the actor then completes the attempt and is stranded, and deadness tests on it and on the actors it could enable are undecided (`unknown(unmodelled)`). A non-constant bound gives `unknown(bound)`.
- **Constant-trip loops** over B constants with relevant events in the body (the core object table loop of `CFE_ES_CreateObjects`; the startup script; record-table scans) → unrolled.
- **Message loops** (`while (CFE_ES_RunLoop(...)) { ReceiveBuffer; dispatch }` and core main loops) → a handler automaton with `handler_begin/end` marks: star-closed in `Sk^cert`, unrolled to h in `Sk^h`.
- **Other loops** containing relevant events → star-closed in `Sk^cert`, unrolled to h in `Sk^h`.

**Once facts.** For every static actor, once(τ) is decided by §1.2 on `Sk^cert`; for every node, once(n) is "once(actor) and not inside a star-closed region". The facts and their derivations go to `skel.kz`.

**Recursion** makes the affected summary `unknown`.

**Inlining and its fallback** (fixes [X1-m7]). Full call-string inlining is worst-case exponential in call depth. If skeleton construction for an actor exceeds its work-unit cap (a deviation, §4.15), the functions responsible fall back to **per-(actor, function) summaries**: one copy of the function body per actor, shared by all its call sites in that actor. The fallback is recorded per function. Precision lost under it, all in the sound direction: T-PO sees spurious paths through the shared body (smaller MB); `Anc(u)` grows (fewer frontier nodes); nodes in a shared body called from more than one site are not once; templates inside the shared body are no longer per-context, which can withhold R2-style certificates for uses inside shared SB functions. Fallback counts and their effect on certificates are measured at S3a.

**Output** `skel.kz`: both views, sizes, local-choice counts, loop summaries with their bounds and policy status, extracted predicates, provenance sets, once facts, fallback list.

Assumption **A-SKEL**: `Sk^cert` over-approximates each actor instance's projected event sequences under B. O1/O2 test it on every trace. `Sk^h` is an under-approximation beyond h by construction and is never used for universal claims.

### 4.4 The MustBefore fixpoint (`kz-static static`)

**Graph.** The nodes of `Sk^cert` for all actors; intra-actor edges plus spawn edges (spawn site → child entry). MB is computed per M (M_impl skeletons differ on the M_impl-relevant operations).

**Definition.** `a ∈ MB(n)` means: in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` (equivalently, for σ_U, every execution of `Exec(B,σ_U,M)`), at every occurrence of `n`, some occurrence of `a` has occurred earlier. MB is the **greatest** solution of the equations below [D] ([A §4.4], corrected per [J1-1], [J2-2], [X1-m2], [X2-01]).

**Base cases** (fixes [X1-m2]): `MB(entry) := ∅` for the initial actor (PSP main), for environment roots, for asynchronous contexts without an in-model registration site (signal handlers, the exception path), and for every actor whose in-model spawn-site list is empty. An empty meet in T-SPAWN is ∅, never ⊤. All other nodes start at ⊤.

| Rule | Equation contribution | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| T-PO | `in_PO(n) = ∩_{p ∈ pred(n)} (MB(p) ∪ {p})` over `Sk^cert` predecessors (including back edges of star-closed loops); terminal ops have no successors (T-TERM) | program order; dominance modulo terminal branches |
| T-SPAWN | for an actor entry `n_τ`: `in(n_τ) = ∩_{c ∈ SpawnSites(τ)} (MB(c) ∪ {c})`, a meet over **all** spawn sites (registration sites for callback actors). If any spawn site of τ is unresolved (`unknown_effect`), `in(n_τ) = ∅` | `OS_TaskCreate` → child start ([R01 §2.2]) |
| T-OBS | for an observing exit `o` with observed conjunct `C` (an instance implied by the extracted `Pred_code`), falsifier `f` (§4.2 step 9) and the writer set `W_o(MB)` defined below: `in(o) = in_PO(o) ∪ ∩_{w ∈ W_o(MB)} (MB(w) ∪ {w})`. Applies only if `W_C` has no `DISABLED` entry, `Pred_code ⇒ C` is established, and `C` is false right after `f` (or at INIT when no falsifier exists). If `W_o = ∅` at the fixpoint, `o` is MB-unreachable and marked | reads-from: some writer between `f` and `o` made `C` true |
| T-TERM | terminal operations have no successor edges | panic and the restart requester end ([J1 §1 check 1]; §3.5) |
| (none) | lock acquisition alone gives no must-order | locks give atomicity, not order ([R04 L5]) |

**The writer set of an observation** [D]. `W_o(MB)` contains every context-qualified node of every writer site in `W_C` (all contexts of each writer site, fixes [X1-m2]), **except** those removed by one of four rules. `kz proofcheck` re-checks each removal relative to its inputs:
- OWN-NOPATH and the PWB rules, by the checker's own reachability on the `Sk^cert` graph;
- KEY-SEP, as consistency of the entry's `NO_KEY` status with the writer section. The status itself is an extracted fact and may rest on A-FRESH (§4.5.1).

The four rules:

1. **OWN-NOPATH** (fixes [X2-01]): `w` lies in `o`'s own actor, `once(actor(o))` holds, and `Sk^cert` has no path `w →* o`. *Soundness:* if `w` occurred between `f` and an occurrence `o_k` in that actor's (unique) run, the run would contain `w … o_k`, so by A-SKEL the skeleton would contain a path `w →* o`. Example: `memset(AppRecPtr, 0, …)` at `cfe_es_start.c:823`, whose only continuation is the panic.
2. **PWB-before-falsifier**: `PWB_MB(w, f)`, by the frontier rule or by once-PWB (§4.5), using the current MB.
3. **PWB-after-observation**: `PWB_MB(o, w)`.
4. **KEY-SEP**: the key status of `w` w.r.t. `C`'s key is `NO_KEY` (§4.2 step 8).

`W_o` is **recomputed on every iteration** from the current MB. The operator stays monotone: as MB shrinks, fewer PWB facts hold, `W_o` grows, and the meet over more and smaller sets shrinks. Starting from ⊤ (where the PWB rules may prune everything and the empty meet is ⊤), iteration converges to the greatest fixpoint.

Priority rules of σ_R1 are **not** in MB; σ_R1 is handled by the explorer.

```
for each actor τ (certificate view, per M):
    MB(entry_τ) := ∅  if τ is the initial actor, an environment root, an asynchronous context without
                      in-model registration, or has no in-model spawn site;   MB(n) := ⊤ otherwise
repeat
  for each actor skeleton, for n in reverse post-order:
     in := in_PO(n)                                         # T-PO; terminal ops have no successors (T-TERM)
     if n is an actor entry with spawn sites: in := ∩_{c ∈ SpawnSites} (MB(c) ∪ {c})   # T-SPAWN
     if n is an observing exit with a valid writer set:
         W := W_o(MB)                                       # recomputed: OWN-NOPATH, PWB (frontier, once), KEY-SEP
         in := in ∪ ∩_{w ∈ W} (MB(w) ∪ {w})                 # T-OBS
     MB(n) := in
until no set changes      # monotone decreasing from ⊤: terminates at the greatest fixpoint
```

**Complexity** ([A §4.4]). With N nodes and R relevant events (bitset width), each update costs `O((indeg + |W_o|) · R/64)` word operations, plus the PWB checks for `W_o` (one reachability query per frontier candidate, cached per iteration). Sets only shrink, so there are at most N·R changing updates. MB is computed once per (B, Φ, M).

**Justifications.** For each fact a certificate uses, the derivation (rule, predecessor, writer exclusions) is reconstructed on demand by backward search over the final equations.

### 4.5 Certificates and the checker (independent inference relative to its trusted base)

Let `Anc(x)` be the set of nodes of `x`'s actor that have a `Sk^cert` path to `x` (including `x`). All certificates are computed per M on `Sk^cert`.

**PWB certificates** [D] (fixes [X1-M2]):
- **FRONTIER**: `PWB(a, b)` if `once(actor(a))` and `∃ m` in `actor(a)` with `m ∉ Anc(a)` and `m ∈ MB(b)` (L-PWB, §6.2).
- **ONCE-PWB**: `PWB(a, b)` if `once(a)` and `a ∈ MB(b)`: `a` occurs at most once, and an occurrence of `a` precedes every occurrence of `b`, so that single occurrence precedes all of them (L-ONCE, §6.2). Example: the raise of ES main (`cfe_es_task.c:134`) is once and in `MB(o_ES) ⊆ MB(f_EVS)`, so `PWB(raise_ES, f_EVS)` (App. A.1).

**SCOPE exclusion** [D] (fixes [X1-M6], [X2-04]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.1): a writer or disabler `d` with `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB(d)` is dropped from certificates for in-scope occurrences, **per conjunct**. An occurrence of `d` before a conjunct LP that lies at or before an occurrence's decision makes that occurrence out of scope, because scope is decided at the decision event (§1.6; L-SCOPE). A conjunct LP after the decision belongs to an occurrence already decided by another false conjunct, or whose call returned before it (`NOT_EVALUATED`, counted by no statement). SCOPE is **not** used inside T-OBS, because MB facts hold for all executions, teardown included.

**FIXED_ORDER certificate** for instance `i` at level M ([J2-1], [J3-M1]). Choose a conjunct `P_j`, with its LP node `ℓ_j` at the use (the capture point for value conjuncts). All of the following must hold:

1. `INIT_FALSE(P_j)` for the instance's key.
2. `W_j` (after `NO_KEY` and SCOPE exclusion) has no `DISABLED` entry.
3. If `W_j = ∅`: provider `none` (or `env-absent` if Φ guards the provider with an absent env capability); the class follows from badness (item 5).
4. **Frontier**: `once(actor(ℓ_j))` and `F = (Nodes(actor(ℓ_j)) \ Anc(ℓ_j)) ∩ ⋂_{x ∈ W_j} MB(x) ≠ ∅`. Pick `m ∈ F` (closest to `ℓ_j`). Equivalently, per writer, `PWB(ℓ_j, x)` by FRONTIER or ONCE-PWB.
5. **Badness**: every not-ready outcome of the use is outside `Allowed` on every `Sk^cert` continuation within the horizon (trivial for `Allowed = none`), i.e. the entry's monitor never ACCEPTs: for an ACCEPT that is a control event (a normal continuation, a fallback operation, a deferral point, a documented exit) by the path check of §1.6; for an ACCEPT that needs a READY occurrence of the same instance (`retry_until_ready`) by the certificate's own INIT_FALSE and frontier or once-PWB facts, which cover every occurrence of `ℓ_j` (EXT-20261012 §4.1). The certificate records the REJECT events on which the item relies, each with whether it lies at the occurrence's decision event. Only a badness item whose REJECTs all lie at the decision (`Allowed none`, `unspecified_by_reference`, an outcome outside the entry's codes) gives `¬OK_i` a decision route (§1.10), in every σ; a REJECT after the decision (a retry's exhaustion, an error exit or return) gives none, because the path to it can be blocked. If only some continuations are disallowed, the certificate establishes "fixed not-ready" (shape WRONG-WAY or ABSENT) and the class stays open.
6. **Reach**, per cell (§1.10): `proved` from an observed occurrence (run id) or a confirmed model path, under the per-cell conditions of §1.10 (at M_api, L-MLEVEL or the M_api-conformance check of the run's prefix; for σ_U∞, a prefix without time-based transitions); otherwise `reach = assumed` ("fixed-order if reached").

Certificate = (INIT_FALSE proof; `derives` fact if `P_j` is a value conjunct; `W_j` with transition and key statuses; the once facts used; the frontier node with its no-path proof, or the ONCE-PWB facts; one MB derivation per writer, each including its T-OBS writer exclusions; badness proof; reach). The proof never needs the use to dominate anything, so uses behind early returns are covered.

**What it proves** (L-FO). In every finite execution of `TS(B,σ_U,M)`, every in-scope occurrence of the use whose LP `ℓ_j` lies at or before its decision has `P_j` false at `ℓ_j`; any other decided occurrence was decided earlier by another false conjunct, or its call returned without passing `ℓ_j` and it is `NOT_EVALUATED` (§1.6). So no reached in-scope occurrence is `READY`, and by item 5 no continuation makes one `TOLERATED`. This is the safety statement `¬OK_i`; item 5's REJECT events give its decision route only if they lie at the occurrence's decision (§1.10). It does not prove that the use is reached, that every execution reaches a decision, or anything about executions that do not reach the use.

**PROTECTED certificate.** For every conjunct `P_j`, one of:

- (a) `P_j` is true at INIT and every `d ∈ D_j` is removed by NO_KEY, SCOPE, or is MB-unreachable; or
- (b) `∃ x* ∈ W_j^must` with key status `MUST_KEY` and transition status `COMPUTED` or `DECLARED_VERIFIED`, with `x* ∈ MB(ℓ_j)`, and every remaining `d ∈ D_j` satisfies `PWB(d, x*)` or `PWB(ℓ_j, d)` (by FRONTIER or ONCE-PWB), or is removed by NO_KEY or SCOPE (fixes [X1-M3](a)).

No `DISABLED` writer may exist; reach as above.

**What it proves** (L-PR). In every finite execution of `TS(B,σ_U,M)`, every conjunct of an in-scope occurrence of the use holds at its LP whenever that LP lies at or before the occurrence's decision, so every decided in-scope occurrence is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED`. This is the statement `¬NR_i`, which the record keeps (§1.10) and which implies `¬Bad_i`. It proves nothing about:
- executions that do not reach the use (deadlock, permanent waits, lost providers or lost spawns);
- reach;
- horizons.

The verdict's progress value is `unknown(not_checked)` unless `exh` sets it (§4.6, §4.10). Because the certificate proves `READY`, it never supports the refinement `tolerated`, which comes only from `exh` (§1.10); a decided not-ready occurrence found against it is a contradiction (from a run) or an O10 disagreement (from `exh`) (§4.12 item 5).

**INACTIVE**: no `Sk^cert` path from any actor entry reaches the template under B constants and message provenance. Proof = the folded path condition plus the provenance sets used. `Sk^cert` over-approximates paths (A-SKEL), so "no path" is sound; `Sk^h` is never used for INACTIVE.

**Provider axis**: `none` iff `P_j` is INIT_FALSE and `W_j = ∅` with no `DISABLED` entry; `env-absent` iff the only `W_j` entries are Φ effects guarded by an `env` predicate that B.Env records false (status `DECLARED_TRUSTED`) and observation agrees (O4).

**Checker `kz proofcheck`: independent inference relative to its trusted base** ([A §4.4]; [J1-1]; scope stated by EXT-20261012 §4.3). It is a separate module of `kz`: it shares no analysis code with `kz-static static` and links no LLVM. Its independence is independence of **inference**, not of **facts**, and it holds **except in the steps that call the shared `libkz-core` (T5) evaluator or rule engine**; reading inputs through T5's container I/O and Φ parser keeps a step own. The steps that call the evaluator or rule engine are marked "re-validated via T5" in the trust ledger.

**Trusted base** (recorded in every certificate's trust ledger, §4.5.1):
- (T1) `facts.kz`: AST facts; `derives` copy chains; the writer sections (W, W^must, D with transition and key statuses and the transition-filter classification of §4.2 step 6); ALLOCS; the A-TYPE listings; the serialized loop-exit expressions with their mod sets and lock regions; the INIT image; constant evaluations (A-EXTRACT).
- (T2) `skel.kz` and `anchors.kz`: the `Sk^cert` graph that `skel` built from `link`'s IR call graph, covering inlining and context qualification, exact unrolling, star-closure, poll-loop recognition, message provenance and `TRUNC` marks. The checker's dominator, reachability and once routines run on this graph; the checker does not rebuild it (A-SKEL).
- (T3) the closure's spawn-site lists (A3).
- (T4) Φ: rules, effects, bindings, LPs, T-TERM declarations, contracts and Allowed entries (A1).
- (T5) `libkz-core`, shared with the producer: Φ parser, core-expression evaluator, rule engine, container I/O (A-CORE). Every step reads its inputs through the container I/O and the Φ parser.
- (T6) the run, `hb-*.kz` and prefix-probe artifacts that the reach step names (A2, A6).

**Re-validated steps**, each relative to T1–T6 ("own" = the checker's own routines; "via T5" = also calls T5's evaluator or rule engine):
- **view** (own): every node is a `Sk^cert` node; any `Sk^h` or `TRUNC` node → reject (fixes [X1-C1]);
- **T-PO** (own): dominance modulo terminal ops, by its own dominator routine;
- **T-SPAWN** (own): the spawn-site list equals the closure's list; base cases ∅;
- **T-OBS**:
  - the falsifier condition (via T5: "C is false right after f" is evaluated on the abstract post-state by the shared evaluator);
  - **the predicate** (own): re-derive `Pred_code` from the serialized loop-exit expressions with its own symbolic evaluator, check the L1 preconditions on the extracted mod sets and lock regions, and decide `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own procedure, where only C's syntax tree comes from the shared Φ parser (fixes [X1-M8]);
  - `W_o` recomputed from the writer sections (own), with each exclusion re-checked: OWN-NOPATH by its own reachability; PWB; KEY-SEP as status consistency;
- **ONCE** (own): spawn-site regions and pairwise exclusivity, by its own reachability (fixes [X1-M2]);
- **DERIVES** (own): the copy chain from the call argument to `loc` in the serialized AST facts (fixes [X1-M1]);
- **FRONTIER** (own): no path `m →* ℓ_j`;
- **SCOPE** (own): `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB(d)` re-derived; its use rests on L-SCOPE (restated, §6.2), listed in ledger part (iii);
- **T-TERM** (own): Φ declares the op terminal for this PSP, with a citation. The declaration belongs to T4; its truth is not checked;
- **INIT** [D] (via T5): INIT_FALSE (FIXED_ORDER) and INIT truth (PROTECTED (a)), each evaluated as the conjunct on the INIT image of T1 by the shared evaluator;
- **badness** [D] (own) (FIXED_ORDER item 5): the `Sk^cert` path check of every continuation within the horizon of §1.6, against the Allowed entry of T4. For an entry whose ACCEPT needs a READY occurrence of the same instance (`retry_until_ready`), the step discharges it with the certificate's own INIT_FALSE and frontier (or once-PWB) facts, which cover every occurrence of `ℓ_j`. The step records the REJECT events it relies on, each with whether it lies at the occurrence's decision; only REJECTs at the decision give the decision route of §1.10;
- **statuses** (own): transition and key statuses are consistent with the writer sections. This is a consistency check; the statuses are not re-derived. Also checked: the list of `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries is complete, and the provider value is consistent with `W_j` and INIT_FALSE;
- **reach** (own): the run of T6 exists, contains the occurrence, and satisfies the cell's condition of §1.10: the M_api-conformance check of the prefix of every closed-cone actor at M_api without L-MLEVEL; for a derived σ_U∞ cell, the absence of time-based transitions on the cone actors, including OSAL-internal timed wakes at M_impl.

The rule engine is used only to resolve which Φ rule instance a step names (e.g. R19's binding, stratum and terminal escape for T-OBS); that resolution is a separate ledger entry marked via T5 [I]; the exact list of T5 calls per step is fixed at S0.

**Not re-validated** (trusted from T1–T5): the completeness of `W_j` (A-WS), the transition-filter classification, the INIT image, the `Sk^cert` graph, the closure lists, Φ and the shared `libkz-core`. Extraction errors are outside the checker's reach. The defences against them are O1, O2, O4, O5, O7, O9, the anchor audit, the litmus suite and the program mutants of §8.5.

**Certificate-mutation tests.** A mutation engine perturbs valid certificates in the following ways:
- drop a spawn site;
- delete a writer;
- swap `m` for a node in `Anc(ℓ_j)`;
- mark a non-terminal op terminal;
- replace COMPUTED by DISABLED;
- **drop a once fact** (e.g. the one that prunes `raise_ES` from `W_o(o_EVS)`);
- **re-insert `cfe_es_start.c:823` into `W_o`** without its OWN-NOPATH proof;
- **move a step into a `TRUNC` region**;
- **replace the extracted predicate by a stronger one** (e.g. one that forgets that RESERVED counts as used);
- **drop a `derives` link**;
- change a `MAY_KEY` into `MUST_KEY`;
- **flip INIT_FALSE** (claim the conjunct false at INIT where the INIT image makes it true, or the reverse for PROTECTED (a));
- **replace `Allowed none` by an allowed entry** in the badness step, or name a continuation that reaches the entry's ACCEPT event (for `returned_and_tested`, its normal continuation);
- **drop the INIT_FALSE or frontier fact** with which the badness step excludes a later READY attempt of a `retry_until_ready` entry;
- **mark a REJECT event that lies after the decision as deciding** in the decision route (e.g. a retry's exhaustion, which can be blocked by a timed wait that σ_U∞ removes);
- **claim σ_U∞ reach from a run whose prefix contains a timeout outcome on a cone actor**, or M_api reach from a run in which a cone actor other than the using actor received `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` or left a type lock held.

Every mutant must be rejected, and the kill rate is a reported checker metric. The tests check the checker, not the analysis; the program mutants of §8.5 test the analysis. The tests perturb certificates only, so they cannot detect an error in `facts.kz`, `skel.kz` or `libkz-core`. Errors there are the target of O1/O2/O7, the anchor audit, the evaluator agreement property test, the litmus suite and the program mutants of §8.5 item 5.

#### 4.5.1 Trust ledger [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.3)

Every certificate and every verdict cell carries a **trust ledger** (§2.3: `KZ_TRUST_LEDGER`, entries `KZ_TRUST`) with four parts:
1. **(i) Re-validated inference steps.** The certificate steps that `kz proofcheck` accepted, by step index, with the check performed (§4.5), each with status `ACCEPTED` (own routines) or `ACCEPTED_VIA_T5` (the step also called the shared evaluator or rule engine). "Re-validated" means correct relative to parts (ii) and (iii).
2. **(ii) Extracted facts.** Every `facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, `anchors.kz`, closure, run and prefix-probe record that the steps of (i) read. They are listed as typed references, whose artifact hashes bind the ledger to the exact extraction.
3. **(iii) Trusted entries.**
   - The Φ entries used: rules, effects, bindings, LPs, T-TERM declarations, the contract and its Allowed entry.
   - The `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries.
   - The assumptions of §6.1, by id; A2 and A6 whenever reach is proved by a run.
   - The lemma obligations whose proofs are still sketches: L-MB, L-ONCE, L-PWB, L-DERIVE, L-FO or L-PR, L1; L-SCOPE where a SCOPE exclusion is used; L-MAPI-CONE where M_api reach or an M_api witness rests on a run; and, for cells other than the certificate's own, L-STATUS, L-MON, L-CONT and L-TRANS, plus L-MLEVEL where rule 2(i) is used, L-REACH-CONE where σ_U∞ reach is proved by a run, and L-CONE-R1 for bounded σ_R1 transfers.
   - The tool base: `libkz-core` and the checker's tool hash.
4. **(iv) Refutable, not proved.** For each entry of (ii) or (iii) that an obligation can refute, the ledger records:
   - the obligation (O1–O10; O7 at model build; O10 when an `exh` record meets the certificate);
   - its status: `not run`, `not refuted on the exercised paths of runs R`, or `refuted`;
   - the exercised-path limit.

   Passing an obligation is never recorded as proof. Reach is the one item that an observation proves.

**Per-cell ledgers.** The certificate's ledger is the ledger of its own cell (σ_U, M, b = ∞). Every other cell's ledger is derived from it: a transferred cell adds the transfer lemmas and the assumptions they rest on (A7-impl, A7-api, A3, A4, A9, A-SKEL) with their obligations; a cell whose reach is proved by a run adds the run and prefix-probe records, A2 and A6, and the per-cell reach check (for a σ_U∞ cell, the TB-freedom of the cone actors with L-REACH-CONE; for an M_api cell, their M_api conformance with L-MAPI-CONE).

**Verdicts without a certificate.** For such a verdict, part (i) is empty. Part (iii) names the route's own basis: G5, L-DPOR, L-MON, the cone rule and the reductions in use for `exh`; L-PRED and A-COMM for prediction; the recorder and the oracle for realized witnesses. Part (iv) lists O1, O2, O6 and O8, and O10 where an `exh` record meets a certificate or a transferred statement.

`kz report` prints the ledger with every certificate-based verdict, and renders the label `ledger:<i>/<ii>/<iii>/<iv>` with the sizes of its parts from the `TRUST_LEDGERS` record; it is not a sub-label and is not stored in the bitset (§1.10).

### 4.6 Census and candidate generation

```
for each M ∈ {M_api, M_impl (if the instance's cone is M_impl-relevant)}:
 for each contract κ in Φ (frozen set), each context-qualified template t matching κ.U (certificate view):
   key := value analysis of κ.sel at t       # concrete | symbolic | ⊤; header MsgIds and table packets (§3.9)
   for each conjunct P_j: (W_j, W_j^must, D_j, statuses) := writer sets (§4.2), keyed by key
   provider := none | env-absent | present | unknown        (§4.5)
   if t unreachable in Sk^cert under B constants and provenance:  emit inactive(B) + proof
   elif key = ⊤:                                                   open(key_unresolved) → probe plan
   elif FIXED_ORDER certificate exists:     emit [statement ¬OK, b = ∞, deciding iff its REJECT is at the decision] → fixed-order(σ_U, M) or unknown(no_decision_route), reach proved|assumed, provider, progress unknown(not_checked), contested mark if the header carries one
   elif PROTECTED certificate exists:       emit protected(σ_U, M) [statement ¬NR (hence ¬Bad), b = ∞], reach proved|assumed, progress unknown(not_checked), contested mark if the header carries one
                                            add i to the progress candidates (§4.10 item 7, all four queries)
   else: open(reason ∈ {unordered, O-ordered-only, conditional-observation, writer_incomplete, horizon,
                         unmodelled}) → probe plan, prediction, exploration
```

Every certificate goes through `kz proofcheck` before the verdict is stored. All census instances get a class or `unknown(reason)` per (σ, M). Latent instances get a model verdict, not just "unobserved" ([J2-7]). Dynamic instance sources (§4.8 step 9, §7.1) add instances to the same census. The census reports the **shares of concrete, symbolic and ⊤ keys** per kind, in particular for transmit sites (RC5) (fixes [X2-09]).

**Progress candidates** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Certified instances are never opened. No route therefore checks their progress, unless the explorer (S7, if not frozen) runs on them. A progress candidate runs **all four queries** of §4.10 item 7: `exh` visits the same reachable product graph for every query, so the safety queries cost nothing extra. Their safety results enter the cell's record at the explored bound, where they either agree with the certificate or disagree with it; a disagreement within that bound (an `exh` bad witness against `¬Bad_i`, a good witness against `¬OK_i`, or any decided not-ready occurrence, TOLERATED and PENDING included, against `¬NR_i`) goes to obligation O10 (§2.5, §4.12 item 5). The only limit on which certified instances are explored is the work-unit caps of D15; no number is chosen here. Without the explorer, every certified verdict keeps `progress = unknown(not_checked)` and is reported as a safety statement. FIXED_ORDER instances may be added as candidates in the same way.

### 4.7 Probe-plan synthesis (`kz-static plan`)

1. **Mandatory synchronization skeleton** for all actors: the anchors of every S and O rule (OSAL task, mutex, semaphore, queue, timer and module operations; ES lock wrappers, waits, `RunLoop`, `ExitApp`, app/task creation; SB create, subscribe, route_lookup, put, receive; the shutdown store). Keeping all of it avoids the third-party-synchronization slicing error noted in v0.1 §7.3.
2. **Candidate obligations**: the LP intervals of each conjunct of open instances, including **capture sites** (§4.8 step 4); outcome capture at contract-use sites only; the results of guards that decide `Allowed`; write watchpoints on readiness fields that no API boundary covers; user stacks where identity frames need them; the writers named in A-COMM checks of open prediction candidates (§4.9).
3. **Joint debug-register allocator** [D] (fixes [X3-m1]). HW watchpoints (perf breakpoint PMU) and ptrace gates share the same four per-thread debug registers [I: through the kernel's hw_breakpoint layer]. `plan` and `kz hold` use one allocator: watchpoints plus gates ≤ 4 per thread; **per-task events only** (a per-CPU breakpoint would take a slot on every thread on that CPU); **write-only watchpoints**, except in a declared experiment (RW watchpoints fire on every read at ≈34 µs, e.g. on `AppTable` at every `GetAppID`, [R07] L339).
4. **Cost**: each site has an instruction class and a unit cost from the **calibration table measured on this VM** (§5.10). The plan records `ΔC_i = Σ_j n_ij · c_j` per thread (v0.1 §11.2).
5. **Selection**: greedy weighted set cover of obligations by sites; optimality not claimed. The plan's hash goes into every trace.
6. **Prefix probes for per-cell reach** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2). For each instance whose reach is to be proved by a run in a cell other than (σ_U, M_impl), the plan adds:
   - (a) at M_api, unless L-MLEVEL applies: the return value of every OSAL call **of every actor of the instance's closed cone** on the prefix up to the occurrence, and whether a type lock is left held by a returned API, so that the M_api-conformance check (§1.10) can be made (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2; rev. 4: the using actor and its spawn chain alone miss a leaked lock or a lost task in a writer, giver or monitored actor);
   - (b) for σ_U∞: the outcome of every non-periodic timed wait and delay-paced retry, and every kill-timer expiry, **of every actor of the instance's closed cone** on the prefix (L-REACH-CONE, §6.2), and, at M_impl, the return cause of the OSAL RESERVED wait's timed wait (`OS_WaitForStateChange_Impl`, `os-impl-idmap.c:138–170`), e.g. at the return of its `pthread_cond_timedwait`, whose result OSAL itself does not test.
   - (c) for held and gated runs: the same probes (b) on the cone actors over the hold and up to the witness's deciding events, so that the naturalness label `timeout-exercised` can be decided (§1.8).

   These sites go beyond "outcome capture at contract-use sites only" (item 2; §5.2) and are costed like any other site (§5.10); no count is chosen. Until they exist, run-based reach in those cells is `assumed`.

### 4.8 Trace analysis (`kz trace`)

1. **Decode and loss.** Decode raw records with the site decoders (site id = attach cookie). Check per-thread `tseq` gaps and per-CPU drop counters. Verdicts whose evidence depends on an interval with loss become `unknown(loss)`.
2. **Identity join, offline**; nothing is looked up in the process (§5.7).
3. **HB.** Compute `VC` (all definite edges) and `VC_R` (unconditional and obs-fatal edges), **with the looper rule: no edge between handlers of one looper** (§1.9). Matching keys: mutexes by `objseq`; queues by descriptor plus FIFO; SB by descriptor plus pipe; semaphores by conservative join; polls only on a success return; registries by outcome-implied reads-from. Ambiguous matches weaken the evidence of the verdicts that use them.
4. **LP intervals per conjunct** (fixes [X1-M1]). Every LP occurrence gets an interval `[the last observed event of its actor before the LP, the first observed event after it]`. For a value conjunct, the LP is the capture point: since only argument evaluation separates the capture load from the call, the interval is `[the actor's previous observed event, the call-entry event]`; where that is too wide for a decision, the plan probes the capture site itself. Two LPs are **HB-decided** if their intervals are HB-ordered or linked by an outcome-implied reads-from. Otherwise their order is open; it is **outcome-decided** if Φ's outcome map determines it from the code, else ambiguous.
5. **Per-use evaluation and routing** ([J3] weakness 6). With L⁻, L⁺ and L∥ the lifecycle LPs on `sel(u)` before, after and unordered with each conjunct's LP:

| Case | Result | Route |
|---|---|---|
| L∥ = ∅, every conjunct holds | READY | supporting evidence only |
| L∥ = ∅, some conjunct fails, every ordering edge used is S (VC_R order) | observed not-ready; Allowed evaluated | ask `static` for a FIXED_ORDER certificate on the template; `VC_R` order is the prefilter |
| L∥ = ∅, but the order between a conjunct's LP and the deciding lifecycle LP needs ≥ 1 O-edge (lock, message, semaphore, soft poll, RF) | observed outcome in that order | **prediction**: is the reverse order feasible? (C1, C5, both App-record windows) |
| L∥ ≠ ∅ | outcome-decided order | prediction for the other order |

   The program's own outcome code (`ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, `NAME_NOT_FOUND`, `NEVER_LOADED`, `RESOURCEID_NOT_VALID`, `Q_FULL`, sentinel success) must agree with the computed state (O4(a)). **O4's checking power is reported only over HB-decided uses**; outcome-decided uses are excluded from that count, because there the state was derived from the code being checked (fixes [X1-m4]).
6. **Allowed** is evaluated by the horizon monitor of §1.6 on the continuation of the entry's monitored actor (the using actor, or the deferring actor for `defer_by_design`). If the trace ends while the monitor is OPEN, the occurrence is `PENDING` and the result is `unknown(horizon)`.
7. **Race coverage** (EventRacer OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs]): if reversing a pair needs an ad hoc synchronization pair reversed first, that pair is reported as the root and the dependents are attached.
8. **Violation chains** from frame containment and Φ `emits` annotations (e.g. a `SEND_NO_SUBS` event emitted inside a transmit with no route).
9. **Template-free sources**: outcome mining and key-pair enumeration (§7.1 items 2–3).
10. **Obligations** O3–O5 and O9 here; O1/O2 in `kz conform`.
11. **Scope**: occurrences whose decision event follows an observed SHUTDOWN_REQ LP are K-TEARDOWN instances (§1.6, §3.5); an occurrence decided before it stays in scope even if a later conjunct LP follows it.

Complexity: `O(N·|T|)` for N events and |T| actors ([M] 27 threads per boot, [BM §0]).

### 4.9 Prediction (`kz predict`)

**Question.** Given trace π and a routed candidate (e.g. publisher `p` before use `u`, observed lock-ordered), is there a feasible execution in which the deciding conjunct's LP at `u` precedes `p`'s LP, or, for capacity, in which a put overflows?

**Model `Pred(π)`** (maximal causal models, RV 2012 [V-abs]; RVPredict's control-flow abstraction, PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; properties as constraints, GPredict ICSE 2015 [V-abs]):
1. **Cone C**: per-actor prefixes up to `u` and `p`, closed backward under S-edges and under the reads-from of control-relevant events. For an instance whose Allowed entry has a horizon (§1.6), the monitored actor's prefix extends to the event at which its horizon monitor decides in π, when π contains it.
2. **Constraints**:
   - per-actor prefix order (including the sequential execution of handlers on a looper, and dequeue order = enqueue order per pipe, §1.9);
   - S-edges;
   - every control-relevant event in `C \ {u}` keeps its observed outcome class: a successful lookup needs a preceding publication and no retire; a poll exit by condition needs the enabling write first; **an acquire needs the lock free** (it is held by no other actor at that point; fixes [X1-M4]);
   - Φ primitive semantics: mutual exclusion with recursion, binsem saturation and flush, non-negative counts, queue FIFO with capacity `cap_B`, MsgLim;
   - the target order or outcome flip.
   - **C_σ is never added.** σ_R1 feasibility is decided only by the explorer on fully modelled cones, never by combining C_σ with an observed σ_E prefix (fixes [X1-m5]).
3. **Lock reversal is allowed** ([J1-7], [J2-3], [J3-M2]). Critical sections are atomic blocks and the registry key is the conflict object; two critical sections on one lock may be reordered whenever constraint 2 still holds. This is what C1, C5 and the App-record windows need, and what sync-preserving prediction forbids ([R04 §2.5]); it is the RVPredict/GPredict-style treatment ([R04 L259, L492]).
4. **A-COMM check and labels** [D] (fixes [X1-M4]). Kairos traces contain the synchronization skeleton, LP intervals and contract-site outcomes, not every shared access. Before a witness is labelled `predicted`, `kz predict` checks **A-COMM** using the read and write sets from S3a: for each cone actor's code between its observed events up to `u` (resp. `p`), every location it may read that another cone actor may write must be either (a) a Φ support location whose writer LPs in the cone are **all observed** in π, or (b) provably constant over the cone (no writer reachable in any cone actor after INIT), or (c) read only through an observed outcome that the witness keeps. If the check passes, the witness is `predicted` (trace-sound relative to A4, A7 and Φ's primitive semantics, L-PRED). If it fails, the witness is **`predicted-modelled`** and ranks with model evidence; the failing locations are listed, and the plan adds probes for them in the next loop iteration.
5. **Search**: depth-first over linearizations of C with sleep-set partial-order reduction. Independence comes from Φ: operations on different keys, locations and objects commute; critical sections on one lock commute as wholes unless they conflict on a key or location. States are memoized. The search stops at the first witness.
6. **Prefilters only.** SHB (OOPSLA 2018 [V-abs]) for unordered pairs, and WCP (PLDI 2017 [V-abs]) / SyncP (POPL 2021 [V-abs]) for pairs ordered through non-conflicting critical sections, run first where their domain applies. Their soundness transfers to the abstract trace only under A-API. Any reversal they propose is re-validated by step 2's checker (Vindicator's candidate-plus-vindication split, PLDI 2018 [V-abs]). Their silence means nothing.
7. **Summary-guided extrapolation** ([B §4.4 (e)]). A retry or poll site observed once may be extended by further not-ready iterations up to its **effective reference bound**. Such a witness is **`predicted-extrapolated`**: model-based, admitted only where the loop summary passed O2 ([J1-7]).
7a. **Horizon completion** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). A predicted reversal makes u's outcome not-ready, so u's continuation in the witness is not in π.
   - If the horizon monitor decides at the use (Allowed `none`, `documented_drop`, `unspecified_by_reference`, or an outcome outside the entry's codes, e.g. `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` for CF), the predicted occurrence has a final status, and the witness is a bad witness with the label of step 4.
   - Otherwise the occurrence is `PENDING` in the predicted prefix: **predicted not-ready evidence**, not a witness, and no class follows from it. It becomes a witness only by horizon completion. Either the monitored actor's continuation from `u` to the monitor's decision event is taken from `Sk^cert` (the path check of §1.6), or, for retry and poll loops, step 7's extrapolation reaches the effective bound. The witness is then `predicted-extrapolated` (model-based), and is admitted only where that region passed O2. The explorer completes the remaining cases.
8. **Budget**: a deterministic work-unit cap (search nodes), a **deviation** (reason: finite resources; effect: `unknown(budget)`; necessity: no reference defines it). Results are reported as an **anytime curve**: the decided share against work units (fixes [X3-M9], [X3-M10]).
9. **Result**: a witness with its realization label, predicted not-ready evidence (`PENDING`; no class), or "not predictable from π within the cap". **None of them is a protected verdict.**
10. **Optional SMT cross-check** after PI decision P-Z3: an order-variable encoding in the style of Alglave, Kroening and Tautschnig (CAV 2013 [V-abs]) and Wang et al. (FM 2009 [V-abs]), emitted as SMT-LIB2 for small cones; never on the critical path.

Complexity: prediction with even one synchronization reversal is NP-complete and W[1]-hard (SyncP [V-abs]); the explicit search is exponential in |C| in the worst case.

### 4.10 Bounded explorer (`kz explore`; stage S7, gated)

1. **Closed model per candidate.**
   - The **closed cone** ([A §4.5], `proposal_A-static-model.md:674`) is built by repeating the following to a fixpoint (EXT-20261012 §4.1):
     - actors containing `u`, `W`, `D` or support objects;
     - every actor that writes a cone location, synchronizes on a cone object, or writes the support of a cone predicate;
     - **cone objects and cone predicates also include the synchronization objects (mutexes, semaphores, queues, pipes) and the awaited predicates that cone actors acquire, take, get or await** on their `Sk^cert` paths (i) to their cone operations, (ii) through the end of every monitored horizon of the instance (§1.6), and (iii) for the actors on the spawn chains, to their spawn sites; **every monitored actor of the instance's Allowed entry and its spawn chain are cone actors**, also when the monitored actor does not exist when the occurrence is decided (e.g. TO_LAB for `defer_by_design`); Without (i), an actor that takes the same locks in the opposite order is outside the cone and a deadlock is missed (litmus LP-DL); without (ii), a wait after a not-ready use on an object given outside the cone becomes a false horizon stall; without (iii), the startup thread's wait at `cfe_es_start.c:835` loses the writers of its predicate and becomes a false pre-use stall; without the monitored actor, ACCEPT cannot occur in the model and a deferred occurrence would stay PENDING;
     - the spawn chains that reach them.

     The closed cone is computed by `static` and `plan` at S3a (§2.4 step 4) on `Sk^cert`, so it exists without the explorer; the explorer, L-MLEVEL, the per-cell reach checks, the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 and the naturalness label `timeout-exercised` reuse it [D] (EXT-20261012; rev. 4).

     The full synchronization skeleton of the cone actors is kept. **Under σ_R1, every actor whose priority is at least the minimum cone priority is kept**, because it interferes through the CPU (fixes [X1-m5]); a bounded σ_R1 model built this way contains the σ_U cone at the same h (L-CONE-R1). Expected size in cFS [I]: the closed cones of the five kill-criterion instances (item 9) contain the startup thread and every core main task created before the instance's actor, because the startup thread awaits each core task's raise on its path to the next spawn and every ES API call takes the ES shared-data lock; the C1-derived, K-PHASE and `SBNSubPipe` cones also contain most script apps. This is not measured; S7's anytime curves will show it (D21, candidate, §10.1).
   - B constants; Φ semantics at level M; awaits per σ; retries unrolled to effective bounds; inputs from B.Input; the exploration view `Sk^h` (h in b).
   - **Trace-anchored windows** (optional, [B §4.4]): the prefix is fixed to an observed run up to the latest common S-predecessor of {p, u}; verdicts are labelled "within window W of run r".
2. **Transition system.** A state is (actor control states, resource states, synchronization states, relevant locals, a discrete clock and ready/blocked status under σ_R1) together with the **instance monitor state** of §1.8: whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP occurred, and per actor its occurrence ordinal per key, the last captured value per capture site, its open occurrence (at most one per template) and its PENDING occurrences with their horizon-monitor states. The visited set of `exh` holds these product states. A transition is one effect at its LP, or one of the following explicit local choices:
   - a havoc value (finite domain from value analysis, else `unknown`);
   - an await outcome: success is enabled iff `Pred_code` holds; timeout is always enabled under σ_U, and under σ_U∞ only for periodic waits;
   - a retry exhaustion, i.e. the failure of the last unrolled attempt at its deciding transition at level M. Under σ_U∞ it is never enabled for delay-paced loops: a last attempt that is one transition at M waits until it succeeds; one that is several transitions completes and can strand its actor (§1.8);
   - the ES kill timer at each scan: `not-yet-expired`, `expire-stopped` or `expire-waiting` (§1.8); under σ_U∞ only `expire-waiting`, the forced kill, is removed;
   - a star-closure iterate or exit within h ([J1 X6]).

   An actor at a `TRUNC` node is **censored**: it is neither terminated nor dead (item 8). The record of every explored path keeps its **choice support**: the havoc values, star-closure choices and unresolved data branches it takes.
3. **Dependence** `D(t₁, t₂)` holds iff both belong to the same actor; or they touch the same key, location or object and at least one changes it; or one may enable or disable the other (lock/unlock of the same lock; give/take of the same semaphore; put/get on the same queue incl. capacity and MsgLim; an await versus any write to its predicate's support; a spawn versus the child's first transition; a join versus the victim's last transition). Under σ_R1, every transition that can change the readiness of a higher-priority actor is dependent with all lower-priority transitions (conservative, [A §4.5]). **Monitor dependences** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1; the instance monitor reads orders that the rules above treat as independent): (i) the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP is dependent with every call and every conjunct LP of the instance's template, because scope is decided by their order (§1.6); (ii) every deciding event of an occurrence (its call and its conjunct LPs) is dependent with every transition of its monitored actor when that actor is not the using actor, and, while the monitored actor does not exist, with the transitions of its spawn-chain actors, because the horizon monitor and its static REJECT read their order. `dpor`'s guarantee covers only monitor properties that are invariant under this extended dependence (L-DPOR).
4. **Two engines** [D] (fixes [X1-M9]):
   - **`exh`**: stateful exhaustive search over the finite closed model with a visited-state set and no partial-order reduction. Its correctness is that of reachability on a finite graph: every reachable state within b is visited. Results from `exh` carry G5 relative to the model (§6.3).
   - **`dpor`**: source-DPOR with sleep sets (Abdulla et al., POPL 2014 [V-abs]), with blocking handled by the enabling/disabling dependences of item 3. Its correctness for systems with disabling is the theorem of the JACM 2017 version (source-DPOR extended to disabling), whose details are **status P** in [R05 §2.2]. Until that theorem's statement is verified against the paper (PI decision P-PAPERS), **G5 for `dpor` results is labelled unverified**. The differential test against `exh` on every model small enough for `exh` is **validation evidence only**, not a proof. Optimal DPOR (wakeup trees, "one execution per class") is not adopted and not claimed.
   - Every verdict records its engine.
5. **Reductions**, each switchable and ablated:
   - **L1** (await summarization with code-extracted predicates; §4.3, §6.2);
   - **L2**: a critical section on lock ℓ is removed only if (i) ℓ is not a cone object and no other cone actor acquires ℓ, and (ii) the body touches no cone location or cone object and **contains no blocking operation** ([A §4.2], `proposal_A-static-model.md:615`). The section's own acquire and release count as touching ℓ, and nested acquisitions count as blocking operations. A section that can take part in a lock-order cycle, including one with an empty body such as τ_B's inner `m1` section in litmus LP-DL, is therefore kept;
   - **L3** (**critical sections as mover-reduced atomic blocks**, fixes [X1-M10]). A critical section on lock ℓ becomes one atomic transition only if a static check shows: the acquire is a right mover and the release a left mover; every access inside to a location protected by ℓ is a both-mover; **at most one non-mover** inside (a blocking call, an operation on another synchronization object, or an access to an unprotected shared location); **the lock is released on every path** (paths that leak the lock, such as K-LOCKBAL under M_impl, fail the check); and **no nested blocking acquire**. `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask` (cancel and join under the ES lock, `cfe_es_api.c:1348, 1383`) fails the check and stays fine-grained, so K-LOCK.cancel executions are not removed. Critical sections that fail stay acquire/release transitions. The left/right-mover reduction theorem is used as proof obligation L3; its citation is an open literature check (not verified in R04/R05).
6. **Bounds.** Horizon h and work-unit caps: h is not chosen; the explorer reports results for each h it completes within the work-unit cap, which is a **deviation** (finite resources), with anytime curves (decided share against work units). Wall time and peak memory are costs only. No preemption bound by default; if one is introduced, BPOR (Coons et al. OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs]) is used, because naive DPOR plus a bound is unsound within the bound, and the bound is swept from 0 upward until the cap (SCTBench norm, [R05 §2.1]).
7. **Four queries** ([R05 §2.3]; revised by EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2), over the finite executions and reachable product states of the closed model at σ, M and b:
   - `Q_reach`: some finite execution contains a decided in-scope occurrence of `u` whose readiness is evaluated (not `NOT_EVALUATED`);
   - `Q_bad`: some finite execution contains a `VIOLATING` (or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`) in-scope occurrence; also answered per occurrence ordinal;
   - `Q_ok`: some finite execution contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence (a `NOT_EVALUATED` one is neither); also answered per occurrence ordinal;
   - `Q_stall`: some reachable product state is a stall state for i (§1.8), with its kind and qualifiers.

   The results are read as follows. Each query sets its own component of the cell's safety record at bound b, and the class follows from the record (§1.10).
   - **Safety record**:
     - `Q_bad` = no → the statement `¬Bad_i` within b; `Q_ok` = no → the statement `¬OK_i` within b; `Q_bad` = yes → bad witnesses; `Q_ok` = yes → good witnesses, each with its ordinal;
     - **safety-side censoring**, symmetric (a bounded statement is withheld when the continuation of a censored or stranded actor could refute it): `Q_ok` = no establishes `¬OK_i` only if no such actor's region beyond the cut contains (i) a writer of a conjunct of the instance (a `W` entry), (ii) the ACCEPT event of a monitored actor that holds a PENDING occurrence, or (iii) a call of the use whose conjuncts can hold there (true at INIT or with a writer in the model); `Q_bad` = no establishes `¬Bad_i` only if no such region contains (i) a disabler (a `D` entry) of a conjunct, (ii) the REJECT event of a monitored actor that holds a PENDING occurrence, or (iii) a call of the use whose conjuncts can fail there (false at INIT or with a disabler in the model). Otherwise the statement is `unknown(bound)` for that side (litmus L-H2);
     - hence (no, no) gives `no-final-status` within b, (yes, no) fixed-order (the bad witness is its decision route), (no, yes) protected (with `tolerated` if a `TOLERATED` occurrence exists), and (yes, yes) schedule-dependent if some ordinal answers yes to both, else `ordinal-split`.
   - **Reach**: `Q_reach` = yes → `model(b)`; `Q_reach` = no → `unreached(b)`.
   - **Progress**:
     - `Q_stall` = yes → `stall-witness`, recording the shortest finite execution to a stall state, its kind, qualifiers and choice support, the witnessing actors and what they wait on, the wait-for root set and the progress chain; a stall state found is a witness even when other deadness tests are undecided;
     - `Q_stall` = no, with no undecided deadness test → `no-stall-within(b)`;
     - an undecided test → `unknown(bound)` (censored, or in a censored actor's closure) or `unknown(unmodelled)` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure).

     A stall never decides the safety record. In particular, a stall without a bad occurrence never yields fixed-order.
   - **Budget**: when the cap is exhausted, the components not yet decided get `unknown(budget)`.

   The queries run under σ_U and σ_U∞ (refinements, §1.8), and under σ_R1[P_sched] when requested. **Each σ's results are computed independently.** Refinements are derived afterwards from the per-σ results and stored with their own bounds; timeout-masked-liveness also compares the σ_U and σ_U∞ hang states at the same product states.

   `dpor` (item 4) decides `Q_reach`, `Q_bad` and `Q_ok` under T-JACM, and only with the extended dependence of item 3 (the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP against the instance's calls and conjunct LPs; an occurrence's deciding events against the transitions of a monitored actor other than the using actor and of its spawn chain), because scope and those monitors read the order of transitions that the plain dependence treats as independent. It does not decide `Q_stall`, because the deadness test needs the full reachable graph. A `dpor`-found candidate stall state is confirmed by `exh` from that state; otherwise the progress result stays `unknown(not_checked)`.
8. **Monitor**. Per finite execution, the monitor checks:
   - Req at every conjunct LP, and the scope of each occurrence at its decision;
   - the horizon monitors of Allowed (§1.6), on the monitored actor of each entry, frozen at the first process-terminal step (occurrences decided after it have no status);
   - the shape (looper rule, API-level edges).

   Per reachable product state, it runs the **deadness test** of §1.8: an actor is dead for i iff it is not terminated and no non-excluded reachable state enables it, on the visited product graph with the states excluded for i made sinks (under σ_R1, with σ_U's enabling relation). The test is computed by backward reachability over that graph, so it is available in `exh` only.
   - **Censoring (transitive).** A censored actor is treated as able to perform every Φ effect of its `Sk^cert` region beyond the cut on every object and location that region names: writes, release, give, put, flush, spawn, exit and object deletion. A **stranded** actor (σ_U∞; its only σ_U transitions are unpaired time-based ones, §1.8) is treated as able to perform every Φ effect of its region after its stale test, as if the test had succeeded. An actor is potentially enabled if some reachable state contains a censored, stranded or potentially enabled actor whose effects could enable it (write its awaited predicate's support; release, give, put to or flush an object it waits on; spawn it; exit while it joins). A deadness test on a stranded or potentially enabled actor is undecided: `unknown(unmodelled)` when the closure starts at a stranded actor, `unknown(bound)` otherwise.
   - **Kinds.** A cycle of dead actors over holder-released objects, each acquired without a timeout, is reported as a `deadlock-cycle`. An untimed acquisition of a holder-released, non-recursive object whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it, and is terminated or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release that does not first acquire the object again, is reported as a `leaked-lock`; this is how OSAL F1 (K-LOCKBAL) shows up under M_impl: not as a cycle, but as dead actors behind a lock that no actor can release, including the leaker itself when it re-enters an API that takes the lock. Stall witnesses record their wait-for root set, computed on the wait-for graph of the stall state (§1.8).
   - **Qualifiers.** `terminal-pending` (a process end, i.e. a process-terminal step or a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP, stays reachable from every successor state without B.Input's stop event; relative to the cone), `idle` (every witnessing actor at an idle wait) and `choice-dependent` (non-empty choice support of the wait-for closure, including the path ahead) are computed and recorded.
   - **Exclusions.** States after a process-terminal step are excluded for every instance; states after the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP are excluded for instances of the default scope (§1.8).
9. **Kill criterion** ([J3-M3]). At the S7 review, the explorer is run on the development-set cones that lack a certificate: both App-record windows, the C1-derived lookup, the K-PHASE CF instance and the `SBNSubPipe` K-CAP instance. It runs up to the PI-set work-unit cap (a deviation; decision D15) on the closed cone of item 1, whose expected size is stated there [I]. Whether the criterion is assessed on that cone or, as an ablation labelled under-approximate, on the narrower cone of v1, is a PI decision (D21, candidate, §10.1); no cap is chosen here. Two of the five development cones, the C1-derived lookup and the K-PHASE CF instance, exist only in C1's derived basis (D22; the lookup is `inactive` in the default B, App. A.4 step 1); whether the S7 review waits for D22, or the criterion is assessed on the reduced set (both App-record windows and `SBNSubPipe`, none of them a design case's instance) and reported with that set named, is PI decision P-D18GATES (b). If none of these cones is decided, or if `dpor` and `exh` disagree on any small model, the explorer is frozen, and its outputs are then reported only as an ablation. If the explorer is frozen or cut (ADR-026), no route sets a **model** progress result and no refinement is established. Every cell then keeps `progress = unknown(not_checked)`, except σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact (§4.13) and the M_impl stall states that L-DEAD (c)/(d) carry from such a realized `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, and every protected verdict is reported as a safety statement only. If the explorer is frozen or cut, the cells that become unanswerable are published per case with that decision: C1's refinements and progress results, C2's refinements, and the fixed sides of C3 and C5 (§9.3).
10. **Fidelity.** Explorer verdicts carry `model(b)` realization. They rest on O1, O2, O6 and O8, and, where the instance also has a certified or certificate-transferred statement, on O10 (§2.5), and are never labelled realized.

### 4.11 Conformance (`kz conform`)

- **O1/O2, per actor, on every trace** ([J3-M9]). Each actor's decoded event sequence is run through its `Sk^cert` automaton by NFA simulation over the explicit local choices; linear in events × automaton states. A failure gives the shortest rejected prefix.
- **O8, global, from stage S5** (fixes [X3-M5]). The question is whether some model execution's projection equals all per-actor sequences, with outcomes and matched synchronization. It is answered by a **trace-constrained search built on S5's prediction engine** (the trace fixes each actor's sequence; the search looks for a consistent interleaving under Φ semantics), not by the gated explorer. Timestamps are only the search order heuristic ([A §4.8]). The sampling rule is pre-registered (the first trace of each pool, plus every trace with an O1–O7 or O9 failure); the work-unit cap is a deviation. Results: conforming, non-conforming (with the region), undecided. Before S5, O8 is reported as `NOT_RUN`.

### 4.12 Combining evidence into verdicts

1. **Cells.** Each instance has one cell per analysed (σ, M). σ_U first, then σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched] on request; σ_E last. Safety records, classes, reach and progress are computed per cell and per bound (§1.10); refinements are derived from the cells and stored with their bounds.
2. **Universal statements** (`¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i`, and hence the classes `protected`, `fixed-order` and `no-final-status`) need either a certificate (b = ∞ on `Sk^cert`), exhaustive exploration within b (`exh`, or `dpor` labelled unverified), or transfer (§1.8). A certificate proves one statement (L-PR, L-FO); `exh` decides both within b, and also reach and progress. When both routes apply to the same σ and M, their statements must agree within the explored bound: an `exh` witness within b against a certified or certificate-transferred statement goes to obligation O10 (§2.5, item 5), which contests the explored bound; only a realized witness is a contradiction (§4.14). Their classes can still differ, because `exh` may add the second statement within b (e.g. `no-final-status` within b where nothing is reached) while the certificate's class holds at b = ∞; the report shows both bounds. Only `exh` gives `tolerated` and model progress values; `exh` and `dpor` (the latter unverified, under T-JACM) give model reach values; a realized wait-for fact gives σ_E progress and, by L-DEAD (c)/(d), M_impl stall states. Observations are otherwise supporting evidence only.
3. **`schedule-dependent`** needs a bad witness and a good witness in the same cell and bound, **at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct finite executions with final statuses there. Bad and good witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split`. The record keeps the weaker of the two **per evidence axis** (realization, naturalness). A stall witness is a progress result and never substitutes for a bad witness.
4. **σ_E cells** come from one of two routes:
   - transfer rule 2: safety statements with b = ∞, only from M_impl or M-agreement;
   - **natural realized witnesses on both sides at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct natural runs, which give `schedule-dependent(σ_E)`; natural witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)`.

   Further rules for σ_E cells:
   - Realized witnesses with held or gated naturalness give `σ_E(held)`.
   - Frequencies (`n of N`) are pooled only over identical pool keys (B hash, plan hash, stratum) and are labelled `σ_E(plan P)`. Equivalence to unobserved runs is claimed only on the measured uninstrumented marginals (§8.6) (fixes [X3-M2]).
   - Absence of bad witnesses is written "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown" (fixes [X1-M11]).
   - The σ_E progress result follows §1.10.
5. **Contradictions** trigger O3/O4/O9: the rule is suspended, a model-fault record is written, the affected verdicts become `unknown(model_fault)`, and the loop ends for this Φ version. An M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary gives `portability-hazard-evidence` instead (§1.4).
   - **What is a contradiction.** Evidence from a run against a statement that comes from a certificate or from a transfer of one, in the same (σ, M) and **within the statement's bound**: a realized bad witness against `¬Bad_i`; a realized good witness against `¬OK_i`; an observed decided not-ready occurrence (TOLERATED and PENDING included) against `¬NR_i` (O4(b)). A witness outside the bound does not contradict a bounded statement.
   - **Model–certificate disagreements (O10, §2.5).** An `exh` bad witness against a certified or certificate-transferred `¬Bad_i`, an `exh` good witness against such a `¬OK_i`, or an `exh` decided not-ready occurrence against such a `¬NR_i`; also an `exh` result in one σ against an `exh` statement transferred from another σ (which L-CONT (a) says cannot happen). The disagreement is reported and the explorer's bound becomes `contested`. A directed check follows: (i) re-explore with L1–L3 switched off; if the witness disappears, the reduction is at fault and is disabled for the instance; (ii) otherwise re-run `kz proofcheck` on the certificate; if it rejects, the certificate is withdrawn; (iii) otherwise the explored bound stays `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`), the certificate's own record at b = ∞ is unchanged, the certificate header gets a persistent contested mark for this Φ version (§2.3), which the census reads, and a directed confirmation of the witness is planned. No component of any other bound is lowered. No Φ rule is suspended and the loop does not end unless the witness is realized, which makes it a contradiction (above). A test pairs a PROTECTED certificate built on a writer set with one disabler removed with an `exh` run that finds a TOLERATED or PENDING occurrence, and checks that O10 fires.
   - **What is not.** A stall witness against any class is **not** a contradiction, because the classes are safety statements.
   - **Model-bound conflicts.** A realized bad or good witness whose conforming model prefix lies within b, met by an `exh` statement `¬Bad_i` or `¬OK_i` within b; a realized stall witness whose conforming model prefix lies within b, met by `no-stall-within(b)` in the same cell; a reach proof whose witness lies within b, met by `unreached(b)`; or a realized prefix to a model stall state after which a witnessing actor is observed to proceed. Each shows that the closed model is too strict. It is reported as O6 (extended, §2.5), and the affected component at that bound moves to the top element `contested`, rendered `unknown(model_fault)` (§4.14). The class is unaffected, and this is not a suspension.
6. **Three judgements** (§1.6). Harm (caller level) comes from the caller-level Allowed and the observed or model continuation; with the caller-level Allowed unspecified, harm is `unspecified_by_reference`, and route/capacity verdicts carry LOSS-UNDER-ORDER. `fw_doc` and `fw_conf` (framework level) come from the op's outcome map, O4(a), the §3.9 oracles and the trace. The consequence (observed) comes from the continuation and the oracles in every case, including an unspecified Allowed. No rule combines the three.
7. **M levels**: M_api always; M_impl for instances whose cone is M_impl-relevant. Disagreements give `portability-hazard` or `implementation-defect-candidate`.
8. **Perturbation qualifier** (fixes [X3-M2]): every realized witness carries its computed perturbation ratio (§4.13); no threshold is applied.
9. **Progress** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1), per cell and bound:
   - it comes from `exh` (§4.10 item 7) or from a realized hang with a wait-for fact (§4.13);
   - it never comes from a certificate, from a prediction, or by transfer between σ, except stall states of kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` (L-DEAD (c), (d));
   - its default is `unknown(not_checked)`; K-TEARDOWN instances have `unknown(teardown_scope)`;
   - dependent progress results are attached to their root by progress chains, and the root is what is reported.

### 4.13 Confirmation on the real binary (`kz hold`, `kz gate`)

**Which verdicts get controlled confirmation** ([A §4.9]):

| Verdict | Action |
|---|---|
| fixed-order (certificate) | passive observation (reach and outcome). A good counterpart exists only under a changed basis B′ (e.g. another core order): a configuration-change experiment, labelled as such. For R2, such a basis is expected to withdraw the certificate without a protected verdict in its place (App. A.1 step 15) |
| protected | none beyond conformance for the safety statement |
| progress stall witness (`model(b)`) | directed confirmation only for **confirmable** witnesses (kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, not idle, not terminal-pending), where a hold can realize the prefix to the stall state. The oracle is the wait-for fact (below), never a timeout. The run ends with a harness kill and a normalization boot. The result is labelled `realized` with its naturalness, and `derived` when the schedule was constructed. If the stall is not realized, the witness stays `model(b)` with "not realized in run R"; this is not a refutation. It is a refutation (O6, contested bound) only if the prefix is realized and a witnessing actor is then observed to proceed. Witnesses of kind `other` (waits on semaphores, queues, messages or predicates) cannot be confirmed by this oracle and stay model candidates |
| schedule-dependent / timeout-dependent | directed confirmation of the bad witness; if natural runs show only the bad order, also of the good witness |
| ordinal-split | passive observation of the ordinals concerned; no directed confirmation, because no single occurrence is claimed to depend on the schedule. A same-ordinal pair found later moves the class to schedule-dependent |
| no-final-status | no confirmation of the class, which says that no reached occurrence has a final status; reach and the progress result are reported with it, and a confirmable stall witness is confirmed as in the progress row |
| unknown(no_decision_route) with `¬OK_i` shown | directed attempts at a bad witness in that cell (a run or a prediction that completes a not-ready occurrence's horizon to its REJECT), as evidence; the class becomes fixed-order only when a bad witness is found in that cell |
| unknown(model_gap, writer_incomplete, conformance) | directed attempts at predicted witnesses, as evidence; the class stays unknown until the model is fixed |
| unknown(budget) | F1 PCT runs if D3 is approved; otherwise remains unknown |
| LOSS-UNDER-ORDER | observation through SB counters and put outcomes |

**Gate placement** ([J2-10], [J3-M10]). To force "event `a` of thread A after event `b` of thread B", hold A at a point before its first event of the pair where:
1. **A's program-lock set is empty**, by the observed lock events and the static lockset (incl. the ES lock, the SB lock, TBL locks, and the window between a `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask` lock and its delete);
2. the point is **not** inside a timebase callback (`handler_mutex` held), a binsem internal section (the internal 2 s `timedlock`, [R01 §6.3]), a signal handler, or before `OS_TaskPrepare` registration;
3. the point is not inside an OSAL RESERVED window, except as a **declared experiment** (App. A.4);
4. the joint debug-register allocator (§4.7) has a free slot on A.

If no point qualifies, the rule is applied symmetrically: hold B before its enabling event.

**Attach strategy** (fixes [X3-m1]): threads that exist at plan time are seized individually (`PTRACE_SEIZE`). A gate on a thread created later (e.g. App. A.4's provider) needs auto-attach at clone (`PTRACE_O_TRACECLONE` on its creator); each new thread then stops once at creation. That stop is an unmeasured perturbation inside the app-start windows; S6 measures its cost, and runs that use it record it.

**Release** is event-based: when the awaited event (with its outcome) appears in the live stream of `kz observe`, or when the other party is blocked or has exited ([POL] L22).

**Watchdog** = the smallest reference timeout among the waits that the hold can make expire, read from Φ and B: e.g. `CFE_PLATFORM_ES_STARTUP_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT_MSEC` for a held startup thread whose child polls `GetTaskFunction`; `CFE_PLATFORM_CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC` for core serialization; **`CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` when the held schedule can lead to a restart request** (the requester aborts after it, §3.5); effective retry bound × effective delay for CF. If the watchdog fires, the result is `inconclusive(await-timeout)`. If a time-based transition (§1.8) of a cone actor occurs during the hold, or after it and before the witness's deciding events, as shown by the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 (c) (timeout outcomes of non-periodic timed waits, retry exhaustion branches, forced kills, the return causes of OSAL-internal timed waits), the run's naturalness is `timeout-exercised`; periodic waits do not count.

**Normalization after abnormal exits** [D] (fixes [X3-M3]). A run that did not end with the console line "Exiting cFE with POWERON Reset status" (`cfe_psp_support.c:64`) — a panic, a PROCESSOR restart, a requester abort, a harness kill of a deadlock — leaves the SysV segments and a boot record that makes the next boot a PROCESSOR reset (`cfe_psp_start.c:411–434`). After every such run:
1. one **unobserved normalization boot** is made, ending with the CI POWERON restart already used as E11(a) (`.github/workflows/build-run-app-reusable.yml` L209–214; [BM] L29), which deletes the segments (`cfe_psp_support.c:62–69`) without any manual cleaning ([R2g]'s "not cleaned (policy)" practice is kept);
2. the persist snapshot after it must equal the canonical S0 (content hash, no mtimes);
3. the normalization boot is excluded from every pool;
4. cold runs (the first after a VM reboot) form their own stratum and are never pooled with warm runs.

**Mechanisms.**
- **F0 `kz hold`** (default): `PTRACE_SEIZE` by root; an **execution breakpoint in a debug register** (DR0–DR3, set through `PTRACE_POKEUSER`) at the gate, so the text is never modified ([B §4.6]). Only that thread stops. On release, the debug register is cleared. Measured unit costs: #DB trap ≈34 µs; gdb breakpoint ≈203 µs ([R07 §4.7]). The holder's stop/resume cost and its interaction with uprobes and signals (the timebase's real-time signal, `SIGUSR1`) are **unmeasured** S6 exit items.
- **F1 `kz gate`** (only after D3): handwritten `__wrap_*` gates for the synchronization-skeleton APIs, in one C99 file built with the cFS flags; the controller enforces witness schedules or PCT. PCT gives probability at least `1/(n·k^(d−1))` per run for a depth-d bug ([V-abs; mechanism P]), with n and k measured per run and d increased from 1 within the PI-set run budget (a deviation), relative to the gated scheduling points.

**Oracles.** `kz trace` re-evaluates the confirmation trace with the same contracts: the target use must show the not-ready state at its LP and the predicted outcome, and harm follows Allowed. For deadlocks and lock leaks (K-LOCKBAL) the oracle is a **wait-for fact**, never a timeout: the blocked thread's state, read from `/proc/<tid>/wchan` or futex tracepoints, while the lock's last acquirer has already returned from its API ([C §4.9]). A wait-for fact is also the oracle of a realized progress witness (§1.10).
