It establishes permanence only in two cases: a cycle over holder-released objects acquired without a timeout (`deadlock-cycle`); or an untimed acquisition of a non-recursive lock whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock (`leaked-lock`, the observable form of §1.8's holder condition). A holder that has returned and still runs elsewhere gives a `leaked-lock` only together with the static condition of §1.8 (no `Sk^cert` path to a release that does not first acquire the lock).

**Labels.** `realized` with naturalness `held-F0` | `gated-F1` | `timeout-exercised`; flag `derived` (kept apart from original-case results, [R06 S10]; [LED] X5); `refuted` (the order was realized and the outcome was good: the model or summary that produced the witness is wrong for that prefix; the witness is withdrawn and reported under O6, and, for an `exh` witness, the explored bound becomes `contested` (§4.12 item 5); no rule is suspended. A progress witness is refuted only if its prefix is realized and a witnessing actor then proceeds; a hold that does not realize the prefix refutes nothing (§1.10)); `inconclusive`.

**Perturbation accounting per run** (fixes [X3-M2]): holds (count, site, duration); probe hits by cost class; observer CPU time and placement; the uninstrumented-observable measures of §8.6; and, **per witness**, the **perturbation ratio** = (probe and hold time added on the involved threads between the two deciding events, from the run's own hits × the S1 class costs, plus hold durations) / (the code-derived separation that protects the other order: e.g. the 50 ms poll delay of `CFE_ES_GetTaskFunction`, `cfe_es_apps.c:559–596`; the ~100 ms core-serialization spacing, [R2g §2]; SCH_LAB's first tick at least 1 s after init). The ratio is reported with no threshold.

### 4.14 The feedback loop, its reproducibility and its termination (`kz loop`)

```
fix (B, Φ, tool);  K0 := static closure of B;  census0 := kz-static static(K0)
i := 0
repeat
   plan_i   := kz-static plan(open instances of census_i ∪ mandatory sync skeleton ∪ A-COMM probes)
   runs_i   := kz observe(B, plan_i) × n_i      # n_i: deviation (PI-set, D14); run IDs; normalization boots
   check O1–O5, O9 (+ O8 on the sample, from S5; + O6 (b) and O10 on the explorer's records, from S7); a suspension (O3/O4/O9) ENDS the loop for this Φ version, while O6 (b) and O10 only contest a bound
   K_{i+1}  := K_i ⊔ keys, targets and paths learned from runs_i                  # F1, F2
   census_{i+1} := kz-static static(K_{i+1}) ⊔ kz trace(runs_i)
                   [⊔ kz predict(runs_i)          from S5]
                   [⊔ kz explore(K_{i+1})          from S7, if not frozen]
   [for each witness w: π_w := kz hold/gate(B, holdplan(w)); census_{i+1} ⊔= kz trace(π_w)   from S6]  # F3
   write loop-i.kz: run IDs, B/plan/Φ/tool hashes, S0 hashes, deltas, obligation results, work units
   i := i + 1
until state(census_i) = state(census_{i-1}) and K_i = K_{i-1}
report the fixed point together with its run set ∪_i runs_i
```

- **Staged availability** (fixes [X3-M5]): at S4 the loop runs with `static` and `trace` only (O1–O7, O9); `predict` joins at S5 (and O8), `hold` at S6, `explore` at S7 if it passes its kill criterion.
- **The fixed-point test** (fixes [X1-m8]) compares `state(census)`. Per instance and per (σ, M) cell and bound, it compares the fields of the `BOUNDS` entry (§2.3): the safety record (statements with their routes, the decision route of `¬OK_i`, and the set of ordinals of the bad and of the good witnesses, read from the witness ranges), hence the class; the refinements; reach; the progress value with its set of clauses, kind, qualifiers, reason, harm and wait-for root set; the flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`; the certificate's contested mark; the provider axis; the **set** of realization and naturalness values present; the key resolution; and K. It excludes counts (`n of N`), pool keys, run IDs and plan hashes, because those change every iteration.
- **Lattice.** Per cell and per bound, the components are ordered as follows; they are independent across bounds.
  - **Safety record.** Each statement (`¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i`, `¬NR_i`) and the decision route of `¬OK_i` move from unset to set; witness sets grow by inclusion. The derived classes are therefore ordered `unknown < protected, fixed-order < no-final-status` and `unknown < ordinal-split < schedule-dependent`, with `unknown(no_decision_route)` (¬OK_i without a decision route) below fixed-order and no-final-status, and `contested` above every class of that bound (stored as `CONTESTED`); `inactive` is static. A certified (or certificate-transferred) statement and a contradicting realized witness at the same bound are a contradiction, handled by O3/O4 and a suspension; it is never a lattice step. An `exh` witness against such a statement moves the **explored** bound to the top `contested` (O10); the certificate's b = ∞ record is not lowered, and its persistent contested mark keeps the census from re-emitting a different value for the explored bound within the Φ version. An `exh`-only statement met by a contradicting realized witness within its bound moves that bound's record to the top `contested` (O6, model-bound conflict).
  - **Refinements and provider** move only from unset to set.
  - **Reach.** At b = ∞: `assumed < proved`. At a bounded b: `assumed < model(b)` and `assumed < unreached(b)`, both below the top `contested` (stored as `CONTESTED`, §2.3). A reach proof whose witness lies within b, met by `unreached(b)`, moves that bound to `contested` (O6). A proof outside b leaves `unreached(b)` in place, because the two bounds are kept apart.
  - **Progress.** `unknown(·) < stall-witness` and `unknown(·) < no-stall-within(b)`, both below the top `contested` (stored as `P_CONTESTED`, rendered `unknown(model_fault)`). A realized stall within b met by `no-stall-within(b)`, or a realized prefix to a model stall state after which a witnessing actor proceeds, moves that bound to `contested` (O6). `stall-witness[model b] < stall-witness[realized]`. A stall witness never moves the class.
  - **Evidence sets** are ordered by inclusion; **keys** `⊤ < symbolic < concrete`; **K** by inclusion.

  Within one Φ version, every non-suspending iteration moves up or stays, so L-LOOP's termination argument is unchanged. A test checks that no contested bound is lowered again within one Φ version (rev. 4).
- **Suspensions are non-monotone**, so a suspension ends the loop for that Φ version; the fix needs a human Φ edit, which produces a new Φ hash and a new loop.
- **Termination**: under fixed B and Φ, the universe of sites, keys (from code constants and finite tables), instances and evidence values is finite, and every non-final iteration strictly moves up the lattice or stops [I].
- **Reproducibility** ([J3-M7]; fixes [X3-M10]): every analysis is a pure function of stored artifacts (sorted iteration, deterministic search order, work-unit budgets, no wall-clock dependence), so re-running from stored traces reproduces the verdict store byte for byte; this is a test. A result produced under a wall-clock cap (only if D15 ever allows one) is labelled and excluded from the replay test. σ_E frequencies are pooled only over identical pool keys. The fixed point is reported **with its run set**.

### 4.15 Complexity and bounds

| Stage | Cost | Bounds and their sources |
|---|---|---|
| Closure and extraction | linear in TUs; [M] 10 s parse, 4.4 s bitcode, 0.35 s core link | — |
| S3a writer extraction | linear in AST size [I]; measured at S3a | — |
| S3b points-to (if built) | Andersen worst case cubic ([R05 §2.5]) | work-unit cap (deviation) |
| Skeletons | worst case **exponential in call depth** under full call-string inlining; per-(actor, function) summary fallback when the work-unit cap is reached (deviation) | poll loops summarized (L1); retry loops at effective constant bounds (CF: effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`; OSAL 5, `osapi-idmap.c:475–480`; `GetTaskFunction` ≤ 21, `cfe_es_apps.c:570–595`) |
| Message provenance | least fixpoint over handlers; at most one round per handler | — |
| MustBefore | `O(N·R·(d̄+w̄)·R/64)` plus cached PWB queries | — |
| Certificates | reachability per frontier; derivations | — |
| Trace HB and contracts | O(N × number of actors) | — |
| Prediction | NP-complete in general (SyncP); exponential worst case | work-unit cap (deviation); anytime curve |
| Exploration | `exh`: linear in reachable **product** states (model state with the instance monitor, §1.8) and their edges, exponential in the model; the deadness tests and the qualifiers add one backward reachability per actor and per instance over the visited graph, with excluded states as sinks; `dpor`: exponential worst case, with the extended dependence of §4.10 item 3; **no "one execution per class" claim** | h not chosen (results per completed h); work-unit cap (deviation); preemption bound none by default (BPOR and sweep if used); restart depth 1 per documented request |
| Conformance | O1/O2 linear; O8 a search (from S5) | work-unit cap (deviation) |
| Confirmation | one run per witness, plus a normalization boot after abnormal exits; PCT ≥ `1/(n·k^(d−1))` per run | watchdogs from platform constants; PCT depth d increased from 1 within the run budget (deviation) |
| Feedback loop | ≤ the lattice height under (B, Φ) | runs per iteration: deviation (D14) |

---

## 5. Observation and instrumentation design

### 5.1 Mechanisms and fidelity classes

Fidelity classes are those of [R07 §7]: F0 = unmodified binary and run conditions, timing perturbed only; F1 = same sources and compiler, link or code-generation option changed; F2 = sources rewritten; F3 = another compiler.

| Mechanism | Class | Role | Unit cost ([M] [R07 §4.7, §5.1] unless stated) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| perf-PMU uprobes + eBPF, root-side, own raw loader, **attach cookies** | F0 | **production observation** of API-boundary and function-boundary events in `core-cpu1` and app `.so` files | 0.57 µs/hit at entry+4 (emulated `push %rbp`); 31.7 µs at `endbr64`, at `mov %rsp,%rbp` (+5) or any non-emulated instruction; BPF body **unmeasured** | callee-side probes also see the 88 intra-TU API sites that `--wrap` misses |
| **perf-sample uprobe backend** (sample flags `PERF_SAMPLE_TID`, `PERF_SAMPLE_TIME`, `PERF_SAMPLE_CPU`, `PERF_SAMPLE_REGS_USER`; `PERF_RECORD_LOST` for loss) | F0 | **zero-install bring-up backend** (fixes [X3-m2]); fd-scoped, cleans itself up | same trap path; sample cost unmeasured | measured to work without tracefs and with registration before the file is mapped ([R07] L340) |
| tracefs dynamic uprobe events | F0 | optional only; if used, the rules of §5.4 apply | same trap path | event definitions are kernel-global and outlive a private mount namespace [I] |
| HW write watchpoints (perf breakpoint PMU, per task) | F0 | readiness-field stores with no API boundary | ≈34 µs/hit; shares the 4 debug registers with gates (§4.7) | only for fields written a handful of times per boot; write-only except in declared experiments |
| ptrace + debug-register execution breakpoints | F0 | confirmation holds (`kz hold`) | #DB trap ≈34 µs; gdb breakpoint ≈203 µs; purpose-built holder **unmeasured** | no Yama; root on a uid-1000 process works |
| sched/syscall tracepoints | F0 | clone cross-check; RacePro-style baseline observation model (§8.3) | not measured | exist on this kernel |
| `ld --wrap` gates | F1 + deviation | PCT and many-point schedules, **only if D3 approves**; perturbation group W | handoff 1.1 µs same CPU / 10.5 µs across CPUs | apps relinked; misses intra-TU calls |
| Source probes | F2 | not planned | — | 12.4% of API sites are inside macro expansions ([R07 §4.6]) |
| Clang-built execution | F3 | never | — | — |
| `LD_PRELOAD` / `LD_AUDIT` | — | rejected: cannot interpose symbols defined in the PIE executable; refused by `run_cfs.sh:154–158` | — | [R07 §4.3] |
| gdb | F0 | reference observation only (the R2 records; ran with ASLR disabled, [R2g]) | +0.6 to +1.1 s to OPERATIONAL ([R2g §1]) | — |
| valgrind helgrind / DRD | F0 binary, serialized threads | **labelled external reference point** (§8.3), run through `RUN_CFS_WRAP` like gdb | large slowdown, not measured on cFS ([R07] L466) | memory-level only; valgrind serializes threads, so its schedule is not README-mode scheduling |

The observer runs as root next to the README-mode `ubuntu` process. This needs a new root-side hook in `env/run_cfs.sh` (PI decision D1, [R07 §9 item 1]).

### 5.2 Probe placement and return-value capture

- **Function entries at entry+4** [D]. gcc emits `endbr64; push %rbp; mov %rsp,%rbp` at `-O0`; checked on nine core functions and one app entry ([B §A.2]) and on `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` ([X3, here]). At entry+4 the argument registers still hold the arguments.
- **Locks and queues without return probes** ([B §5.5]; [J3-M4]). Mutex order from `objseq` at `OS_MutSemGive` entry; queue edges from put entry and the get outcome where an edge or a contract needs it.
- **Return values only at contract-use sites**, where an outcome decides an edge, and at the prefix sites of §4.7 item 6. The capture mechanism is **chosen per site by measurement in stage S1**:
  - (R-a) uretprobe on the callee. This kernel has the uretprobe syscall trampoline (`__x64_sys_uretprobe`, `arch_uretprobe_trampoline` in `/proc/kallsyms`, [X3, here]), so R-a may be cheaper than the #DB class [I];
  - (R-b) uprobe at the caller's instruction after the `call` (typically `mov %eax,-0x..(%rbp)`), inferred to be in the 31.7 µs class like the measured `mov` at +5 [I];
  - (R-c) uprobe at the callee's epilogue `leave`: class unmeasured;
  - (R-d) **deferred read**: out-pointer targets captured at entry are dereferenced at the same thread's next probe hit; valid only where the static check shows that no event of that thread between the return and the next probe can write the target. Used at sites that ignore the status, e.g. `CFE_ES_GetAppID(&CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` (fixes [X3-m3]);
  - (**R-e**) **the first emulated instruction after the call while the value is still in a frame slot** (fixes [X3-m3]). At `-O0`, gcc spills the result and tests it: e.g. after `call CFE_ES_GetAppID` in `CFE_EVS_TaskInit`, `mov %eax,-0x124(%rbp)`, `cmpl $0x0,-0x124(%rbp)`, `je` ([M] [X3, here], `0x2aca1`–`0x2acae`). Branches are emulated by x86 uprobes (`branch_emulate_op` present), so a probe on the `je` that reads the slot at `rbp-0x124` is plausibly in the 0.57 µs class [I].

  Each planned probe records its cost class. No overhead claim is made before S1.
- **Internal points** (publication stores not at an API boundary): an entry probe on the out-of-line local copy of the `static inline` helper, bound by DWARF compile unit (copies exist, §3.17); otherwise a write watchpoint; otherwise the nearest instruction of a cheap class.
- **Capture sites** (value conjuncts, §4.8 step 4): the call-entry probe of the consuming API usually brackets the capture point closely enough; the plan adds a probe at the capture load only where a decision needs it.
- **Site validator.** Every planned address is disassembled with LLVM MC and looked up in the calibration table (§5.10). Expensive sites are reported, never accepted silently.
- **App `.so` files: register by inode, system-wide, before launch** (fixes [X3-M1], [X3-m4]). Perf-PMU uprobes are attached to (file inode, offset) for every process, before `core-cpu1` starts; this was shown to count hits for a file mapped after registration ([R07] L340, tested on the main binary only; S1 tests it on an app `.so`). The v1 fallback (holding the startup thread at each `OS_ModuleLoad` return) is **removed from the default design**: it delays app loading, the very variable that changed the init interleaving in [BM] (849 ms load span instead of 2.2–3.3 ms; 10 apps initialized before the last was loaded). If it is ever used, its traces are excluded from σ_E pools and say so.

### 5.3 eBPF data path

- **Site keying by attach cookie, never by runtime address** [D] (fixes [X3-M1]). ASLR is on (`randomize_va_space` = 2, [M] [X3, here]; `mmap_rnd_bits` 28, [R07] L77); `core-cpu1` is a PIE and apps are `dlopen`ed at random bases. A map keyed by the probed runtime IP would have to be filled per module after mapping, racing first execution (all 15 apps load within 2.2–3.3 ms, [BM] L278). Instead each site's **attach cookie** equals its site id:
  - option 1: one perf uprobe fd per site, linked with `BPF_LINK_CREATE` carrying the cookie;
  - option 2: one `uprobe_multi` link per binary with per-offset cookies.

  The program reads the cookie with `bpf_get_attach_cookie`. The kernel symbols for both options exist (`bpf_perf_link_attach`, `bpf_get_attach_cookie_pe`, `bpf_get_attach_cookie_uprobe_multi`, [M] [X3, here]; presence only). S1 probes both functionally.
- **One generic BPF program per phase** (entry, point, return, watch). The cookie indexes a **site-descriptor array map**: registers to copy, stack slots and bounded dereferences, whether to read a bounded string (bounds `OS_MAX_API_NAME` 20 and `OS_MAX_PATH_LEN` 64 from the built `osconfig.h`, [B §A.2]), whether to assign an `objseq`, whether to capture a user stack. Descriptors are **data**; there is no per-site code generation ([C §5.3]).
- **Within verifier limits** [I] (fixes [X3-M1]): `pt_regs` is read only at constant offsets, so the program copies a fixed register set at constant offsets and then selects with bounded code; stack slots and dereferences are read with `bpf_probe_read_user`; strings with a constant maximum.
- **No libbpf** ([R07 §2.3]): helper prototypes are written by hand against the UAPI `linux/bpf.h` (present, [J3 §2.4]); the BPF ELF object (system clang-18 `-target bpf`) is parsed with glibc `<elf.h>`. Neither needs an install.
- **Per-thread `tseq`** in task-local storage (or a map keyed by tid). **Per-object `objseq`** by atomic add on a per-object map entry, assigned only at in-critical-section points [I].
- **Buffers**: per-CPU ring buffers (or a perf event array). Nothing is shared with the traced program in user space; the traced threads make no extra system calls and take no user-space lock ([R06 F10]).
- **Stacks**: `bpf_get_stack` with `BPF_F_USER_STACK` (frame-pointer unwinding of gcc `-O0` code), at sites that need on-behalf frames; depth from the maximum static call depth, capped by `perf_event_max_stack`; truncation is flagged.
- **Feasibility** [U]. Raw `bpf()` load and attach to a uprobe were probed ([R07 §4.7]). Ring buffers, task storage, `bpf_get_stack`, attach cookies and `uprobe_multi` were **not** probed. S1 tests each one. The fallback is the perf-sample backend.

### 5.4 The tracefs option (only if used)

tracefs is no longer the bring-up path (fixes [X3-m2]). If it is used anyway: a **dedicated tracing instance**; an explicit cross-CPU trace clock set for that instance (the default "local" clock is per CPU [I]); every event definition removed at exit; the use recorded in RUNMETA as a **host-state change**. Per-thread order then comes from timestamps under assumption **A-CLK-PO** (clocksource `tsc` with `constant_tsc` and `nonstop_tsc` on all four vCPUs, [B §A.2]). Cross-thread order comes only from matched protocol keys; lock order inferred from timestamps is `clock-ordered` evidence, excluded from VC, VC_R, certificates and prediction constraints ([J2-4]).

### 5.5 Event record and trace container

The raw record `kz_raw_ev` (64 bytes, §2.3) carries a timestamp (evidence only), tid, `tseq`, site id (= cookie), phase, flags, `objseq` and four decoded values. Strings and stacks go into side sections keyed by `(tid, tseq)`.

A `.kzt` container has these sections: `SITES` (copied from the plan, with the binary build-id, inode and offsets); `RAW_EVENTS`, `STRINGS`, `STACKS`; `LOSS`; `RUNMETA` (B hash, plan hash, S0 hash, stratum, kernel version, backend, holds, observer CPU time and placement, harness version); `PERTURBATION` (per-run accounting, §4.13).

`kz trace` decodes `RAW_EVENTS` into typed `EVENTS`: actor instance, operation, resource, outcome, identity quadruple, template link and per-conjunct LP intervals.

### 5.6 Loss accounting

Ring-buffer reservation failures increment per-CPU, per-site counters (perf backend: `PERF_RECORD_LOST`). Per-thread `tseq` gaps identify the lost records of each actor. Any loss marks the affected actor's interval as incomplete, and every verdict whose evidence depends on that interval becomes `unknown(loss)`. **Loss is never treated as the absence of events.** Buffer size is set from a calibration run's high-water mark and recorded ([B §5.2]).

### 5.7 Identity bootstrap without `GetAppID`

No in-process lookup is ever made by the instrumentation; all joins are offline. An instrument that called an OSAL name lookup could trigger the F1 lock leak it is meant to detect ([R01 §6.3]); calling `GetAppID` early would itself hit the App-record window ([v0.1] §8.4).

1. Every record carries the kernel tid.
2. Creator side: `OS_TaskCreate` entry (name, entry pointer, priority) and its out-parameter.
3. Child side: `OS_TaskEntryPoint(osal_id)` (exported, `osapi-task.c:124`) binds tid ↔ OSAL id before `OS_TaskPrepare`; `OS_PthreadTaskEntry` (`os-impl-tasks.c:131`) is a `.symtab` fallback ([J3 §2.4]).
4. ES side: `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` arguments (`ParentAppId`), the task-record publication, and the App-record publications (AST-bound sites, §3.17). These joins also check A-OWNER and A-FRESH (O9).
5. Timebase thread: `OS_TimeBase_CallbackThread(timebase_id)` (`osapi-timebase.c:365`); callbacks attributed through `OS_TimerAdd` records.
6. Raw threads by creation site; PSP-set kernel thread names (15 characters, `cfe_psp_start.c:197–201`) are an independent second check.
7. Generations from OSAL and cFE serials; across runs, `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)`.
8. `svc` frames by symbolizing user stacks against the gcc binary; `stamp` from arguments, using Φ's parameter roles.

### 5.8 Causal tokens versus timestamps

Cross-thread order comes **only** from matched synchronization: `objseq` for mutexes; descriptor plus FIFO for queues; descriptor plus pipe for SB; outcome-implied reads-from for registries; the recorded exit status for polls; OSAL ID for task creation. Semaphore matching uses the conservative join. Where a protocol key is insufficient, a timestamp window may be used as *matching evidence*: the edge is then ambiguous and the evidence weakened. **`CLOCK_MONOTONIC` values never become HB edges** ([R04 §2.1, §4 row 16]; v0.1 §9.3).

### 5.9 Safety rules for observation and control

- eBPF runs in kernel context. It calls no OSAL API, takes no user lock, never prints, and cannot be cancelled mid-record, which removes by construction the in-process hazards of [R01 §6.3].
- What remains is timing. A trap inside an OSAL table-locked region lengthens the hold and could consume bounded RESERVED-wait attempts (§3.2: by broadcast **or** by timeout). The planner prefers entry probes outside OSAL internal locks and flags any probe inside an idmap critical section, a timebase callback or the binsem internal section.
- Holds follow §4.13. Observer CPU time and placement are recorded in every run, because the drainer competes for the 4 vCPUs during the startup burst ([BM §1.3] could not separate this effect for a root-side hook).

### 5.10 Cost model and measurement obligations

Per thread `i`: `ΔC_i = Σ_j n_ij · c_j` (v0.1 §11.2); per confirmation run add `Σ_holds (c_hold + hold duration)`. **Six costs are reported separately**: analysis, exploration, recording, control, profile authoring (person-hours), and triage (rater hours, §8.5) ([R06 R4]; [J1-15]; [X3-M7]).

| Quantity | Value | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `c_trap` at entry+4 (`push %rbp`) | 0.57 µs | [M] |
| `c_trap` at `endbr64`, `mov %rsp,%rbp` (+5) or another non-emulated instruction | 31.7 µs | [M] |
| R-b post-call `mov %eax,-0x..(%rbp)` | inferred 31.7 µs class | [I]; S1 |
| R-e emulated branch after the spill | inferred 0.57 µs class | [I]; S1 |
| R-a uretprobe (syscall trampoline present); R-c `leave` | — | unmeasured; S1 |
| `c_bpf` (program body, cookie lookup, string fetch, stack capture) | — | unmeasured; S1 |
| `c_wp` (HW watchpoint hit) | ≈34 µs | [M] |
| `c_hold` (gdb breakpoint) | ≈203 µs | [M]; purpose-built holder and auto-attach at clone unmeasured (S6) |
| ptrace × uprobe and ptrace × signal interactions | — | unmeasured; S6 |
| gate handoff (F1) | 1.1 µs same CPU / 10.5 µs across CPUs | [M] |
| `n_ij` per boot | — | unmeasured for the full sync skeleton; for scale only: the retired CORD recorder logged 626–632 events per boot ([R2c §4.1]); 240–258 `sb.appid` uses per boot ([R2c §7]) |
| static API call sites | 3,762 | [M] static count ([R07 §4.2]) |

Arithmetic on measured values only, not a prediction [I]: 626 hits at 0.57 µs is about 0.36 ms per boot; at 31.7 µs about 20 ms. OPERATIONAL is about 1.6 s ([M] 1613–1636 ms, [R2g §1]); CORE_READY → OPERATIONAL is 103.2–104.6 ms in cFE time across nine runs ([R2c §4.4]). The startup burst (`SBNSubPipe` overflow about 47–49 ms after OPERATIONAL, [BM §1.3]) is where probe time concentrates and where the per-witness perturbation ratio (§4.13) will be largest.

**Calibration table.** S1 measures each instruction class with a micro-benchmark on this VM, by the method of [R07 §4.7]: `push`, `mov`, `leave`, the emulated branch (R-e), uretprobe, the BPF body with and without string and stack capture, cookie lookup, the perf-sample path, and the holder's stop/resume. The table is versioned by kernel. **Group A is re-measured on `-fc-v114`** before any comparison ([R07 §1]; D11).

### 5.11 Untested items and fallbacks

| Item | Status | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| inode-registered uprobes on an app `.so` before `dlopen` | untested on a `.so` ([R07 §9 item 6]); works on the main binary | none needed by design; the startup-thread hold is excluded from pools if ever used |
| attach cookies (`BPF_LINK_CREATE` / `uprobe_multi`) | kernel symbols present; not functionally probed | perf-sample backend (site from the event's perf fd) |
| ring buffer, task storage, `bpf_get_stack` | not probed | perf-sample records with registers and user stack |
| return-capture mechanisms (R-a…R-e) | unmeasured | calibration decides per site; deferred read only where statically valid |
| `c_bpf` with stack capture | unmeasured | capture stacks only at sites that need frames |
| purpose-built ptrace holder cost; auto-attach at clone; ptrace × uprobe interaction | unmeasured | gdb-equivalent bound (≈203 µs per stop) for planning only |
| BPF ELF loading without libbpf | design inference | install libbpf (P-LIBBPF) |
| out-of-line copies of `static inline` helpers at `-O0` | **closed**: copies exist ([X3, here]) | — |

---

## 6. Guarantees

All statements are relative to the basis B, the profile Φ (hashed, every rule cited), the model level M, the semantics σ, the bound b, the skeleton view, and the assumptions A below. Terminology follows [R05 §0.5]: "over-approximate relative to M and A", "complete up to b"; never a bare "sound". This is "soundy" in the sense of Livshits et al. (CACM 2015 [V-abs]).

### 6.1 Assumptions A (the soundiness manifest)

| Id | Assumption | Effect if violated | Mitigation / visibility |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Rule validity: every Φ rule, effect and binding is correct for the pinned revision | wrong edges or effects | citations; O3/O4/O7 refute; certificates list the rules used |
| A2 | Binding correctness: Clang sites and AST patterns map to the gcc binary's instructions | wrong probes or anchors | anchor audit; DWARF compile-unit binding |
| A3 | Closure: actors come only from the startup script **selected by B**, the generated object table, `CreateChildTask` sites, OSAL timebase/timer creation and OSAL-internal threads, PSP threads and environment inputs; `dlsym` targets are resolved by configuration; residual indirect calls are `unknown_effect`; code outside the compile DB is out of model | missed actors or targets | closure boot incl. the opened script (O5); `unknown_effect` → T-SPAWN ∅, writer sets DISABLED |
| A4 | Memory model: sequential consistency at event granularity on x86-64 TSO at `-O0`; ad hoc flag synchronization is an assumed-visibility edge | weak-memory effects missed | edges tagged `assumed`; sub-label; D10 |
| A5 | API-level edges only for shapes; OSAL-internal locks constrain interleavings only under M_impl | — | ablation 11 |
| A6 | Observation completeness for planned sites, with loss accounting | missed events | `tseq` gaps; `unknown(loss)` |
| A7-impl | Φ_L-1 semantics (pthread mutex, condvar incl. timed wait, `sem_*`, `mq_*`, `clock_nanosleep`, thread create/cancel/join) over-approximate kernel, libc and OSAL POSIX behaviour on the M_impl paths at LP granularity (equal-priority FIFO for `mq` is UNVERIFIED, [R04 §4 row 7]; OSAL always uses priority 1) | containment fails | litmus suite on the real OSAL (§9.2); O4 |
| A7-api | Every OSAL primitive **not** covered by M_impl (A10) is linearizable at its LP on the real backend | containment fails for those primitives | litmus tests; O4 |
| A8 | Out of model: inline assembly, signals beyond the PSP exception path and `SIGUSR1` shutdown, ISRs, DMA, weak-memory effects | — | listed |
| A9 | Inputs: B.Input is the complete set of external events of the run (message provenance relies on it) | ground-driven paths latent; handlers wrongly folded | scenario families as B′; O5 (an observed message outside `Prov(p)` is a closure failure) |
| A10 | M_impl covers the OSAL idmap, task, binsem and queue paths only | other primitives at API level in both | listed; A7-api |
| A11 | Table values: from `.tbl` on POWERON; **from the decoded CDS image for critical tables on PROCESSOR resets**; runtime table loads only via B.Input | wrong table contents | table cross-check; CDS decoding in `basis` |
| A12 | One process; SBN peers and cpu2 out of model | cross-instance contracts not covered | listed ([R03 §9 item 8]) |
| A-SKEL | `Sk^cert` over-approximates each actor instance's projected behaviour under B. This supports safety statements. It does not make progress results sound for B, because deadness is preserved neither by over- nor by under-approximation (L-DEAD, G11) | spurious or missing model behaviour | O1, O2, O8 |
| A-WS | Writer-set completeness: S3a (and S3b) find every writer of every support location, given A-TYPE, and every `DECLARED_TRUSTED` guard is correct | unsound T-OBS | O7; trusted entries listed per certificate; O3/O4/O9 refute; ablation 17 |
| A-TYPE | Typed access: every access to a support location goes through its declared type | untyped writes missed | the untyped-write and address-escape listings (§4.2 step 3): each hit disables the location |
| A-EXTRACT | Extraction faithfulness: the facts in `facts.kz` are faithful to B's source as compiled. These are the AST copy chains, writer sites and their transition classification, serialized loop-exit expressions with mod sets and lock regions, the INIT image, constant evaluations and ALLOCS | certificates re-validated on wrong facts | O4, O7, anchor audit, litmus suite, program mutants (§8.5); listed in part (ii) of every trust ledger |
| A-CORE | The shared `libkz-core` components (Φ parser, core-expression evaluator, rule engine, container I/O) are correct | producer and checker share an error | evaluator agreement property test (§2.3), golden tests (S0); listed in part (iii) of every trust ledger |
| A-FRESH | Distinct reservation nodes allocate distinct, previously unused records | wrong key separation | `DECLARED_TRUSTED` per use; O9 |
| A-OWNER | The App record resolved for a task created by `StartAppTask(…, ParentAppId)` is the record of the reservation whose `PendingAppId` flowed to `ParentAppId` | wrong ownership | `DECLARED_TRUSTED` per use; O9 |
| A-COMM | Per predicted witness: every location read in a cone actor's unobserved code that another cone actor may write is observed, constant, or read through a kept outcome | a "predicted" witness infeasible | checked per witness (§4.9); failure → `predicted-modelled` |
| A-API | The abstract trace is faithful enough for SHB/WCP/SyncP soundness to transfer | prefilter proposals wrong | always re-validated (§4.9 step 6) |
| A-CLK-PO | tracefs option only: a cross-CPU-consistent clock for per-thread order | wrong program order | recorded; the eBPF and perf-sample backends remove it |
| A-ZT | σ_R1 only: computation between blocking points takes zero time | σ_R1 orders that depend on execution time are wrong | sub-label on every σ_R1 verdict that depends on a delay |
| L1, L2, L3 | Lemma obligations (await summarization with extracted predicates; removal of a critical section whose lock is not a cone object and is acquired by no other cone actor, and whose body touches nothing in the cone and contains no blocking operation; mover-reduced critical sections) | model behaviour changed, including its stall states | each switchable per instance; ablated; L1's preconditions checked by `kz proofcheck` on the extracted mod sets and lock regions (A-EXTRACT); proofs written before F1; whether L2 and L3 preserve stall states is part of their obligations (litmus LP-DL checks that L2 keeps every section of a lock-order cycle), and L1's obligation includes that the summarized poller is dead exactly where the unsummarized one is stranded on a test whose predicate can never become true (§6.2) |
| T-JACM | The correctness theorem of source-DPOR with sleep sets extended to disabling (JACM 2017), status P | `dpor` results unverified | `dpor` verdicts labelled unverified; `exh` mode available; differential test as validation |

### 6.2 Lemmas and proof sketches

The model at level M consists of per-actor certificate-view skeleton automata plus Φ semantics at level M, taken as the transition systems `TS(B,σ,M)` of §1.8. Every safety lemma below is stated over finite executions `Pref(B,σ_U,M)`. Its proof is an induction over positions in a finite execution, so it uses neither maximality nor fairness.

**L-MB (MustBefore soundness).** For every finite execution `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, every node `n`, every occurrence `n_k` of `n` in `ρ` and every `a ∈ MB(n)`, an occurrence of `a` precedes `n_k` in `ρ`. The statement is conditioned on occurrences. It says nothing about finite executions in which `n` does not occur, which is why no certificate built from MB facts can establish progress.

*Sketch.* Induction on the position of `n_k` in `ρ`; the fixpoint equations hold for the computed MB. Case by the equation that kept `a` in MB(n):
- **Base cases.** Entries with `MB = ∅` contain nothing to prove (fixes [X1-m2]: no empty meet yields ⊤).
- **(T-PO)** The actor reached `n_k` from some `Sk^cert` predecessor occurrence `p_j`, which is earlier: in `Sk^cert` every loop is either unrolled exactly to a reference bound or star-closed, so **every** actual predecessor of `n_k` (also in any iteration of a message loop) is a skeleton predecessor (A-SKEL; this is where the h-cut of v1 broke the argument, [X1-C1]). Terminal ops have no successors. Since `a ∈ MB(p) ∪ {p}`, the hypothesis on `p_j` gives an earlier occurrence of `a`.
- **(T-SPAWN)** An actor's entry is preceded by one of its spawn sites (A3: the list is complete). `a` is in the meet over all spawn sites.
- **(T-OBS)** `o_k` observed `Pred_code` true; by L1, the observation is equivalent to an atomic test at the await's success point, and `Pred_code ⇒ C`. `C` was false right after the latest occurrence `f_j` of `f` before `o_k` (falsifier obligation; `f` dominates `o` modulo T-TERM). So some write `w_l` between `f_j` and `o_k` made `C` true. By A-WS, `w ∈ W_C`. It is not removed from `W_o`: (OWN-NOPATH) if `w` were in `o`'s once actor with no path `w →* o`, its occurrence between `f_j` and `o_k` in that actor's unique run would give such a path; (PWB-before-falsifier) `PWB(w, f)` would put `w_l` before every occurrence of `f`, contradicting `f_j < w_l`; (PWB-after-observation) `PWB(o, w)` would put `w_l` after `o_k`; (KEY-SEP) a `NO_KEY` writer cannot change `C`'s key. Each PWB fact used was derived from the same fixpoint and is justified by the induction hypothesis at earlier occurrences ([X1 §1]). So `w ∈ W_o`, and `a ∈ MB(w) ∪ {w}` gives an occurrence of `a` before `w_l`, hence before `o_k`.

Locks contribute nothing. ∎

**L-ONCE.** If `once(a)` and `a ∈ MB(b)`, then `PWB(a, b)`, over the finite executions `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` (§1.9).

*Sketch.* By L-MB, every occurrence of `b` is preceded by an occurrence of `a`. `a` occurs at most once (its actor has at most one instance, and `a` lies outside every star-closed region, so it occurs at most once in that instance's run). That single occurrence therefore precedes every occurrence of `b`. ∎

**L-PWB (frontier).** If `once(actor(a))`, `m` is in `actor(a)`, `m ∉ Anc(a)` and `m ∈ MB(b)`, then in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` containing occurrences of `a` and `b`, every occurrence of `a` precedes every occurrence of `b`.

*Sketch.* By L-MB, an occurrence of `m` precedes each occurrence of `b`. If some occurrence of `a` came after an occurrence of `m`, the actor's unique run (once) would contain `m … a`, and by A-SKEL `Sk^cert` would contain a path `m →* a`, contradicting `m ∉ Anc(a)`. So every `a` precedes every `m`, hence every `b`. Without once, `m` and `a` could lie in different instances, which is why once is a premise (fixes [X1-M2]). ∎

**L-SCOPE** (restated by EXT-20261012 §4.1). If `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB(d)`, and an occurrence of `d` precedes the decision event of an occurrence `u_k` of a use in a finite execution ρ (in particular, its call or one of its conjunct LPs at or before its decision), then `u_k` is out of scope.

*Sketch.* By L-MB, a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes that occurrence of `d`, hence the decision event. Scope is decided at the decision event (§1.6), so `u_k` is out of scope. ∎ A conjunct LP after the decision does not affect scope. The lemma is what PROTECTED (a)/(b), L-PR and L-FO need when they drop a SCOPE disabler or writer for one conjunct: a conjunct LP at or before the decision is covered by the lemma, and an occurrence whose conjunct LP lies after its decision was decided by another false conjunct, or its call returned before that LP and it is `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts (§1.6). Scope decided at the first conjunct LP fails for a `d` between two conjunct LPs, and scope decided over every conjunct LP fails for code that does not short-circuit (litmus LP-SCOPE2). It is listed in trust ledger part (iii) wherever SCOPE is used.

**L-DERIVE.** If `derives(arg, loc, c)` holds, the value the API checks at its slot LP equals the value of `loc` at `c`.

*Sketch.* The argument is a copy chain from the load at `c` through stores, loads of thread-local temporaries, parameter passing and casts without arithmetic; none of these reads shared memory again. ∎ Hence a value conjunct evaluated at `c` decides the API's outcome (fixes [X1-M1]).

**L-FO (fixed-order certificate).** Suppose a FIXED_ORDER certificate (§4.5) exists for instance `i` at level M, with conjunct `P_j` and LP node `ℓ_j`. Take any `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)` and any in-scope occurrence `u_k` of the use decided in ρ. If `ℓ_{j,k}` lies at or before the decision of `u_k`, then `P_j` is false at `ℓ_{j,k}`; otherwise either `u_k` was decided earlier by another false conjunct, or its call returned without passing `ℓ_j`, and then `u_k` is `NOT_EVALUATED` (§1.6). So `u_k` is not READY, and, with the badness condition, no extension of ρ makes it `TOLERATED`. Hence `¬OK_i(σ_U,M,∞)`. The badness condition also names the REJECT events that decide not-ready occurrences; those that lie at the occurrence's decision are its decision route (§1.10). Two things are not claimed: that some occurrence is reached, and that every execution reaches a decision rather than leaving occurrences `PENDING`. These are reach and progress matters.

*Sketch.* Suppose `P_j` true at `ℓ_{j,k}` at or before the decision. `P_j` was false at INIT, so some writer `x ∈ W_j` (complete by A-WS; `NO_KEY` writers cannot change the key's state) occurred before `ℓ_{j,k}`. A SCOPE-excluded writer would precede the decision and make `u_k` out of scope (L-SCOPE). By L-PWB or L-ONCE, every occurrence of `ℓ_j` precedes every occurrence of `x`. Contradiction. For a value conjunct, L-DERIVE transfers the result to the API's check. No dominance of the use is needed, so early returns are covered ([J2-1]). ∎

**L-PR (protected certificate).** Suppose a PROTECTED certificate (§4.5) exists for `i` at level M. Take any `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, any in-scope occurrence `u_k` of the use, and any conjunct `P_j` whose LP `ℓ_{j,k}` lies in ρ at or before the decision of `u_k`. Then `P_j` holds at `ℓ_{j,k}`. Hence no in-scope occurrence is decided by a false conjunct, every decided in-scope occurrence is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED` (`¬NR_i(σ_U,M,∞)`), `¬Bad_i(σ_U,M,∞)` holds, and no occurrence is `TOLERATED` or `PENDING`. Nothing is claimed about finite executions that do not reach the use, about stall states, about reach, or about maximal executions.

*Sketch.* Case (a) is immediate: every disabler is NO_KEY, MB-unreachable, or SCOPE-excluded, and a SCOPE-excluded disabler that occurs before `ℓ_{j,k}`, hence before the decision, makes `u_k` out of scope (L-SCOPE). Case (b): by L-MB, `x*` occurred before `ℓ_{j,k}`, and as a `MUST_KEY` must-writer it established `P_j` **for this key** (fixes [X1-M3](a)). Every remaining disabler `d` satisfies either `PWB(d, x*)` (it occurred before every `x*`) or `PWB(ℓ_j, d)` (it occurs after every `ℓ_j`), or is SCOPE-excluded as above. Hence no disabler of the key lies between the last `x*` and `ℓ_{j,k}` in an in-scope occurrence, and `P_j` holds there. Applied to the first conjunct LP of `u_k` at which the conjunct would be false, this shows that no in-scope occurrence is decided not ready. ∎

**L-CONT (prefix containment, per level)** (fixes [X1-C2]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2).
- **(a)** `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M) = {ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M) : ρ contains no time-based transition}`.
  *Sketch.* Both systems have the same states and successor function, and `en_σU∞(q) = en_σU(q) \ TB` in every state (§1.8). Induction on length gives ⊆, and, for σ_U finite executions without time-based transitions, ⊇. Strong fairness constrains only infinite executions. ∎
- **(b)** `π(Pref(B,σ_R1[P_sched],M)) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`. σ_R1 is a separate system (§1.8); π erases its clock-advance and wake steps, keeps every other step (a delay's block step is σ_U's delay transition), and forgets the clock and the ready/blocked status.
  *Sketch.* σ_R1 enables a σ_U transition only if that transition is enabled in the projected σ_U state: priority and blocking only remove choices, timeouts are enabled as in σ_U, A-ZT adds no effect, and the erased steps change no σ_U component. ∎ [I]
- **(b′) L-CONE-R1** [I] (obligation). If a bounded σ_R1 model's cone contains the σ_U model's cone at the same h, erasing from a σ_R1 execution the erased steps of (b) and the transitions of actors outside the σ_U cone gives a finite execution of the σ_U model. *Sketch:* by the cone closure (§4.10 item 1), those actors write no location of the σ_U cone and synchronize on none of its objects; they interact with it only through the CPU, which σ_U ignores. ∎
- **(c)** The projection of every finite real run of B onto LP-granular events is in `Pref(B,σ_U,M_impl)`.
  *Sketch.* By A7-impl, every real step on an M_impl path is a step of Φ_L-1 at LP granularity, including timed-wait timeouts and broadcast wakes. By A7-api, the other primitives are atomic at their LPs. A real timeout is one of σ_U's timeout transitions. By A-SKEL, each actor's real sequence is a `Sk^cert` path. By A3 and A9, no actor or input is missing. By A4, interleaving at event granularity is adequate. Induction on the run's length then gives a step simulation. It says nothing about the run's end state. ∎
- **(d) Not claimed.**
  - `Max(B,σ_U∞,M) ⊆ Max(B,σ_U,M)` is false. In litmus LP-TW, the finite σ_U∞ execution that ends with its waiter blocked is σ_U∞-maximal, while σ_U enables the waiter's timeout.
  - Real runs are not maximal in (σ_U, M_impl). This fails for runs ended by `abort()`, a kill or the stop, whose end states still enable σ_U transitions.
  - Maximal containment of σ_R1 modulo π [I] is plausible under §1.8's clock rule. It is not proved and not used.
- **(e)** σ_E against M_api is **not** claimed: the real POSIX backend has executions (e.g. `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`) that M_api excludes by design. L-MLEVEL (below) gives the conditional version.

**Corollary (reverse prefix inclusion).** Every finite execution of σ_U without time-based transitions is a finite execution of σ_U∞. In particular, the state just before the first time-based transition of a σ_U execution is σ_U∞-reachable.

**L-REACH-CONE (TB-freedom relative to the cone)** [I] (obligation). Let C be the closed cone of instance i (§4.10 item 1). If the transitions of C's actors in ρ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` contain no time-based transition, then some ρ′ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M)` has the same projection onto C's actors. *Sketch:* delete from ρ, for each actor outside C, its transitions from its first time-based transition on, and then, repeatedly, every transition of an actor outside C that a deleted transition enabled; by the cone closure no actor outside C writes a location of C, synchronizes on an object of C, spawns an actor of C or writes the support of a predicate of C, so C's transitions stay enabled. ∎ Run-based σ_U∞ reach (§1.10) uses it.

**L-MAPI-CONE (M_api conformance relative to the cone)** [I] (obligation). Let C be the closed cone of instance i and ρ the LP-granular projection of a finite real run. If every OSAL call made by C's actors in ρ returned an outcome that M_api allows, and no M_impl-only behaviour (an `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` return, a lost task, a type lock left held by a returned API) occurred on C's actors, then some ρ′ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U,M_api)` has the same projection onto C's actors. *Sketch:* map each OSAL call of C's actors to its M_api transition at its LP with the observed outcome; an actor that takes a type lock that a cone actor also takes synchronizes on a cone object, so by the cone closure it is in C; delete the transitions of actors outside C from their first non-conforming one on, and, repeatedly, every transition of an actor outside C that a deleted one enabled; C's transitions stay enabled. ∎ Run-based M_api reach and M_api run witnesses (§1.8 rule 3, §1.10) use it.

**L-STATUS (statuses are determined by the prefix).** The status and the scope of an in-scope occurrence in a finite execution ρ (§1.6) depend only on the effect events of ρ. The state at each conjunct LP is computed from the lifecycle effects that precede it; scope asks only whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes the occurrence's decision event, which is in ρ once the occurrence is decided; and the horizon monitor reads only the monitored actor's events (its spawn, if it did not exist at decision) and the resource's effects. Hence:
- (a) the status is invariant under π, and is the same whether ρ is read as a finite execution of σ′ or of σ_U;
- (b) `READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING` and `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`, and the scope of a decided occurrence, are final in every extension of ρ;
- (c) every extension of a bad finite execution is bad. ∎

**L-MON (instance monitor).** The instance monitor of §1.8 is deterministic and never blocks a transition. Hence (a) product executions project exactly onto `Pref(B,σ,M)`, and the monitor state after ρ is a function of ρ; (b) an actor is dead in a product state iff it is dead in the underlying state; (c) the status and the scope of an occurrence decided by a transition t from a product state `(q, m)` are a function of `(q, m, t)`, so `Bad_i(o)`, `OK_i(o)` and `NR_i` are properties of the edges of the product graph, and the stall predicates of §1.8 are functions of the product state. Decided statuses are not part of the monitor state. *Sketch:* every TS path lifts to exactly one product path; deadness reads only enabledness; a deciding transition reads the conjunct values and horizon states held in m and its own effect. ∎

**L-TRANS (transfer soundness).**
- **(1a)** If `¬Bad_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬Bad_i(σ′,M,b)`, if `¬OK_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬OK_i(σ′,M,b)`, and if `¬NR_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬NR_i(σ′,M,b)`, for σ′ = σ_U∞, and for σ′ = σ_R1[P_sched] with b = ∞ or, with a bounded b, under L-CONE-R1. A σ′ finite execution with a `VIOLATING` (resp. `READY`/`TOLERATED`, resp. a decided not-ready) occurrence would be, after π and, for bounded σ_R1, after the cone projection, a σ_U finite execution with the same status (L-CONT (a)/(b)/(b′), L-STATUS (a)). For σ_U∞, the bounded models of σ_U and σ′ share `Sk^h`, the cone and b.
- **(2)** With b = ∞ and M = M_impl, the same argument with L-CONT (c) gives the σ_E statement for every finite real run. For M_api it needs L-MLEVEL or the agreeing M_impl result.
- **(3)** By the same lemmas, a good or bad witness of σ′ is a witness of σ_U at the same M, and so is a real run at M_impl, or at M_api under L-MLEVEL or with an M_api-conformance check of its prefix.
- **(Negative)** The following do not transfer down: `Reach_i`, `OK_i` and `Bad_i` as existence claims, witnesses, `tolerated`, and stall states. Removing transitions can remove reaching finite executions and can make actors dead (LP-TW). A stall state does not transfer up either: adding transitions can revive a dead actor (LP-TW under σ_U). The only exceptions are L-DEAD (c), (d). A frequency is a measure on σ_E and is not defined by σ_U. ∎

**L-MLEVEL** [I] (restated over prefixes by EXT-20261012 §4.2). If an instance's closed cone and certificate contain no M_impl-relevant operation, then the projections of `Pref(B,σ,M_api)` and `Pref(B,σ,M_impl)` onto the cone's events coincide. Transfer rule 2(i), transfer rule 3 at M_api, per-cell reach at M_api and G2's M_api branch use this prefix form.

*Sketch.* The two levels differ only in the transitions of M_impl-relevant operations (§1.4). If none occurs in the cone, the cone's transitions and their enabling conditions are identical at both levels; actors outside the cone affect the cone only through cone objects (by the cone's closure), which are not M_impl-relevant. ∎ The lemma is an obligation to prove in the paper; until then transfer rule 2(i) is applied with the sub-label `mlevel-lemma`.

**L-REF (refinements are well defined)** (fixes [X1-M5]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). At the same M and b:
- `timeout-dependent` is `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent ∧ ¬Bad_i(σ_U∞) ∧ Reach_i(σ_U∞)`;
- `timeout-rescued` is `OK_i(σ_U) ∧ ¬OK_i(σ_U∞)`;
- `timeout-masked-liveness` is, per instance: some product state reachable under σ_U∞ is a hang state for i under σ_U∞ (a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending) and not a hang state for i under σ_U;
- `tml-root(i₁)` is: no progress chain leads into `i₁`, and some `i₂` with `i₁ ⇝_p* i₂` (`i₂ = i₁` included) has `timeout-masked-liveness`; an unchained instance with TML therefore carries it itself.

**Well-definedness.** Each refinement is a Boolean combination of the per-σ queries of §4.10 item 7, of progress chains, and of deadness and reachability tests on the shared product state space. Progress chains are decidable: their conditions (§1.8: the root inside the use or horizon; the root's satisfiers dominated by the READY outcome of the use's call; a lock leaked or a thread lost by a not-ready occurrence) are read from the product state, its finite execution and the certificate view. None uses maximal executions, fairness or quiescence. Each is therefore defined whenever the per-σ results exist, and `exh` decides each within b. An undecided deadness test gives `unknown(bound)` (censored) or `unknown(unmodelled)` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure) for `timeout-masked-liveness`. Without the explorer, none is established: the first two need universal σ_U∞ statements, which neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide, and the last two are progress results.

**Agreement with v1.** v1's `timeout-dependent` required (i) that every bad σ_U execution takes a timeout transition and (ii) that the σ_U∞ continuations from the state just before its first timeout transition reach only good outcomes, i.e. neither bad outcomes nor stalls. By L-CONT (a), `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds iff every bad finite execution of σ_U contains a time-based transition, which is (i). The "not bad" half of (ii) follows, because that state is σ_U∞-reachable by a prefix that is not bad; the "no stall" half is the progress refinement. Every v1 `timeout-masked-liveness` instance whose v1 stall state is a hang state under σ_U∞ has the new progress refinement; v1 also counted states in which the use's actor waits at an idle wait, which the new refinement excludes. The converse does not hold (progress chains attach the extra instances to their root).

**The v1 gap is closed for last attempts that are one transition at level M.** Under σ_U∞, such an actor is never left without an enabled transition while its predicate can still become true: that attempt is enabled exactly when it would succeed. An actor at a last attempt that is several transitions at M (an OSAL lookup at M_impl, a test that reads several values) completes the attempt and can be stranded; its deadness tests, and those of its closure, are undecided rather than wrongly decided. ∎

**L-DPOR (explorer correctness statement)** (fixes [X1-M9]). Local nondeterminism (havoc values, await outcomes, retry exhaustion, star-closure choices) is made into explicit transitions of the acting actor, so each actor is deterministic given its choice transitions; dependence is as in §4.10 item 3.
- **`exh`**: every **product state** (model state together with the instance monitor state, L-MON) reachable in the finite closed model within b is visited (finite-graph reachability with a visited set of product states), and every enabled edge from it is examined. `Q_reach`, `Q_bad` and `Q_ok`, also per occurrence ordinal, are properties of deciding edges, whose status is a function of the source product state and the transition (L-MON (c)), so they are decided exactly within b; deduplication by product state neither loses nor invents a deciding edge. The deadness test and the qualifiers are backward reachability properties over the visited product graph with excluded states as sinks, so `Q_stall` is also decided exactly within b, except where a test is undecided: censored by a `TRUNC` region, stranded, or in the closure of either (§4.10 item 8). A visited set over model states alone would not suffice, because the stall predicates and the deciding edges depend on the occurrence history held in the monitor state. No external theorem is needed.
- **`dpor`**: every equivalence class with a bad (or good) occurrence of `u` within b is represented **if** the JACM 2017 theorem for source-DPOR with sleep sets extended to disabling holds for this transition system class. This is assumption T-JACM, status P in [R05 §2.2]. The equivalence is that of the **extended dependence** of §4.10 item 3: the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP is dependent with the instance's calls and conjunct LPs, and an occurrence's deciding events are dependent with the transitions of a monitored actor other than the using actor and of its spawn chain. Without these, two equivalent interleavings could give an occurrence different scopes or horizon verdicts. That every status, scope and horizon verdict is invariant under the extended dependence is an obligation [I]; `dpor` claims cover only monitor properties that are. `dpor` verdicts are therefore labelled **unverified** until the theorem's statement is checked (P-PAPERS). With a preemption bound, BPOR ([V-abs]) is used and the claim is "under bound p". Under σ_R1, the conservative priority dependences keep coverage under the scheduler restriction [I]. `dpor` makes no claim about stall states, since deadness is not a property of single executions; candidates are re-checked by `exh` (§4.10 item 7).
- The differential test (`dpor` vs `exh` on every model small enough for `exh`) is validation evidence, not proof. No "one execution per class" (optimality) claim is made.

**L-DEAD (stall states)** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
- **(a)** In a finite transition system, deadness of an actor for instance i in a product state is decided by one backward reachability per actor over the reachable product graph with the states excluded for i made sinks, and `terminal-pending` (`AG EF` of a process end, on that graph without B.Input's stop event) by another; idle waits, kinds, wait-for roots and choice support are read from the state, the path and the path ahead. A stall state for i is therefore decidable. In a bounded model, a test on a stranded or potentially enabled actor (§4.10 item 8) is undecided.
- **(b)** Deadness is relative to the transition system. Adding transitions can revive a dead actor, and removing transitions can make one dead. A stall state of one σ is therefore, in general, neither a stall state of a σ with more transitions (LP-TW: σ_U∞ against σ_U) nor implied by a stall state of one with fewer.
- **(c)** **Exception: deadlock cycles.** Suppose the dead actors in q contain a cycle in which each actor waits, without a timeout, to acquire an object that only its holder can release (a mutex or an OSAL type lock), held by the next actor in the cycle, and the stall's witnessing actor belongs to the cycle or waits, without a timeout, to acquire such an object held by a cycle member, directly or through a chain of such waits. Then no transition of any σ releases any of these objects, because every holder is blocked untimed, and the witnessing actor is dead in q under every σ in which q's finite execution is admissible. The stall state transfers; its qualifiers are recomputed per σ.
- **(d)** **Exception: leaked locks.** Suppose a witnessing actor waits, without a timeout, to acquire a holder-released, non-recursive object whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and is terminated, or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release of the object that does not first acquire it again (K-LOCKBAL). Then no actor can release it under any σ, and the waiting actor is dead in every σ in which the finite execution is admissible.

*Sketch.* (a) follows from the definitions and L-MON. (b) is shown by LP-TW. (c): only a holder releases such an object, and every holder's only next transition is a blocked, untimed acquisition; an untimed wait on a cycle member's object inherits this. (d): only the holder can release the object; a terminated holder never does; any other holder must first acquire the non-recursive lock it already holds, which blocks it forever. Neither (c) nor (d) depends on which transitions σ enables. ∎

**L1 (await summarization with extracted predicates).** Suppose the poll loop satisfies the preconditions of §4.3: the body's mod set contains no relevant location other than the counter and the budget, and all test reads happen inside one critical section of one lock, or in one atomic read. Then every exit by condition of the real loop corresponds to an `await(Pred_code)` success transition at the test's atomic read point, and every exit by budget corresponds to its timeout transition.

*Sketch.* Between tests the loop changes no relevant state. The test is evaluated atomically, so its value equals `Pred_code` in the state at the read point, and `Pred_code` is extracted from that same exit branch. ∎

Under σ_U∞, the summary's timeout transition and the unsummarized loop's budget exit are both time-based transitions, and both are removed. The summary therefore does not change σ_U∞'s finite executions on the loop's relevant events, up to the unsummarized loop's failing test reads, which change no relevant state (stuttering) [I]. **It changes deadness** (EXT-20261012; rev. 4): under σ_U∞ the summarized poller is dead in a state where `Pred_code` can never become true, while the unsummarized one is stranded there on its stale failing test, so its tests and their closure are undecided. The obligation therefore includes: the summarized poller is dead exactly in the states where the unsummarized one is stranded with a predicate that can never become true, so the summary turns an undecided test into a decided one without inventing a stall. `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`) satisfies the preconditions (all reads inside one ES-lock critical section, `:878–888`) at both M; with L1 switched off, the progress results that rest on it are `unknown(unmodelled)`. `kz proofcheck` re-derives the predicate from the same serialized exit trees that the extractor produced (§4.1 step 8); those trees are extracted facts (trust ledger part (ii)). The lemma is still to be written in full before F1.

**L2, L3.**
- **L2** is stated as in [A §4.2] (`proposal_A-static-model.md:615`), with an added lock condition. Take a critical section on lock ℓ such that (i) ℓ is not a cone object and no other cone actor acquires ℓ, and (ii) its body touches no cone location or cone object and contains no blocking operation, counting its own acquire and release as touching ℓ. Removing it changes neither the reachable states and outcomes on the cone under σ_U, nor the cone's stall states, modulo its actor's intermediate control nodes [I]. *Argument:* the acquire succeeds whenever the actor reaches it, because no other cone actor holds ℓ; the body neither blocks nor changes anything another cone actor reads or waits on; the release enables no other cone actor. The removal is therefore a stuttering change for its actor, which is never dead inside the section. Condition (ii) alone is not enough. In litmus LP-DL, τ_B's inner `m1` section has an empty body; removing it breaks the lock-order cycle and with it the deadlock, so the stall-preservation claim fails under (ii) alone. Condition (i) keeps that section. The argument is a proof obligation before F1.
- **L3** (mover reduction) is stated with the conditions of §4.10 item 5: right-mover acquire, left-mover release, both-mover protected accesses, at most one non-mover, release on every path, no nested blocking acquire. Whether L3 preserves stall states is part of its proof obligation.
- Until they are proved in the paper, both lemmas are listed as assumptions, are switchable per instance, and are ablated (§8.4). The citation of the reduction theorem is an open literature check.

**L-PRED (prediction).** Every `predicted` witness is a linearization of Pred(π) that passed the A-COMM check. Under A4, A7 and Φ's primitive semantics it is feasible for every program that produces π **and** whose unobserved shared reads in the cone are covered by A-COMM (maximal causal models, RV 2012; RVPredict PLDI 2014 [V-abs], whose guarantees concern traces that contain the shared accesses). A `predicted-modelled` witness has no such guarantee and ranks with model evidence; a `predicted-extrapolated` witness is additionally relative to A-SKEL for the extrapolated loop (fixes [X1-M4]), and so is a witness completed by **horizon completion** from `Sk^cert` (§4.9 item 7a; EXT-20261012 §4.1). **Predicted not-ready evidence** (a `PENDING` occurrence in a predicted prefix) is not a witness and supports no class.

**L-LOOP (termination).** Under fixed (B, Φ, tool), the loop's comparable state (§4.14) lives in a finite lattice, every non-final iteration moves strictly up or stops, and a suspension ends the loop. A Φ edit starts a new loop. ∎

### 6.3 Guarantee statements

- **G1 (census over-approximation, soundy).** For B, Φ, M and A, every contract use reachable in `Sk^cert` yields an instance with a class or `unknown(reason)` per analysed (σ, M). `inactive` is issued only from `Sk^cert`. Instances outside the closure are counted and listed. Claim template C-1 of [R05 §5.2].
- **G2 (certified verdicts, per M; safety only).** A fixed-order or protected verdict with realization `certificate` at level M states `¬OK_i` (deciding by itself only when the certificate's REJECT is at the occurrence's decision; otherwise the cell shows `unknown(no_decision_route)` until a bad witness) or `¬NR_i` (no reached occurrence decided not ready, hence `¬Bad_i`), respectively, over every finite execution of `TS(B,σ_U,M)` under A, by L-FO / L-PR.
  - **Other σ.** By L-CONT and L-TRANS, the same safety statement holds over the finite executions of σ_U∞ and σ_R1 at the same M and b; whether a transferred `¬OK_i` keeps its decision route is decided per σ (§1.10).
  - **σ_E.** **It holds for every finite real run of B (σ_E) only if M = M_impl, or if M = M_api and either the cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL) or the M_impl verdict agrees.** Otherwise it is a claim relative to the OSAL API contract.
  - **Not stated.** It states nothing about reach except through the reach axis. It states nothing about progress: permanent waits, deadlock, lost providers or spawns, horizons that never close. It states nothing about maximal executions.
  - **Checking.** Each certificate passed `kz proofcheck`, names its view (`Sk^cert`) and M, and carries its trust ledger (§4.5.1). `kz_verdict` stores per-M results.
- **G3 (observed violations).** An "observed violation" is reported only when, in the recorded trace, the state at some conjunct's LP computed from HB-ordered lifecycle LPs is outside Req, the program's own outcome code agrees (A2, A6, O4), **and** the occurrence's status in the trace is `VIOLATING` (its horizon monitor REJECTed in the trace, or `Allowed = none`) or, with Allowed unspecified, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` (EXT-20261012 §4.1). A `PENDING`, `TOLERATED` or `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrence is not an observed violation; a trace that ends while the monitor is OPEN gives `unknown(horizon)`. No claim that the contract is the "right" requirement; its provenance is shown.
- **G4 (predictive soundness relative to the trace model).** Every `predicted` witness is in Pred(π) and passed A-COMM (L-PRED); `predicted-modelled` and `predicted-extrapolated` witnesses, including those completed by horizon completion (§4.9 item 7a), are model evidence. Predicted not-ready evidence (`PENDING` in the predicted prefix) is neither a witness nor a class. Prediction is not maximal; "not predictable" is never "protected".
- **G5 (bounded model classification).** Within the closed model (the closed cone of §4.10 item 1 and the reductions in use), σ, M and b, `exh` decides the safety record (hence the class, including `no-final-status` and `ordinal-split`), the reach value and the refinements exactly, on product states and their deciding edges. It decides the progress result exactly too, except where a deadness test is undecided: censored or in a censored actor's closure, giving `unknown(bound)`, or stranded or in a stranded actor's closure, giving `unknown(unmodelled)` (L-DPOR, L-DEAD, L-MON). Statements that safety-side censoring withholds are `unknown(bound)`. `dpor` classes hold if T-JACM holds and the monitor properties used are invariant under the extended dependence of §4.10 item 3, and are labelled **unverified** until then; `dpor` sets no progress result. This is C-2 of [R05 §5.2]. Fidelity evidence: O1, O2, O6, O8; disagreements with certificates go to O10.
- **G6 (confirmation).** A `realized` verdict was reproduced on the gcc-built binary of B under the declared control, with the contract oracle evaluated on the confirmation trace, its naturalness label, and its perturbation ratio (C-3 of [R05 §5.2]; precedents RacePro, DESCRY, CTrigger [V-abs]). Realized reports contain no false alarm relative to the recorder's oracle. A σ_E `schedule-dependent` class needs natural bad and good witnesses at the **same occurrence ordinal**, in two distinct runs; natural witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)` (§1.10).
- **G7 (PCT, if D3 is approved).** Each F1 run finds a depth-d bug, relative to the gated scheduling points, with probability at least `1/(n·k^(d−1))`, with n and k measured (C-4 of [R05 §5.2]).
- **G8 (loop termination and reproducibility).** Under fixed (B, Φ, tool) the loop terminates (L-LOOP). Re-running from stored artifacts reproduces the verdict store byte for byte, because every budget is a deterministic work-unit count.
- **G9 (inconsistency surfacing).** Every contradiction between layers (O1–O10, CC) is reported and suspends or downgrades the rule or model involved; a suspension ends the loop for that Φ version. None is resolved silently.
- **G10 (transparency of trusted facts).** Every certificate and every verdict cell carries a trust ledger (§4.5.1) that lists:
  - (i) the inference steps that `kz proofcheck` re-validated;
  - (ii) the extracted facts those steps read, with the hashes of `facts.kz`, `skel.kz` and `anchors.kz`;
  - (iii) the Φ entries, `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries (A-FRESH, A-OWNER, relational guards), assumptions and lemma obligations it relies on;
  - (iv) the observation obligations that can refute an entry of (ii) or (iii), with their status and the exercised-path limit.

  The census reports the number of conjuncts with T-OBS disabled.
- **G11 (progress, bounded and model-relative)** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
  - **`no-stall-within(b)`** is issued only by `exh`. It states that no stall state for i (§1.8) is reachable in the closed cone model at σ, M within b. The statement is relative to the closed-cone rule (§4.10 item 1), the reductions in use, the censoring rule, the exclusions of §1.8 and A.
  - **`stall-witness`** is either a finite execution of the model that ends in a stall state (`model(b)`), with its kind, qualifiers and choice support, or a realized run with a wait-for fact (§4.13).
  - **`terminal-pending` is relative to the closed cone**: an actor outside the cone whose process end stays reachable could make a cone hang state terminal-pending in the whole process.
  - **Neither value is about the binary.** Deadness is preserved neither by over- nor by under-approximation (L-DEAD (b)), so neither value is a statement about the binary of B. Model stall witnesses are candidates until confirmed; only `deadlock-cycle` and `leaked-lock` witnesses that are neither idle nor terminal-pending can be confirmed by the wait-for oracle; witnesses on semaphores, queues, messages or predicates, `idle` and `terminal-pending` witnesses, and `choice-dependent` witnesses are never counted as hangs (§8.5). `no-stall-within(b)` is a statement about the model.
  - **Not modelled.** Undefined behaviour after a VIOLATING occurrence (e.g. C3's unchecked index into `AppData`) is a harm, not a transition: the model lets the process continue, so progress results after such an occurrence are relative to that continuation [I]. K-TEARDOWN instances get no progress result (`unknown(teardown_scope)`). Executions in which the use's actor instance terminates without an occurrence, or that end in a panic, are covered by neither the safety nor the progress axis (§6.4 item 34). σ_U∞ results that depend on a give-up that Φ has not declared time-based carry `tb-undeclared` (§1.8). Results that rest on L1's summary of a poll are relative to L1 (§6.2).
  - **Excluded routes.** No progress result is derived from a certificate, from prediction, from observation without a wait-for fact, or by transfer between σ, except L-DEAD (c), (d).

### 6.4 Not claimed

1. "Sound", "complete", "detects all races" or "no false positives", without the qualifiers above ([R06 §6.3 item 1]).
2. "Safe" or "no violation exists" from the absence of an alarm; from a bounded exploration beyond its bound; from prediction or prefilter silence; or from a model whose obligations failed.
3. **"Timing-protected in σ_E"** or any σ_E class from the absence of bad runs. Only "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown" ([X1-M11]).
4. "Fixed-order" or "deterministic" from repeated failing runs; only from a σ- and M-stated certificate or exhaustive bounded exploration ([R06 S9]).
5. "Fixed-order" from a trace-local robust chain (VC_R) without a static certificate ([J1] §8).
6. Universal verdicts from the h-cut exploration view without h in b ([X1-C1]).
7. σ_E validity of an M_api-only verdict whose cone is M_impl-relevant ([X1-C2]).
8. "Trace-sound" for a predicted witness that failed A-COMM ([X1-M4]).
9. "Exact within b" for `dpor` while T-JACM is unverified; "one execution per Mazurkiewicz class" at all ([X1-M9]).
10. A σ_E frequency or class from a σ_U prediction or model witness; a σ_E class from held, gated or timeout-exercised runs (only `σ_E(held)`); any priority-based verdict carried between σ_R1 and σ_E ([R06 §6.3 item 14]).
11. "No transfer across σ" (too weak), or σ_U-to-σ_E frequency transfer (too strong).
12. "R2 is a race": its evidence and proof shape make it a fixed-order dependency error ([R06 S6]). Nor that R2's certificate comes from a generic template alone: it uses the generic K-ID contract **and** the framework rule R19 (§7.2 refinement accounting, [X1-M8]).
13. "C3 or C5 reproduced on v7.0.1": their buggy code is absent there ([R06 §6.3 item 9]).
14. "Existing tools fail on C1–R2": every external process-level tool is `NOT_RUN`; NOT_RUN is never a false negative ([R06 S5]).
15. "MLIR improves detection or precision", unless G-MLIR shows a measured win.
16. Generality from the five design cases; "new bugs" for derived, role-derived, seeded, mutant or configuration-changed tests ([R06 §6.3 items 7–8]).
17. Flight-representative timing or overhead from Linux `SCHED_OTHER` in permissive mode ([R06 §6.3 item 10]); RT verdicts for RTEMS/VxWorks before their profiles exist.
18. Novelty of HB/vector clocks, DPOR, PCT, lockset, delay injection, predictive analysis, SMT encodings, points-to analysis, **key-conflict lock reversal** (RVPredict/GPredict), **late-registration loss** (InitRacer) or **predicted use-before-init** (ExceptioNULL) or **framework typestate** (predictive typestate checking, Joshi & Sen; Lifestate) as mechanisms. Novelty is claimed only for the combination around the cFS semantics; the novelty of the G3 formalization is an open literature check ([R04 §8 item 5]; [X1-M12]).
19. Anything about F Prime or other frameworks beyond hypothesis H-F (§7.6).
20. Memory-model correctness of the ad hoc synchronization sites (assumed, A4).
21. Any R2 count as a Kairos result before Kairos's own recorder reproduces the per-use set ([R06 §6.3 item 13]); "24" for any basis other than one with ENV's version table.
22. "Lost packets" for rejected event requests: EVS rejects them before any packet is built ([R06 §6.3 item 15]).
23. OSAL F1 (lock leak), F7 (silent task loss), the timebase handshake loss or either App-record window as bugs before confirmation runs; derived confirmations are labelled derived.
24. "First process-race detector" ([R06 §6.3 item 11]).
25. A recall figure for real ordering bugs in general: the recall protocol measures detection of history-derived program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, labelled synthetic (§8.5).
26. Any progress, termination, deadlock-freedom or reachability claim from a certificate. A certified protected or fixed-order verdict says nothing about executions that do not reach the use; about permanent waits, deadlocks, lost providers or lost spawns, before, inside or after the use; or about whether an Allowed horizon closes (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
27. Containment of maximal executions. `Max(σ_U∞) ⊆ Max(σ_U)` is false (LP-TW). Real runs are finite executions, not maximal ones. σ_R1 maximal containment is not used (EXT-20261012 §4.2).
28. Transfer across σ of any of the following: reach; witnesses, hence `tolerated`, `schedule-dependent` and `ordinal-split`; stall witnesses (except deadlock cycles and leaked locks, L-DEAD (c), (d)); any progress result. Also not claimed: any σ_E progress statement obtained by transfer, any bounded (b ≠ ∞) statement transferred to σ_E, and any bounded σ_R1 statement transferred from a σ_U model whose cone the σ_R1 model does not contain.
29. "No stall", "deadlock-free" or "live" outside `exh` within b, and even there only about the closed cone model. Nothing about livelock, starvation or divergence: the progress notion is deadness only. Under σ_R1, whose deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation, priority starvation never makes the starved actor itself dead, but an actor that waits on a starved giver can be dead under unbounded σ_R1; bounded σ_R1 models avoid this only by truncation at h.
30. That an `ordinal-split` instance depends on the schedule. Schedule dependence is claimed only for `schedule-dependent`, whose two witnesses concern the same occurrence ordinal. Once nodes always satisfy this.
31. "Independent verification" of a certificate in any sense stronger than this: `kz proofcheck` re-validated its inference steps relative to the extracted facts, the skeleton, the closure lists, Φ and `libkz-core`, all of which it trusts, and the steps marked "via T5" also used the shared evaluator or rule engine (EXT-20261012 §4.3).
32. Any progress statement for K-TEARDOWN instances (`unknown(teardown_scope)`).
33. That a model stall witness of kind `other`, or one qualified `idle`, `terminal-pending` or `choice-dependent`, is a hang of the binary; that a refinement exists without the explorer.
34. Anything about executions in which the use's actor instance, or an actor on its spawn chain, terminates without an occurrence (a task lost to F7 after `OS_TaskPrepare` fails, `osapi-task.c:128–137`; the timebase thread's silent return, `osapi-timebase.c:386–390`), or that end in a panic. They are covered by neither the safety nor the progress axis. Their only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence that led to them, with the harm of its consequence chain (`task_never_runs` for a lost task, `panic` for a core-sync failure, `process_abort` for a restart request, §3.3); a σ_U panic reached through a spontaneous timeout has no not-ready root and leaves no record (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
35. That every execution of a `fixed-order` instance reaches a decision: a decision route says only that a bad witness exists in the cell, or that not-ready occurrences are bad at their decision. That `terminal-pending`, computed on the closed cone, holds for the whole process.
36. That σ_U∞ is free of give-ups that Φ has not declared time-based (HS's application monitor, `hs_monitors.c:107–160`; TIME's flywheel entry, `cfe_time_tone.c:1172–1186`): results that depend on them carry `tb-undeclared`. Anything about occurrences decided after a process-terminal step, which have no status. That a result resting on L1's summary of a poll holds with L1 switched off.
37. That specification-based or GPredict-style techniques cannot express Allowed outcomes, finite retries or bounded horizons. A same-information re-implementation's result is a result of that re-implementation as built, and an ablation that removes an element from Kairos says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3, §8.4; EXT-20261012 §5.1).
38. What certificates or prediction add, from detection (alarm yes/no) alone. A plain state monitor given the same Req is expected to alarm on every realized R2 occurrence [I]; the value of certificates and prediction is assessed only under classification and evidence (§8.3, §8.5; EXT-20261012 §5.2).
39. Blindness for any item without an exposure and freeze record that shows no exposure before its governing freeze; blindness because expected verdicts come from fix texts; "blind evaluation" from rater masking alone (§7.2, §7.5 item 5; EXT-20261012 §5.3).
