40. Intervals over occurrences, instances, mutants or runs as statements about independent defects or causes; any count of dynamic occurrences as a sample size; a count pooled across binaries, observation methods or pool keys (§8.2, §8.7; EXT-20261012 §5.4).
41. Class accuracy without a class label. R2 has no reference class statement (its fixed-order reading rests on code reading, `cases/R2.md` L45). C1, C2, C3 and C5 have reference statements that they are races (issue texts, the 6.4.2 VDD, CF's v7.0.1 code comment, C1's fix commit; §8.2), and some recall fix texts say "race condition" (§8.5 items 2–3); how such a statement maps to the per-(σ, M) classes is not fixed by any reference, so until PI decision P-CLASSGT no class label exists. Agreement with a pre-registered expectation, and agreement or disagreement with a reference statement under a named mapping option, are reported as such, never as accuracy (§8.5).
42. "Reproduced" for C1, C2, C3 or C5 in the default configuration of v7.0.1: C1's lookup is latent (both semaphore names are empty, `cf_def_config.c:60, 78`), C2's apps are private (role-derived pair), and C3's and C5's buggy code is absent (item 13).
43. A result on one design case as evidence for another, or the five design cases as five independent samples: C3 and R2 share a resource family, C3 and C5 share one fix release, C5 and the App-record windows share a shape, and C1, C5 and R2 share the reserve-then-finalize shape ([LED §3.6], all hypotheses) at different layers (C1: OSAL object table, M_impl; C5 and R2: ES app record) (§8.2). In particular, an S3a result on R2's template in the TIME/TBL contexts at v7.0.1 is not a result on C3, a result on the App-record window A.3 is not a result on C5, and C1's `inactive(B)` proof in the default B and the K-PHASE census of the v7.0.1 bundle are generic-layer results on v7.0.1 counterparts, not results on C1 or C2 (§9.3, per-case paths and controls).

---

## 7. Openness

### 7.1 Discovery sources (the union)

Contracts are written per **API and resource kind**, never per case, and the census instantiates them at every context-qualified site in cFE core and the 15 bundled apps. Candidates come from the union of these sources ([J1-13]):

| # | Source | Mechanism | Output | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Static census | every Φ template at every matching context-qualified site; key resolution by value analysis, header-MsgId analysis and table decoding | instances with a class or `unknown(reason)` | [A], [C] |
| 2 | Outcome mining | every not-ready outcome code observed at the API boundary (vocabulary from the extracted `\retval`/`@retval` lists) creates an instance even when no template names the API | `template: none` instances, triaged | [B §4.1] |
| 3 | Key-pair enumeration | for every resource key with lifecycle transitions and uses in different actors, the pairs Φ marks as conflicting | candidates | [B §4.1] |
| 4 | Retry/timeout-loop-derived contracts | every loop recognized by the poll/retry pattern around an operation implies a contract for it, with `Allowed = retry_until_ready(effective bound)`; provenance kind `code_pattern`, side `rule` (§1.6: no text states the acceptance; reported as rule-backed) | templates | [C §7.1] |
| 5 | Lifecycle symmetry | every kind with create and delete operations automatically gets K-REG, K-LIFE and K-GEN | templates | [C §7.1] |
| 6 | Context-lookup symmetry | every caller of a context-resolving helper (`GetAppRecordByContext`, `GetTaskRecordByContext`) gets a K-SELF instance with its own not-ready behaviour (§3.3) | templates | [X2-02] |
| 7 | API documentation | every `\par Assumptions` block (138 in cFE core API headers) is an annotation candidate; a human turns it into a Φ entry and the effort is counted. Examples: `cfe_evs.h:168–172` (K-ID); `cfe_es.h:400–403` (K-PHASE); `cfe_sb.h:449–464` (buffer lifetime) | templates | [R06 §5.3] |
| 8 | Code admissions | comments stating an ordering assumption or an acknowledged race: CF "start up race condition" (`cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`); CF TOCTOU (`cf_cfdp_s.c:447–463`); the OSAL lock invariant (`osapi-idmap.c:384–385`); the PSP exception-ring race (`cfe_psp_exception.c:72–86`); ES's "informational" wake (`cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:254–257`); the EVS guard (`cfe_evs_utils.c:613–623`); the TBL lock rationale (`cfe_tbl_api.c:145–149`); TIME's unregistration comment (`cfe_time_tone.c:1322–1325`); the PSP restart comment "if problems occur (e.g. deadlock) eventually this timeout will expire" (`cfe_psp_support.c:91–98`) | templates | [A §7.1], [C §7.1] |
| 9 | Belief deviance | for each API, the fraction of call sites that test its status; deviants are ranked (e.g. `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, `cfe_tbl_task.c:196` ignore `GetAppID`'s status). Engler et al. SOSP 2001 [V-me]. **A ranking only, never a verdict** | probe priorities | all |
| 10 | Always-one-order pairs | pairs observed in only one order across runs become flip candidates for prediction (CloudRaid ESEC/FSE 2018 [V-abs]) | candidates | [B §7.2] |
| 11 | Per-app profiling under variant bases | one-app-at-a-time runs collect potential publish → use communications independent of boot order (Snowboard SOSP 2021 [V-abs]); candidates only; also a cross-check of static publisher sets | candidates | [A §7.3] |
| 12 | Watchpoint discovery | during a publication window, a write watchpoint on the field records stores by other actors (DataCollider OSDI 2010 [V-abs]); read discovery only as a declared experiment (RW watchpoints cost ≈34 µs per read) | candidates | [B §7.2] |
| 13 | Configuration variants | new bases B′ (§7.4) | per-B′ results | all |
| 14 | Held-out protocol | §7.5 | held-out results | [R06 §5.6] |

Program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs (§8.5) are an **evaluation** instrument, not a discovery source: they measure what the union of sources misses.

### 7.2 Contract governance and anti-circularity

- **Two readiness oracles.** Φ-declared effects and bindings (the working model) and framework validity predicates (an independent oracle: support locations statically, outcome codes dynamically). Agreement is checked by O7 statically and O4 dynamically ([J1-12]; [J1 X9]).
- **Provenance per Φ entry.** Kind (`api_doc`, `requirement`, `code_admission`, `code_pattern`, `issue`, `researcher`), side (`supplier`, `caller`, `rule`, §1.6), `path:line` or requirement ID, sha256 of the cited text, author, authoring time, and which design cases the author had inspected for that API ([R06 §5.3 item 4]).
- **App-profile layer** (fixes [X2-13]). App-private protocols (e.g. FM's parent–child queue) are never encoded in engines. If they are modelled at all, they go into `profiles/apps/<app>/*.kzp` with the same provenance and authoring record, and their authoring cost is reported separately from the framework profile. Without such an entry, instances that depend on them are `unknown(unmodelled)`.
- **Freezes by hash** ([J3-M11]; extended by EXT-20261012 §5.3):
  - **F1**: Φ, extractor, engines, calibration table, lints, **all comparator implementations and their same-information encodings with their authoring records** (§8.3), **the evaluation criteria** (the scoring rules and comparison-record fields of §8.5, the class-label rule (P-CLASSGT: the mapping of reference class statements to classes, or `unspecified_by_reference` while it is undecided), the unit rule of §8.2 and the statistical assumptions of §8.7), **and the pre-registered expectation sheet**: per development case, basis, (σ, M, b) cell and instance, the expected class, refinements, progress value and outcome label, and, where a reference makes a class statement, that statement with its citation; frozen before the first evaluation run on the development cases and, if the PI adopts P-F1GATE, not before D13, D22 and P-C2 are each decided or declined with the consequence recorded (§9.3);
  - **F2**: frozen before any held-out item is opened; **the mutation operators, the history query (keywords, matched field and case, and date cut), the expected-verdict rule, the equivalent-mutant exclusion rule** (§8.5) **and the exposure record of every held-out and recall item** (§7.5 item 5) are frozen before F2;
  - **post-freeze change log**: every rule, Φ entry, exception, encoding, scoring or expectation-sheet change after F1 is logged with its time, its reason, the item that prompted it and the verdicts it changes. Each item has a **governing freeze**: F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items. A change after an item's governing freeze marks it `post-freeze-adjusted`, and its changed verdicts are reported apart, with one exception: a Φ, rule or exception refinement between F1 and F2 that touches a development item is the planned `oracle refinement` of "Template-only first" below, labelled and reported apart as that line says. A change between F1 and F2 does not mark held-out or recall items, which F2 freezes with the change. A change to the evaluation criteria, an encoding or the expectation sheet after F1 is never an `oracle refinement`.
- **Template-only first.** The first evaluation run uses no case-specific entry. Later refinements are labelled `oracle refinement`, and the verdicts they change are reported separately ([R06 T2]).
- **Refinement accounting names framework rules** (fixes [X1-M8]). Every verdict lists the Φ entries its proof used, split into three groups: generic contracts, generic machinery, and **framework rules authored with a case known**. R2's certificate is reported as "generic contract K-ID + framework rule R19 (CORE-SERIAL)". R19 binds the loop, its stratum and its terminal escape. Its predicate is extracted from the code, and the checker re-derives it from the extractor's serialized exit trees, which are extracted facts (trust ledger part (ii)).
- **Ground truth versus blindness** (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.3). A fix or issue text is an external ground-truth source: it gives an item's instance, expected direction and whether the expectation concerns readiness or progress (§8.5 item 3). It does not make the item blind. Blindness is a property of an item relative to the people who author Φ, engines, comparator encodings and scoring rules, and to the raters; it is claimed only where the exposure and freeze record (§7.5 item 5) shows that none of them saw the case, the fix or the expected verdict before the freeze that governs the item. The authors know R2, C1, C2, C3, C5 and both App-record windows, so every development-set run is non-blind; there, anti-circularity rests on header provenance, code-extracted predicates and the template-only-first rule. Items already named in this specification or in the case records are non-blind: the commits of §8.5 item 2 (`58abd38d` is also in the C5 case record), the mutant of the operator modelled on C1's buggy revision at C1's own site, the C1-derived table variant of §7.4, and the R2 history items named as controls in §8.2 and §9.3 (`b9bec567^`/`b9bec567`, `v7.0.0`, PR #2820). Rater masking (§8.5) is assessor masking, not dataset blinding.
- **Authoring effort** (person-hours, number of entries, lines of Φ, per layer) is measured and reported ([R06 R4]).
- **Lints** ([A §9.3]): constants in tool code only as `KZ_REF(value, "path:line")`; no case identifiers or case-specific symbols (e.g. `CFE_SB_Global`, `sem_name`, `C1`) in engine code outside `profiles/` and `tests/`.

### 7.3 Candidates beyond the five cases

Every row comes from a generic template applied by the census, not from case knowledge. Each stays a **candidate** until the loop produces evidence. A confirmation that needs a constructed schedule is labelled derived. All expected verdicts are [I].

| Candidate | Template | Source facts | Expected first verdict [I] | Path to evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Core-task App-record window** (EVS, ES, SB, TBL, TIME, and their children created in TaskInit, before `cfe_es_start.c:818`) | K-SELF.es (all seven callers), K-SPAWN.app-record | §3.3; [J1 §1.1]; [LED §3.4] | open: O-ordered (ES lock) → prediction/explorer; `schedule-dependent(σ_U, M_api and M_impl)` and also under σ_U∞ (only a 50 ms yield separates them; no refinement). Progress under σ_U∞, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`): a skipped raise leaves the startup thread dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` and the created core tasks dead in their `CORE_READY` polls; every later instance with a hang state there carries timeout-masked-liveness and is chained to this instance, which carries `tml-root`. σ_R1[`P_sched^posix`]: expected protected within b by `exh` on one CPU [I] (the startup thread outranks the tasks; no certificate or transfer route exists there); σ_E: no bad witness expected in natural runs, so "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown" | lock-reversing prediction from default boots; derived F0 hold between `:810` returning and `:818` (D16) |
| **SB/TBL identity never published** (consequence of the window; status ignored at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, `cfe_tbl_task.c:196`) | K-ID with shape ABSENT; chain root = the window instance | §0.5 | the post-publication SB-AppId uses (211–229 ordered per boot, [R2c §7]) and TBL identity uses: no protected certificate; schedule-dependent(σ_U), ABSENT, chained | as above; the K-ID oracle (`ILLEGAL_APP_ID` on every later SB event) |
| Script-app App-record window; `GetAppIDByName` success with `0xFFFFFFFF`; `RunLoop` false → zombie | K-SELF.es, K-REG.es | §3.3 | open: O-ordered → prediction; schedule-dependent(σ_U, σ_U∞); harms per chain | App. A.3 |
| OSAL lookup of a RESERVED record leaks the type lock (F1) | K-LOCKBAL (M_impl, by-name path) + K-REG.osal | `osapi-idmap.c:384–385` vs `:401–404` | implementation-defect candidate under M_impl, if reachable. Pairs where creation is certified earlier are protected as safety statements; their progress is open. The leaked lock shows up as confirmable `leaked-lock` stall witnesses, one wait-for root (the type lock), for every instance whose LP lies behind it: the creator's later uses (it is dead in `FinalizeNew`) and later operations on that object type that take the type lock (create, delete, by-name lookup, by-ID lookups in GLOBAL or EXCLUSIVE mode, and object iteration, `OS_ForEachObject*`, which takes every type's lock in GLOBAL mode, `osapi-idmap.c:1246–1256, 1360–1367`; for countsems not `Give`, `Take` or `TimedWait`, which use `OS_LOCK_MODE_NONE`, `osapi-countsem.c:147, 168, 189`), including the leaker's own re-entry (an instance of its own where the re-entry is another calling context) and `ES_BG_TASK` when it cleans up the leaker's exited app (`CFE_ES_CleanupTaskResources` → `OS_ForEachObject`, `cfe_es_apps.c:1565, 1759`), after which no app is cleaned up, restarted or reloaded. They are chained to the leaking lookup's not-ready occurrence [I] | explorer (`exh`, progress); declared-experiment hold + wait-for oracle (App. A.4); litmus LP-LEAK |
| Silent task loss after the OSAL bounded wait (F7) | K-SPAWN.osal (M_impl) | `osapi-idmap.c:475–480`; `os-impl-idmap.c:138–170` | schedule-dependent under σ_U; under σ_U∞ still reachable through four broadcast wakes from unrelated task-table unlocks [I] — not timeout-dependent | hold the creator between thread creation and finalize until the child's preparation fails |
| **Timebase callback thread loses its handshake** | K-SPAWN.osal (M_impl) | `osapi-timebase.c:383–390` | open. Harm `task_never_runs` of the K-SPAWN.osal occurrence: a safety result, since the self-lookup fails (with a specified `appreq`; until then NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with that consequence, §1.6, PI item QT-9). Consequence on the progress axis: `idle` pre-use stalls of `Local1HzTask`'s uses (`cfe_time_tone.c:1266`, untimed take at the loop head) and of SCH_LAB's loop (`sch_lab_app.c:98`), chained to this occurrence by progress chains [I]. The App. A.7 lookups are not affected | hold `OS_TimeBaseCreate`'s creator inside the RESERVED window (declared experiment) |
| Cancellation while a program lock is held | K-LOCK.cancel | `cfe_es_api.c:1348, 1383`; [R01 §2.4] | open; needs a delete input (B′); not hidden by L3 (§4.10) | `B_restart` family |
| SB pipe name visible before the descriptor commits | K-REG.sb-pipe-name | `cfe_sb_api.c:153, 163, 190–194, 802–839` | open where a by-name lookup exists | observation; prediction |
| SB pipe deleted between route snapshot and put | K-LIFE.sb-put | `cfe_sb_priv.c:1195–1200` | open; needs a delete input | `B_restart`; `InternalErrorCounter` |
| TBL owner load vs ground load claim | K-ATOM.tbl-load | `cfe_tbl_api.c:305–309`; `cfe_tbl_task_cmds.c:423–431` | `inactive` in the default B (message provenance) | input variant B′ |

| Post-OPERATIONAL burst: SB's subscription replay (triggered by SBN's commands) vs TO_LAB's 37 subscriptions | K-MODE + K-CAP + K-CAP.msglim | §3.9; [R03 §5.2]; [M] `SBNSubPipe` overflowed in 5 of 6 M1 runs ([BM §3.3]) | `schedule-dependent(σ_E)` if, for some put ordinal, a natural run delivers that put and another overflows it; one overflowing run alone gives `ordinal-split(σ_E)`; LOSS-UNDER-ORDER | uncontrolled runs give σ_E witnesses; App. A.5 |

| K-PHASE: CF's retry window vs the soft startup barriers | K-PHASE (consumer side, level-indexed) + K-REG.osal | [R03 §2.6]; `cfe_es_start.c:210–228` | in a C1-derived B′: timeout-dependent if `exh` shows `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` (the violation needs a soft-barrier timeout or retry exhaustion) and the use is reached under σ_U∞; not if a TB-free bad path exists (at M_impl `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`; a failed provider whose STOPPED store satisfies a barrier, `cfe_es_start.c:882`); without the explorer only schedule-dependent | explorer under σ_U vs σ_U∞ |

**The candidates and the five design cases** [I] (EXT-20261012 §7; ADR-044). No candidate above is a design case, and no result on a candidate is evidence on a case. On the ENV basis, however, several candidates exercise, before any case basis exists, a mechanism that a case needs on its own basis, so the mechanism is tested there first; each case's own test stays on its path in §9.3, per-case paths and controls:

| Mechanism | Case that needs it on its own basis | ENV candidate that exercises it first (not case evidence) |
|---|---|---|
| lock-reversing prediction with A-COMM labels; schedule dependence from a same-ordinal pair; held confirmation (S5, S6) | C1 (D22); C3 and C5 (D13) | both App-record windows (App. A.2, A.3): prediction from default boots, holds under D16 |
| the explorer's bounded classes, `unreached(b)` and the kill criterion (S7) | C1, C2, C3, C5 | the App-record windows and `SBNSubPipe` (§4.10 item 9; the two C1-derived cones need D22) |
| an M_api/M_impl disagreement, K-LOCKBAL and `leaked-lock` stall witnesses | C1 at M_impl | the OSAL lock-leak row (F1) above |
| a PROTECTED certificate | no issue instance of the five; conditionally EVS's own-identity context in C3's 6.4.1 census (D13) and `58abd38d^` on the A.3 line (P-RGIT) | App. A.7's timebase lookups; RC10 and RC11 (§3.15) |
| `inactive(B)` with a proof | C1 in the default B (configuration closure while C1 is latent) | `K-ATOM.tbl-load`; the HS rows above with default tables |
| Allowed other than `none` | C1 (`retry_until_ready`); C5 (a) if Φ_6.4.x authors its propagation entry (Q16) | TO_LAB's `defer_by_design` (App. A.1 step 13); EVS's own-ID guard (`documented_drop`, §3.15 RC6) |
| barrier semantics, K-PHASE and the app-profile layer | C2 (P-C2) | the in-tree K-PHASE deviations of §3.6 (SBN, CS; development-set items found with C2 known, which the census must rediscover) |
| natural runs that may contain both outcomes at one ordinal | none of the five | `SBNSubPipe` (App. A.5) |

Mechanisms that no ENV candidate exercises are tested only on a case basis, and until then only by the litmus programs of App. A.8, which are evidence about the definitions, not about the cases: the plain reordering of an HB-unordered pair with extrapolation (C2, C1); σ_U∞ as a positive differentiator (`timeout-dependent`: C1, and C2 only if its provider's late init cannot fail, Q22); σ_R1 decided by a cited `P_sched` (C3 at 6.4.1); symbolic keys with ordinals per key (C1); merged-fix pre/post pairs (C1, C3, C5; C2's 2 × 2); version transfer (H-V, §7.6: C3, C5); and the wrong-identity adequacy gaps (C3, C5). Kinds that no design case and no candidate covers enter through the census and the held-out sources, and are reported in the openness indicators (§7.7).

### 7.4 Configuration variants and scenario families

The dependency graph is configuration-driven: six apps subscribe from tables, several take names or symbols from tables, and the schedule decides who wakes when ([R03 §7]). Each variant is a new basis with its own manifest and provenance status, never pooled with the default B. Nothing found only in a variant is a "new bug" of the default configuration (D18):

- tables that activate latent paths (CF `sem_name` with a provider, public-code-derived from nasa/bp [LED §1a]; C1's B′ is decided under D22; LC watchpoints/actionpoints; MD dwell symbols; HS AMT/EMT/XCT; CS tables);
- alternate schedule tables, including the wakeups absent from the default schedule ([R03 §6.2]);
- startup-script order; `MISSION_CORE_MODULES` order (a configuration-change experiment, never schedule evidence; the mechanism is documented, "Missions may add/remove/replace components in this list as needed. For the CFE core, the order of this list is the order in which they will be initialized", `cfe/cmake/mission_defaults.cmake` L25–L26, generated by `cmake/target/CMakeLists.txt` L55–L70; no reference gives an order with `sb` before `es` and `evs`; the only order with public precedent is the **task** order EVS, SB, ES, TIME, TBL before `b9bec567`, which corrects R2's ES half only; the precedent covers the task order only, because one v7.0.1 list drives both the EarlyInit phase and the TaskMain phase (`cfe_es_start.c:751`, `:775`) and ES's entry carries the CDS EarlyInit (`cfe_es_objtab.c`), so a transplant also sets an EarlyInit order (ES's CDS EarlyInit then follows EVS's and SB's) and the positions of `config`, `fs`, `osal`, `psp`, `msg`, `sbr` and `resourceid`, which are `unspecified_by_reference` and set by the PI as recorded deviations under D18; P-R2CTRL);
- the cpu2 target present in the build tree;
- effective queue depth: `msg_max` (D19), and **euid** (running as root removes the truncation, §1.3, so any root-run arm is a new B′);
- **reset type**: POWERON vs PROCESSOR with a preserved CDS, a volatile startup script on `/ram`, and restored critical tables (run sequences);
- inputs: restart/reload/delete (`B_restart`), table load/validate/activate, SB pipe info, the stop command (`B_stop`, K-TEARDOWN).

### 7.5 Held-out protocol

Adopted from [R06 §5.6]:
1. **Freeze F2** (§7.2) before any held-out item is opened.
2. **Held-out sources**, each counted separately and never added to "new real bugs"; each item carries the label of item 5:
   - (a) other cFS revisions (version transfer; the profile is re-bound by AST pattern); the 6.4.x revisions on which C3 and C5 are analysed are development bases, not held-out;
   - (b) other configurations (cpu2; table variants not derived from a design case); the C1-derived `sem_name` variant of §7.4 is a development basis, not held-out;
   - (c) issues mined with a query fixed before F2 (keywords and date cut), excluding C1, C2, C3, C5 and R2; an issue or fix already named in this specification or in the case records is labelled non-blind, and whether it is also excluded is part of D8 (item 4);
   - (d) **the recall protocol's program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs** (§8.5), labelled synthetic; a mutant of an operator modelled on a design case, at that case's site, and items from fixes named during design are non-blind;
   - (e) in-code admissions not used during design; an admission cited by a Φ provenance record was used.
3. Per-class metrics with denominators (§8.5); template-only vs refined on the five design cases.
4. Who runs the issue and history queries, and when relative to F2, is PI decision D8. Also part of D8, for both queries alike: whether they exclude the fixes of the design cases that lie in the queried histories (CF #184's `833fdbb`, which a keyword query over the CF history selects; cFE #198's barrier commits; the 6.4.2 fixes of cFE #72 and #73 predate the git import, [LED] C5 row) and the items already named in this specification or in the case records. Until D8 is decided, such items are kept and labelled non-blind (item 5).
5. **Exposure and freeze record** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.3). For every development, held-out and recall item, an append-only record frozen with F2 gives:
   - the item, its kind and its source;
   - for each person who authors Φ, engines, comparator encodings or scoring rules, and for each rater: whether and when they saw the case, its fix and its expected verdict, and how (case record, design document, search, review);
   - when the item was assigned to its evaluation set, and by whom;
   - the F1 and F2 hashes and times, and the hash of the pre-registered expectation sheet (§7.2);
   - its governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items) and the post-freeze changes that touched it (§7.2).

   An item is labelled `blind` only if the record shows no exposure of any of these people before its governing freeze. Otherwise it is `non-blind(case | fix | expected)`. `unknown-exposure` is treated as non-blind. An item touched by a change after its governing freeze, other than a labelled `oracle refinement` of a development item between F1 and F2, is `post-freeze-adjusted` and reported apart (§7.2). Results are reported per label, and no blind claim is made before the record exists.

### 7.6 Generalization hypotheses (stated, not claimed)

- **H-V (cFS versions).** Only Φ and the scheduling profile `P_sched` are version- and backend-specific; rules carry version guards. Test: with Φ_6.4.x, the 6.4.x backend's cited `P_sched` and unchanged engines, C3 at 6.4.1 should give, for each of its two chained instances (the K-ID use at TIME's `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` event and the use of EVS's own AppID in TIME's context), fixed-order under σ_R1 by `exh` only (S7) and schedule-dependent under σ_U, with Allowed `unspecified_by_reference` until Φ_6.4.x cites 6.4.1 references, and with the misattribution branch READY, which K-ID cannot express (an adequacy gap of the contract, not an engine change), and C5 at 6.4.1 as schedule-dependent ([R06 §4 T5]; needs D13). If an engine change is needed, H-V is refuted in its strong form. Without a cited `P_sched` for that backend, no σ_R1 claim is made.
- **H-C (configurations).** Verdicts change only through B. Tested by §7.4.
- **H-F (other flight frameworks).** A profile for another framework (e.g. F Prime) would need actor classes, kinds, operations with bindings and LPs, rules, contracts and configuration readers. F Prime's mechanisms were **not examined** in R01–R07, so nothing is claimed.

### 7.7 Openness indicators reported

Following [R06 §5.7]: contract kinds covered vs kinds found in the census, with every kind that has no authored template reported as not authored (§9.3, expansion rule); the share of instances discovered automatically (sources 1–6, 10–12) vs authored; the share of verdicts that needed a case-specific refinement or a framework rule authored with a case known; held-out and mutant results kept apart from design-case results, and results on v7.0.1 counterparts kept apart from the results on the cases they resemble (§9.3); instances per variant B′, kept apart from the default B; recall per layer (§8.5).

---

## 8. Evaluation plan

### 8.1 Research questions

- **RQ1 Openness.** How many contract instances exist in cFE core and the 15 apps under the default B and its variants, per kind and RC? What share is decided by certificate, observation, prediction, exploration and confirmation, per (σ, M)? What share stays unknown, and why? What are the concrete/symbolic/⊤ key shares (esp. transmit sites)?
- **RQ2 Development cases.** With the template-only profile, what verdict records does Kairos produce, reported per case on its own phenomenon as detection, classification (classes and refinements per (σ, M, b), and the progress result) and evidence (§8.5), and which change with refinements? No case's result stands for another's (§8.2). R2 at v7.0.1; C1 in a derived configuration (D22); C2 role-derived (P-C2); C3 (two chained instances; the misattribution recorded as a K-ID adequacy gap) and C5 (three instances) at 6.4.1, with Φ_6.4.x outcome maps, bindings and Allowed references (if D13 is approved); the two App-record windows (C5-shaped residuals at v7.0.1, derived). Each case is evaluated on its own basis against its control (§8.2 per-case subjects; §9.3, per-case paths and controls); a case whose basis is not decided at F1 is reported as not evaluated at tool level, with the recorded reason (P-F1GATE), and never through its v7.0.1 counterpart.
- **RQ3 Held-out generalization.** On the frozen tool (F2), what does Kairos find in sources (a)–(e), with what sampled triaged precision (per instance, with the number of distinct causes among the Reported instances), realized share and unknown share, reported per blind label (§7.5 item 5)?
- **RQ4 Model fidelity.** O2 conformance on every trace and O8 on the sample; obligation failures per iteration; checker pass rate and certificate-mutation kill rate; T-OBS disablement, A-TYPE listing and `DECLARED_TRUSTED` counts; O9 refutations; anchor-audit coverage; closure resolution per rule; havoc and local-choice counts; summary-fallback counts ([J1-14]).
- **RQ5 Measured phenomena** ([A §8.1]; [J2-12]). Does Kairos classify the order-dependent losses measured in the default bundle (queue-full drops 4–49 per boot; `NoSubscribersCounter` 15–26; overflows of `SBNSubPipe`, `TO_LAB_TLM_PIPE` and `DS_CMD_PIPE`, [BM §0, §3.3]) at both capacity levels (queue depth and MsgLim)? Does it predict which pipes can overflow? Oracles: put outcomes and the four SB counters; `PeakQueueDepth` only as SB-accounted depth. Label: LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm unspecified (caller level); the framework-level judgement (`fw_doc`, `fw_conf`) and the observed loss counts are reported beside it (§1.6 "Three judgements").
- **RQ6 Value of each layer and of the loop**, scored on labelled units (§8.2, §8.4).
- **RQ7 Cost and perturbation.** The six costs; uninstrumented-observable shifts; per-witness perturbation ratios (§8.6).
- **RQ8 Comparison** (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.1–§5.2). On the same labelled units, what do original tools (where runnable), re-implementations of prior techniques given the same property information or their own information, and reference points report, as detection, classification and evidence, each compared separately (§8.3)? Ablations answer RQ6, never RQ8.
- **RQ9 Recall.** On pre-registered program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, what share does each layer detect, leave unknown (by reason), or miss; of the detected, what share is class-correct where a class label exists (P-CLASSGT); and how many certificates are contradicted (soundness failures)? Reported per mutant and per originating fix and cause, and per blind label (§8.5, §8.7).

### 8.2 Subjects

- **Development set** (labels per [LED], [R06 S10]): R2 (current code; observed with gdb and with the retired recorder in three run groups that are never pooled, [R2c §8]; one cause, 3 violating static sites of the 39 SB sites that pass SB's AppId, 6 task × function contexts, 12 call strings, and 24 occurrences per boot, ES 20, EVS 4, in the basis with ENV's version table, 11 with a `NULL` table, [R2g §6]); C1 derived (public-code-derived; latent in the default B; its basis is D22); C2 role-derived (a constructed app pair, synthetic control; P-C2); C3 and C5 at the 6.4.1 tarballs on the shared derived 6.4.x platform ([LED] X3; D13; whether that basis is placed before F1, or C3 and C5 are recorded as post-freeze by design, is D13 (c)). **Dependence between cases**: C3 and R2 share a resource family ([LED §3.1]); C3's and C5's fixes are in the same 6.4.1 → 6.4.2 release diff (Trac #40–#42, [LED §3.3]), so their pre/post-fix pairs share one fix history; C5 and the two App-record windows share the K-SELF.es shape; C1, C5 and R2 share the reserve-then-finalize shape ([LED §3.6], all hypotheses) at different layers (C1: OSAL object table, M_impl; C5 and R2: ES app record); the operator "empty a retry bound" is modelled on C1 (§8.5). None of these is counted as independent. **R2 is the only natural, reproduced case on the target revision.** It has no reference class statement: its fixed-order reading rests on code reading (`cases/R2.md` L45) and on three-of-three observations per run group, which do not prove it (§6.4 item 4). **C1, C2, C3 and C5 have reference statements that they are races**: CF #184's title and body, CF's v7.0.1 comment at `cf_cfdp.c:1273` and the fix commit `833fdbb` (C1); cFE #198's body (C2); cFE #73's and #72's titles and the 6.4.2 VDD's "startup race conditions … in cFE build 6.4.1" (C3, C5) ([LED]; the case records). How such a statement maps to the per-(σ, M) classes is not fixed by any reference; it is PI decision P-CLASSGT. Until then, classification on the development set is reported against the pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1 (§7.2), and each case's agreement or disagreement with its reference statement is reported as an outcome, not scored as accuracy (§8.5). C3 is a case where they can disagree: its references say "race", while H-V expects `fixed-order` under σ_R1 (§7.6). The schedule-dependent class has no natural ground truth on the target binary. That is why RQ9 exists ([X3-C1]).
- **Per-case subjects and controls** [D] (EXT-20261012 §7; ADR-044). Each design case is evaluated on its own phenomenon, on its own basis and against its control; the paths, stages and decisions are in §9.3, per-case paths and controls. A v7.0.1 counterpart of C1, C2, C3 or C5 tests generic layers only and is never a subject for that case. The design cases' pre/post-fix pairs are development-set controls: non-blind (§7.5 item 5), scored by the movement rule of §8.5 item 7, reported per case under RQ2, and never counted as held-out pairs, since EXT-20261012 §7 step 5 asks for pairs not used in design. A case whose basis is not decided at F1 is reported as not evaluated at tool level, with the recorded reason (P-F1GATE).

  | Case | Bug-bearing subject (U3) | Control (U2) and its expected movement [I] | Basis decision | v7.0.1 counterpart (not a subject for the case) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | R2 | the ENV basis (v7.0.1) | no merged fix, hence no pre/post-fix pair; substitutes under P-R2CTRL: `b9bec567^`/`b9bec567`, the bundle tags `v7.0.0` → v7.0.1, the PR #2820 head (no movement expected), constructed core orders (§7.4); on a corrected half fixed-order is withdrawn and no protected verdict replaces it (App. A.1 step 15): configuration or revision sensitivity, never schedule evidence | none for its own instances (D1 for runs); P-R2CTRL for the control | none: R2 is on the target revision; its in-basis negative control E-post/E-TBL (§9.3) is part of R2's evidence |
  | C1 | CF `2a024d8` (the reported revision) or `281a941` (the fix base) on a derived B′; CF `15a871e` on a v7.0.1 B′ ([LED §1b]) | CF `833fdbb`, the minimal pair with `281a941`; movement only in the (σ_U∞, M_api) cell (no refinement → `timeout-dependent`, `exh` only) and in the naturalness of the final failure (§8.5 item 7) | D22 (with D18's C1 part); C1 D2–D4 (`cases/C1.md`) | the default B, where both lookups are `inactive(B)` (`cf_def_config.c:60, 78`) |
  | C2 | a role-derived provider/consumer pair with the single-OPERATIONAL counter barrier (the 6.4.2 tarball, or v6.5.0a) or with the old provider ([LED §1b]) | a 2 × 2: the two-phase barrier (v6.6.0a) and the new provider; which contrast counts, and whether the buggy and fixed designs can be told apart (Q22), is P-C2 | P-C2; D13 for a 6.4.2 row | the v7.0.1 barrier's writer set and the K-PHASE census of the bundle |
  | C3 | the 6.4.1 tarball | the 6.4.2 tarball, whose diff carries Trac #40, #41 and #42 together, a fix history shared with C5 ([LED §3.3]); schedule-dependent → protected within b, by `exh` only | D13 (a)–(c), Q16 | the SB-stamped events of TIME's and TBL's TaskInit, inside E-post and counted with R2 |
  | C5 | the 6.4.1 tarball (6.4.0's `cfe_es_apps.c` is identical, [LED §1b]) | the 6.4.2 tarball (Trac #41, the lock-scope extension, in the same diff as C3's fixes); it moves only through `exh`, and to `unknown`, not protected, without S7; `58abd38d^`/`58abd38d` lies on the A.3 line, not #72 (P-RGIT) | D13, Q16 | App. A.3, a residual candidate kept apart from #72 |

- **Census subject**: the v7.0.1 bundle under the default B, plus the variants of §7.4.
- **Recall subjects** (§8.5): program mutants of v7.0.1 and pre/post-fix pairs, labelled synthetic.
- **Held-out**: §7.5 (a)–(e), each counted separately.
- **Units of observation** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.4). Every count names one unit. A count in one unit is never used as the sample size of another, counts are never added across units, and runs are never pooled across program variants, observation methods or pool keys:

  | Unit | Definition |
  |---|---|
  | U1 defect cause | one fault, as stated by its issue or fix text or, for a candidate, by its code-reading account |
  | U2 fix history | one fix commit or release diff, with the issue texts it cites |
  | U3 program variant | one analysed artefact: a source revision or mutant, built into one binary, under one basis B or B′ |
  | U4 static instance | an instance `(κ, t, key, scope)` with `t` a context-qualified template (§1.6) |
  | U5 dynamic occurrence | one occurrence of an instance in one execution, with its ordinal |
  | U6 run | one boot of one U3 under one pool key and one observation method |

  Example, R2 [M]: U1 = 1; no U2 (the only PR was closed unmerged, `cases/R2.md` L49–52), so R2 has no pre/post-fix pair; U3 = the ENV binary and two CORD copies; U4 = the instances at 3 violating static sites, reached through 6 task × function contexts and 12 call strings ([R2g §6]); U5 = 24 per boot (11 with a `NULL` version table); U6 = 3 per group, never pooled ([R2c §8]). The other development cases have no U5 or U6 yet. Their U2–U4 are recorded per case, and C3 and C5 share one U2.

### 8.3 Baselines and reference detectors

Rules (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.1). Three kinds of comparison are kept apart in names, tables and conclusions. **(K1) Original tools**, run as released; `NOT_RUN` is never a false negative ([R06 S5]). **(K2) Re-implementations of prior techniques** on the same subjects, each stating its information: **same information** (the instance's Req, oracle outcome codes and caller-level Allowed entries (§1.6) with their B-derived bounds and continuation events, encoded as far as the technique's formalism permits) or **own information** (the technique's own HB rules or learned invariants). A re-implementation's result is a result of the re-implementation as built, not of the original tool. **(K3) Kairos ablations** (§8.4) remove one element of Kairos. **An ablation result is never evidence that a prior technique cannot express a property**, and no statement about what a prior technique can express is made without a primary source (P-PAPERS). Reference points and reference observations are labelled as such. Thread data-race tools are not baselines (the user's decision, [R06 S13]) and appear only as labelled reference points ([J3-M12]). The norm is to compare against the strongest runnable tool of the same family on the same subjects ([R05 §6.3] L446); for K2 this means the most capable encoding that the family's documented formalism allows, not a version stripped of elements.

**Three outcomes, not one metric** [D] (fixes [X3-M6]; revised by EXT-20261012 §5.2). For every labelled unit (§8.2, §8.7), each comparator and Kairos yield three separate records (§8.5, comparison record): **detection** (whether a not-ready occurrence of the expected instance is reported, with either outcome label: a VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence for Kairos, an alarm or a `not-ready-unjudged` report for a comparator, the same rule for every tool; or, for a hang-documented item, a counted stall witness; reported as detected, missed or false alarm, where a false alarm is such a report on a unit whose label expects no bad occurrence, such as the post-fix side of a pair); **classification** (class and refinements per (σ, M, b), the outcome label, and the progress result, or "no class output" for a tool that has none); and **evidence** (route, realization and naturalness, bound, σ and M scope, reach, trust ledger, checkability). Only detection is compared with a paired test, for comparators and ablations alike (§8.4), at the unit of analysis fixed in §8.7; the test, the unit and its independence assumption are recorded as assumptions before F1 ([POL] L17; P-STATS). Classification is reported against the pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1 (§7.2), as agreement with an expectation; where a reference makes a class statement (§8.2), it is scored as accuracy only under the mapping that P-CLASSGT adopts, and until then reported as agreement or disagreement under each named mapping option. Evidence is reported descriptively and never collapsed into yes/no. Comparator outcomes are not predicted as results. Design-rationale statements [I] about what a metric can separate are allowed, labelled and never scored: for example, the plain state monitor below is expected to alarm on every realized R2 occurrence, so detection cannot show what R2's certificate adds.

**Implementation discipline.** Every reimplemented comparator is frozen at F1, has golden tests taken from the worked examples of its original paper (where the paper is accessible; P-PAPERS), and records who implemented it. A same-information encoding also records who wrote it and which design cases they had inspected (as for Φ, §7.2), and its faithfulness to the technique's formalism is [U] until the paper is read (P-PAPERS). Every comparator that runs over Kairos's traces depends on Kairos's probe plan; this dependence is stated in every comparison, and the external reference points below do not have it.

| Comparator | Kind | Input | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| FastTrack-style HB (PLDI 2009 [V-abs]): lifecycle LPs as writes, uses as reads, lock edges | reimplementation, reference point | Kairos traces | unordered-pair detector on the same evidence |
| WCP- and SHB-style relations (PLDI 2017, OOPSLA 2018 [V-abs]) | reimplementation, reference point | Kairos traces | lock-order-preserving prediction |
| **GPredict-style predictor, same information** (MCM RV 2012, GPredict ICSE 2015 [V-abs]): key as conflict object, lock as atomicity; per instance the property is Req plus the caller-level Allowed entries with their B-derived bounds and continuation events, encoded as a parametric regular property over events that carry the key, the captured value and the returned code, as far as the formalism permits (expressibility table below) | same-information re-implementation, same-family comparator (fixes [X1-M12]; revised by EXT-20261012 §5.1) | same traces and cones | explicit equivalent search; SMT-LIB variant after P-Z3; faithfulness to GPredict's formalism [U] and no golden test before P-PAPERS; it lacks by construction certificates, the σ split, M levels, the progress axis and a third horizon verdict. The former variant without Φ outcome semantics, Allowed, capture points and certificates is not a baseline; whether it is kept as a labelled bundle ablation is PI decision P-ABL-ALLOWED (§10.1) |
| **Plain state monitor, same information**: Req, oracle codes and caller-level Allowed entries with Kairos's horizons, checked on observed traces; no prediction, exploration, certificates or σ split | same-information re-implementation (runtime-verification family) | Kairos traces | design rationale [I]: given K-ID's Req it alarms on every realized R2 occurrence, as Kairos's trace layer does, and ties on realized occurrences are expected in the other cases too; the value of certificates and prediction is therefore assessed under classification and evidence only (§8.5), never by detection |
| **DefUse-style learned def-use invariant** (OOPSLA 2010 [V-abs]): invariants learned from N natural runs, violations flagged | reimplementation | Kairos traces | [I] expected to learn R2's wrong definition as normal; a contrast for reference-based contracts |
| DCatch-style framework HB (ASPLOS 2017 [P]) with that technique's own information: framework HB rules, without contracts, outcomes or conditional edges | own-information re-implementation | Kairos traces | what a framework-HB detector reports on the same evidence. The value of contracts is measured by ablation 16 (§8.4), not by this row; no single-element ablation of Allowed exists unless P-ABL-ALLOWED adds one |
| RacePro-style syscall model (SOSP 2011 [V-abs]) over syscall tracepoints of the same runs | reimplementation of the observation model | tracepoints | the records found zero syscalls for an OSAL name create plus lookup ([R06 §6.2 C5]) |
| Uncontrolled repeated boots with SB/EVS counters | reference observation | runs | what counters reveal without order. This depends on the conjunct, not the case: at v7.0.1, EVS's not-registered branch increments `UnregisteredAppCounter` (`cfe_evs_utils.c:161`), but the `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` branch that R2 takes increments no counter (`cfe_evs.c:185–188`). So this row does not observe R2's rejections and is not a state monitor |
| gdb records ([R2g]) | reference observation | — | the reporter's method and its perturbation (ASLR disabled) |
| **valgrind helgrind and DRD** on the unmodified binary through `RUN_CFS_WRAP` | **external reference point, actually run** (fixes [X3-M6]); deviation: wrapped run command, as for gdb ([R2g] L11); P-VALGRIND | the binary | memory-level only; valgrind serializes threads, so schedules are not README-mode; expected to answer a different question ([R05 §6.3 (c)]) |
| ThreadSanitizer (gcc variant) | reference point, only if P-TSAN approves the rebuild deviation | rebuilt binary | memory-level races |
| Original process-level tools (RacePro, SimRacer, …) | `NOT_RUN` with recorded blockers ([R06 K6]) | — | — |
| Nidhugg | `NOT_RUN`; **blocker: an install decision** (`llvm-18-dev`; Nidhugg's README supports LLVM 8–19 and LLVM 18 is installed as runtime only, [R05 F11]; [R07] L126–127). After P-NIDHUGG: an optional cross-check of the explorer on the litmus programs that use only pthread create, join and mutex, on the same closed harnesses ([R05 §6.3] L452) | — | — |
| GenMC, CBMC | `NOT_RUN` (GenMC's README requires LLVM 19–22, not available; CBMC not installed) | — | — |

**Expressibility of a readiness contract as a GPredict-style property** (design rationale [I]; whether GPredict's formalism, adapted from MOP, has each feature is [U] until P-PAPERS). Events carry the key, the captured value and the returned code; bounds and continuation nodes are Kairos's (§1.6):

| Contract element | As a finite-trace parametric regular property [I] | The comparator needs |
|---|---|---|
| Req with oracle codes; `Allowed = none` | yes | the use's code; the capture-point value for value conjuncts |
| `documented_drop(src)` | yes, if the drop is an event; a silent drop returns no code | a probe on the drop branch |
| `returned_and_tested(codes)` | yes, per actor and key | the normal-continuation node, the REJECT events, a documented propagation |
| `retry_until_ready(codes, bound)` | yes for the constant bound | the bound evaluated from B |
| `fallback(op′)`, `defer_by_design(src)`, `exit(code, src)` | yes | the next, deferral-point or exit event |
| `unspecified_by_reference` | a labelling rule, not a property: a match is reported with the outcome label `not-ready-unjudged` and counts for detection like an alarm (§8.5) | — |
| scope | yes (a prefix condition) | the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP event |
| a trace that ends inside an open horizon | no two-valued match gives `unknown(horizon)` or PENDING | a third verdict |
| stall and progress; certificates over `Pref(B,σ,M)`; the σ split and refinements; M levels; same-ordinal pairing; provenance | no: these are analysis or bookkeeping features, not property-language features | — |

So a difference between Kairos and this comparator on finite-horizon Allowed entries is attributed to the analysis, not to the property language. The development cases on which this matters are C1 (`retry_until_ready`, and `returned_and_tested` at its buggy revision), where the entry decides detection and class, and C5 (a) (a documented propagation, if Φ_6.4.x authors it), where it decides only the outcome label. R2's Allowed is `none`.

### 8.4 Ablations

One element is removed at a time, with the same engine and the same traces ([R06 S11]; [J1-14]; [J3-M8]). An ablation measures what an element adds to Kairos; it says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3; EXT-20261012 §5.1). **Every ablation is scored on the labelled set** (development cases, recall mutants and pairs, the triage sample): per-unit detection deltas against the full system, at the unit of analysis of §8.7, with a paired test and an effect size (test, effect size, unit and independence assumption recorded as assumptions); class, outcome-label and evidence changes per unit reported descriptively, with class-correctness only where a class label exists (P-CLASSGT), plus the decided share, the unknown share by reason, the false candidates removed by O1–O10, and the cost change (fixes [X3-M8]).

1. **Static-only** (formerly "≈ A"): census + certificates (+ explorer if S7 passed); no traces. *Conditional on S7 for its explorer part.*
2. **Dynamic-only** (formerly "≈ B"): trace HB + prediction + confirmation; no census, no certificates.
3. **No feedback loop**: a single forward pass.
4. **No obligations** (O1–O10 off).
5. **σ_U∞ off**: no refinements.
6. **Robustness tags off**: no VC_R prefilter.
7. **M_api only** vs **M_impl**.
8. **Collapsed identities** (one identity per event).
9. **Atomic APIs** (no LPs; whole API at entry); **no capture points** (value conjuncts at the API's check).
10. **Conditional edges made unconditional** (soft barriers as must-edges).
11. **Implementation-level OSAL locks as synchronization**.
12. **No certificates first** (explore everything). *Conditional on S7.*
13. **No prediction**.
14. **Configuration closure off** (pointer analysis only); **message provenance off**.
15. **Looper restriction off** (thread program order between handlers).
16. **Contracts off** (pure HB race reporting).
17. **Writer sets from Φ only** vs **from S3a only** vs **S3a + S3b** (the value of two oracles and of points-to).
18. **L1, L2, L3 off**. *Conditional on S7 for L2 and L3.*
19. **`dpor` vs `exh`** on small cones. *Conditional on S7.*
20. **Syscall-only observation; memory-only HB**.
21. **Once and KEY-SEP off** (frontier only on single-spawn actors with no key separation): what the X1-M2/M3 machinery buys.
22. **A-COMM check off** (every prediction labelled `predicted`): how many witnesses change label, and how many of those are refuted on the binary.

For the loop ablation, it also reports keys and indirect targets resolved, unknowns removed and model gaps detected per iteration.

### 8.5 Metrics

**Recall protocol** [D] (fixes [X3-C1]); frozen before F2 (§7.2):
1. **Operators** ([R06 §5.6]; [R05 §6.2]): remove a wait; move a publication after a spawn; swap object-table entries; empty a retry bound (a single attempt, as at C1's buggy revision; the operator is recorded as authored with C1 known, and its mutant at C1's own site is non-blind, §7.5 item 5); drop a status check. Every operator is shaped on a design case [I]: swapping object-table entries on R2 (ES and EVS precede SB since `b9bec567`, [LED §1b]); emptying a retry bound on C1 (the inverse of its fix); moving a publication after a spawn on C5 and App. A.3; removing a wait on C3 (the inverse of its 6.4.2 order guarantee, Trac #40, [LED §1b, §3.3]) and, at v7.0.1, on App. A.3 (the trampoline's task-record poll); dropping a status check on the ignored statuses of R2's chain (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`) and of C5's `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` use. Recall is therefore reported per operator with that shape named, apart from the inverses of history fixes not read during design, and never as recall on order bugs in general (§6.4 item 25).
2. **History query** over the offline cFE (2047 commits, full history, [M] [X3, here]), OSAL and app histories: keywords and a date cut fixed before F2 (e.g. the 23 cFE commits whose full message matches `race|startup sync|order` case-insensitively at the pin are a starting set whose relevance is untriaged (matching subjects only gives 8 case-sensitive or 11 case-insensitive; over the CF history the same keywords select C1's own fix `833fdbb`; [M], read-only `git log`, EXT-20261012 §5.3), and the matched field and case are frozen with the query, [X3 §1]; examples `6932f1fe` "Fix #950, race condition in control request", `58abd38d` "Fix #978, store task parameters in task record"; both were named during design ([X3] L59), and `58abd38d` is also in the C5 case record, so items derived from them are non-blind, §7.5 item 5). For each relevant fix whose code is present at the pin, the **inverse** of the fix is re-created on v7.0.1 as a mutant. Whether the query excludes the design cases' fixes that lie in the queried histories is part of D8 (§7.5 item 4). Where a fix text makes a class statement (e.g. "race condition" in `6932f1fe` and `58abd38d`), it is recorded with the expected verdict (item 3).
3. **Expected verdict** of each mutant: taken from the fix's issue text and commit (POL reference kinds 1–2, L7–8): the contract violated, the instance, the expected direction (e.g. "a use can precede the publication"), and whether the expectation concerns readiness or progress (a documented hang); before F2, the expected-verdict rule takes this last field from each mutant's fix text. Where the fix text makes a class statement in its own words (e.g. `6932f1fe` "race condition in control request"; `58abd38d` "Fixes a race condition …"), that statement is recorded with its citation. The fix text is a ground-truth source for these fields. It is not a source of blindness (§7.2). A recorded class statement becomes a class label (fixed-order or schedule-dependent, per σ) only under the mapping that PI decision P-CLASSGT adopts; until then, and for a fix text that makes no statement, the class label is `unspecified_by_reference`. No class rule or mapping is invented here.
4. **Scoring per mutant and per layer** (merged with the progress counting rule of EXT-20261012 §4.1). A mutant counts as:
   - `detected`: a fixed-order or schedule-dependent class (or `ordinal-split`, where the fix text documents an ordinal-specific defect) with the expected instance and a compatible shape; or, for a mutant whose fix text documents a hang, a **counted** stall witness on the expected instance's progress axis or on the root of its progress chain. A stall witness is counted only if it is `realized`, or a model witness that is confirmable (kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, neither idle nor terminal-pending) and not choice-dependent (§1.10). Counted witnesses are grouped by wait-for root set, and a root set counts once. A `fixed-order` class counts only because its `¬OK_i` is deciding (§1.10), so a `¬OK_i` without a decision route (`unknown(no_decision_route)`) is never `detected`;
   - `class-correct`, a sub-count of `detected` reported apart: the class per σ equals the mutant's class label. It is scored only for mutants that have a label under the mapping of P-CLASSGT (item 3); for the others, and for every mutant until P-CLASSGT is decided, class-correctness is `not-scored`, never counted as correct or incorrect;
   - `unknown(reason)`: this includes `progress unknown(not_checked)` and uncounted model stall witnesses for hang-documented mutants; their numbers are reported per qualifier;
   - `missed`: for a hang-documented mutant, protected with `no-stall-within(b)`; for the others, protected, inactive, or no instance.

   Results are reported per layer (static, trace, prediction, exploration, confirmation) and in total, **per mutant and per originating fix (U2) and cause (U1)**, and per blind label (§7.5 item 5). Exact binomial intervals over mutants are reported only with their unit and independence assumption (§8.7). Mutants from one fix, or from one operator at sites of one contract, are not independent, so an interval over mutants is not a statement about fixes or causes. The share of model stall witnesses that are idle, terminal-pending or choice-dependent is reported as a model-precision measure; it is unknown [U] until S7.
5. **Soundness failures.** The following are reported as soundness failures, and confirmed on the mutant binary where possible:
   - a `protected` or `fixed-order` certificate on a mutant whose fix text documents the opposite **readiness** behaviour. This is the program-level test of the certificates; the certificate-mutation kill rate (§4.5) tests only the checker.
   - a `no-stall-within(b)` result, within the model's b, on a mutant whose fix text documents a hang. This is reported as a progress-model failure.

   A certificate on a mutant whose fix text documents only a hang is **not** a soundness failure, because certificates make no progress claim (§6.4 item 26).
6. **Equivalent mutants**: excluded only by a rule pre-registered before F2: the mutated site is `inactive(B)` with its proof, or the mutant's objects are byte-identical to the original's.
7. **Pre-fix/post-fix pairs** ([R05 §6.1 (iii)] L431) where fixes exist: the verdict must move in the direction the fix implies, on the axis the fix concerns: the safety class for a readiness fix, the progress result (a counted stall witness on the pre-fix side, none on the post-fix side within the same b) for a hang fix, and the refinement for a fix that trades an ordering-logic violation for a timing margin (C1: schedule-dependent under σ_U∞ before, timeout-dependent after; `exh` only; the direction holds under either reading of the normal continuation of `returned_and_tested`, §1.6).
8. **Time to first detection**: for σ_E observation, the number of natural runs (U6, within one pool key) until the first bad witness per mutant; for confirmation, the number of hold attempts; distributions, not means ([R05 §6.5]). Runs within a pool key are not independent samples of a population (same host, persisted state; §8.7), so these distributions are descriptive, and mutants from one fix are reported together.
9. **Build budget**: mutants are built incrementally in **one variant tree** (one full serial build ≈15 min, [R07] L423; one variant tree ≈0.8 GB, [R07] L420; 18 GB free, [R07 §1] and measured here); per mutant only the binaries, the compile DB and the hashes are archived. All mutants are labelled synthetic, never "new bugs".

**Triage** [D] (fixes [X3-M7]):
- **Reported** = either a class `fixed-order` (whose `¬OK_i` is deciding), `schedule-dependent` or `ordinal-split` with outcome label `violation`, or a counted progress `stall-witness` (label `liveness`; counted once per wait-for root set as in item 4), reported on the root of its progress chain where one exists. Uncounted model stall witnesses are listed separately as progress candidates and are not triaged as reports.
- Scale [M] [X3, here]: 1,208 `CFE_EVS_SendEvent` and 133 `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg`/`Subscribe*` call sites in `cfe/modules` and `apps` (tests and stubs excluded, link membership not filtered), before context qualification; thousands of instances are likely [I]. Triage therefore uses a **stratified random sample per (class, RC kind, deciding layer)**; the sample size is a **deviation** (finite rater time). Exact CIs are reported for the sampled instance population of each stratum: a design-based estimate that says nothing about how many causes there are. The number of distinct causes (U1) among the sampled Reported instances is therefore reported beside it.
- **Raters**: two, masked to the producing layer, to the ablation, and to each other. This is assessor masking, not dataset blinding (§7.2): each rater's prior exposure to the design cases, their fixes and Φ is entered in the exposure record (§7.5 item 5). Rater identity and independence from the Φ authors are PI decision P-TRIAGE. The agreement statistic is recorded as an assumption before triage.
- **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER** verdicts are not rated: their agreement with the SB-counter and put-outcome oracle is reported instead. `not-ready-unjudged` verdicts are counted, not rated (their "correctness" is undefined by construction).
- **Rater effort** is reported with the other costs.

**Other metrics**:
- Instances per class, refinement, σ, M, provider, reach, progress value (with stall clauses, kinds, qualifiers and the counted/uncounted split, counted per wait-for root), evidence (both axes), RC kind and source (§7.1), always with denominators ([R06 R3]). `NOT_RUN`, `NOT_MEASURED` and `NOT_FROZEN` are never zero or "pass" ([R06 R2]).
- Decided share per layer and per (σ, M); unknown share by reason; **anytime curves** (decided share against work units) for prediction, exploration and O8.
- **Comparison record** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.2), per (tool, labelled unit):
  - **detection** ∈ {alarm or `not-ready-unjudged` report on the expected instance, report elsewhere only, no report, `NOT_RUN`, inapplicable (the tool lacks the input)}, scored as detected, missed or false alarm against the unit's label, with the same rule for Kairos (a VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence) and every comparator; a false alarm is such a report on a unit whose label expects no bad occurrence;
  - **classification**: the tool's own class output per σ, M and b, the outcome label of its reports (`violation`, `not-ready-unjudged`, …), with the progress result, or "no class output"; for Kairos, also the agreement with the pre-registered expectation sheet and, under each named P-CLASSGT option, with the reference's class statement;
  - **evidence**: route (certificate, `exh` within b, prediction with its label, realized natural or held run); realization and naturalness; bound b; σ and M scope; reach; the trust-ledger part sizes and the steps re-validated via T5; `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries; the refutable items with their obligation status; whether `kz proofcheck` accepted the certificate;
  - **modelling cost** (EXT-20261012 §5.1): for Kairos's Φ and for each same-information encoding, the stated authoring scope, the authoring hours, the number of entries or rules, and the modifications made after the freeze (§7.2).

  A tool without a field reports it as absent, never as zero. Field names are proposals for S9.
- Confirmation: realized (by naturalness), refuted and inconclusive shares; gate-unreached share; holds per confirmation; perturbation ratios.
- Static coverage ([R06 T3]): entry functions resolved; indirect call sites resolved per rule; key shares; message-provenance folding counts.
- Fidelity: as RQ4.
- Cost: work units, wall time and peak memory per stage; the six costs separately; profile size and authoring hours.

### 8.6 Overhead and perturbation protocol

Groups ([J3-M6]; [A §5.8]; [B §5.6]):

| Group | Configuration | Separates |
|---|---|---|
| A | unmodified binary, no observer, **re-measured on `-fc-v114`** | reference |
| B | uprobes registered with empty BPF programs | trap cost |
| C′ | minimal R2 plan (R2 sites and identity bootstrap only) | cost of a narrow plan |
| C | full plan | recording cost |
| D | C plus holds, **analysed per hold plan** against C runs with the same plan hash (fixes [X3-M2]) | control effect |
| W | the F1 `--wrap` build with gates disabled (only if D3 is approved) | code-layout effect of the variant |

**Measures, split by what can observe them** (fixes [X3-M2]):
- **Uninstrumented-observable** (compare A against B, C′ and C): from the console, in cFE time, **CORE_READY → OPERATIONAL** ([R2c §4.4]: 103.2–104.6 ms across nine runs, a tight measure) and the **span of the 15 "Loading file … APP:" lines** ([BM] L278: 2.2–3.3 ms warm); per-app init offsets; the OPERATIONAL line's wall time; counters read at stop ([BM §2.3]): `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` (4–49 in six identical runs, low power), `NoSubscribersCounter` (15–26), `MsgLimitErrorCounter`, `InternalErrorCounter`; which pipes overflowed.
- **Recorder-only** (compare C′, C and D): the per-use R2 set (task, SB function, EID), probe hits per site, drops, observer CPU time and placement.

Distributions are compared, not means. The unit is the run (U6) within one group, stratum and pool key, and runs are treated as exchangeable within interleaved groups only under the assumption recorded in §8.7. **Per-witness perturbation ratios** (§4.13) are reported with every realized witness, with no threshold. Every σ_E frequency is labelled `σ_E(plan P)`; equivalence to unobserved runs is claimed only for the uninstrumented-observable marginals that were measured.

**Controls**:
- **strata**: cold (first run after a VM reboot) and warm are separate strata, never pooled ([BM]: 2570 ms vs 1613–1633 ms; 849 ms vs 2.2–3.3 ms load span);
- groups are run **interleaved**, not in blocks; the interleaving order is recorded as an **assumption** with its reason (the host is shared and drifts, [R07 §1]); no order is presented as from-reference;
- **normalization boots** after every abnormal exit, excluded from pools; Init checked against the canonical S0 by content hash (§4.13);
- observer CPU time and placement recorded in every run.

### 8.7 Statistics and reproducibility

- **N** (runs per group, per loop iteration, per σ_E frequency) is a **deviation** (fixes [X3-M9]): reason, finite resources; effect, bounded results reported with exact intervals or as `unknown(budget)`; necessity, no reference defines it. **The N for S1's characterisation is decided at S1** (decision D14 moved to S1); later Ns are decided before S9. Event-based stopping is preferred wherever it exists: exhaustion, fixed point, loop termination.
- **Statistical procedure**, recorded as an **assumption** (methodological literature is not a reference kind under [POL] L5–10): full distributions; Mann–Whitney U for times and counts over runs; Fisher's exact test for incidence over runs; a paired test for per-unit detection comparisons (§8.3, §8.4); standardized effect sizes; exact binomial confidence intervals for proportions. **Every test and interval states its unit (§8.2) and its independence assumption** (EXT-20261012 §5.4). Arcuri and Briand (ICSE 2011 [V-abs]) are cited as guidance; Klees et al.'s about 30 trials are known only from a secondary summary ([R05 §6.5], P) and are not used as a value.
- **Units of analysis and dependence** [D]. The handling of dependence is an assumption under P-STATS. No N, cluster size or threshold is chosen here.

  | Statistic | Unit | Independence assumption to record | Known dependence [I] | Options (P-STATS; none chosen) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | paired detection comparison (§8.3) and ablation deltas (§8.4) | the labelled unit of one evaluation set (development, recall, triage sample, each held-out source); never pooled across sets | units' outcomes are independent given the tools | instances of one contract share one certificate (R2's covers every R2 instance); instances and occurrences of one cause; mutants of one fix or of one operator at sites of one contract | aggregate to U1/U2 clusters before testing; or report per unit with a cluster-level summary; or descriptive only |
  | exact binomial interval for recall (§8.5 item 4) | mutant (U3) | mutants are independent Bernoulli trials | inverses of one fix; one operator at several sites; mutants derived from a design case | report per mutant and per U2/U1; read the interval only for the frozen mutant population |
  | triage precision (§8.5) | sampled instance (U4) in a stratum | the stratified random sampling design | many Reported instances can share one cause | design-based estimate for the instance population; distinct causes reported beside it; no interval over causes unless the PI adopts one |
  | Fisher's exact test, Mann–Whitney U (§8.6) | run (U6) within group, stratum and pool key | runs are exchangeable within interleaved groups | a shared, drifting host; persisted state; one binary per group | test only under the recorded assumption; otherwise "n of N (pool key)" |
  | time to first detection (§8.5 item 8) | runs per mutant within one pool key | as above | as above, and mutants clustered by fix | descriptive distributions |

  No count of U5 occurrences is ever a sample size: R2's 24 occurrences per boot are one cause at 3 static sites (§8.2).
- σ_E frequencies are pooled only over identical pool keys (B hash, plan hash, stratum), never across binaries or observation methods (e.g. R2's gdb, C-init and C-restored groups, [R2c §8]).
- Every reported number can be regenerated from stored artifacts: the deterministic replay test of §4.14.

### 8.8 Threats to validity

- **Internal**: profile errors (certificates, checker, O3/O4/O7/O9); Clang/gcc differences (anchor audit); model fidelity (O1, O2, O6, O8); probe perturbation (§8.6, perturbation ratios); kernel drift and a shared host; persistent state across runs (normalization boots); `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries (listed; O9); comparator quality depending on Kairos's own probe plan (stated; helgrind/DRD as an independent point); same-information encodings that also depend on Kairos's static facts (bounds, continuation nodes) and are written by the authors with cases known (authoring record, §8.3).
- **External**: one platform (Linux `SCHED_OTHER`, 4 vCPU, permissive mode, `msg_max` 10, euid ≠ 0); one OSAL backend (POSIX); one bundle version; RT behaviour only in the model (σ_R1[P_sched]); derived cases for C1, C2, C3 and C5; recall measured on synthetic mutants.
- **Construct**: contracts are authored, so the oracle is only as good as its provenance (shown per verdict); harm is judged only from caller-side references, and a supplier's documented result never makes an outcome allowed (§1.6); route and capacity losses are not violations without a must-deliver `appreq` (LOSS-UNDER-ORDER), and their observed counts are reported regardless; mutants are inverses of fixes, which may not represent unfixed bugs; an ablation measures Kairos's elements, not what prior techniques can express, and a re-implementation measures itself as built; detection alone cannot show what certificates add, because a same-information monitor ties on realized occurrences [I]; references call C1, C2, C3 and C5 races, and R2 has no reference class statement, but no reference maps "race" to the per-(σ, M) classes, so no class label exists until P-CLASSGT, and a reference statement and a Kairos expectation can disagree (C3 under σ_R1), which is reported as an outcome.
- **Conclusion**: five design cases with one cause each and declared links (C3–R2 resource family; C3–C5 one fix release; C5 and the App-record windows one shape; C1, C5 and R2 the reserve-then-finalize shape at different layers, [LED §3.6]), so they are not five independent samples; only R2 has runs, and most classification differentiators on C1–C5 need the explorer (S7) and the case bases (D22, P-C2, D13); for C1, C2, C3 and C5 the thin MVC gives only generic-layer results on their v7.0.1 counterparts (C1's `inactive(B)` proof, the K-PHASE census, R2's template in C3's TIME/TBL contexts, the App-record window A.3), which are not evidence on those cases (§9.3, per-case paths and controls), so a result confined to R2 is reported as a single-case result; recall mutants and pairs cluster by originating fix and cause; triage intervals hold for instances, not causes; non-blind items are reported apart; possible held-out scarcity; N, sample sizes, caps and the handling of dependence are deviations or recorded assumptions and are reported.

---

## 9. Implementation roadmap

### 9.1 Directory layout (to be created later; nothing created now)

```
kairos/
  CMakeLists.txt                     # find_package(LLVM, Clang) from the pinned LLVM 23 build 1053047a; MLIR only after G-MLIR
  include/kz/schema/kz_schema.h      # X-macro field lists: the only record-layout definition (C, _Static_assert)
  include/kz/core/*.hpp              # KEM types, Φ core expression AST, abstract-domain interface, component interfaces
  profiles/cfs-v7.0.1/*.kzp          # Φ: kinds, ops, effects+per-conjunct LPs, bindings, rules, contracts, constants
  profiles/apps/<app>/*.kzp          # optional app-profile layer (provenance, separate authoring cost)
  profiles/freeze/                   # F1/F2 freeze manifests (hashes): comparators and their encodings, evaluation criteria, expectation sheet, recall protocol, exposure record
  lib/core/      container I/O, Φ parser, one evaluator (3 domains), rule engine, typestate, vector clocks, verdicts
  lib/llvm/      basis, extract (AST facts, derives, S3a writers, loop-exit trees), link (IR call graph, DWARF, MC,
                 anchor audit, app-unit probe), skel (two views, provenance, once, predicate extraction), modref (S3b)
  lib/static/    MustBefore (per M), PWB/once, certificates, census, plan (joint debug-register allocator)
  lib/proofcheck/ checker with independent inference (no analysis code shared with lib/static; links lib/core, the trusted T5; no LLVM) + certificate-mutation engine
  lib/trace/     decode, identity, HB (VC, VC_R, looper rule), per-conjunct LP intervals, routing, chains, mining
  lib/conform/   per-actor acceptance (O1/O2); global conformance O8 (on lib/predict's engine, from S5)
  lib/predict/   cone, A-COMM check, explicit search, prefilters, extrapolation, SMT-LIB2 emitter (optional)
  lib/explore/   model builder, exh and dpor engines, σ variants (σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched]), monitor, BPOR, L3 checks
  lib/confirm/   hold plans, ptrace + debug-register holder, attach-at-clone, wait-for oracle, F1 controller, PCT,
                 normalization-boot driver, perturbation ratio
  lib/baselines/ comparator re-implementations, same- or own-information (frozen at F1; golden tests from the papers' examples)
  probe/bpf/     kz_probe.bpf.c (one generic program per phase; cookie-indexed descriptors; clang-18 -target bpf)
  probe/loader/  raw bpf()/perf_event_open loader (hand-written helper prototypes, <elf.h>), drainer;
                 perf-sample backend; optional tracefs backend
  gate/          kz_wrap.c (C99, cFS flags; F1 only, after D3)
  tools/kz/        core-only CLI: trace|conform|predict|explore|proofcheck|observe|hold|gate|loop|report|dump
  tools/kz-static/ LLVM CLI: basis|extract|link|modref|skel|static|plan
  tests/unit  tests/golden (canonical dumps)  tests/litmus (gcc-built C99 programs on the real OSAL, expected
              classes per σ and M; incl. App. A.8)  tests/mutation (certificates)  tests/property (evaluator
              agreement, HB, containment, loop monotonicity, dpor vs exh)  tests/recall (mutant manifests)
              tests/cfs (integration)
  docs/          formal model, Φ reference (grammar + semantics), provenance rules, ADRs (§11), diagrams (§12)
```

### 9.2 Engineering discipline (how messy code is avoided)

- **Interfaces first.** `kz_schema.h`, the Φ grammar **and its semantics** (core expressions, bindings, patterns), the verdict record and the component interfaces are reviewed before any implementation (S0).
- **One library per component plus a thin CLI subcommand**; components communicate only through KZ containers. Two binaries (§2.2).
- **No generated code** ([R06 K8]): X-macros instead of a schema compiler; no TableGen/ODS unless G-MLIR passes; a generic data-driven BPF program; handwritten gate wrappers; the explorer **interprets** the model.
- **No case-specific code; no bare constants** (§7.2 lints).
- **Tests at six levels**: unit tests; golden tests with canonical dumps (every Φ rule has one); a **litmus suite** on the real OSAL with expected classes per σ and M (incl. App. A.8); **property tests** (evaluator agreement across the three domains; HB transitivity; prefix containment (L-CONT (a), (b)) on small random models, with LP-TW as a negative test of maximal containment; loop monotonicity; `dpor` vs `exh`); **certificate-mutation tests**; and the **recall suite** of program mutants (§8.5).
- LLVM conventions for code linked with LLVM (no exceptions; `LLVM_ENABLE_EH=OFF`, [R07 §2.1]).
- **Provenance in every artifact**: B, Φ, tool and parent hashes; LLVM `1053047a`; gcc 13.3.0; kernel; backend; fidelity class.
- **Deterministic outputs**: sorted, hashed, work-unit budgets; golden tests and replay are stable.

### 9.3 Stages and exit gates

Each stage has its own exit gate and its own claims (claims table below); whether a cut is publishable depends on differentiation and evaluation, not on the stages completed (EXT-20261012 §9). The recorder stays narrow and model-driven (v0.1 §15; [J3-M5]; [J1-11]; [J2-15]). **The thin minimum viable core (MVC) is S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a** (fixes [X3-M5]). Effort estimates are **[I] planning guesses by the architect, not research conditions, not measured, and not used by any tool**; they exist so that the PI can cut scope.

**First target: the validation ladder** (ADR-044; PI decision P-LADDER). The first target is not the whole thin MVC but rung **V1** of the ladder below: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2 and S2-R2 that take from the stage gates only the items R2's chain needs. The full gates of S0–S3a are unchanged and form rung **V1b**. The ladder orders the dynamic end-to-end pipeline only by stage dependencies, inputs and decisions; R2's control (V2) and C1's rung (V3) run in parallel, and the order of EXT-20261012 §7 is the order in which results are reported. Before any V1 result is worded as a certificate, the lemma obligations of that wording are written and reviewed (V0-P). Case tracks run beside it, so that every design case is validated at its cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned public revisions and a frozen expectation sheet for its bug-bearing and fixed revisions) and enters at tool level as soon as its basis decision lands. A result on a v7.0.1 counterpart of C1, C2, C3 or C5 is never reported as evidence on that case. **The estimates are not summed here.** The external review's sum of the S0–S3a estimates (19–28 person-weeks) is correct arithmetic on [I] inputs. It is effort, not duration (S2 and S3a can overlap, §9.4), and it covers the thin MVC only. No estimate exists for V1 or for any other rung; that is `unspecified_by_reference` and is P-LADDER (b).

**Validation ladder and case tracks** [D] (ADR-044). The paper track (reading at pinned revisions, and an expectation sheet frozen by hash before the first tool output on each case, for R2, C1, C2, C3 and C5) needs no stage and no decision. Every tool output on a case or control is compared with its frozen sheet; a mismatch is investigated and reported, never tuned. The rungs are ordered only by the stage dependencies of the stage table, the inputs each rung names and the decisions it needs: V2 and V3 both follow V1 and V1b and do not wait for each other, and the order of EXT-20261012 §7 (step 3, then step 4) is the order in which their results are reported. E-post and E-TBL are defined with the V1 sub-gates below; the per-case paths and controls are in the table after them.
