**Expansion.** MLIR (S8), SMT (P-Z3), DPOR (`dpor` after `exh` in S7) and Φ templates that no rung, track or step needs are added only after a rung (V1–V3), a case track (VP-D3, VP-D4, VP-D5) or an ENV-window step (S5 and S6 on the App-record windows, S7 on the development cones) shows a need. A kind is needed when a gate item of a rung or track, or a development cone of the S7 kill criterion (§4.10 item 9), names it. Kinds without an authored template are reported as not authored in the openness indicators (§7.7) and in RQ1's denominators, never omitted.

**V1 sub-gates** [D]. Each item is taken from the stage gate named; the rest of that gate is V1b's.

**E-post and E-TBL**, R2's in-basis negative control (used by V1, V1b and the per-case table). **E-post** is the set of K-ID instances in the default B whose value conjunct reads SB's identity cell `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` after SB's publication at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, in TBL, TIME, SB itself and the apps: the "post-publication SB-AppId uses" of §7.3 (row "SB/TBL identity never published"). **E-TBL** is the set of K-ID instances whose value conjunct reads TBL's identity cell, published at `cfe_tbl_task.c:196`: the "TBL identity uses" of the same row. Expected [I]: neither carries a fixed-order verdict, and both are open and chained to the App. A.2 root (§7.3). C3's v7.0.1 site, the SB-stamped events of TIME's and TBL's TaskInit, lies inside E-post and is counted with R2 (§8.2); E-TBL is another cell and holds no C3 instance. The negative-control list is kept per template and split by cell.

V1's Φ subset: K-ID.send-with-appid (`cfe_evs.h:168–172`) with its value and registry conjuncts and the capture point; `derives`; INIT facts (`cfe_sb_task.c:40`, `cfe_sb_init.c:50`); the `CFE_ES_GetAppID` out-pointer effect (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`; `cfe_es_api.c:758–760`); the ES App record's `AppState` (`cfe_es_start.c:803`, `Type = CORE` at `:795`); R1, R2/P-TASK, R18, R19; T-PO, T-OBS, T-SPAWN, T-TERM, ONCE-PWB, FRONTIER, OWN-NOPATH, KEY-SEP; A-FRESH, A-OWNER and the `CleanUpApp` guard; `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` with `appreq` = `cfe_evs.h:169–172` (caller side) and the caller-level `Allowed = none` (App. A.1 step 8) | S0 |

**Per-case paths and controls** [D] (ADR-044). For each design case: the cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned public revisions and a frozen, hashed expectation sheet; no implementation and no decision); the first tool step that needs no new decision, and what it tests; the first tool step on the case's own phenomenon, with its decision; and the control. Results in the fourth column on C1, C2, C3 and C5 are **not** evidence on those cases. Every expected movement is [I] until run. Without D13, D22 and P-C2, tool-level evidence on a case's own phenomenon exists for R2 only; that is reported, not hidden (P-F1GATE). No cell is a "safe" reading.

**Claims available at each cut** [I]:

| Cut after | What the paper can claim |
|---|---|
| S0 + S1 | a zero-install F0 recorder for cFS on the unmodified gcc binary; R2's per-use set re-observed with measured costs and perturbation |
| + S2 | dynamic contract checking with per-conjunct LPs and handler-scoped HB; R2 classified per use; chains; LOSS-UNDER-ORDER counts with SB oracles |
| + S3a (**thin MVC**) | the census; R2 certified fixed-order (σ_U, M_api and M_impl) as a safety statement, its inference steps re-validated independently relative to a stated trusted base (the extracted facts, skeleton and Φ; trust ledger); inactive and provider verdicts from configuration closure and message provenance; recall of the static and trace layers on program mutants |
| + S4 | reproducible fixed points; model fidelity (O1–O7, O9) |
| + S5 | schedule-dependent witnesses (predicted / predicted-modelled) for the App-record windows; for the C1-derived instance, at M_impl a `predicted` bad witness (`OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` decides at once) together with an observed good run gives schedule-dependent, while at M_api a reversal gives only predicted not-ready evidence (attempts stay PENDING under `retry_until_ready`), or a `predicted-extrapolated` witness where the retry summary passed O2; recall of the prediction layer |
| + S6 | realized (held) witnesses with perturbation ratios |
| + S7 | bounded safety records and classes in M under b (including `no-final-status` and `ordinal-split`); the σ_U∞ refinements, none of which exists without S7; model progress results (stall witnesses with kinds, qualifiers and progress chains; `no-stall-within(b)`) |

**Cut list, in the order scope would be dropped** (lowest claim value first) [I]: S8 (MLIR) → S10 arms → S3b → S7 (explorer; without it no refinement and no model progress result is established, and only σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact remain) → O8 → S6 → S5. The thin MVC and the recall protocol for its layers are never cut.

The ladder's V1 and the paper track for all five cases are never cut. Every cut is reported with the case evidence it removes: cutting S10 removes C3's and C5's tool-level evidence and C2's 6.4.2 row, unless D13 (c) has placed the 6.4.x basis before F1; cutting S7 leaves C1's refinements and progress rows, C2's refinements, and the fixed sides of C3 and C5 unanswerable; cutting S5 or S6 removes C1's predicted and held arms. A cut that leaves tool-level evidence on R2 alone is reported as a single-case result (ADR-042).

### 9.4 What is built first

**Build order: the validation ladder** (ADR-044). V0 first: the formal amendment (ADR-039–ADR-043) is adopted, and before any certificate or transfer wording is used the lemma obligations of that wording are written and reviewed (V0-P, §9.3 ladder table). Then V1: S0-R2 (formats, the K-ID subset of Φ, the evaluator's concrete and abstract domains), then S3a-R2 and S1-R2, which can overlap (S3a-R2 needs only S0-R2, plus S1-R2's run for reach), then S2-R2. V1b (the rest of the S0–S3a gates) overlaps V1. V2 and V3 then run in parallel and do not wait for each other: V2 needs from V1b only the App. A.2 instance of SB's `GetAppID`; V3 needs from V1b the app-unit closure and the app `.so` probe registration (CF is an app), plus D22, S5–S7 for classes and S6 with P-C1HOLD for holds. Their results are reported in the order of EXT-20261012 §7 (step 3, then step 4); that is a reporting order, not a dependency. V4 lies inside S9. The case tracks run alongside the ladder and start when their decisions land. At stage level the reasons are unchanged: S0 comes first because every later component depends on the shared formal core and the formats; S1 because ground truth must come from the new recorder before any analysis is trusted; S2 and S3a can overlap, so the stage estimates add up to effort, not to calendar time. The core that the stage plan never cuts (the R2 certificate, the census, the recorder, and recall for those layers) does not wait for prediction, confirmation or the explorer. With tool-level evidence on R2 alone it is a single-case result (ADR-042), and whether it is publishable depends on differentiation and evaluation (EXT-20261012 §9), not on its completion. Evidence on C1's, C2's, C3's and C5's own phenomena waits for those stages and for each case's basis decision (§9.3, per-case paths and controls).

## 11. Architecture decision records

Format: **Context**; **Decision**; **Alternatives**; **Consequences**. Status: *accepted for SPEC FINAL*. ADRs that depend on a PI decision say so; ADRs changed by the reviews name the issue.

**ADR-001 — C/C++ core and typed artifacts; no Python/JSON pipeline.** Context: the user discarded the Python/JSON prototype (CORD, [R06 K7]). Decision: all components in C++17 (C for BPF and gate objects); all artifacts in the typed KZ container; JSON appears only as CMake's input `compile_commands.json`. Alternatives: Python orchestration over C++ kernels; a schema compiler. Consequences: one toolchain (LLVM 23 + gcc 13.3 + clang-18 for BPF); review via `kz dump`; no generated code.

**ADR-002 — One formal core interpreted three ways.** Context: static, trace and model layers must agree ([J1], [J2], [J3]). Decision: one KEM type set, one Φ, **one evaluator with three abstract domains** (except that `kz proofcheck` decides `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own symbolic procedure; ADR-041) (revised by [X3-M11]), and a concretization relation `t ⊒ e`. Alternatives: separate models per layer. Consequences: O1–O10 are possible; `libkz-core` stays LLVM-free; an agreement property test is mandatory.

**ADR-003 — Φ is an interpreted DSL with provenance and a specified semantics.** Context: framework knowledge must be versioned, cited and reusable (H-V); the three interpreters need one meaning per predicate ([X3-M11]). Decision: a hand-parsed language with three sublanguages fixed at S0: a typed core expression language over resource state (bounded quantifiers over declared kinds only), bindings from C fields to resources (checked by O7), and an AST-pattern language with a match semantics. Alternatives: C++ rule classes; MLIR ODS; quoted predicate strings (v1). Consequences: engines are framework-neutral; a Φ edit restarts the loop.

**ADR-004 — KZ container with X-macro schema, typed references and per-section versions.** Context: BPF, gate objects and C++ must share layouts; v1's layout had implicit padding and bare references ([X3-m6]). Decision: X-macro field lists expanded into struct, dump printer and offset asserts; explicit reserved fields; `sizeof` equals the sum of fields; typed references `(artifact sha256, section, index)`; Merkle parent hashes. Alternatives: schema compiler; JSON lines. Consequences: layout changes touch one list; hashes never depend on uninitialized bytes.

**ADR-005 — Static ordering is a MustBefore greatest fixpoint on the certificate view.** Context: v1 cut message loops at h while claiming b = ∞ ([X1-C1]); base cases and W_o recomputation were unstated ([X1-m2]). Decision: T-PO, T-SPAWN, T-OBS, T-TERM on `Sk^cert` (every unbounded loop star-closed); ∅ at every root; W_o recomputed per iteration with four checked exclusions (OWN-NOPATH, PWB, KEY-SEP); no lock rule. Alternatives: may-HB over templates (C); h-cut skeletons (v1). Consequences: sound but incomplete; loops cost precision (frontier nodes after loops only).

**ADR-006 — Certificates by PWB with once(τ), a checker that re-validates inference relative to a stated trusted base, and program-level falsification.** Context: dominance fails on early returns ([J2-1]); PWB needs one instance per actor ([X1-M2]); v1's R2 certificate missed a writer ([X2-01]). Decision: FRONTIER and ONCE-PWB rules with proved once facts; MUST_KEY must-writers for PROTECTED; SCOPE exclusion; `kz proofcheck` re-validates the inference steps relative to a stated trusted base (`facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, closure lists, Φ, `libkz-core`, run artifacts), recorded per certificate in a trust ledger (revised by ADR-041); certificates prove safety statements only (ADR-039); certificate mutations test the checker; program mutants test the analysis. Alternatives: `u ∈ MB(x)` (A); trace-local VC_R certificates (B). Consequences: R2 certifiable with listed trusted entries; once analysis and checker are extra code.

**ADR-007 — Writer sets from S3a AST extraction plus Φ effects, with key statuses and allocation records.** Context: v1 specified a from-scratch Andersen analysis over opaque-pointer IR inside the MVC ([X3-M4]); key semantics were may-only ([X1-M3]). Decision: field-based writer extraction at the AST level under A-TYPE with untyped-write and escape listings; out-pointer summaries; transition-aware filter; MUST_KEY / MAY_KEY / NO_KEY; allocation records with A-FRESH and A-OWNER as listed trusted facts refuted by O9; S3b points-to only if measured necessary. Alternatives: Andersen first (v1); declared effects only (C). Consequences: the MVC is smaller; ablation 17 measures what S3b would add.

**ADR-008 — Semantics family with per-M containment and well-defined refinements.** Context: v1's containment ignored M ([X1-C2]); σ_U∞ was ill-defined for exhausted retries and liveness ([X1-M5]); σ_R1 ignored OSAL's RT policy ([X2-07]). Decision: σ_U with exact retry bounds; σ_U∞ as σ_U without time-based transitions (revised by ADR-040); safety on finite executions and progress on dead actors (ADR-039, ADR-040); σ_R1[P_sched] with cited profiles and blocking delays (A-ZT); prefix containment of finite real runs in `Pref(σ_U, M_impl)` (ADR-040); M_api as API-contract claims; refinements per σ with their own bounds. Alternatives: σ_U + σ_E only (A); v1's family. Consequences: two analyses per OSAL-touching cone; no σ_R1 verdicts for backends without a cited profile.

**ADR-009 — A linearization point per conjunct, with capture points.** Context: v1 evaluated value-carried conjuncts at the wrong LP ([X1-M1]). Decision: every conjunct declares its LP; value conjuncts use the capture point found by `derives`; T-DERIVE is the generic translation from a stamp parameter to `cell(loc)`; proofcheck re-checks `derives`. Alternatives: one LP per use (v1). Consequences: traces bracket capture points; O4 maps outcome codes per conjunct.

**ADR-010 — Two OSAL model levels with faithful M_impl waits.** Context: POSIX internals create windows, timed waits and a lock leak ([R01 §7 item 6]; [X2-03]). Decision: M_api atomic at LPs; M_impl projects OSAL shared + POSIX source onto Φ_L-1, with by-ID vs by-name token conversion and attempts woken by broadcast or timed wake. Labels portability-hazard, implementation-defect-candidate, portability-hazard-evidence. Alternatives: API level only; always M_impl. Consequences: litmus tests validate Φ_L-1.

**ADR-011 — A verdict record with orthogonal axes and no "safe".** Context: [R06] R1–R7, S1; v1 mixed causes with outcomes and realization with naturalness ([X1-m3], [X1-M11]). Decision: per-(σ, M) cells, each with a safety class, a reach value and a progress result (ADR-039); provider axis; scope; realization and naturalness axes; σ_E classes only from natural witnesses on both sides. Alternatives: a single severity ladder; v1's single evidence ranking. Consequences: reports are multi-axis; tests check that no path produces "safe" or "timing-protected".

**ADR-012 — Context-qualified templates with a summary fallback.** Context: SB event sites are shared by all callers; full inlining is exponential ([X1-m7]). Decision: templates `(actor instance, call string, site)`; per-(actor, function) summaries when the skeleton work-unit cap is reached, with the precision loss stated. Alternatives: site-only templates. Consequences: per-context certificates; fallback counts measured.

**ADR-013 — F0 observation with perf-PMU uprobes and an own raw loader; perf-sample bring-up.** Context: highest fidelity on the unmodified binary; no libbpf ([R07 §4.7]); tracefs changes global state ([X3-m2]). Decision: uprobes at entry+4 registered by inode before launch; one generic BPF program per phase; perf-sample backend for bring-up; tracefs optional with host-state rules. Alternatives: `--wrap` (F1); source probes (F2); gdb; libbpf. Consequences: S1 must probe cookies, ring buffers, task storage and stack capture.

**ADR-014 — Causality only from tokens and protocol keys.** Context: timestamps give no HB ([R04 §2.1]; [J2-4]). Decision: per-thread `tseq`; per-object `objseq`; descriptor/FIFO; outcome-implied reads-from; poll exit status. Alternatives: clock reconstruction as HB. Consequences: no take-return probes on hot sites.

**ADR-015 — Return values captured only where needed, by a mechanism chosen by measurement.** Context: post-call `mov` probes are likely in the 31.7 µs class ([J3 §2.3]); a cheaper emulated-branch option exists ([X3-m3]). Decision: capture only at contract-use and edge-deciding sites; R-a…R-e measured per class in S1; deferred reads where statically valid. Alternatives: caller-side probes everywhere. Consequences: S1 produces the calibration table before a full plan.

**ADR-016 — Prediction by explicit-state, lock-reversing search with an A-COMM check.** Context: the central cFS class needs lock reversal; no solver is installed; sparse traces weaken MCM/RVPredict guarantees ([X1-M4]). Decision: Pred(π) with the key as conflict object and the lock as atomicity; "an acquire needs the lock free"; A-COMM check per witness, else `predicted-modelled`; prefilters with re-validation; work-unit caps with anytime curves; SMT-LIB only after P-Z3. Alternatives: SMT-only; sync-preserving prediction only. Consequences: no install; the predictor's soundness is stated relative to A-COMM.

**ADR-017 — Explorer with a verified exhaustive mode and a conditionally verified DPOR mode; gated.** Context: a test is not a proof; the disabling theorem is status P ([X1-M9]); v1's L3 condition was insufficient ([X1-M10]). Decision: `exh` (stateful exhaustive search) and `dpor` (source-DPOR + sleep sets, labelled unverified until T-JACM is checked); L3 as a mover reduction with statically checked conditions; differential test as validation; no optimality claim; stage S7 with a kill criterion. Alternatives: optimal DPOR claimed exact; no explorer. Consequences: the explorer may end as an ablation. Without it, no refinement is established, because `timeout-dependent` and `timeout-rescued` need universal σ_U∞ statements that neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide; prediction can only rule a refinement out with a σ_U∞ witness. No model progress result exists either: every progress value stays `unknown(not_checked)`, except σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact (ADR-039, ADR-040).

**ADR-018 — MLIR behind gate G-MLIR.** Unchanged from v1: plain C++ skeletons and core; G-MLIR at S8 with criteria (verdict equality, provenance completeness, code size, runtime); no ODS before the gate passes.

**ADR-019 — Version-robust site binding.** Context: line-number probes break across revisions ([J2-9]); same-named local copies exist ([X3-m3]). Decision: AST patterns with a match semantics, resolved through each basis's gcc DWARF (compile unit for local copies) and MC; version guards; anchor audit. Consequences: unresolved patterns listed.

**ADR-020 — Confirmation by F0 holds with explicit safety rules and normalization.** Context: constructed schedules must be feasible, safe and honestly labelled ([J2-10]; [J3-M10]); abnormal exits contaminate later runs ([X3-M3]); debug registers are shared ([X3-m1]). Decision: ptrace with debug-register breakpoints; empty program-lock set; placement rules; event release; reference watchdogs incl. `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; wait-for oracle; joint debug-register allocator; attach at clone measured; a normalization boot after every abnormal exit; naturalness labels and perturbation ratios. Alternatives: `--wrap` gates by default; fixed sleeps (rejected by [POL] L22). Consequences: at most 4 debug registers per thread for watchpoints plus gates.

**ADR-021 — Consistency obligations O1–O10 and the certificate check.** Context: authored rules need a "how do you know the model is right" answer ([J1 §6]). Decision: per-actor acceptance; outcome agreement with power reported on HB-decided uses; writer-set agreement incl. A-TYPE; allocation/ownership refutation (O9); global conformance from S5; contradictions (O3/O4/O9 against certified statements) suspend rules and end the loop; O6 (b) and O10 contest a bound without a suspension (ADR-039, ADR-040). Consequences: obligation failures are a fidelity metric.

**ADR-022 — A feedback loop with reproducibility.** Context: v1's fixed-point test included counts and never converged ([X1-m8]); budgets were wall-clock ([X3-M10]). Decision: per-iteration records; a fixed-point test over the comparable state only; an explicit lattice; suspension ends the loop; work-unit budgets; replay byte for byte; staged availability of components. Consequences: reproducible numbers.

**ADR-023 — Clang/LLVM for analysis only; the gcc binary is what runs.** Unchanged: Clang facts and IR for analysis; DWARF/MC anchoring to the gcc binary; two diagnostic-only flag adaptations recorded in B; IR used for the call graph only (pinned pipeline if ever used for writes).

**ADR-024 — Openness through a union of discovery sources, with governance.** Context: five cases cover 3 of 15 contract kinds ([R06 §5.1]). Decision: the 14 sources of §7.1; freezes F1 (incl. comparators and, since ADR-042, the evaluation criteria and the pre-registered expectation sheet) and F2 (incl. the recall protocol and the exposure record); template-only first; refinement accounting that names framework rules; an app-profile layer. Consequences: authoring effort is real and is measured.

**ADR-025 — Comparators reported by detection, classification and evidence, frozen code and an external reference point.** Context: v1's comparators were all self-reimplemented with no metric, and the strongest same-family comparators were missing ([X3-M6]; [X1-M12]). Decision (revised by ADR-042): detection, classification and evidence per comparator and labelled unit, with a paired test on detection only; comparators frozen at F1 with golden tests from their papers; a GPredict-style lock-reversing predictor and a plain state monitor given the same property information, and a DefUse-style learner, added; a stripped variant, if kept, is a bundle ablation (P-ABL-ALLOWED); helgrind/DRD run through `RUN_CFS_WRAP`; the Nidhugg blocker stated as an install decision. Consequences: comparisons answer "what each model sees on the same labelled units", with Kairos-plan dependence stated.

**ADR-026 — A staged build with a thin MVC, estimates and a cut list.** Context: v1's MVC included the whole L1 layer and Andersen; stages had dependency inversions ([X3-M5]). Decision: thin MVC = S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a, reached through the validation ladder of ADR-044, whose first target V1 takes sub-gates from these stages; S4 without O8, predict and hold; O8 on S5's engine; D14 at S1; [I] estimates and a cut list. Consequences: the explorer, S3b and F1 can be cut without losing the core that the stage plan never cuts; with tool-level evidence on R2 alone that core is a single-case result, and cutting S5–S7 or S10 removes the other cases' own evidence (§9.3, ADR-044). Cutting the explorer removes the only route to model progress results and to refinements, so protected verdicts are then reported as safety statements with progress `unknown(not_checked)`; σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact remain possible.

**ADR-027 — Two skeleton views.** Context: [X1-C1]. Decision: `Sk^cert` (star-closed, for every universal claim and `inactive`) and `Sk^h` (h-cut, for exploration only, h in b); proofcheck rejects `Sk^h`/`TRUNC` steps; litmus App. A.8. Consequences: universal verdicts are horizon-free; exploration results are bounded.

**ADR-028 — Teardown as a scope, modelled as on pc-linux.** Context: v1 modelled restart as non-terminal with `OS_DeleteAllObjects` concurrent with tasks, defeating the calibration certificates ([X1-M6]; [X2-04]). Decision: `es.shutdown` kind; requester terminal after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; teardown only on the PSP main thread after `CFE_ES_Main` returns; contracts scoped "before the shutdown request"; K-TEARDOWN family; SCOPE exclusion in certificates. Consequences: default-B certificates are stated outside teardown; every experiment's stop is a teardown and is reported separately.

**ADR-029 — Message provenance and the looper rule.** Context: [X1-M7]. Decision: `Prov(p) = (Routes_B(p) ∩ Tx_B) ∪ Input_B(p)` as a least fixpoint over handler reachability, folding dispatch in both views; no between-handler HB edge; FIFO only as a prediction constraint. Consequences: command-only paths are `inactive` without input; shapes follow CAFA/DCatch.

**ADR-030 — Code-extracted await predicates.** Context: v1's T-OBS rested on authored predicate strings ([X1-M8]). Decision: poll loops recognized by pattern; predicates extracted by symbolic evaluation of the exit branch (three recognized forms); re-derived by proofcheck; L1 preconditions checked; Φ binds only names, strata and escapes. Consequences: R2's proof no longer depends on an authored predicate; unextractable loops are `unmodelled-sync`.

**ADR-031 — Generic K-SELF contract and branch-complete chains.** Context: [X2-02]. Decision: `es.self(exec) = LIVE` instantiated at all seven context-resolving callers with per-caller outcomes and harms; consequence chains record every branch, labelled [I] until run. Consequences: new harms (`app_zombie`, `service_task_absent`, `process_abort`, `panic`).

**ADR-032 — Two-phase SB transmit and two-level capacity.** Context: [X2-06], [X2-05], [X2-09]. Decision: route_lookup LP under the lock plus one put LP per destination; K-CAP and K-CAP.msglim; four counters as oracles, `PeakQueueDepth` labelled SB-accounted; header-carried MsgIds with encode/decode summaries and a table-packet decoder; K-MODE in SB's actor via R7 on SB's command pipe. Consequences: RC5 stays partial until key shares are measured.

**ADR-033 — Basis closure covers reset type, script selection, euid, CWD and CDS images; canonical Init.** Context: [X2-08], [X3-M3]. Decision: these items in B with sources; the script-selection rule evaluated in `basis`; `cap_B` conditioned on euid; A11 split by reset type; content-only Init hash; strata; normalization boots. Consequences: root-run arms are new bases.

**ADR-034 — Deterministic work-unit budgets and POL-compliant labels.** Context: [X3-M9], [X3-M10]. Decision: every analysis budget is a work-unit count with a deterministic search order; wall time and memory are costs only; PI-chosen N, caps and sample sizes are deviations; event-based stopping preferred; anytime curves; statistics as an assumption. Consequences: byte-exact replay; honest bounds.

**ADR-035 — Recall measured on program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs.** Context: v1 measured nothing about misses; one natural case ([X3-C1]). Decision: §8.5 protocol frozen before F2; `detected` and `class-correct` scored apart, per mutant and per originating fix; blindness only from the exposure record (ADR-042); soundness failures counted; equivalent-mutant rule pre-registered; one incremental variant tree. Consequences: RQ9; mutants labelled synthetic.

**ADR-036 — Attach cookies and inode registration.** Context: IP-keyed descriptors break under ASLR; the `.so` fallback perturbed the phase under study ([X3-M1]; [X3-m4]). Decision: site id as attach cookie (per-site perf links or `uprobe_multi`), registration by inode before launch, verifier-conscious generic program, hand-written helper prototypes and `<elf.h>`. Consequences: no per-module map filling; the hold fallback is excluded from pools if ever used.

**ADR-037 — Corrected prior-work positioning.** Context: [X1-M12]. Decision: credit InitRacer (late registration), RVPredict/GPredict (key-conflict lock reversal), ExceptioNULL (predicted use-before-init) and DefUse; record G3 novelty as an open literature check; claim only the combination (revised by ADR-042: GPredict is described as [R04] records it; predictive typestate checking (Joshi & Sen) and Lifestate are credited as prior art for framework typestate; no comparison or ablation is presented as evidence that a prior technique cannot express a property). Consequences: §1.11 and §6.4 item 18.

**ADR-038 — Triage by stratified sampling with masked raters and oracle agreement for losses.** Context: [X3-M7]. Decision: "reported" defined; stratified random sample per (class, RC kind, deciding layer); raters masked to the producing layer, the ablation and each other (assessor masking, with each rater's exposure recorded; ADR-042); LOSS-UNDER-ORDER checked against the SB oracle; rater effort as a cost. Consequences: precision with exact CIs instead of full triage.

**ADR-039 — Safety statements, classes from a safety record, and a separate progress result.**
- **Context.** `protected` required `S_i = ∅`, but no certificate, lemma or checker step establishes it: L-MB and L-PR are conditioned on occurrences (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Rev. 1 of the amendment also showed that "one class per cell" fails when both universal statements hold, and that a schedule-dependent class from occurrences at different ordinals claims a schedule dependence that no single occurrence has.
- **Decision.**
  - Occurrences begin at their call; captures are attached to the call they feed; a call that returns before one of its conjunct LPs gives `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts; scope is decided at the decision event and is part of the instance; ordinals count per key over canonical actor-instance names, mapped to run identities through the closure. `returned_and_tested` accepts only at a normal continuation: the cited one, or by default the nearest post-dominator of the test, per loop iteration, that is not the function's return and at which neither the status nor the not-ready branch's writes are live; a REJECT at the same node wins; an error exit or an error return is a REJECT; a documented propagation needs a reference. No status or horizon verdict is decided after or by a process-terminal step.
  - Each cell keeps, per bound, a safety record: the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` (with its decision route) and `¬NR_i` with their routes, and witnesses with their occurrence ordinals. Classes are a function of the record: `protected`, `fixed-order` (`¬OK_i` deciding: a bad witness in the cell, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the decision), `no-final-status` (both statements), `schedule-dependent` (a same-ordinal pair), `ordinal-split` (witnesses at different ordinals only); `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)`. Existence is the reach axis. An `exh` witness against a certificate goes to obligation O10, which contests only the explored bound.
  - Permanent waits form a separate per-cell progress result (`stall-witness` with the set of clauses pre-use, in-use, horizon; `no-stall-within(b)`; `unknown(reason)`), evaluated on the product with an instance monitor, with kinds, qualifiers, wait-for roots (terminal strongly connected components of the wait-for graph) and progress chains (decidable conditions on the stall state). Only `exh` decides it; a realized hang with a wait-for fact, and deadlock cycles and leaked locks transferred between σ, can witness a stall. Timeout-masked-liveness is per instance, with the flag `tml-root` on every TML-relevant instance into which no chain leads.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Option B: keep `S_i = ∅` in protected and add a progress obligation to certificates. Rejected for the first implementation, for four reasons. It needs a new lock-order analysis, must-occurrence with progress, spawn completion, and checker steps of its own. It would withhold protected wherever M_impl models F7-type task loss. `S_i` is not preserved across σ. Star-closure exits in `Sk^cert` make it unprovable for actors that wait on loopers.
  - Reporting a stall as schedule-dependent (X1-M5). Withdrawn.
  - Making the classes exclusive with `unknown(horizon)` for the both-statements case (rev. 1). Rejected: the class then depends on the order of routes.
- **Consequences.** Certified verdicts are rendered as safety statements with reach and progress. Model stall witnesses count as detections only when confirmable and choice-free. A progress certificate can be added later as a new progress value without changing any class.

**ADR-040 — Finite-execution semantics, prefix containment and deadness.**
- **Context.** Maximal-execution containment fails for σ_U∞. The quiescence rule is ill-defined when several retries are pending, almost never holds with perpetual actors, and makes σ_U∞ depend on L1 (EXT-20261012 §4.2).
- **Decision.**
  - Pref and Max are separated, and safety is defined on Pref; results are kept per bound.
  - L-CONT is prefix containment. For σ_U∞ it is an equality with the σ_U prefixes without time-based transitions, because σ_U∞ removes every time-based transition (non-periodic timeouts, delay-paced last-attempt failures at their deciding transition at level M, and the give-ups that Φ declares, among them the ES kill timer's forced kill `expire-waiting`); periodic waits, timer ticks and the cleanup of exited apps stay; give-ups that Φ has not declared yet (HS's application monitor, TIME's flywheel) are labelled `tb-undeclared`. A poll that L1 summarizes is one transition, so its actor can be dead under σ_U∞, and deadness preservation is part of L1's obligation. σ_R1 is a separate system with a projection.
  - Stall states are defined by dead actors on product states, per occurrence, with the excluded states as sinks and no fairness assumption; σ_R1 deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation. Actors left with only unpaired time-based transitions are stranded and, like censored actors, make the tests in their closure undecided.
  - `dpor` uses an extended dependence (shutdown request against the instance's LPs; decisions against a monitored actor's transitions).
  - Refinements are derived from per-σ results; without the explorer none is established.
  - One process-terminal list is used everywhere; states after it are excluded, and stalls before a process end that stays reachable without the stop input are `terminal-pending`. `TRUNC` regions and stranded actors are censored transitively.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Keep maximal containment with stuttering. This needs a new execution notion and a re-proof of every transfer.
  - Keep the patient-exhaustion rule and make quiescence cone-relative.
  - Exclude idle loopers and stalls behind pending panics from stall evaluation. Rejected: qualifiers keep them visible without counting them.
- **Consequences.** Transfer moves safety statements only, each separately. A.2, A.3, A.4 step 3, A.5, A.6, A.7, L-PATIENT, L-TIMEDWAIT and L-H2 are restated, and progress results are relative to the closed-cone model.

**ADR-041 — Trust ledger and the checker's stated base.**
- **Context.** `kz proofcheck` trusts `facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, the closure, Φ and the shared `libkz-core`, and some of its steps call `libkz-core`'s evaluator. The specification nevertheless said that it re-derived "everything except extraction" and shared no code (EXT-20261012 §4.3).
- **Decision.**
  - §4.5 names the trusted base and the re-validated steps, and marks the steps that call `libkz-core` beyond reading inputs ("via T5").
  - Every certificate and verdict cell carries a four-part ledger (`KZ_TRUST_LEDGER`, `KZ_TRUST`); the ledgers of derived cells list the transfer lemmas and run evidence they add.
  - A-EXTRACT and A-CORE become listed assumptions.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Rebuild `Sk^cert` inside the checker. This needs LLVM inside the checker, which conflicts with its independence from producer code; it is left as an open question.
  - A second, checker-private evaluator for Φ conjuncts. Not adopted in the first implementation; the evaluator agreement property test and A-CORE cover it.
  - A report-only view.
- **Consequences.** Claims of independent verification are scoped, and the S3a gate requires the R2 ledger.

**ADR-042 — Comparison kinds, three outcomes, an exposure record and units (EXT-20261012 §5.1–§5.4).**
- **Context.** The stripped MCM/GPredict-style row was called a same-family baseline but explained as an ablation. GPredict was characterized by an inference about its formalism. Alarm yes/no could not show what certificates add, since a plain monitor given the same Req detects observed R2. Fix texts were taken as a source of blindness. Occurrences, instances, mutants and runs were tested as if independent.
- **Decision.**
  - Original tools, re-implementations with the same or their own information, and one-element ablations are kept apart. No ablation is read as a statement about a prior technique.
  - GPredict is described as built; its formalism's expressiveness stays [U] (P-PAPERS).
  - Detection, classification (with the outcome label and the progress result) and evidence are reported separately. Detection counts an alarm and a `not-ready-unjudged` report alike, for every tool. Only detection gets a paired test, for comparators and ablations alike. Classification is compared with a pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1; reference class statements ("race" for C1, C2, C3, C5) are scored only under a mapping the PI adopts (P-CLASSGT), and until then agreement or disagreement with them is reported as an outcome.
  - Blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record, relative to each item's governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items). Rater masking stays, as assessor masking.
  - Units U1–U6 are named, and every statistic states its unit and independence assumption (P-STATS).
  - Single-element ablations of Allowed and of outcome semantics are candidates (P-ABL-ALLOWED).
- **Consequences.** RQ8 compares models given the same information. Claims of added value rest on classification and evidence. Every design case is reported on its own phenomenon, and a result on R2 alone, or on R2's template in another context, is a single-case result.

**ADR-043 — Three judgements: framework level, caller level, observed** [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4). Context: Allowed mixed supplier-documented results with caller handling, a supplier requirement was proposed as a caller acceptance (STATUS T2), and unspecified Allowed erased observed effects. Decision: `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}` on every provenance record; entries with a `caller` record are horizon monitors, `rule`-only entries are monitors under the default and reported as rule-backed, supplier-only entries decide nothing; new field `appreq` with a precedence rule (it prevails over rules; a conflict with a `caller` entry leaves the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED until the PI sets a precedence); framework-level `fw_doc` per instance and `fw_conf` per cell in the reserved bytes; `consequence` always filled; outcome-label names kept. Alternatives: a full split of Allowed into two fields (more churn, same information); keeping the mix (the documented error return of R2 and the documented drop of SB then decide nothing consistently); treating the generic rules as caller-side (then a documented return code plus a branch discharges the caller's obligation with no caller text). Consequences: under the defaults no status of the five cases' rows changes, by a walk-through of the five cases made for the review response (`REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md`) [I]; supplier-postcondition contracts without an `appreq` become `not-ready-unjudged`; one litmus expectation is restated; tiers and rule-backed acceptances are reported apart; the census reports Allowed entries by side.

**ADR-044 — A validation ladder with per-case tracks as the first target (EXT-20261012 §7, §8).**
- **Context.** The thin MVC (S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a) was the first target. Its exit gates mix R2's chain with calibration items and items for other cases, so an R2-only slice cannot pass them as written. The external review found the plan large (its sum of the [I] estimates for S0–S3a is 19–28 person-weeks) and recommended the order: formal fixes, R2 end to end, a corrected-order control, C1's three outcomes, then held-out cases and pre/post-fix pairs. The PI found that checking concentrated on R2. A case-by-mechanism matrix showed that, without the basis decisions for C1 (D22), C3 and C5 (D13) and C2 (P-C2), Kairos can produce tool-level evidence on R2's phenomenon only. On the ENV basis C1 is latent, C2's own instance is absent, C3's site is part of R2's in-basis neighbours, and A.3 is a residual candidate apart from C5's #72.
- **Decision.**
  - The first target is rung V1: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2 and S2-R2. The full stage gates stay, as rung V1b.
  - V0 (the formal amendment, ADR-039–ADR-043) comes first. Its proof item V0-P writes and reviews the lemma obligations that V1's certificate and transfer wording uses before that wording is used; the rest are written before F1. The review's step-1 condition (definitions, proofs and result display agree) is met in full only then.
  - V2 and V3 follow V1 and V1b and run in parallel; neither waits for the other. Their results are reported in the review's order (step 3, then step 4), which is a reporting order, not a dependency.
  - V2 evaluates a corrected-order control basis chosen under P-R2CTRL. Its pre-registered record is "fixed-order not issued; open, chained to the App. A.2 root; not protected", and it is labelled configuration or revision sensitivity.
  - V3 activates C1 on a v7.0.1 B′ (D22), with a five-arm outcome partition (App. A.4 step 6). Arm A4 has no referenced basis and is shown by litmus unless the PI sets one as a recorded deviation.
  - V4a compares with same-information comparators after F1. V4b evaluates held-out sources and pre/post-fix pairs after F2, and reports added rules, manual cost and `unknown` reasons.
  - Case tracks run in parallel. All five cases are read and pre-registered now. C3 and C5 go on the 6.4.x pair (D13), C2 is role-derived (P-C2), and the A.3 line uses `58abd38d` (P-RGIT). Their decisions are requested now, and F1 waits for them to be decided or declined (P-F1GATE). The per-case paths and controls are normative (§9.3).
  - No case is reported through a v7.0.1 counterpart.
  - MLIR, SMT, DPOR and Φ templates that no rung, track or step needs are added only after a rung, a case track or an ENV-window step shows a need; the S3a census covers the templates the ladder needs, and other kinds are reported as not authored.
  - No effort figure is given for any rung.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - The thin MVC as the first target. Rejected: its gates contain non-R2 items, and the review's scale concern stands.
  - A strict R2 → C1 ladder as the only validation. Rejected: C2, C3 and C5 would meet the design only after F1, and the S5 gate and the S7 kill criterion depend on C1's B′.
  - C1 first. Rejected: C1 needs a derived basis and S5–S7 for any class, while V1 needs only D1.
  - V2 strictly before V3, as a dependency. Rejected: V3 needs nothing from V2, V2's default is "no referenced control", and the edge would make C1 wait for R2 twice.
  - Summing the estimates into a plan figure. Rejected: they are [I] planning guesses.
- **Consequences.**
  - R2's concentration remains at the evidence level until D13, D22 and P-C2 land, and this is reported, not hidden.
  - A cut that leaves R2 alone yields a single-case result. The core that the plan never cuts is not, by its completion, a publishable result (EXT-20261012 §9).
  - The S5 gate and the S7 review depend on D22 (P-D18GATES).
  - RQ1's coverage depends on which templates are authored, and unauthored kinds are reported as such.

## 12. Diagrams to draw

Each diagram lists the nodes and edges it must show. Edge labels follow a colon. Dashed edges are PI-gated or feedback paths.

### D1 — System context
- **Nodes**: PI/Researcher; `kz` and `kz-static` (root session); pinned cFS tree (read-only); `build-native_std` (compile DB, `link.txt`, generated files, `.tbl`, gcc `core-cpu1` and app `.so` with DWARF); LLVM 23 build `1053047a`; system clang-18 (BPF backend); Linux kernel `6.18.44-fc-v114` (perf uprobe PMU with attach cookies, `bpf()`, perf breakpoint PMU, ptrace, tracepoints); run harness `env/run_cfs.sh` with the root-side hook (D1) and `RUN_CFS_WRAP` (gdb, valgrind); cFS process `core-cpu1` (README mode, user `ubuntu`, 27 threads); persistent state (`/dev/shm/osal:RAM`, `EEPROM.DAT`, SysV segments, key files); research records; KZ artifact store; dashed PI-gated nodes: Z3 (P-Z3), libbpf (P-LIBBPF), `llvm-18-dev` + Nidhugg (P-NIDHUGG), F1 `--wrap` variant (D3), MLIR (D6), TSan variant (P-TSAN).
- **Edges**: PI → kz: Φ edits, decisions, freezes F1/F2; cFS tree → kz-static: sources; build tree → kz-static: compile DB, link, tables, binaries + DWARF; LLVM 23 → kz-static: libraries; clang-18 → kz: BPF object; harness → cFS process: launch as `ubuntu`, stop method (D17), normalization boots; kz → kernel: perf_event_open (uprobes by inode, breakpoints), `bpf()`, ptrace; kernel → cFS process: traps, holds; kernel → kz: ring buffers / perf samples; cFS ↔ persistent state; kz → persistent state: S0 snapshot (read-only); records → kz: reference values, policy; kz → store → PI: reports; dashed: the gated nodes → kz.

### D2 — Components and data flow
- **Nodes (components)**: `kz-static` {basis, extract, link, modref (dashed, S3b), skel, static, plan}; `kz` {proofcheck, observe, trace, conform, predict, explore (dashed border, S7), hold, gate (dashed, D3), loop, report, dump}; `libkz-core` (shared).
- **Nodes (artifacts)**: `basis.kz`, `profile.kzp/.kz`, `facts.kz` (incl. derives, writers, loop-exit trees), `anchors.kz`, `skel.kz` (cert view, exploration view, once, provenance), `census.kz`, `plan.kz`, `run-*.kzt`, `hb-*.kz`, `witness-*.kz`, `holdplan-*.kz`, `verdicts.kz`, `loop-*.kz`.
- **Edges**: basis → `basis.kz` → extract, link, skel; extract → `facts.kz`; link → `anchors.kz`; facts + anchors + profile → skel → `skel.kz`; skel + facts + profile → static → `census.kz` (annotate O7); census → proofcheck: certificates; facts + skel + anchors + profile + runs → proofcheck: trusted inputs (labelled "trusted"); proofcheck → census: accept/reject (CC) and trust ledgers; census + anchors → plan → `plan.kz`; plan → observe → `run-*.kzt`; run → trace → `hb-*.kz` (O3–O5, O9); run + skel → conform (O1, O2; O8 from S5); hb + facts → predict (A-COMM) → witness; skel + census → explore → witness (O6); witness → holdplan → hold/gate → `run-*.kzt`; analyses → `verdicts.kz` → report; loop orchestrates; dashed feedback F1 (keys, targets, opened script) → basis/skel; F2 (model gaps) → skel; F3 confirmation runs → trace; F4 obligation failures → profile (human review); `libkz-core` linked by everything except BPF.

### D3 — Analysis workflow (per contract instance and (σ, M) cell)
- **Decision nodes**: "template matched (certificate view)"; "key resolved?"; "reachable under B + provenance?"; "writer sets complete? (A-TYPE, O7)"; "provider: none / env-absent / present"; "FIXED_ORDER certificate? (once, frontier/once-PWB, derives)"; "PROTECTED certificate? (MUST_KEY, SCOPE)"; "kz proofcheck (re-validates inference steps; trusts facts, skel, Φ)"; "progress candidate (certified instances)"; "open: probe obligations"; "observation"; "routing" (conforming / wrong-way / O-ordered / unordered); "prediction (A-COMM label)"; "explorer (exh | dpor; σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched]; exploration view, h in b; Q_reach, Q_bad, Q_ok, Q_stall)"; "confirmation (naturalness, perturbation ratio)"; "combine per cell (§4.12)"; "transfer to σ_E (M_impl or M agreement)"; "obligation failure".
- **Terminal nodes**:
  - classes (per cell and bound, derived from the safety record): `inactive`, `fixed-order` (with a decision route), `protected` (safety statement; `tolerated` from `exh` only), `no-final-status`, `schedule-dependent`/`timeout-dependent`, `ordinal-split`, `unknown(reason)` (including `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown, and `contested`);
  - per-cell reach: `proved`, `assumed`, `model(b)`, `unreached(b)`, `contested`;
  - per-cell progress: `stall-witness` with its set of clauses (pre-use, in-use, horizon), kind, qualifiers and wait-for root, `no-stall-within(b)`, `unknown(not_checked | bound | budget | unmodelled | teardown_scope | …)`, with the per-instance refinement `timeout-masked-liveness`, the flag `tml-root` and progress chains;
  - axes: provider, scope; `σ_E(held)`.
- **Edges** as in v1, plus:
  - provider none → class by badness (not a separate terminal);
  - certificate → one safety statement with progress `unknown(not_checked)` and a progress candidate, which goes to the explorer if one is available;
  - explorer → safety statements and witnesses (Q_bad, Q_ok, per ordinal), reach (Q_reach) and progress (Q_stall), as separate outputs; a stall never leads into a class;
  - transfer → a safety statement in the receiving cell, combined with that cell's own statements;
  - progress result → its root via a progress chain;
  - M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary → portability-hazard-evidence;
  - σ_E cell → `unknown(model_level)` when transfer rule 2 fails;
  - transfer to σ_E carries the safety statement only (b = ∞);
  - suspension → loop end.

### D4 — Research workflow
- **Nodes**: Φ authoring (provenance, authoring records, refinement accounting); validation ladder (first target, ADR-044): V0 formal fixes (with V0-P, the written lemma obligations) → V1 R2's K-ID chain on the ENV basis (sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2, S2-R2); V1b (the rest of the thin MVC gates); V2 R2's control basis B′ and V3 C1 on its B′, in parallel; V4a same-information comparison; V4b held-out and pre/post-fix pairs; case tracks: paper reading and frozen expectation sheets for all five cases, C3 and C5 on the 6.4.x pair, C2 role-derived, the A.3 line; stages S0 → S1 → S2 → S3a (thin MVC) → S4 → S5 (+O8) → S6 → S7 (kill criterion) → S8; S3b (dashed, conditional); freeze F1 (incl. comparators, evaluation criteria and the pre-registered expectation sheet); template-only development runs; labelled refinements; freeze F2 (incl. recall protocol and the exposure record); held-out sources (a)–(e); recall mutants and pre/post-fix pairs; sampled triage (masked raters; rater exposure recorded); ablations (22, scored on labels); comparators (original tools; same- and own-information re-implementations) with detection / classification / evidence, and helgrind/DRD; exposure and freeze record; perturbation groups A, B, C′, C, D (per plan), W; normalization boots; statistics (assumptions; N and caps as deviations); claims table; PI decisions; deviation records.
- **Edges**: the stage sequence; S3a → F1 → template-only → refinements → F2 → held-out and recall; S1 → perturbation groups; stages → ablations, comparators, triage → statistics → claims table; exposure and freeze record → F1, F2; V0 → V1; V0-P → V1's certificate wording; V1 → V2; V1 → V3 (no edge between V2 and V3: the order of EXT-20261012 §7, step 3 then step 4, is a reporting order); S0, S1, S2, S3a → V1 (sub-gates) and V1b (full gates); V1b → V2 (the App. A.2 instance); V1b → V3 (the app-unit closure and the app `.so` probe registration); S5, S6, S7 → V3 (classes, holds); F1 → V4a → F2 → V4b; paper track → V1 and every case track (sheets frozen before tool output); case tracks → F1 (template-only on their own basis, or a recorded reason); the 6.4.x track ⇢ S10 unless D13 (c) places it before F1; decisions → the rungs, tracks and stages they gate (P-LADDER → V1; D20 → V0; D1, D17 → S1; D14 → S1, S9; P-R2CTRL → V2; D22, P-C1HOLD → V3; D13 → the 6.4.x track; P-C2 → the C2 track; P-RGIT → the A.3 track; P-D18GATES, D15 → S5/S7; D16 → S6; P-F1GATE → F1; P-RECALL, P-TRIAGE, P-STATS, P-CLASSGT → S9; D3, D4 → S10); every deviation → deviation record.

### D5 — cFS layer mapping
- **Left (cFS)**: PSP (main thread → `OS_Application_Run`, `soft_timebase`/`cFS-Master`, reset decision and boot record, persistence areas, `CFE_PSP_Panic` (terminal), `CFE_PSP_Restart` (terminal for the requester; ShutdownReq; teardown on the main thread), exception ring); OSAL (idmap with by-ID/by-name tokens and timed waits, tasks, mutex (PI), binsem, countsem, queues (`msg_max`, euid), timebase/timers, modules/symbols with scope, files); cFE ES (startup thread, `CreateObjects` (EarlyInit; core loop: reserve `:803`, start `:810`, publish `:818`, clear `:823`, sync `:835`, panic), script selection and `StartApplications`, `AppCreate` (reserve `:821`, publish `:869`, free `:893`), trampoline, App/Task/Lib records, `SystemState`, `WaitForSystemState`, `MainTaskSyncDelay`, background task, `CleanUpApp` (core-table cleanup order)); EVS (register with memset, `SendEventWithAppID` two conjuncts, own-ID guard); SB (pipes, routes, route_lookup and puts, MsgLim, receive, subscription reporting in SB's task, header MsgIds, recursion guard); TBL; TIME (1 Hz chain, seqlock, synch slots by index); FS background; CDS; apps with tables and the selected script.
- **Right (Φ)**: kinds (incl. `es.self`, `cell(loc)`, `sb.msglim`, `es.shutdown`, `loader.sym`); operations with per-conjunct LPs; rules R1–R19, SR, P-*, RF; contracts (K-REG, K-ID, K-SELF, K-SPAWN, K-PHASE, K-ROUTE, K-CAP, K-CAP.msglim, K-MODE, K-TBL, K-PERSIST, K-HANDOFF, K-LIFE, K-GEN, K-LOCKBAL, K-LOCK.cancel, K-ATOM, K-TEARDOWN); actor classes; model levels; P_sched profiles.
- **Edges** (examples): `:835` + panic → R19 (S, obs-fatal, predicate from code); `:803/:818/:823` → `es.app` RESERVED/LIVE/clear + allocation record ρ; `cfe_sb_task.c:138` → `cell(CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` + K-ID capture point; `OS_QueuePut` full → K-CAP; MsgLim → K-CAP.msglim; `osapi-idmap.c:384–385` vs `:401–404` → K-LOCKBAL (M_impl, by-name); `cfe_psp_support.c:87` → `es.shutdown` SHUTDOWN_REQ.

### D6 — Verdict state machine (per cell)
- **States** (class region, per cell and bound): `candidate`, `open`; `inactive`; `protected[cert]`, `protected[model b]`, `tolerated[model b]`; `fixed-order[cert]`, `fixed-order[model b]`; `no-final-status`; `ordinal-split`; `schedule-dependent` with refinement `timeout-dependent`; `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`); `unknown(not_reached | model_gap | bound | budget | key_unresolved | loss | unmodelled | writer_incomplete | horizon | no_decision_route | conformance | model_fault | model_level)`.
- **Parallel regions**:
  - evidence realization: `none` → `predicted-modelled` | `predicted-extrapolated` | `model(b)` | `predicted` → `realized`;
  - naturalness: `natural` | `held-F0` | `gated-F1` | `timeout-exercised`; `derived`;
  - provider;
  - **reach**: `assumed` → `proved` (b = ∞); `assumed` → `model(b)` | `unreached(b)` → `contested` (bounded b);
  - scope;
  - **progress**: `unknown(not_checked)` → `unknown(bound | budget | unmodelled | model_gap)` | `stall-witness[model b]` → `stall-witness[realized]` or `contested` | `stall-witness[realized]` (σ_E) | `no-stall-within(b)` → `contested`; `unknown(teardown_scope)` for K-TEARDOWN; flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`, set of clauses, kind, qualifiers and wait-for root; progress chain;
  - trust ledger (present on certificate-based states).
- **Transitions** (class region):
  - candidate → inactive / fixed-order[cert] / protected[cert] (static, per M; progress stays `unknown(not_checked)`);
  - candidate → open;
  - open → schedule-dependent (bad + good witness at the same ordinal);
  - open → ordinal-split (bad + good witnesses at different ordinals); ordinal-split → schedule-dependent (a same-ordinal pair found);
  - open → fixed-order[model b] / protected[model b] / tolerated[model b] / no-final-status (`exh`; (no, no) gives no-final-status);
  - a certified `¬OK_i` whose REJECT events lie after the decision, in its own cell or transferred → unknown(no_decision_route) with the statement shown; a later bad witness in the cell or `¬Bad_i` moves it to fixed-order or no-final-status;
  - protected or fixed-order → no-final-status (the other statement established, by `exh` or transfer);
  - schedule-dependent → refinement (derived from per-σ cells; `exh` only);
  - open → unknown(budget);
  - any → unknown(model_fault) (O3/O4/O9 against a certified statement; loop ends);
  - model-only statement + contradicting realized witness within its bound → contested (O6; no loop end);
  - certified or certificate-transferred statement + contradicting `exh` witness within its bound → the explored bound becomes contested (O10; directed check; the certificate's b = ∞ record keeps its state with a persistent contested mark; no loop end unless the witness is realized);
  - M_api cert + contradicting observation → portability-hazard-evidence (no state change at M_api);
  - unknown(model_gap | model_fault) → candidate (new Φ hash; new loop);
  - evidence refuted → model-fault record.
- **Transitions** (progress region):
  - unknown(not_checked) → stall-witness / no-stall-within(b) (`exh`, `Q_stall`);
  - unknown(not_checked) → stall-witness[realized] (σ_E: a wait-for fact that shows permanence, without a model witness);
  - stall-witness[model b] → stall-witness[realized] (wait-for oracle; confirmable kinds only);
  - stall-witness[model b] → contested (O6: the prefix to the stall state is realized and a witnessing actor is then observed to proceed);
  - no-stall-within(b) → contested (realized stall within b; O6);
  - no transition from a certificate into the progress region;
  - there is no protected[cert] → tolerated transition.
- **Invariants**:
  - no state is named "safe", "timing-protected", "live" or "deadlock-free";
  - no transition goes from "no alarm" to a universal class or to `no-stall-within(b)`;
  - σ_E universal (safety) states arise only via transfer rule 2 with b = ∞;
  - a stall witness never moves the class region;
  - every non-suspending transition moves up within its bound.

### D7 — Observation pipeline
- **Nodes**: `plan.kz` (sites, cookies, decoders, cost classes, debug-register allocation); loader (raw `bpf()`/`perf_event_open`, `<elf.h>`) | perf-sample backend | optional tracefs instance; uprobe registrations by (inode, offset) on `core-cpu1` and app `.so` (before launch); per-task write watchpoints; BPF programs (entry, point, return, watch); site-descriptor array indexed by cookie; per-thread `tseq`; per-object `objseq`; per-CPU ring buffers / perf buffers; drop counters / `PERF_RECORD_LOST`; drainer (CPU time and placement recorded); `run-*.kzt` (SITES, RAW_EVENTS, STRINGS, STACKS, LOSS, RUNMETA, PERTURBATION); `kz trace`; `kz hold` (ptrace, DR0–DR3, attach at clone); harness root hook; normalization-boot driver; cFS threads.
- **Edges**: plan → loader: register by inode, load descriptors; thread hits probe → BPF: trap (0.57 µs at entry+4; 31.7 µs class otherwise); BPF → cookie → descriptor; BPF → `tseq`/`objseq`; BPF → buffer (failure → drop counter); buffer → drainer → `run-*.kzt`; drainer → `kz hold`: live stream (release on event); `kz hold` → thread: stop/continue; harness hook → loader: start before launch; abnormal exit → normalization boot (unobserved, excluded); `run-*.kzt` → `kz trace`.

### D8 — The R2 certificate (ES and EVS halves)
- **Nodes**: capture points `c` (loads of `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` at argument evaluation in the SB event sites inlined in `CFE_ES_TaskInit` / `CFE_EVS_TaskInit`) and the slot checks; `m_ES` (return of the `CFE_ES_TaskInit` call, `cfe_es_task.c:114`), `m_EVS` (`cfe_evs_task.c:213`); raises `cfe_es_task.c:134`, `cfe_evs_task.c:229`; falsifiers `f_ES`, `f_EVS` (`cfe_es_start.c:803` per iteration) with allocation records ρ_ES, ρ_EVS; failure clears `:823` per iteration; observations `o_ES`, `o_EVS` (`:835`) with the extracted predicate; panic branches; SB spawn (`:810`, SB iteration); SB entry; writer `x` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`); INIT (zero-initialized `CFE_SB_Global`).
- **Edges**: `c` ↛ `m` (no path: FRONTIER); `m` →T-PO raise →T-OBS `o`; `:823` ✕ (OWN-NOPATH); `raise_ES` ✕ from `W_o(o_EVS)` (ONCE-PWB); `o` →T-PO (panic cut by T-TERM) SB spawn →T-SPAWN SB entry →T-PO `x`; INIT → "AppId = 0" (INIT_FALSE); `derives(c, CFE_SB_Global.AppId)`; a box listing every writer entry with transition and key status, and a trust-ledger box with the four parts of App. A.1 step 9a (re-validated steps, extracted facts, trusted entries, refutable items).

### D9 — Teardown scoping
- **Nodes**: requester actor (`CFE_ES_ResetCFE` → `CFE_PSP_Restart`: shm delete if POWERON → boot record → `ShutdownReq` store → `SIGUSR1` → delay `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` → `abort`, terminal); PSP main thread (`CFE_ES_Main` → return → `OS_Application_Run` sigwait loop → observe `ShutdownReq` → delay 100 → `OS_DeleteAllObjects`); in-scope uses; K-TEARDOWN uses; next run's B.Init.
- **Edges**: `ShutdownReq` store →T-OBS `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (SR ∈ MB); `OS_DeleteAllObjects` ⇢ disabler of every `osal.obj` (SCOPE-excluded from in-scope certificates); occurrences decided after SR → K-TEARDOWN; POWERON → clean Init; other exits → PROCESSOR reset → normalization boot.

### D10 — Two skeleton views
- **Nodes**: an actor CFG with init prefix, message loop (handlers H1, H2), exit; `Sk^cert` (loop head, back edge, iterate/exit choice, dispatch folded by `Prov`); `Sk^h` for h = 1, 2 (unrolled copies, `TRUNC` marker); consumers: MustBefore/certificates/inactive (from `Sk^cert` only), explorer/O8 (from `Sk^h`, h in b), proofcheck view check.
- **Edges**: CFG → `Sk^cert`; CFG → `Sk^h`; `Sk^cert` → static; `Sk^h` → explore; certificate step naming a `Sk^h` node → proofcheck: reject; litmus App. A.8 annotated "writer only in handler iteration 2".
