## 13. Glossary

| Term | Meaning in this specification |
|---|---|
| **Actor / actor instance** | a thread of execution (OSAL task, raw thread or environment root); an instance is (static actor, generation) |
| **Allocation record ρ(n)** | the abstract record allocated by reservation node n (§4.2) |
| **Allowed** | the not-ready outcomes a contract accepts, from a finite vocabulary, each with a reference horizon; at the caller level only entries with a caller-side reference count (§1.6) |
| **Anchor / anchor audit** | the mapping of a source site or AST pattern to an instruction of the gcc binary; the audit lists sites without a unique anchor |
| **appreq** | the reference stating what a use must achieve for the calling application or the mission; default `unspecified_by_reference`; never a supplier-side text or a rule; it prevails over `rule`-only Allowed entries, and a conflict with a `caller` entry is recorded and leaves the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED until the PI sets a precedence (§1.6) |
| **A-COMM** | the per-witness check that unobserved shared reads in the cone are observed, constant or covered by kept outcomes (§4.9) |
| **Assumed visibility** | an edge that holds only under A4; flagged on every verdict that uses it |
| **A-TYPE, A-FRESH, A-OWNER** | typed-access, allocation-freshness and ownership assumptions (§6.1) |
| **B (basis)** / **B′** | everything fixed except the schedule (§1.7) / a configuration or input variant: a new basis, never a "new bug" |
| **Bad / good witness / stall state** | a finite execution with a VIOLATING (or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED) in-scope occurrence / a finite execution with a READY or TOLERATED in-scope occurrence, each recorded with its occurrence ordinal (§1.6) / a reachable product state in which an actor dead before, inside or holding the horizon of an occurrence of the use exists (§1.8) |
| **Capture point** | the load from which a value-carried argument derives; the LP of a value conjunct (§1.4) |
| **Census** | the instantiation of every contract at every context-qualified site, each with a class or `unknown(reason)` per (σ, M) |
| **Certificate** | a checkable proof object (rule instances, writer entries with statuses, once facts, derives facts, extracted predicates, frontier, reach) for one **safety statement**: `¬OK_i` (fixed-order when its REJECT is at the occurrence's decision), or `¬NR_i`, no reached occurrence decided not ready (protected). `kz proofcheck` re-validates its inference steps relative to a trusted base, marking the steps that call `libkz-core`, and its trust ledger records them |
| **Certificate view `Sk^cert` / exploration view `Sk^h`** | skeleton with every unbounded loop star-closed / skeleton unrolled to horizon h with truncation marks (§4.3) |
| **Cell** | the part of a verdict record for one (σ, M) |
| **Chain** | a violation link `i₁ ⇝ i₂` between occurrences, recorded with every branch (§1.6); a **progress chain** `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` attributes a stall of i₂ to a not-ready outcome of i₁ (§1.8) |
| **Comparison kinds (K1, K2, K3)** | an original tool run as released (K1; `NOT_RUN` is never a false negative) / a re-implementation of a prior technique given the same property information or its own (K2) / a Kairos ablation that removes one element (K3), which says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3, §8.4) |
| **Cone** | the closed set of actors and objects relevant to a candidate (§4.10) |
| **C_σ** | schedule constraints of σ_R1; never HB; never combined with an observed prefix |
| **Dead actor** | an actor instance that is not terminated and that no reachable state enables (§1.8); independent of fairness; under σ_R1 measured with σ_U's enabling relation |
| **DECLARED_TRUSTED** | a writer, allocation or ownership entry that rests on a Φ guard or assumption the analysis could not verify; listed in every certificate that uses it |
| **derives(arg, loc, site)** | the fact that an argument's value is a copy chain from one shared location read at site |
| **Detection / classification / evidence** | the three outcomes recorded separately per tool and labelled unit: whether a not-ready occurrence of the expected instance is reported (an alarm and a `not-ready-unjudged` report count alike) / the class, refinements, outcome label and progress result / route, realization, bound, scope, reach and trust ledger (§8.3, §8.5) |
| **E-post / E-TBL** | R2's in-basis negative control on the ENV basis: the K-ID instances that read SB's identity cell after its publication at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` / TBL's identity cell; no fixed-order verdict is expected; C3's v7.0.1 site lies in E-post (§9.3) |
| **Evidence: realization / naturalness** | how a class is supported (certificate … realized) / how natural a realized run was (natural … timeout-exercised) |
| **exh / dpor** | the explorer's exhaustive stateful engine / source-DPOR engine (unverified while T-JACM is P) |
| **Exposure and freeze record; blind** | the append-only record of who saw which case, fix and expected verdict, and when, with the freezes and the post-freeze changes / an item label given only where that record shows no exposure before the item's governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items) (§7.2, §7.5 item 5) |
| **F0–F3** | fidelity classes of [R07 §7] |
| **Framework-level judgement (`fw_doc`, `fw_conf`)** | what the supplier's references say about a not-ready result, and whether the result occurred as documented; never combined with the outcome label (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **Finite execution Pref / maximal execution Max** | the finite sequences of enabled transitions (safety is defined on them) / the finite executions with no enabled transition plus the fair infinite ones (used only for non-claims) (§1.8) |
| **Falsifier** | an event in the observer's actor that dominates an observation and definitely makes the observed conjunct false |
| **Fixed-order** | class: `¬OK_i` established and deciding (a bad witness in the cell, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the occurrence's decision), `¬Bad_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is READY or TOLERATED, and not-ready ones are shown bad; that every execution reaches a decision is not claimed; without a decision route the class is `unknown(no_decision_route)` |
| **Frontier node m** | a node in the use's once actor with no path to the conjunct's LP and `m ∈ MB(x)` for every writer x |
| **Generation** | an incarnation of a resource or actor |
| **HB_π, VC, VC_R** | happens-before on a trace (looper rule applied); vector clocks over all definite edges and over unconditional/obs-fatal edges |
| **Horizon h** | message-loop iterations in the exploration view; not chosen; results reported per completed h |
| **Idle wait / hang state** | the first blocking wait of an iteration of a star-closed message or periodic loop / a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending (§1.8) |
| **Inactive** | class: no execution reaches the use under B (certificate view, with message provenance) |
| **KEM** | Kairos Event Model |
| **Key / key status** | the identity of a resource within its kind / MUST_KEY, MAY_KEY or NO_KEY of a writer w.r.t. an instance key |
| **K-SELF.es** | the generic contract "the caller's own App and task records are LIVE", at seven call sites |
| **K-TEARDOWN** | the instances `(κ, t, key, teardown)`, holding the occurrences whose decision event follows a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP: the complement of the default scope, classified by the same statuses and reported apart; progress `unknown(teardown_scope)` |
| **KZ container** | the typed binary artifact format (§2.3) |
| **L1/L2/L3** | lemma obligations: await summarization with extracted predicates; removal of a critical section whose lock is not a cone object and is acquired by no other cone actor, and whose body touches nothing in the cone and contains no blocking operation; mover-reduced critical sections |
| **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER** | caller-level outcome label for route/capacity contracts whose caller-level Allowed is unspecified by reference; the framework-level judgement and the observed loss are reported beside it (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **LP** | linearization point, declared per conjunct |
| **M_api / M_impl** | OSAL model levels: API-contract model atomic at LPs / OSAL POSIX internals analysed |
| **M_impl-relevant** | an operation modelled differently at the two levels (idmap, task, binsem, queue paths) |
| **MB (MustBefore)** | static must-precedence, the greatest fixpoint of §4.4 |
| **Message provenance Prov(p)** | the MsgIds (and codes) that can reach pipe p under B (§4.3) |
| **Normalization boot** | an unobserved boot ending with the POWERON restart, run after an abnormal exit (§4.13) |
| **No-final-status** | class: both `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` established; every reached occurrence is PENDING (or `NOT_EVALUATED`), or none is reached (§1.10) |
| **Normal continuation / error exit** | for `returned_and_tested`: the node named in the Allowed entry, by default the nearest post-dominator of the test, per loop iteration, that is not the function's return and at which neither the status nor the not-ready branch's writes are live (ACCEPT; a REJECT at the same node wins) / a call of `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_PSP_Restart`, or a terminal op (REJECT); an error return is also a REJECT unless a reference documents propagation (§1.6) |
| **O1–O10, CC** | consistency obligations and the certificate check (§2.5); O10 is model–certificate agreement: an `exh` witness against a certified or transferred statement makes the bound contested and starts a directed check |
| **Occurrence / ordinal / ordinal-split** | an occurrence begins at its call, with its value conjuncts' captures attached, and is decided by the call's return at the latest (`NOT_EVALUATED`, counted by no statement, if the call returned before one of its conjunct LPs) / (actor instance named across executions, k) for the k-th occurrence of the template with the instance's key begun by that actor / class: bad and good witnesses exist only at different ordinals; no schedule dependence is claimed (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **once(τ), once(n)** | the actor has at most one instance per execution / the node occurs at most once per execution (§1.2) |
| **On-behalf frame** | an identity frame for code run in one actor on behalf of another |
| **PENDING / horizon monitor** | a not-ready occurrence whose Allowed horizon has not ended; never counted as TOLERATED / the ACCEPT–REJECT–OPEN reading of an Allowed entry on its monitored actor (§1.6) |
| **Periodic wait** | a timed wait inside a star-closed loop whose timeout and success outcomes both continue the loop; its timeout is not a time-based transition (§1.8) |
| **Perturbation ratio** | probe and hold time added between two deciding events, over the code-derived separation protecting the other order (§4.13) |
| **Φ (profile)** | the declarative cFS knowledge base, with core expressions, bindings and patterns |
| **P_sched** | a cited target scheduling profile for σ_R1 |
| **Portability hazard (-evidence)** | protected under M_impl but violable under M_api / an M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary |
| **Pred(π)** | the predictive model of a trace |
| **Pre-registered expectation sheet** | per development case, basis, (σ, M, b) cell and instance: the expected class, refinements, progress value and outcome label, with any reference class statement; frozen by hash at F1 (§7.2) |
| **Process-terminal step** | a terminal step that ends the process (on pc-linux: `abort`, `CFE_PSP_Panic`, the restart requester's abort, `main`'s return after the teardown); later states are excluded from stall evaluation, no status or horizon verdict is decided by or after it, and a stall before a still-reachable one is terminal-pending (§1.6, §1.8) |
| **Progress result** | per cell and bound: stall-witness (pre-use, in-use or horizon; kind, qualifiers, harm, progress chain), no-stall-within(b) (`exh` only), or unknown(reason), by default not_checked; never "live" (§1.8, §1.10) |
| **Protected / tolerated** | class: `¬Bad_i` established, `¬OK_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED (with a certificate, `¬NR_i`: none is decided not ready); says nothing about executions that do not reach the use / protected with a TOLERATED occurrence (`exh` only) |
| **Provider axis** | present, none, env-absent, unknown |
| **PWB** | precedes-whenever-both-occur (FRONTIER or ONCE-PWB) |
| **Reach** | per cell and bound: proved (a run or confirmed model path of that σ at that M, with the per-cell conditions of §1.10), assumed, model(b), or unreached(b) (`exh`); a bound refuted by evidence is `contested` |
| **Recall protocol** | detection measured on history-derived program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs (§8.5) |
| **Robustness tag** | per trace edge: unconditional, obs-fatal, obs-soft, schedule-chosen, assumed |
| **Schedule-dependent / refinements** | class: a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal / timeout-dependent (`exh` only; needs σ_U∞ reach); flag timeout-rescued; per-instance progress refinement timeout-masked-liveness, with the flag `tml-root` on every TML-relevant instance into which no progress chain leads (a chain root, or an unchained TML instance) |
| **Scope** | part of the instance `(κ, t, key, scope)`: an occurrence belongs to the default-scope instance iff no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes its decision event, decided when the occurrence is decided; otherwise it belongs to the K-TEARDOWN instance |
| **Shape** | of a bad finite witness: WRONG-WAY, UNORDERED, ABSENT (no extension can contain the enabling transition), NOT-OBSERVED, STALE (§1.10) |
| **Side (`supplier` / `caller` / `rule`)** | of a provenance record, for a given contract: whether it speaks for the supplier's behaviour, is a text saying what the using code must achieve or accepts, or is no text but a fixed rule of Kairos applied to the using code (acceptances by `rule`-only entries are reported as rule-backed) (§1.6) |
| **σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched], σ_E** | untimed; σ_U without time-based transitions (non-periodic timeouts; the failing last attempt of a delay-paced retry, which then waits if it is one transition at M and otherwise leaves its actor stranded; the Φ-declared give-ups, among them the forced kill of an app that has not exited; the expiry for an exited app stays); fixed-priority one core under a profile, a separate system projected into σ_U; the real environment (finite runs) |
| **σ_E(held), σ_E(plan P)** | realized but not natural / a σ_E statement under observation plan P |
| **Stranded actor** | under σ_U∞, an actor whose only σ_U-enabled transitions are unpaired time-based ones (the failing-result branch of a last attempt that is several transitions at M); its deadness tests and those of its closure are undecided (§1.8) |
| **Support locations** | the memory a conjunct's oracle reads, its bindings, or its `derives` location |
| **T-PO, T-SPAWN, T-OBS, T-TERM** | the MustBefore rules (§4.4) |
| **Template-only run** | an evaluation run with the frozen profile and no case-specific refinement |
| **Time-based transition** | a non-periodic timed wait's timeout, the failing outcome of a delay-paced retry's last attempt, or a give-up that Φ declares time-based (every countdown or clock comparison whose expiry abandons a wait or acts on another actor must be declared; undeclared ones are labelled `tb-undeclared`); σ_U∞ removes them (§1.8) |
| **Transition status** | COMPUTED, DECLARED_VERIFIED, DECLARED_TRUSTED, DISABLED |
| **Trust ledger** | the four-part record of a certificate or verdict cell: re-validated steps (own or via T5), extracted facts, trusted entries, refutable items (§4.5.1) |
| **Units U1–U6** | defect cause, fix history, program variant, static instance, dynamic occurrence, run; every count names one, and no count of one unit is the sample size of another (§8.2) |
| **unspecified_by_reference** | no reference fixes the value, and no value is chosen; chosen values are deviations |
| **v7.0.1 counterpart** | a site or window at v7.0.1 that shares a design case's shape while the case itself is latent, absent or older (C1 in the default B, C2's barrier, C3's TIME/TBL contexts, App. A.3); it tests generic layers only and is never reported as evidence on the case (§9.3) |
| **Validation ladder (V0–V4, V0-P) / case track** | the first target and the order of the dynamic end-to-end work: the formal fixes (V0, with the written lemma obligations V0-P), R2's chain (V1), the rest of the thin-MVC gates (V1b), R2's control (V2) and C1 (V3) in parallel, comparison (V4a) and held-out evaluation (V4b) / a per-case path that starts when its basis decision lands (§9.3, ADR-044) |
| **Wait-for root** | of a stall: a terminal strongly connected component of its wait-for graph (a lock cycle, or a cycle of predicate and object waits), or a sink with the wait it leaves unsatisfied; for a leaked lock, the object and its holder; counted detections are grouped by the root set (§1.8) |
| **Work unit** | a deterministic count (states, transitions, search nodes, fixpoint updates) used for every analysis budget |
| **Writer set W / W^must / disabler set D** | stores that can make a conjunct true / definitely make it true / can make it false, for a key |

---

## Appendix A. Worked examples (expected behaviour; nothing here was run)

### A.1 R2: fixed-order from a generic contract plus framework rule R19, fully written out

1. **Census.** `K-ID.send-with-appid` (provenance `cfe_evs.h:168–172`) is instantiated at every context-qualified `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` site. The SB sites pass `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` as `stamp` (39 SB sites, [R2c §2]). Contexts inlined from `CFE_ES_TaskInit` and `CFE_EVS_TaskInit` are distinct templates (ADR-012). No SB-specific contract exists.
2. **Conjuncts and T-DERIVE** (fixes [X1-M1]). `Req = valid(arg.AppID) ∧ state(evs.reg(arg.AppID)) = REG`. At each SB event site, `derives(arg AppID, (CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId), c)` holds, where `c` is the load at argument evaluation (e.g. `cfe_sb_api.c:226–228`, re-read). The value conjunct `V` is evaluated at `c` over `cell(CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` (L-DERIVE). The registry conjunct plays no role here: EVS tests index validity first (`cfe_evs.c:185–189`), and `V` already fails.
3. **INIT and the writer set of V.** INIT: `CFE_SB_Global` is a zero-initialized global (`cfe_sb_task.c:40`, re-read), so `AppId = 0 = CFE_ES_APPID_UNDEFINED`: `INIT_FALSE(V)`. S3a writers of `(CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId)`:
   - `x`: the success-branch out-pointer store of `CFE_ES_GetAppID` instantiated at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` — W, COMPUTED, MUST_KEY (same location);
   - the failure-branch store of `UNDEFINED` (`cfe_es_api.c:758–760`) — neutral;
   - the EarlyInit `memset(&CFE_SB_Global, 0, …)` (`cfe_sb_init.c:50`, re-read) — writes 0, neutral;
   - A-TYPE listings must show no untyped write and no escaping address (`&CFE_SB_Global.AppId` is taken only at `:138`, into `GetAppID`'s summarized formal); otherwise the writer set is DISABLED and the certificate is withheld. Any further store found is listed.
4. **Observation `o_ES`** (ES iteration of the unrolled core loop; fixes [X2-01], [X1-M8]). `o_ES` is the success exit of `MainTaskSyncDelay` at `cfe_es_start.c:835`. `Pred_code` is extracted (form (b), §4.3): `∀ r ∈ AppTable: ¬(IsUsed(r) ∧ r.AppState < RUNNING)`. `C_ES` is its instance at `ρ_ES`, the record allocated by the falsifier `f_ES` = `SetUsed(RESERVED)` at `:803`. Right after `f_ES`, `ρ_ES` is used and its `AppState` is 0 (UNDEFINED): before `f_ES` no task exists, and the only `AppState` writer that can precede it is the zeroing memset at `:82`, so no freshness assumption is needed here.

   | Writer | Site | Actor | Treatment | Status |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | raise | `cfe_es_api.c:573` via `cfe_es_task.c:134` | ES main | kept; `m_ES ∈ MB` (T-PO) | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` STOPPED | `cfe_es_api.c:399` via `cfe_es_task.c:200` | ES main | kept; it comes after `raise_ES` and `m_ES` (T-PO), so it does not affect the result | COMPUTED [I] |
   | `RunLoop` raise | `cfe_es_api.c:471` | none of the concurrent actors | absent (call graph) | COMPUTED |
   | scan WAITING | `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` | `ES_BG_TASK` | neutral (same-record guard) | COMPUTED |
   | `CleanUpApp` record free | `cfe_es_apps.c:1407–1640` | `ES_BG_TASK` | excluded (external app with `AppState > RUNNING`; relational guard) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** |
   | failure clear `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:823` (ES iteration) | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH (only the panic follows) | COMPUTED |
   | EarlyInit `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:82` | startup thread | ONCE-PWB(w, `f_ES`) | COMPUTED |
   | later startup-thread writers (`:823` of later iterations, `cfe_es_apps.c:893`, script reservations) | — | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH (no path back to `o_ES`; the core loop is unrolled) | COMPUTED |
   | writers in EVS, SB, TBL, TIME, script apps and their children | — | spawned after `o_ES` | FRONTIER: `PWB(o_ES, w)` | COMPUTED |
   | `cFS-Master`, console | — | raw threads | no writers (S3a over their call graphs) | COMPUTED |

   Result: `m_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)` and `raise_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)`.
5. **Observation `o_EVS`** (EVS iteration; fixes [X1-M3](b)). Falsifier `f_EVS` = `:803` in the EVS iteration, record `ρ_EVS`. Right after `f_EVS`, `ρ_EVS` is used and `AppState(ρ_EVS)` = 0: this needs **A-FRESH** (`ρ_EVS` ≠ `ρ_ES` and never used before) and **A-OWNER** (ES main's and `ES_BG_TASK`'s `AppState` writers target `ρ_ES`, hence `NO_KEY` for `ρ_EVS`).

   | Writer | Site | Actor | Treatment | Status |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | raise | `cfe_es_api.c:573` via `cfe_evs_task.c:229` | EVS main | kept; `m_EVS ∈ MB` | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` stores | `cfe_evs_task.c:220, :257` | EVS main | kept; after `m_EVS` (no-ops inside the window, §3.3) | COMPUTED |
   | raise | `cfe_es_task.c:134` | ES main | **ONCE-PWB**: `once(raise_ES)` and `raise_ES ∈ MB(o_ES) ⊆ MB(f_EVS)` ⇒ `PWB(raise_ES, f_EVS)` | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` STOPPED | `cfe_es_task.c:200` → `cfe_es_api.c:399` | ES main | excluded: guard `Type != CORE` on the owner record, which is `ρ_ES` (A-OWNER) with `Type = CORE` (MUST_KEY store at `:795`, ES iteration); also KEY-SEP vs `ρ_EVS` under A-FRESH. Not dominated by `m_EVS` and not PWB-ordered (an early `ExitApp` after a timed-out CORE_READY wait is reachable in σ_U) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** (A-OWNER) |
   | scan WAITING | `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` | `ES_BG_TASK` | neutral | COMPUTED |
   | `CleanUpApp` record free | `cfe_es_apps.c:1407–1640` | `ES_BG_TASK` | excluded (relational guard) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** |
   | failure clears `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:823` (ES and EVS iterations) | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH | COMPUTED |
   | EarlyInit `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:82` | startup thread | ONCE-PWB | COMPUTED |
   | writers in SB, TBL, TIME, script apps | — | spawned after `o_EVS` | FRONTIER | COMPUTED |

   Result: `m_EVS ∈ MB(o_EVS)`.
6. **MustBefore chain.** `m_ES ∈ MB(raise_ES)` (T-PO) → T-OBS → `m_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)` → T-PO along the startup thread (panic branches have no successors, T-TERM; the failure branch through `:823` leads only to the panic) → `spawn_SB` (SB iteration) → T-SPAWN (single spawn site; `once(SB main)`) → SB entry → T-PO → `x`. Hence `m_ES ∈ MB(x)`. The EVS half is the same with `m_EVS` through `o_EVS`; the EVS iteration precedes the SB iteration in the generated core order ([R02 §1.3]).
7. **Frontier and once.** `once(ES main)` and `once(EVS main)`: each has one spawn node in the unrolled core loop, on the once startup thread, outside every star-closed region. `m_ES ∉ Anc(c)` for every capture point `c` inside `CFE_ES_TaskInit` (called once, before the message loop; in `Sk^cert` the loop's back edge returns to the loop head after `m_ES`). **The early returns do not matter**: `CFE_ES_TaskInit` returns at L340, L371, L381 or L393 after failures, and `CFE_ES_TaskMain` still raises at L134 (re-read); the proof never needs the use to dominate the raise ([J2-1]).
8. **Badness, in three judgements.** Caller level: `appreq` = `cfe_evs.h:169–172` (caller side), `Allowed = none` (the header names the system log, a different API, as the alternative); harm `event_lost_silently`. Framework level: `CFE_EVS_APP_ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, documented as `\retval` (`cfe_evs.h:199`), error severity (`cfe_error.h:115–119, 267`), with no counter and no event (`cfe_evs.c:186–189`): `fw_doc` = {`documented-result`}; `fw_conf` = `as-documented` in the cells backed by the runs (every violating use is followed by that result, [R2g §7]), `unknown` at M_api. Observed: every SB caller discards `ILLEGAL_APP_ID`; consequence "status ignored and effect lost silently".
9. **Verdict.**
   - **σ_U.** `fixed-order(σ_U, M_api)` and `fixed-order(σ_U, M_impl)`: the safety statement `¬OK_i` at b = ∞, deciding because `Allowed = none` decides at the occurrence (§1.10). By L-FO, every reached in-scope occurrence has `V` false at its capture point, so it is decided at its call and is `VIOLATING`. The ES-context `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses come from several templates: the version-event loop template has 13 ordinals per boot with the reference version table (one per module version event from `CFE_Config_IterateAll`'s loop, `cfe_config_get.c:180–187`; `cfe_es_task.c:235–241, 265, 466`; [R2g §7]), and the INIT_INF, INITSTATS, Mission-version and BUILD_INF events (EIDs 1, 2, 91, 92; `cfe_es_task.c:442, 449, 259, 287`) are four templates with one ordinal each. Whether the extractor resolves the loop's indirect callback call (`cfe_config_get.c:184`) into the same call strings is [U]. The frontier puts every occurrence of every template before the store at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, so there is no ordinal-split. The certificate contains task creation, which is an M_impl-relevant operation, so the M_impl certificate is computed on the M_impl skeleton, where T-SPAWN still holds.
   - **σ_U∞ and σ_R1.** These cells hold the same statement by transfer rule 1(a) (same M, b = ∞), with the flag `SAFETY_TRANSFERRED`; it stays deciding, because a REJECT at decision is not a time-based transition.
   - **Reach, per cell.**
     - (σ_U, M_impl): `proved` once S1 shows a run with an occurrence (L-CONT (c)).
     - (σ_U, M_api): the cone contains task creation, so L-MLEVEL does not apply; `proved` only if the prefix of every closed-cone actor up to the occurrence passes the M_api-conformance check (L-MAPI-CONE; §4.7 item 6: no `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`, no lost task, no leaked type lock); otherwise `assumed`.
     - σ_U∞ (both M): in addition, the transitions of the closed cone's actors on the prefix contain no time-based transition (L-REACH-CONE): no soft-barrier or poll timeout, no exhausted delay-paced retry, and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake, which the ES, EVS and SB children's self-lookups in `OS_TaskPrepare` can take (§3.3) and which only the prefix probes show; otherwise `assumed`.
     - σ_R1: `assumed`.
   - **σ_E.** The cell comes from M_impl by transfer rule 2: no in-scope occurrence of any real run is `READY`. Reach is `proved` once S1 re-observes an occurrence.
   - **Progress.** Every cell has progress `unknown(not_checked)`, because certificates make no progress claim. If the explorer runs, at M_api σ_U∞ stall states of these instances are expected, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`), only behind a skipped core raise, chained to the App. A.2 root (`tml-root`); at M_impl also behind a core task lost to F7 (`OS_TaskEntryPoint` calls `OS_TaskExit` after a failed `OS_TaskPrepare`, `osapi-task.c:128–137`), chained to that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence. They are not R2 findings, and the statement is relative to default B [I].
   - **Evidence.** Realization is `certificate`, plus `realized/natural` observations `n of N` with their pool key.
   - **Shape.** WRONG-WAY where SB publishes later, in the witness or its recorded run. ABSENT where SB's own window is hit: SB's `GetAppID` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` is a once node whose failure branch has then already been taken, so no extension of the witness contains the success store (§1.10). Both are bad.
   - **Trust ledger**: see step 9a. Part (iii) contains the `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries:
     - ES half: the `CleanUpApp` relational guard;
     - EVS half: A-FRESH, A-OWNER and the `CleanUpApp` guard.

     The sub-label `trusted:<n>` counts these entries. All of them can be refuted by O9 and O3/O4, and none is proved by them.
9a. **Trust ledger of the R2 certificates** (EXT-20261012 §4.3). There is one certificate ledger per half and per M, for the cells (σ_U, M_api) and (σ_U, M_impl) at b = ∞. The ledgers of the other cells are derived from them (§4.5.1). The contents below are expected [I]; nothing was run.
   - **(i) Re-validated by `kz proofcheck`** ("own" unless marked "via T5").
     - *view*: every step lies on `Sk^cert`.
     - *ONCE*: the following once facts:
       - once(PSP main), by definition;
       - once(ES main), once(EVS main) and once(SB main): each has a single spawn node in the unrolled core loop, on the once startup thread, outside every star-closed region, so the exclusivity condition of once(τ) holds trivially; the three spawn nodes are distinct and run one after another;
       - once(`raise_ES`) and once(`cfe_es_start.c:82`).
     - *T-PO*, by the checker's dominator routine:
       - `m_ES ∈ MB(raise_ES)` (ES half) and `m_EVS ∈ MB(raise_EVS)`, the raise at `cfe_evs_task.c:229` (EVS half);
       - the kept `ExitApp` stores after their half's `m`: ES main's `ExitApp` STOPPED via `cfe_es_task.c:200`, which follows `:134` (step 4's `ExitApp` row); EVS main's `ExitApp` stores at `cfe_evs_task.c:220, :257`, which follow `m_EVS` (A.1 step 5);

       - SB entry → `x`;
       - for every FRONTIER exclusion, the startup-thread path from `o_ES` (resp. `o_EVS`) to the spawn site of the writer's actor or of its spawner.
     - *T-TERM*: the declarations of `CFE_PSP_Panic` and `abort` for pc-linux, with their citations, on which the panic cuts of T-PO rest. Their truth is a T4 entry.
     - *T-SPAWN*: SB main's spawn-site list equals the closure's list (one site, `:810` in the SB iteration); and, for every actor whose writers the FRONTIER exclusions of A.1 steps 4–5 remove (EVS, SB, TBL, TIME, the script apps and their children), its spawn-site list equals the closure's list, each site lying after `o_ES` (resp. `o_EVS`) on the once startup thread or in an actor spawned after it.
     - *T-OBS* at `o_ES` and `o_EVS`:
       - the falsifier condition at `:803` (via T5: evaluated on the abstract post-state);

     - *INIT_FALSE(V)* (via T5): evaluated on the INIT image.
     - *badness*: `Allowed = none`, so every not-ready occurrence is `VIOLATING` at decision.
     - *statuses*: every transition and key status is consistent with the writer sections, and the `DECLARED_TRUSTED` list is complete.
     - *reach* (per cell, step 9): after S1, the run exists, contains an occurrence, and, for the (σ_U, M_api) cell, passes the M_api-conformance check on every closed-cone actor. The absence of time-based transitions on the cone actors is checked in the derived σ_U∞ cells' ledgers, not here (§4.5.1).

10. **Refinement accounting** ([X1-M8]): "generic contract K-ID + framework rule R19". R19 binds `MainTaskSyncDelay`'s loop at `:835`, its stratum and its terminal escape; the predicate is extracted from the code and re-derived by `kz proofcheck` from the extractor's serialized `:835` exit trees (trust ledger part (ii)).
11. **S3a exit mutations** (each must be rejected): `m_ES` replaced by a node inside `CFE_ES_TaskInit` before `c` (in `Anc(c)`); the `once(ES main)` fact dropped; the ONCE-PWB fact for `raise_ES` dropped from the EVS certificate; `:823` re-inserted into `W_o(o_ES)` without its OWN-NOPATH proof; the extracted predicate replaced by one that forgets that RESERVED counts as used; the `derives` link dropped; a certificate step moved into a `TRUNC` region.
12. **Expected per-use set** (a check of basis sensitivity and the S1 gate, not new evidence): ES 20 = 17 `SEND_NO_SUBS` + 2 `SUBSCRIPTION_RCVD` + 1 `PIPE_ADDED`; EVS 4 = 1 `SEND_NO_SUBS` + 2 `SUBSCRIPTION_RCVD` + 1 `PIPE_ADDED` (the measured split, `results/R2_gdb_v701.md:47–49`; the earlier "2 + 1 + 1", read in ES's order, contradicted it); the 17 ES `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses come from five context-qualified templates, 13 from the version-event loop and one each from EIDs 1, 2, 91 and 92 (`results/R2_gdb_v701.md:101`); total 24 per boot with ENV's version table ([R2g §2]; [R2c §7]); 11 (ES 7, EVS 4) in a basis whose version table is NULL ([R2c §4.2]).
13. **Chain.** The 17 `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses (13 from the version-event loop template and one from each of four single-ordinal templates, step 9) are linked to K-ROUTE instances for ES's event packets (MsgId 0x808), transmitted before any subscription ([R2g §2]). Their consumer is TO_LAB's telemetry pipe, which subscribes to `CFE_EVS_LONG_EVENT_MSG_MID` from its table (`to_lab_sub.c:50`, re-read; that this MID is 0x808 is [I]), and whose documented deferral (TO_LAB subscribes after `CFE_ES_WaitForStartupSync`, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`) is `defer_by_design` (§3.15 RC5): the monitored actor is TO_LAB's main task, which does not exist yet at ES's transmits, so each occurrence is PENDING at its transmit and TOLERATED when TO_LAB executes its subscription, or VIOLATING at the same ordinal when TO_LAB terminates or can no longer subscribe first, e.g. when its own App-record window makes `TO_LAB_init`'s `CFE_EVS_Register` fail (`to_lab_app.c:134`, App. A.3); the K-ROUTE instance is therefore schedule-dependent, chained to that window's occurrence [I]. A panic decides none of these occurrences (§1.6). A K-ROUTE instance whose consumer has no documented deferral has `Allowed = unspecified_by_reference` and the label LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm unspecified. The K-ROUTE template for ES's version-event packets is reached once per module version event from `CFE_Config_IterateAll`'s loop (`cfe_config_get.c:180–187`; `cfe_es_task.c:235–241, 265, 466`), so it has several ordinals per boot and is classified per ordinal. For a K-ROUTE instance whose consumer has **no** documented deferral, early-without-route and later-with-route occurrences give `ordinal-split`, not schedule-dependent, unless a same-ordinal pair is shown; for TO_LAB's pipe, whose deferral is documented, the early occurrences are PENDING and then TOLERATED, or VIOLATING at the same ordinal, as above.
13a. **The chain in three judgements.** Framework level for the 17 K-ROUTE occurrences of step 13 (ES's event packets; MsgId 0x808 [I], [R2g §2]): `fw_doc` = {`documented-not-error`} (cSB4305.5's rationale); `fw_conf` = `deviates` in the cells and runs of this window, because the `SEND_NO_SUBS` events that cSB4305.5's shall-text requires are rejected by EVS with `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` (17 in ES's context [M, R2g §2]; one more `SEND_NO_SUBS` use occurs in EVS's context, 18 in all, and its MsgId attribution is open [U]). The window's `NoSubscribersCounter` increments are cleared later when SB initializes its HK packet (`cfe_sb_task.c:164–166`; `CFE_MSG_Init` clears the message, `cfe_msg_init.c:45`) [I]; that is recorded as an observed consequence, not as a deviation, since the shall-text requires only the increment. Caller level: as in step 13 (TO_LAB's `defer_by_design`, or LOSS-UNDER-ORDER where no consumer reference speaks). Observed: "message lost" and "event rejected"; in this window the loss leaves no trace that survives it [I].
14. **Not claimed**:
   - that R2 is a race;
   - any count as a Kairos result before S1;
   - determinism from repeated runs;
   - that the certificate uses no framework rule;
   - that the uses are reached in every run (reach is a separate axis);
   - any progress statement;
   - that the checker verified the extracted facts or the skeleton.

15. **Expected record on a corrected-order control** (V2; pre-registered before any run; [I]). On a built basis in which SB's publication precedes a half's uses (P-R2CTRL options (c1)–(d)): FIXED_ORDER item 4 fails for that half, because the success store of SB's `CFE_ES_GetAppID` call (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`) now precedes the uses, so **fixed-order is not issued**. PROTECTED (b) fails as well, because `CFE_ES_GetAppID`'s failure branch stores `UNDEFINED` and SB ignores the return value (which clause fails is settled at S3a), so the verdict is **not protected**. That call is the App. A.2 K-SELF.es instance, which is open, so the half is **open, chained to the App. A.2 root**, with class `unknown` until S5/S7 and schedule-dependent(σ_U) with shape ABSENT expected after them. Natural runs give "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown". On a half whose order is unchanged, fixed-order is expected again; on a no-change control (option (b)), fixed-order is kept. Line numbers are v7.0.1's (failure store `cfe_es_api.c:758–760`) and are rebound per basis by the anchor audit (the store is at `cfe_es_api.c:741` at `v7.0.0`). For option (e), the lazy-resolution change, the expectation is written and frozen only after the code exists, because the halves it moves and whether the App. A.2 chain exists depend on the code written. For option (f), the model-level mutant, only the static part applies (no run, O4 or reach is exercised). Every such result is configuration or revision sensitivity, never schedule evidence and never evidence that the default configuration races.

### A.2 The core-task App-record window and the unpublished SB/TBL identity (INFERENCE until run; [J1 §1.1], [LED §3.4])

1. **Instances.** K-SELF.es at every context-resolving call made by a core task (and by children it creates in TaskInit) before `cfe_es_start.c:818`: EVS `GetAppID` (`cfe_evs_task.c:273`); ES's `CFE_EVS_Register` in `CFE_ES_TaskInit`; SB `GetAppID` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`); TBL (`cfe_tbl_task.c:196`); TIME's, incl. its `CreateChildTask` calls (`cfe_time_task.c:160, 173`); `ES_BG_TASK`'s creation (`cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:214`); every `WaitForSystemState` raise.
2. **Static.** `W` = `CFE_ES_AppRecordSetUsed(ρ, PendingAppId)` at `:818` (startup thread). The child's MB contains the spawn and, through T-OBS at `GetTaskFunction`, the task-record fill (`cfe_es_apps.c:694–703`), but not `:818`. No certificate either way → open, routed **O-ordered** (both LPs under the ES lock).
3. **Prediction.** From a default boot, reverse the startup thread's critical section at `:816–818` and the child's `GetAppID` critical section; the child's poll keeps its success outcome because the task record was filled earlier. A witness is expected → `schedule-dependent(σ_U, M_api and M_impl)`; A-COMM decides between `predicted` and `predicted-modelled`. Under σ_U∞ no timeout is involved → still schedule-dependent (no refinement). The EVS caller's `GetAppID` (`cfe_evs_task.c:273`) is tested, and its not-ready branch returns `Status` from `CFE_EVS_TaskInit` (`:274–278`), so every post-dominator of the test is the function's return, which returns the status: there is no default normal continuation (§1.6), and the return is a REJECT before `CFE_EVS_TaskMain` calls `CFE_ES_ExitApp(CORE_APP_INIT_ERROR)` (`:215–220`). The occurrence is VIOLATING, not TOLERATED. Progress under σ_U∞ [I], with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`): when a raise is skipped, the startup thread is dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` and the core tasks already created are dead in their `CORE_READY` polls (`cfe_es_task.c:134`); under σ_U the startup thread panics; that panic is this instance's safety consequence (harm `panic`), not a progress result, and it decides no horizon (§1.6). The instances behind it (the later core tasks, the script apps, the R2 and A.7 uses, TO_LAB's deferred K-ROUTE occurrences) have hang states there; each carries timeout-masked-liveness on its own cell and is attached by a progress chain to this instance, which carries `tml-root` and is what the report lists. This instance's own σ_U∞ progress can be `no-stall-within(b)`: its occurrence is decided at the call and has no horizon. At M_impl the same stall states can also arise from a core task lost to F7 (`osapi-task.c:128–137`), with that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence as the chain root. Under σ_R1[`P_sched^posix`] the startup thread outranks the tasks, so [I] the window cannot open on one CPU.
4. **Consequences** (every branch, §3.3): EVS (a1) `service_task_absent`, (a2) `panic` after 30 s, (b) `process_abort` after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` = 10 s (the abort precedes the 30 s panic); ES/SB/TBL/TIME raise skipped → `panic`; SB/TBL identity UNDEFINED for the run → every later SB/TBL `SendEventWithAppID` use bad with shape ABSENT, chained to this root (the root is reported, not the 211–229 dependents); TIME's children → `task_never_runs` if `CreateChildTask` fails.
5. **Confirmation (D16, derived).** Hold the startup thread after `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` returns (`cfe_es_start.c:810–814`) and before `CFE_ES_LockSharedData` at `:816`; its program-lock set is empty there. Release when the child's `GetAppID` returns.
   - Watchdog: the smallest reference timeout the hold can make expire — the already-created core tasks wait in `WaitForSystemState(CORE_READY, CFE_PLATFORM_CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC)` = 30000 ms (`default_cfe_core_private_internal_cfg.h:68`), and where the predicted continuation includes a restart request, `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` = 10000 ms (`pc-linux/inc/cfe_psp_config.h:57`) → 10000 ms for EVS's window.
   - Oracle: the `GetAppID` outcome; for SB, `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` on every later SB event; for EVS, which branch of the chain occurred (abort, panic or missing EVS task).
   - The run may end abnormally, so a **normalization boot** follows (§4.13). Labels: `realized`, `held-F0`, `derived`, with the perturbation ratio over the 50 ms poll separation.
6. **σ_E**: no natural bad witness is expected; the record reads "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown". No claim of timing protection.

### A.3 The script-app App-record window (correcting [B §7.2])

1. **Instances.** K-SELF.es at each script app's context-resolving calls (`CFE_EVS_Register` → caller context → `GetAppID`; `RunLoop`; `WaitForSystemState`; `CreateChildTask`, e.g. FM at `fm_child.c:86`); K-REG.es at peers' `GetAppIDByName`, whose oracle classifies the sentinel success as not-ready ([J1 §1 check 4]).
2. **Trace routing.** The publication (`cfe_es_apps.c:861–869`) and the use's LP are both under the ES lock: the **O-ordered** route to prediction, not "unordered → SHB" ([J1 §1 check 3]; [J3 §2.2]).
3. **Expected**: for K-SELF.es, `schedule-dependent(σ_U, σ_U∞)` at ordinal 1 (`RunLoop` runs on every iteration and so has several ordinals, but only ordinal 1 can fall in the window); for K-REG.es at a peer that calls `GetAppIDByName` periodically (HS's application monitor, `hs_monitors.c:69`, where its table is active; CS's application checksum, `cs_compute.c:307`, where enabled [I]), the ordinal that falls into the target's window varies by execution, so `schedule-dependent` needs a same-ordinal pair at some k and is otherwise `ordinal-split`; evidence `predicted` or `predicted-modelled`; harms per branch: registration failure → app error exit; `RunLoop` false with the window open → `app_zombie` plus, under σ_U, two soft-barrier timeouts; with the window closed → `app_error_exit`. Under σ_U∞ the soft barriers do not time out: on the zombie branch the startup thread's barrier poll (with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, `cfe_es_start.c:860–918`) and every app in `WaitForStartupSync` are dead (TO_LAB's deferred K-ROUTE occurrences then have a horizon stall, harm `hang_or_deadlock`), which gives hang states for the instances behind them; each carries timeout-masked-liveness and is attached by a progress chain to this instance, which carries `tml-root` [I]. σ_E: "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown".
4. **Confirmation** ([B §7.2], corrected placement). Hold the startup thread in `CFE_ES_AppCreate` after `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` returns (`:848–854`) and before the lock at `:861`; its lockset is empty there. Release when the child's first context-resolving call returns.
   - Watchdog: the smallest timeout among the waits of already-started actors that the held startup thread can make expire (their `WaitForSystemState`/`WaitForStartupSync` arguments, constant-evaluated; the script barrier `CFE_PLATFORM_ES_STARTUP_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT_MSEC` once the script phase has begun).
   - Oracle: `CFE_ES_ERR_RESOURCEID_NOT_VALID` (`cfe_es_api.c:757–761`); `RunLoop` returning false; the sentinel success for peers. Labelled derived; normalization boot if the run ends abnormally.

### A.4 C1 in a derived configuration, and the OSAL lock-leak path under M_impl

1. **Default B.** CF's lookup sits behind a non-empty `sem_name` test (`cf_cfdp.c:1270`); both default entries are empty (`cf_def_config.c:60, 78`) → `inactive(B)`; the proof is the folded path condition in `Sk^cert`.
2. **B′** (public-code-derived, [LED §1a]; D22): a non-empty name and a provider app that creates the counting semaphore in its init. From references: the names `BP0_tsem`/`BP1_tsem` (nasa/bp `b051eb0`, for another CF revision and table layout); the provider protocol, create in init (`OS_CountSemCreate` from `BP_FlowInit`, initial count 10); CF before the provider in BP's CI script; at v7.0.1 CF `15a871e` with 25 × 100 ms retries (`cf_internal_cfg.h` L172–L173, L184–L185) and CF's ENV startup entry at priority 70. `unspecified_by_reference` at v7.0.1: the provider's build (nasa/bp needs a TinyCBOR install, a mutable PSP branch and bplib) or a role-derived provider's code, name, entry, stack, priority and script position; the channel count; RT enforcement, CPU count and affinity; any provider delay (event-based holds only); the hang bound of step 5's declared experiment. Neither app waits on a phase → no MB relation → open. Observation shows either success (outcome-implied reads-from from the provider's publication, an O-edge through the countsem table lock) or `NAME_NOT_FOUND` followed by retries.
3. **M_api.**
   - **σ_U**: the retry loop is unrolled exactly to the effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`; exhaustion is reachable only after the last attempt. The explorer finds an execution in which every attempt precedes the creation (delays are yields) → `CF_INIT_SEM_ERR_EID` (`cf_cfdp.c` ~L1295) and engine initialization fails (consequence per [A §A.2]) → `schedule-dependent(σ_U)`. Prediction may extend an observed retry to the effective bound; such a witness is `predicted-extrapolated`.
   - **σ_U∞**:
     - **The loop under σ_U∞ at M_api.** The loop is delay-paced. The failing outcome of its last attempt is a time-based transition, so σ_U∞ removes it. At M_api the attempt is one transition (the lookup), so under σ_U∞ the last attempt is enabled exactly when the lookup would succeed.
     - **While the provider can still run.** No finite execution of σ_U∞ is bad: a not-ready attempt's monitor (`retry_until_ready`) can only ACCEPT, when a later attempt is READY, or stay OPEN, because its REJECT event, the exhaustion, is time-based. So `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds, which `exh` decides within b. Since `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent` and σ_U∞ reaches the use, the refinement is **timeout-dependent** (§1.8, L-REF) [I]. Without the explorer no refinement is established.
     - **At the buggy revision** (CF 2a024d8, a single attempt, a derived basis), the instance's Allowed entry is `returned_and_tested`. The test (`cf_cfdp.c:1016` @2a024d8) lies in the channel loop, which is unrolled exactly; its not-ready branch raises `CF_EID_ERR_INIT_SEM` and jumps to `err_out: return ret;` (`:1018–1021, 1052–1053` @2a024d8), which the success branch also reaches, and `ret` is returned. There is therefore no default normal continuation (§1.6), the return of the error is a REJECT, and CF then exits with `APP_ERROR` ([LED §1a]): the occurrence is VIOLATING without any time-based transition. Under the stricter reading in which every entry must cite its continuation (Q15), the entry is `unspecified_by_reference` and the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED, with the same class. It stays schedule-dependent under σ_U∞: the fix of C1 ([LED §1b]) turned an ordering-logic violation into a dependence on a timing margin.
     - **If the provider never runs**, no cFS basis is used (none has a reference); litmus L-PATIENT variant 2 (App. A.8) shows the result: the certificate's REJECT, the exhaustion, lies after the decision, so at b = ∞ the class is `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown in every σ; within b, `exh` gives fixed-order under σ_U (the exhausting execution is a bad witness) and `no-final-status` under σ_U∞; under σ_U∞ the retrying actor is dead at the call node of its last attempt (`stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})`, a hang), and σ_U escapes only by exhaustion, hence **timeout-masked-liveness** [I].
     - **At M_impl** the lookup is several transitions (§1.4), so the time-based transition is the branch at `cf_cfdp.c:1285` taken with `NAME_NOT_FOUND` in the last iteration; after a failing last lookup CF has released the type lock and is **stranded** (§1.8): its progress, and that of the actors it could enable (the startup thread's script barrier), is `unknown(unmodelled)`. A provider spawn lost to F7 is treated in step 4.
     - **A single early lookup** is `tolerated` (`retry_until_ready`, from `exh`).
4. **M_impl.** The loop is unrolled, not summarized. `OS_CountSemGetIdByName` uses a **by-name** token, so in the RESERVED window with the name visible it does not wait: it returns `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` at once (`osapi-idmap.c:401–404`), which ends CF's loop (`cf_cfdp.c:1285–1288`, [R01 §1.8]); by K-LOCKBAL the countsem table lock stays held (vs the documented invariant `:384–385`); the provider then blocks in `FinalizeNew`. CF's not-ready outcome `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` is outside its `retry_until_ready` codes, so its monitor gives REJECT at decision: `VIOLATING` (safety). No time-based transition is needed, so `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds at M_impl, and there is no timeout-dependent refinement there. CF has returned from the lookup that took the countsem type lock and then either exits through its init-failure path (its `CFE_ES_ExitApp` stores STOPPED and loops in `OS_TaskDelay`, `cfe_es_api.c:399, 410–413`) or, with a re-bind command in B.Input, re-enters an API that takes the same lock (through `CF_CmdEnableEngine`, another calling context and therefore a separate instance); either way the lock is leaked (§1.8). CF's own instance has no stall state from the leak: CF holds the lock and does not wait on it. The deadness test finds, as dependents chained to CF's VIOLATING attempt with one wait-for root (the countsem type lock): the provider dead in `FinalizeNew`; every later operation that takes the countsem type lock (create, delete, by-name lookup, GetInfo, and object iteration, `OS_ForEachObject*`, which takes every type's lock in GLOBAL mode, `osapi-idmap.c:1246–1256, 1360–1367`; not `OS_CountSemGive`, `Take` or `TimedWait`, which use `OS_LOCK_MODE_NONE`, `osapi-countsem.c:147, 168, 189`) blocked before its LP, e.g. SCH_LAB's `OS_CountSemCreate` (`sch_lab_app.c:167`) with its lookup at `:176` ahead; and `ES_BG_TASK`, dead in `CFE_ES_CleanupTaskResources` when it cleans up CF after the STOPPED store (`expire-stopped`; `CFE_ES_CleanUpApp`, `cfe_es_apps.c:1249`, calls it outside the ES lock at `:1508, 1565`, and it calls `OS_ForEachObject` at `:1759`), after which no app is cleaned up, restarted or reloaded. All are confirmable `leaked-lock` stall witnesses, harm `hang_or_deadlock` (litmus LP-LEAK, LP-CASE-C1). On the `NAME_NOT_FOUND` path (e.g. a provider spawn lost to F7), CF's last attempt is not atomic at M_impl, so under σ_U∞ CF is stranded after it: progress `unknown(unmodelled)`, and timeout-masked-liveness is not established at M_impl. The result is an **implementation-defect candidate** (F1). The M_api result for this instance is not transferred to σ_E (the cone is M_impl-relevant and the levels disagree): `portability-hazard` is not the label here, since the violation exists only under M_impl.
5. **Confirmation plans.** For (3): hold the provider before its create call (lockset empty) until CF's loop exit is observed; the provider is created after observation starts, so the holder auto-attaches at clone on the startup thread (§4.13), and that stop is recorded; watchdog = effective retry bound × effective delay. For (4), a **declared experiment**: hold the provider at `OS_CountSemCreate_Impl` entry (inside the RESERVED window, name visible, type lock **not** held, [R01 §1.5 step f]) until CF's lookup returns, then release; the oracle is a wait-for fact (the provider blocked on the countsem table lock by futex/wchan while CF has already returned). It confirms CF's VIOLATING occurrence and the leaked lock (K-LOCKBAL), which are safety results; it is a realized progress `stall-witness` only where it also shows permanence, i.e. CF has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock (§1.10 σ_E cells, §4.13). The harness kill of the deadlocked run is followed by a normalization boot. Labelled derived.
6. **Outcome partition** (per key; ordinal (CF, k) = attempt k; V3). A1 immediate success: (CF, 1) READY. A2 success after retry: (CF, 1 … k−1) TOLERATED, (CF, k) READY, k ≤ 25. A3 final failure with a late provider: (CF, 1 … 25) VIOLATING, all rejected by the exhaustion (`CF_INIT_SEM_ERR_EID`, `cf_cfdp.c:1295`). A4 final failure with no provider for the name: **no referenced basis exists** (the referenced provider creates both configured names, and a cFS basis without the provider has no reference); A4 is shown by litmus L-PATIENT variant 2 and LP-CASE-C1 (App. A.8) with the provider never running, or recorded as "not realized: no referenced configuration"; only on a basis that the PI sets under D22 as a recorded deviation is it realized, told apart statically by provider `none` for that key. A5, at M_impl only, the RESERVED hit: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` ends the loop at once (`cf_cfdp.c:1285–1288`), VIOLATING at the decision, with the lock-leak path of step 4. READY and TOLERATED at one ordinal are both good; schedule dependence needs a good and a bad witness at the same ordinal in one build, configuration and semantics. What each stage decides: S3a, the census (open), and A4 only on a D22 basis that realizes it; S1 + S2, the natural statuses per ordinal (`n of N` per arm), no class; S5, the reversal evidence of the S5 gate; S6, A3 under a hold (`held-F0`, `timeout-exercised`, `σ_E(held)`) and A5 by the declared experiment (P-C1HOLD); S7, the classes and refinements of step 3 and the stalls of step 4.

### A.5 Startup-burst capacity: `SBNSubPipe` (K-MODE + K-CAP + K-CAP.msglim, LOSS-UNDER-ORDER)

1. **Instances** (re-stated per [X2-05], [X2-06]).
   - **K-MODE**: SBN transmits `ENABLE_SUB_REPORTING` and `SEND_PREV_SUBS` to SB's command pipe (`sbn_subs.c:43–60`); SB's main task flips `sb.mode.subreport` (LP `cfe_sb_task.c:1226`, reached from `:296`) and runs the replay `CFE_SB_SendPrevSubsCmd` (`:1169`) as a transmit loop in **SB's actor**. Subscribers that subscribe after the flip read the flag after the SB unlock without a lock (`cfe_sb_task.c:659`, `assumed`) and transmit a `ONESUB` report from **their own task** (`cfe_sb_api.c:1192–1195`). The chain runs through R7 on SB's command pipe, with K-CAP and K-CAP.msglim instances for that pipe.
   - **K-CAP** at each put into `SBNSubPipe` (effective capacity 10, [BM §3.1]); the puts come from SB's replay loop and from subscriber tasks (TO_LAB's 37 global subscriptions from its table, [R03 §2.3, §2.15]).
   - **K-CAP.msglim** for SBN's subscriptions to the report MsgIds, with the limit constant-evaluated at SBN's subscribe sites.
2. **Tier 1.** The maximum-weight-closure backlog bound over the certificate-view MB order of puts and gets exceeds 10 (TO_LAB alone can add up to 37 reports, and the replay loop is star-closed), so there is no protected certificate → open. The attribution of the burst between SB's replay and the subscribers' reports was an inference in [R03 §5.2] ([J2 §7]); the census's instance identification checks it.
3. **σ_E.** Overflow was observed in 5 of 6 M1 runs, and the sixth (A3) showed a different interleaving with `TO_LAB_TLM_PIPE` overflowing instead ([BM §3.3]). These are prior observations by the M1 harness (D12); Kairos's recorder re-observes them in S1/S2.
4. **Verdict.** The puts into `SBNSubPipe` from one template have occurrence ordinals (e.g. TO_LAB's reports, from one looped subscribe template). `schedule-dependent(σ_E)` needs a same-ordinal pair: some put ordinal k delivered in one natural run and overflowing in another, e.g. a run like A3 against one of the five overflowing runs [I]. One overflowing run, in which early puts are delivered and later ones overflow, gives only `ordinal-split(σ_E)`. Either is transferred up to σ_U as witnesses; label LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm `unspecified_by_reference` (caller level); framework level: cSB4305.3, `documented-result`; observed: the per-run `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` values ([M] 33, 37, 4, 49, 45, 41 in six M1 runs, [BM §0]). Oracles: per-put outcomes, `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` and `MsgLimitErrorCounter`; `PeakQueueDepth` only as SB-accounted depth. Part of RQ5.

### A.6 HS system monitor: provider `env-absent` (correcting [A], [R03 §3 row 20])

`HS_SysMonInit` finds the `linux_sysmon` device: the module is in the PSP's fixed list (`psp_module_list.cmake:9`), so `FindByName` succeeds; a failure there would be handled (`hs_sysmon.c:50–54`). The `SET_RUNNING` command then fails, and HS returns `CFE_STATUS_EXTERNAL_RESOURCE_FAIL` (`:65–72`), because `/proc/schedstat` is absent on this kernel ([R01 §2.6]; [R07 §1]).

Φ declares the device's RUNNING transition guarded by `env(/proc/schedstat present)`, and B.Env records it absent. Outcome mining finds the not-ready code, and O4 agrees → provider `env-absent`. With no writer enabled in B, every reaching execution has the not-ready outcome: class `fixed-order` with outcome label `not-ready-unjudged` (Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference`), reach proved by the observed failure in (σ_U, M_impl); at M_api only by the M_api-conformance check of the prefix of every closed-cone actor (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2), because the closed cone contains HS's spawn chain with task creation (an M_impl-relevant operation), so L-MLEVEL does not apply, as for R2 and LP-DL; under σ_U∞ only if the cone actors' prefix shows no barrier timeout and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake (§4.7 item 6), else `assumed`. This is not an order problem. It is reported separately, with the measured consequences: HS init stops at `HS_SysMonInit`; its startup sync is skipped; CPU utilisation is reported as `0xFFFFFFFF` ([BM §0]).

### A.7 Calibration: timebase lookups are protected outside teardown (safety statements)

`cFS-Master` is created and started by the PSP's module init on the main thread before `CFE_ES_Main` (§3.5). `x*` (its publication LP, name constant `"cFS-Master"`) is a `W^must` entry with `MUST_KEY`, COMPUTED, and is in MB of every cFE task entry by T-PO on the PSP main thread and T-SPAWN.

Disablers of `osal.obj<timebase>("cFS-Master")` (fixes [X1-M6], [X2-04]):
- `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (`cfe_psp_start.c:507`): `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB` by T-OBS on the `ShutdownReq` loop → **SCOPE-excluded**;
- any `OS_TimeBaseDelete` site found by the census: none is expected in the default B [I]; any found must be PWB-ordered, or the certificate is withheld.

TIME's and SCH_LAB's lookups (`cfe_time_task.c:311`, `sch_lab_app.c:176`) are therefore `protected(σ_U, M_api)` and, since the record is already LIVE when any lookup runs (`x*` is the final publication), `protected(σ_U, M_impl)` [I], as safety statements (`¬NR_i`: every reached lookup is READY). The σ_U∞ and σ_R1 cells follow by transfer rule 1(a), and the σ_E cell follows from M_impl by transfer rule 2. Progress is not claimed: it is `unknown(not_checked)` in every certified cell. With the explorer at M_api, a σ_U∞ stall state for these lookups is reachable only behind a skipped core raise, which leaves the startup thread dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` with TIME's and SCH_LAB's spawns ahead [I] (with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, `cfe_es_start.c:860–918`, §6.2; with L1 switched off the result is `unknown(unmodelled)`); it is chained to the App. A.2 root, which carries `tml-root`. At M_impl it is also reachable behind a core task lost to F7 (its `OS_TaskPrepare` fails and it calls `OS_TaskExit`, `osapi-task.c:128–137`), chained to that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence. These statements are relative to default B: in C1's B′ at M_impl, SCH_LAB's lookup also lies behind a leaked countsem type lock (its `OS_CountSemCreate` at `sch_lab_app.c:167`), a `leaked-lock` stall chained to CF's VIOLATING attempt (A.4 step 4). A lost timebase handshake does not affect these lookups: they run in TaskInit and AppInit before `OS_TimerAdd` and wait for no callback (`cfe_time_task.c:311`, `sch_lab_app.c:176`, re-read); it affects only the 1 Hz pipeline and SCH_LAB's loop (§3.13). All are stated **outside teardown**. SCH_LAB's lookup may run after `CFE_ES_Main` returns past a soft timeout (`cfe_es_start.c:225–228`); it is still in scope and still protected, because teardown needs a shutdown request. Command-triggered resets need no special treatment: their teardown disablers are SCOPE-excluded too, and with an empty B.Input the reset handlers are inactive by message provenance anyway. The timebase thread's own handshake (K-SPAWN.osal, §3.3) is a separate instance. This is the check that the tool does not invent races where the code orders.

### A.8 Litmus programs added by the reviews (gcc-built C99 on the real OSAL; expected classes per σ and M)

| Litmus | Program shape | Expected result | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L-H2** (fixes [X1-C1]) | a looper whose handler for message M1 sets a flag; its handler for M2 creates resource R only if the flag is set; another actor sends M1 then M2 (in-B transmits); a consumer looks R up | provider `present`, not `none`; not `inactive`; no certificate in either direction from `Sk^cert`; explorer: with h = 1 the writer is not reached, `Q_bad` gives a bad witness, and `¬OK_i` is withheld by safety-side censoring (the looper's region beyond the cut contains the writer), so the class within b = 1 is `unknown(bound)` with the bad witness recorded; with h = 2 schedule-dependent at the consumer's single ordinal | the certificate view star-closes the loop; `kz proofcheck` rejects a fabricated certificate that names a node beyond h |
| **L-ONCE** (X1-M2) | a spawn site inside a loop creates two instances of one entry; a frontier argument would put a use in instance 1 before a writer in instance 2 | once(τ) fails; frontier and once-PWB forbidden; schedule-dependent | the once analysis and its proofcheck re-validation |
| **L-OWN** (X2-01) | an observer with a falsifier and a writer on a failure branch that ends in `abort()` | the certificate passes with OWN-NOPATH; a variant in which the failure branch rejoins the observation must lose the certificate | OWN-NOPATH and its reachability re-check |
| **L-SCOPE** (X1-M6) | a disabler that runs only after a shutdown-request store; a use before and a use after the store | the first use protected outside teardown; the second a K-TEARDOWN instance | SCOPE exclusion |
| **L-PROV** (X1-M7) | a command-only handler that writes a resource; B.Input empty vs one command | inactive vs active | message provenance and its fixpoint |
| **L-CAPTURE** (X1-M1) | a value loaded from a shared cell, then the publisher writes, then the API checks the copy | the outcome follows the capture point; O4 agrees; a predictor that targeted the check LP would accept a false good witness | per-conjunct LPs and `derives` |
| **L-PATIENT** (X1-M5; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2) | a delay-paced retry with a provider; variant with a provider that never runs (a unit-test condition) | **Variant 1** at M_api: σ_U is schedule-dependent at ordinal 1 (attempt 1 READY in one execution, VIOLATING by the exhaustion's REJECT in another); σ_U∞ is protected with `tolerated` (`exh`) and reached, because the last attempt is one transition and waits until it succeeds → timeout-dependent (`exh` only); progress is `no-stall-within(b)` in both σ. At M_impl, if the attempt is an OSAL by-name lookup: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` decides an attempt VIOLATING without a time-based transition, so `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` and no timeout-dependent; the leaked type lock gives `leaked-lock` stall states to the provider and later lock-taking operations; on the `NAME_NOT_FOUND` path the actor can be stranded (`unknown(unmodelled)` in its closure). **Variant 2**: at b = ∞ the certificate's `¬OK_i` (later attempts excluded from ACCEPT by its INIT_FALSE and empty writer set) has no decision route, because its REJECT, the exhaustion, lies after the decision: `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown in σ_U and σ_U∞; within b, σ_U is fixed-order (`exh`: the exhausting execution) and σ_U∞ `no-final-status` (every reached attempt stays `PENDING`); progress at M_api under σ_U∞ is `stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})`, a hang (the retrying actor is dead at the call node of its last attempt) → σ_U cell flags timeout-masked-liveness and `tml-root`; at M_impl, if the attempt is an OSAL lookup, the actor is stranded → `unknown(unmodelled)`. **Variant 3** (a retry paced by `OS_BinSemTimedWait` on a semaphore nobody gives, provider absent): the exhaustion is not time-based, but each timed wait is; σ_U `unknown(no_decision_route)` at b = ∞ and fixed-order within b; σ_U∞ `no-final-status` within b and `unknown(no_decision_route)` at b = ∞ (no σ_U∞ execution is bad, although the REJECT event itself is not time-based); progress σ_U∞ `stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})` → TML and `tml-root` on σ_U | σ_U∞ without time-based transitions at the deciding transition per M; horizon monitors; decision routes only from REJECTs at the decision; per-bound safety records; deadness; stall clause sets; stranding; refinements |
| **L-MIMPL** (X1-C2) | a by-name lookup racing a creation (RES_CN window) | M_api protected only if certified; M_impl: `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` reachable; no σ_E transfer from M_api; an observed `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` yields `portability-hazard-evidence`, not a model fault | transfer rule 2; O4(b) at M_api |
| **L-L3** (X1-M10) | a critical section containing a cancel-and-join of another thread (as in `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask`) | the L3 check fails; the section stays fine-grained; the cancellation-with-lock-held execution is reachable | mover conditions |
| **L-TIMEDWAIT** (X2-03) | a by-ID lookup of a RESERVED record with unrelated unlocks of the same type; a variant whose creator is held or lost before `FinalizeNew` | σ_U: exhaustion by timed or broadcast wakes. σ_U∞: exhaustion by broadcast wakes only. While the creator can still finalize, its finalizing unlock broadcasts and wakes the waiter to a LIVE record (the wait loop re-checks the record after every wake), so there is no stall. In the variant, from a state in which no further unlock of the type is reachable, the waiter is dead under σ_U∞: an in-use stall, because the waiter is inside its call and the deciding check is still ahead [I]; σ_U escapes by the timed wake (`timeout-masked-liveness`) | the two wake causes; time-based transitions; deadness relative to the remaining unlocks |
| **LP-DL** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | (a) `loc` VALID at INIT, with no writer or disabler. A once actor T0 creates mutexes `m1` and `m2` and spawns τ_A and τ_B. τ_A: take `m1`, take `m2`, use `loc`, give `m2`, give `m1`. τ_B: take `m2`, take `m1`, give `m1`, give `m2`. `OS_MutSemTake` has no timeout. (b) τ_A runs its body twice (constant-trip loop, unrolled) and gives a semaphore `go` after each iteration; τ_B first takes `go` | **Certificate only**: `protected(σ_U, M)` (b = ∞, `¬NR_i`), reach `proved` or `assumed` per cell, progress `unknown(not_checked)`. **`exh`**: protected within b; progress `stall-witness({pre-use})`, kind `deadlock-cycle`, wait-for root the cycle, confirmable, harm `hang_or_deadlock`, in (a) and, at ordinal (τ_A, 2), in (b). The same in σ_U∞ (recomputed) and at M_impl. σ_E: the safety statement by rule 2; progress `unknown(not_checked)` unless a run hangs with a wait-for cycle. Never "protected" without its progress value, and never `no-stall-within(b)` | L-PR's scope; progress candidates with all four queries; the closed cone includes the locks on τ_A's path, so τ_B is in the cone; **L2 keeps τ_B's inner `m1` section** (its lock is a cone object acquired by τ_A), which a body-only L2 condition would remove; L3 keeps τ_A's `m1` section fine-grained; per-occurrence pre-use stalls (b); product states; the rendering rule |
| **LP-TW** (EXT-20261012 §4.2) | (a) W waits `OS_BinSemTimedWait(s, t)` on a semaphore that no actor gives, then uses `loc2` (VALID at INIT) on both outcomes. (b) Bases `B_give`/`B_skip` select whether P gives `s`; W's success branch gives `s3`, its timeout branch calls `abort()`; V takes `s3` without a timeout and then uses `loc2` (V's use is the instance). (c) W takes `s` without a timeout and then uses `loc2`; only a litmus teardown actor X, which runs after a litmus SHUTDOWN_REQ store made on B.Input's stop event, gives `s` | **(a)** σ_U: protected, reach `model(b)` through the timeout and `proved` by a run at M_impl, progress `no-stall-within(b)`, flags `timeout-rescued` and timeout-masked-liveness (with `tml-root`, since no chain leads into W's instance). σ_U∞: protected at b = ∞ (rule 1(a)), `no-final-status` within b, reach `unreached(b)`, progress `stall-witness({pre-use})`, a hang; timeout-dependent not set (not reached). σ_E: safety statement by rule 2; reach proved. **(b)** σ_U: `stall-witness({pre-use})` qualified `terminal-pending`, harm `terminal_pending(abort)` (not a hang), in both bases; post-abort states are excluded sinks. σ_U∞: in `B_skip` a hang (`hang_or_deadlock`), so σ_U has timeout-masked-liveness; in `B_give` `no-stall-within(b)`. **(c)** every σ: W is dead for the default-scope instance (excluded states are sinks), a hang of kind `other`, not terminal-pending (the stop input is not counted); the use after X's give is a K-TEARDOWN instance | prefix containment holds, while maximal containment fails; transfer rules 1–3 give one consistent record per cell and bound; refinements as derived predicates; the unified process-terminal list, the exclusion, the sink rule and the terminal-pending qualifier |
| **LP-LEAK** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | (a) M_impl: P creates counting semaphore "S"; La looks "S" up by name once, gives `b` and exits; Lb takes `b` without a timeout, then looks "S" up. (b) A two-conjunct litmus contract at a call `use_iu(x)` by U: `x` is a capture of `loc3`; the body takes `m3` untimed, reads the registry `r`, gives `m3`; H takes `m3` and waits forever on a semaphore nobody gives | **(a)** La's lookup: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` in P's RESERVED window → REJECT at decision (outside `codes`), VIOLATING; schedule-dependent at ordinal (La, 1). Lb's lookup: `stall-witness({in-use})` (Lb is inside its call), kind `leaked-lock` (the non-recursive type lock's holder La returned and exited), wait-for root the type lock, confirmable, harm `hang_or_deadlock`, progress chain from La's lookup. M_api: no leak, no stall; levels disagree → implementation-defect candidate. **(b)** `stall-witness({in-use})`, kind `other` (H has a path to its release), not confirmable | per-occurrence occurrences that begin at the call; the in-use clause; leaked-lock by holder state; progress chains; the M_api/M_impl split |

(early-W): W can pass before `cnt` is set, a TB-free bad execution → `Bad_i(σ_U∞)`, no timeout-dependent; TML still holds | an API that tests internally before propagating, then an ignored status; ignored status; NOT_READY_UNJUDGED; ordinals with only ordinal 1 in the window; a progress chain with a not-ready root; a counter race that must be excluded before timeout-dependent is claimed |

**Completion conditions of EXT-20261012.**
- **§4.1.** LP-DL has a deadlock before the use while readiness at the use is proved. No route yields an unconditional protected: every cell carries its reach value and its progress value (`unknown(not_checked)` or a stall witness), and G2 and §6.4 item 26 state that the certificate makes no progress claim. The looper variant and the L2 lock condition make sure that `exh` never prints `no-stall-within(b)` for it.
- **§4.2.** LP-TW progresses with timeouts and stops without them. Its σ_U∞ finite executions are σ_U finite executions, while its σ_U∞-maximal execution is not σ_U-maximal. The transfer rules carry only safety statements down and recompute reach and progress per σ, and the classes are computed per bound from the combined record, so the σ_U and σ_U∞ cells do not contradict each other in any order of evaluation.
- **Beyond the two conditions.** LP-SCOPE2, LP-EARLY, LP-ALLOW (with the real code shapes (b6)–(b9), (d3), (d4)), LP-RESTART, L-PATIENT variant 3 and LP-CASE-C1/C2/C3/C5 make each Allowed kind and each design case's mechanism and code shape checkable on the v7.0.1 OSAL; they are evidence about the definitions, not about the cases.
